Discord 워크플로 기본 설정 동시성: 워커 0292의 워크플로 기본 설정 쓰기 잠금을 사용하며 업데이트된 Discord 워커는 설정을 읽기 전에 워크플로 공유 잠금을 잡아 발송 예약 트랜잭션이 끝날 때까지 유지한다. 설정 변경이 먼저 잠금을 잡고 커밋하면 그 설정을 사용하고, 예약이 먼저 잠금을 잡으면 설정 변경은 커밋을 기다리며 이미 예약된 외부 발송을 취소하지 않는다. 발송당 추가 DB 왕복은 없다. 설정 변경 트리거는 모든 채널의 설정 쓰기에 잠금을 추가하므로 대기·데드록과 대량 업데이트 영향을 검토한다. 적용 시 구버전 Discord 워커를 중지하고 워커 0292와 새 워커를 배포한다. 구버전, 특권자의 트리거 우회, 복구로 유실된 DB 상태 및 다른 미검증 채널에는 보장되지 않는다. 격리 PostgreSQL에서 API 트리거·Discord 큐 처리·모의 공급자로 설정 변경 선행 시 발송 중단, 예약 선행 시 설정 변경 대기 및 기존 발송의 완료를 시험했다. 저장소 루트에서 `TMPDIR=/tmp TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-discord-workflow-local.sh`로 재현한다. 실제 Discord·높은 경합·다중 호스트 운영 HA는 미검증이다.

Telegram 워크플로 기본 설정 동시성: 워커 0292는 `novu_rust_workflow_preferences`의 삽입·수정·삭제 전에 관련 워크플로 행을 잠그는 트리거를 추가한다. 업데이트된 Telegram 워커는 설정을 읽기 전에 워크플로 행에 공유 잠금을 획득하고 예약 트랜잭션이 끝날 때까지 유지한다. 제한 DB 모드의 보안 정의 함수에도 같은 순서를 적용한다. 설정 변경이 먼저 잠금을 잡아 커밋하면 이후 예약은 새 설정을 읽는다. 예약이 먼저 잠금을 잡으면 설정 변경은 예약 커밋을 기다리며 이미 예약된 외부 발송은 취소하지 않는다. 워커의 기존 워크플로 조회 순서를 바꾸며 발송당 DB 왕복을 추가하지 않는다. 트리거는 모든 채널의 워크플로 기본 설정 쓰기에 잠금을 추가하므로 대기·데드록과 대량 업데이트 영향을 검토해야 한다. 트리거 소유자는 워크플로 테이블의 행 잠금 권한을 보유하고 보호되어야 한다. 적용 시 구버전 Telegram 워커를 모두 중지하고 0292와 새 워커를 배포한다. 구버전·특권자의 트리거 비활성화·직접 변경, 복구로 유실된 DB 상태 및 다른 미검증 채널에는 이 보호를 보장하지 않는다. Discord는 위 항목을 참고한다. 격리 PostgreSQL에서 제한·일반 실제 워커의 설정 선행 변경과 모의 공급자 미발송, 예약 선행 시 설정 변경 대기, 제한 설정 작성자와 워크플로 삭제 연쇄 롤백을 검증했다. API 트리거부터 워크플로 발송까지의 기존 시험도 통과했다. 워커 디렉터리의 `TMPDIR=/tmp TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-telegram-local.sh`와 저장소 루트의 `TMPDIR=/tmp TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-telegram-workflow-local.sh`로 재현한다. 실제 Telegram·높은 경합·다중 호스트 운영 HA는 미검증이다.

Telegram 발송 전 조치 API 장애 전환: `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=telegram-api-predispatch scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh`는 제한 DB 로그인·시험 JWT를 사용하는 실제 API 프로세스와 로컬 동기 복제 PostgreSQL을 사용한다. 이전에 커밋된 재시도를 복제한 후, 별도 트랜잭션이 실패 작업을 잠그고 실제 취소 HTTP 요청이 그 잠금을 기다리는 동안 주 서버를 중단·복제본을 수동 승격한다. 요청은 성공 응답을 받지 못했고 DB에 취소가 없었다. API 재연결 후 같은 요청 ID로 조회·제출·재제출했을 때 취소와 감사가 하나씩 기록됐고 이전 재시도는 중복 변경 없이 재응답했다. 권한 없는 시험 JWT의 접근도 거절됐다. 또 다른 재시도·취소는 실제 API가 커밋하고 반환한 성공 응답을 로컬 프록시에서 버려 클라이언트가 응답을 받지 못하도록 했다. 두 감사가 복제된 뒤 승격하고 같은 요청을 제출해 중복 변경 없이 재응답함을 확인했다. 단일 호스트에서 응답 유실과 장애 전환을 순서대로 통제한 시험이며 실제 네트워크 장애·자동 승격·비동기 복제·분할 뇌·실제 IdP·다중 호스트 운영 HA는 미검증이다.

Telegram 발송 전 조치 장애 전환: `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=telegram-predispatch scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh`는 자체 생성한 로컬 PostgreSQL 주·동기 대기 서버와 제한 로그인으로 재시도·취소를 기록하고, 두 작업 상태와 감사가 대기 서버에 재생된 뒤 주 서버를 즉시 중단해 대기 서버를 수동 승격한다. 승격 후 같은 요청의 재제출이 재응답되고 감사가 한 건씩 보존된다. 별도의 제한 로그인 트랜잭션에서 재시도와 취소를 실행하고 커밋 전 주 서버를 중단하는 경우, 승격된 DB에 두 변경과 감사가 모두 보이지 않고 같은 요청을 다시 제출하면 각각 한 번 기록된다. 새로 시작한 제한 권한 실제 워커 두 개가 재시도 작업만 모의 Telegram에 한 번씩 발송하는지 확인한다. 첫 실행은 디스크 부족으로 WAL 생성에 실패했고 재생성 가능한 테스트 실행 파일을 정리한 후 통과했다. 단일 호스트의 통제된 DB 트랜잭션 시험이며 자동 승격·비동기 복제·분할 뇌·실제 Telegram·다중 호스트 운영 HA는 미검증이다.

Telegram 발송 전 조치 이력: 워커 0291 적용 후 `delivery-reconcile telegram-predispatch-history ENV JOB [AFTER_REQUEST AFTER_ACTION]`으로 재시도·취소 기록을 한 페이지 50건씩 조회한다. DBA는 로그인에 `public.novu_rust_telegram_predispatch_history(text,uuid,uuid,text)` EXECUTE와 기존 환경별 `novu_rust_telegram_history_viewers` 등록을 부여한다. 함수 소유자는 조회 권한 테이블과 두 감사 테이블의 SELECT가 필요하다. 기존 Telegram 이력 조회자의 정보 범위가 확대되며 사유와 DB 로그인/OID가 노출되므로 권한을 검토한다. 응답 `next`의 요청 ID와 조치 종류를 함께 다음 인자로 사용한다. 같은 UUID가 두 종류에 있을 수 있어 양쪽이 필요하다. UUID 순서는 시간순이 아니며 각 페이지는 별도 시점이다. API를 통한 조치의 DB 요청 ID는 JWT 요청 ID와 다르며 이 CLI는 JWT 운영자를 식별하지 않는다. 마이그레이션의 인덱스 생성은 쓰기를 지연시킬 수 있다. 격리 PostgreSQL·제한 로그인·실제 CLI에서 권한 회수, 환경 격리, 동일 UUID가 걸친 페이지와 64비트 리비전을 검증했다. `TMPDIR=/tmp TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-telegram-local.sh`로 재현한다. API 0196과 관리 화면은 JWT 요청의 운영자 연결을 제공하며 [API 문서](../../api/rust-api/README.md)를 참고한다. 이력 보존 정책과 실제 IdP·운영 HA는 미구현 또는 미검증이다.

Telegram 발송 전 실패 취소: 워커 0290을 적용하고 DBA가 취소 로그인에 `public.novu_rust_telegram_predispatch_cancel(text,uuid,uuid,bigint,text)` EXECUTE와 `novu_rust_telegram_predispatch_cancel_grants`의 환경·로그인 이름·OID를 별도로 등록한다. 함수 소유자는 권한 테이블과 큐·시도 조회, 취소 감사 삽입 및 큐 갱신 권한이 필요하다. `delivery-reconcile cancel-telegram-predispatch ENV REQUEST JOB REVISION REASON`은 실패 중인 Telegram 작업을 잠그고 리비전 일치와 시도 기록 부재를 검증한 뒤 사유·로그인과 함께 `cancelled`로 전환한다. 같은 요청 ID의 같은 입력은 재응답하고 충돌이나 권한 회수 후 재제출은 거절한다. 재시도와 취소가 경합하면 하나만 성공한다. 취소된 작업은 기존 Telegram 실패 경보 집계 및 목록에서 제외되지만, 기록 유실 가능성 때문에 실제 미발송을 보증하지 않으며 이미 시작된 외부 발송을 취소하지 않는다. 감사 사유에 비밀이나 개인정보를 넣지 않는다. 격리 PostgreSQL·제한 로그인·CLI·실제 워커와 모의 공급자에서 권한, 리비전, 기존 시도, 감사 실패 롤백, 경합, 집계 감소와 취소 작업 미발송을 확인했다. `TMPDIR=/tmp TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-telegram-local.sh`로 재현한다. JWT API는 [API 문서](../../api/rust-api/README.md)를 참고한다. 취소 화면과 브라우저 시험은 [콘솔 문서](../../dashboard/swallow/README.md)를 참고한다. CLI 이력은 위 항목을 참고하며 이력 보존, 실제 Telegram·운영 HA는 미구현 또는 미검증이다.

Telegram 발송 전 실패 재시도: 워커 0289와 `delivery-reconcile retry-telegram-predispatch ENV REQUEST JOB REVISION REASON`은 운영자가 확인한 실패 리비전의 작업을 다시 pending으로 바꾼다. DBA는 호출 로그인에 `public.novu_rust_telegram_predispatch_retry(text,uuid,uuid,bigint,text)` EXECUTE 및 기존 환경별 Telegram `can_retry`를 부여한다. 함수 소유자에게 접근 검사 함수와 큐·시도 조회 및 감사 테이블 삽입·큐 갱신 권한이 필요하다. 기존 `can_retry`의 권한 범위가 확대된다. 요청 ID·작업·리비전·사유·DB 로그인/OID를 원자적으로 기록하며 동일 요청은 중복 변경 없이 재응답하고 입력이나 호출자가 다르면 거절한다. 권한 회수 후 재조회용 재제출도 거절된다. 실패 작업의 행을 잠그고 동일 리비전과 시도 기록 부재를 확인한다. 이 기록은 실제 미발송의 증거가 아니므로 DB 복구나 특권자 변경 등으로 이력이 유실되었다면 중복 발송이 가능하다. 원인이 계속되거나 수신자·설정이 바뀌면 워커가 다시 실패할 수 있으며, 큐에 넣는 것은 발송 성공을 뜻하지 않는다. 조회 JSON의 `failureRevision`은 64비트 정수이므로 JavaScript 등 정밀도가 제한된 소비자는 값을 직접 숫자로 변환하지 말아야 한다. API 0193은 정확한 문자열 리비전과 재시도·요청 조회를 제공한다. 임시 PostgreSQL·제한 로그인·CLI와 실제 워커·모의 공급자에서 권한 회수, 다른 환경, 오래된 리비전, 기존 시도, 동시 중복 요청, 감사 실패 롤백 및 후속 발송을 검증했다. 재현은 `TMPDIR=/tmp TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-telegram-local.sh`. Angular 재시도 화면에서 실제 워커와 모의 Telegram까지 확인했으며 재현은 [콘솔 문서](../../dashboard/swallow/README.md)를 참고한다. 별도 확인 처리, 감사 조회·보존, 실제 Telegram 및 운영 HA는 미구현 또는 미검증이다.

Telegram 발송 전 실패 조회: 워커 마이그레이션 0288 적용 후 조회 로그인에 `public.novu_rust_telegram_predispatch_list(text,uuid)` EXECUTE와 기존 환경별 Telegram 운영자 `can_view`를 부여한다. 함수 소유자는 기존 접근 검사 함수와 큐·시도 테이블 조회 권한이 필요하다. `delivery-reconcile list-telegram-predispatch ENV [AFTER_JOB]`은 실패 상태이며 시도 기록이 없는 독립·워크플로 Telegram 작업의 ID, 종류, 선점 횟수, 실패 리비전과 허용된 진단만 UUID 순으로 50건씩 반환한다. 알려지지 않은 오류는 `other`로 표시하고 본문·주소·임의 오류는 반환하지 않는다. 다음 페이지에는 `next`를 사용하며 페이지마다 조회 시점이 다르다. 기존 `can_view` 권한으로 새 정보가 공개되므로 권한 보유자를 검토한다. 시도 기록이 없다는 것은 이 DB에 기록이 없다는 뜻이며 데이터 손실이나 특권자의 변경 이후 미발송을 증명하지 않는다. API 0192와 Angular 화면에서도 조회할 수 있으며 설정은 [API README](../../api/rust-api/README.md)를 참고한다. CLI 재시도는 위 항목을 참고한다. 임시 PostgreSQL과 제한 로그인·실제 CLI에서 환경 격리, 권한 회수, 민감정보 제외, 기존 시도 제외와 52건 페이지를 검증했다. `TMPDIR=/tmp TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-telegram-local.sh`로 재현한다. 실제 Telegram·운영 부하 및 다중 호스트 HA는 미검증이다.

Telegram 워크플로 장애 전환 시험: `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=telegram-inflight scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh`는 격리된 로컬 PostgreSQL 주 서버와 동기 대기 서버를 만들고 제한 DB 로그인으로 실제 Telegram 워커 두 개를 실행한다. 모의 공급자 요청이 진행 중일 때 `dispatching` 시도가 대기 서버에 재생된 것을 확인하고 주 서버를 즉시 중단한 뒤 대기 서버를 수동 승격한다. 시험은 임대를 강제로 만료시키며 기존 작업이 `failed`/`unknown`으로 복구되고 재발송되지 않는 것, 두 워커의 재연결 및 새 작업 처리를 확인한다. 모의 공급자 응답과 승격 사이 경합 결과는 실제 배달 여부를 증명하지 않는다. 단일 호스트·동기 복제의 통제된 경우이며 실제 Telegram, 자동 승격, 비동기 복제, 네트워크 분리·분할 뇌 및 운영 HA는 검증하지 않았다. 스크립트는 자체 생성한 임시 DB만 중단한다.

Twilio 웹훅 토큰 상태 경보: API의 선택형 `/health/twilio-webhook-bundle`을 활성화하고 `SWALLOW_TWILIO_WEBHOOK_KEY_PROBE_URL`에 해당 URL을 지정해 기존 `send-database-alert` 또는 database 모드 fanout·scheduler로 Slack·메일 경보를 보낼 수 있다. DB·운영자 키·Resend 키 검사와 동시에 지정할 수 없다. URL은 HTTPS 또는 로컬 루프백 HTTP만 허용하며 자격증명·쿼리·리다이렉트를 거절한다. API 인스턴스와 검사 유형별로 고유한 대상 ID를 사용한다. 기본 대상 ID는 `twilio-key-<label>`이다. DB·운영자 키·Resend 키 검사와 상태 비트 20을 공유하므로 대상 ID 재사용 시 경보가 억제되거나 복구 상태가 섞일 수 있다. 경보 발송 결과가 불명이면 자동 재발송하지 않는다. 제한 DB 계정·임시 PostgreSQL·실제 API·스케줄러에서 파일 오류·복구와 중복 억제를, 모의 Slack·Resend에서 경보 본문을 확인했다. 실제 공급자 수신·다중 호스트 HA는 미검증이다. 재현은 `TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-resend-webhook-bundle-local.sh`.

Resend 웹훅 키 상태 경보: API의 선택형 `/health/resend-webhook-bundle`을 활성화하고 `SWALLOW_RESEND_WEBHOOK_KEY_PROBE_URL`에 해당 URL을 지정해 `send-database-alert` 또는 database 모드 fanout·scheduler로 Slack·메일을 보낼 수 있다. DB 검사 URL이나 운영자 키 검사 URL과 동시 지정할 수 없다. URL은 HTTPS 또는 로컬 루프백 HTTP만 허용하고 자격증명·쿼리·리다이렉트를 거절한다. 각 API 인스턴스와 검사 유형에 고유한 대상 ID를 사용한다. 기본 대상 ID는 `resend-key-<label>`이다. 기존 DB·운영자 키 검사와 상태 비트 20을 공유하므로 대상 ID를 재사용하면 경보 억제·복구가 섞인다. 파일 오류에서 경보 발송 결과가 불명이면 자동 재발송하지 않으므로 운영자가 확인해야 한다. 임시 PostgreSQL의 제한 로그인·실제 API·경보 실행기와 스케줄러에서 파일 오류·복구 및 불명 경보 중복 억제를 검증했고 모의 Slack·Resend에서 경보 본문을 확인했다. 실제 공급자 수신·다중 호스트 HA는 미검증이다. 재현은 `TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-resend-webhook-bundle-local.sh`.

FCM 반복 503 지수 대기 장애 전환 시험: 모의 FCM이 안내 없이 503을 두 번 반환하도록 하고, 첫 10~12초 대기를 시험상 강제로 앞당긴 뒤 두 번째 20~25초 대기와 두 결과 불명 시도가 동기 복제됐음을 확인했다. 주 서버 중단·복제본 승격 후 실제 워커 두 개가 보존된 일정보다 일찍 연결하지 않았고 일정을 다시 강제로 앞당기자 미시도 세 번째 대상만 연결했다. `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=fcm-exp-repeat scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh`로 재현한다. 공급자의 120초 안내를 사용하는 기존 `fcm-exp` 시험도 다시 통과했다. 단일 호스트·관리자 DB 계정·모의 공급자 시험이며 실제 FCM·비동기 복제·네트워크 분리 및 운영 HA는 미검증이다.

FCM 반복 선점 조사 목록: 워커 0201을 적용하고 모니터 로그인에 `public.novu_rust_fcm_unrecorded_claim_list(text,integer,integer,uuid)` EXECUTE와 환경별 `alert_view`를 부여한다. 함수 소유자에게 큐·시도 테이블 SELECT 및 권한 확인 함수 실행 권한이 필요하다. `delivery-reconcile list-fcm-unrecorded-claims ENV MIN_GAP GRACE_SECONDS [AFTER_JOB]`은 0200 집계와 같은 조건으로 작업 ID·상태·가용 시각·선점 수·기록된 시도 수·차이만 반환한다. 결과의 `next`가 있으면 다음 요청의 AFTER_JOB으로 사용한다. 한 페이지는 최대 100건이고 UUID 순서는 시간순이 아니며 각 페이지와 건수는 서로 다른 시점이므로 작업 변화에 따라 누락이나 중복이 생길 수 있다. 목록은 조사 대상이며 구버전 오류나 미발송의 증거가 아니다. 제한 로그인·임시 PostgreSQL에서 별도 실행 권한, 환경 격리, 정보 제외 및 두 페이지를 확인했다. 실제 운영 부하·HA는 미검증이며 HTTP·관리 화면은 [API README](../../api/rust-api/README.md)를 참고한다. 재현은 `TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-fcm-unrecorded-claims-local.sh`.

FCM 반복 선점 진단·경보: 워커 0200을 적용하고 모니터 DB 로그인에 `public.novu_rust_fcm_unrecorded_claim_health(text,integer,integer)` EXECUTE와 환경별 `alert_view`를 부여한다. 함수 소유자는 큐와 FCM 시도 테이블 SELECT 및 권한 확인 함수 실행 권한이 필요하다. `delivery-reconcile check-fcm-unrecorded-claims ENV MIN_GAP GRACE_SECONDS`는 조회 시점에 실행 가능 시각이 유예 시간보다 오래된 pending/running FCM 작업 중 선점 횟수에서 기록된 대상별 시도 수를 뺀 값이 MIN_GAP 이상인 건수를 반환한다. MIN_GAP은 2~100000, 유예 시간은 1~86400초이며 결과 0은 종료 0, 양수는 2, 조회 오류는 1이다. 미래 예약 작업은 제외하고 다른 환경의 정보나 payload는 반환하지 않는다.

Slack·메일 경보에는 `SWALLOW_ALERT_FCM_UNRECORDED_CLAIM_MIN_GAP`과 `SWALLOW_ALERT_FCM_UNRECORDED_CLAIM_GRACE_SECONDS`를 함께 지정한다. 대상과 `alert_send`, 스케줄은 기존 경보 설정을 사용한다. `new-condition`을 선택하면 `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2=true`가 필요하고 별도 조건 비트 49·50을 사용하므로 동일 대상의 실행기를 모두 업데이트해야 한다. 이 수치는 구버전 차단 여부의 증거가 아니다. 정상 공유 대기 재선점, 발송 전 장애나 관리 작업 등에서도 차이가 누적될 수 있고 임계값·DB 시계·경보 스케줄에 따라 누락 또는 지연될 수 있다. 작업이 완료되면 집계에서 사라진다. DB 로그·워커 버전·시도 기록을 함께 조사한다. 대량 데이터 성능·실제 공급자 발송·운영 HA는 미검증이다. `TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-fcm-unrecorded-claims-local.sh`로 제한 DB 권한, 환경 격리, 경보 실행기 및 실제 구버전 반복 선점을 재현한다. 모의 Slack·Resend 전송 단위 시험도 수행했다.

FCM 지수 대기 구버전 프로세스 시험: 보호 도입 직전 커밋 `2fd94b90`의 실제 FCM 워커를 임시 소스에서 빌드했다. 임시 PostgreSQL에서 구버전이 작업을 선점한 후 0199 트리거가 시도 삽입을 거절하고 로컬 프록시에 외부 연결이 없음을 확인했다. 구버전 중지와 임대 만료 처리 후 최신 실제 워커가 작업을 복구해 프록시에 연결했고 연결 중단은 결과 불명으로 기록됐다. `TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-fcm-exponential-local.sh`로 재현한다. 단일 DB·관리자 계정·모의 프록시 시험으로 제한 운영 계정의 전체 워커 실행, 실제 FCM 및 운영 HA는 미검증이다.

FCM 지수 대기 버전 보호: 워커 0199는 환경의 등록 정책이 `exponential`인 동안 호환 워커가 임대를 확인하고 트랜잭션 한정 표시를 설정한 발송 시도만 허용한다. 구버전은 시도 삽입에서 거절되므로 외부 요청을 시작할 수 없지만 작업을 선점해 임대 만료까지 지연시킬 수 있다. 0198까지만 지원하는 워커도 이 보호에는 구버전이다. 배포 시 최신 워커를 먼저 준비하고 기존 워커 중지·진행 작업 확인 후 0199를 적용하며, 지수 정책 전환 절차도 아래 설명대로 수행한다. 트리거 함수 소유자는 정책 테이블의 SELECT와 행 잠금 권한, 큐 테이블 SELECT가 필요하다. SECURITY DEFINER 함수 소유자와 스키마를 보호하고 특권 DB 사용자의 직접 수정·표시 위조는 방지하지 못한다. 이미 발송 시도를 기록한 요청, 정책 미등록 환경, 구버전 함수 재적용과 DB 복구는 보호 범위 밖이다. 임시 PostgreSQL에서 제한 역할의 구버전 방식 삽입 거절 및 새 큐의 모의 FCM 503과 후속 발송을 검증했다. 실제 구버전 워커 프로세스 시험은 위 최신 항목을 참고하며 실제 FCM·다중 호스트 HA는 미검증이다. 재현 명령은 아래 FCM 지수 대기 항목을 참고한다.

FCM 지수 대기 장애 전환 시험: 로컬 동기 복제에서 모의 503의 `Retry-After: 120`이 지수 정책의 기본 대기보다 길 때 120초 일정과 결과 불명 시도가 복제되는지 확인했다. 주 서버를 중지하고 복제본을 승격한 뒤 `exponential` 설정의 실제 워커 두 개가 이 일정을 지키고, 테스트에서 일정을 강제로 앞당긴 후 미시도 대상만 연결을 시작했다. `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=fcm-exp scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh`로 재현한다. 이 시험은 120초 공급자 안내를 사용하며 반복 503 지수 증가의 장애 전환 시험은 위 최신 항목을 참고한다. 단일 호스트·관리자 DB 계정·모의 공급자 시험이며 실제 FCM·비동기 복제·네트워크 분리 및 운영 HA는 미검증이다.

FCM 다중 대상 503 지수 대기: 워커 마이그레이션 `0198` 적용 후 `SWALLOW_FCM_MULTICAST_503_POLICY=exponential`을 선택할 수 있다. 같은 작업에서 기록된 503 횟수마다 아직 시도하지 않은 다음 대상의 기본 대기를 10초부터 두 배로 늘려 최대 3600초로 제한하고 0~25%의 무작위 지연을 더한다(합계 상한 3600초). 유효한 `Retry-After`가 더 길면 최대 86400초까지 그 값을 따른다. 해당 503 대상은 결과 불명으로 남으며 자동으로 재발송하지 않는다. 횟수는 연속 횟수가 아닌 작업별 누적 횟수로, 다른 작업이나 환경·FCM 프로젝트에 공유하지 않는다. 다른 선택지는 그대로이며 기본값은 `continue`다. 정책을 변경할 때 기존 워커를 모두 중지하고 진행 중 발송을 정리한 후 아래 등록 절차대로 DB의 해당 설정을 초기화하고 모든 워커에 동일하게 설정한다. 구버전 워커는 새 선택지를 인식하지 못하며 기존 작업을 차단하지 않으므로 혼합 운영은 안전하지 않다. 마이그레이션은 테이블 잠금이 필요하며 기존 체크 제약을 식별할 수 없으면 실패한다. 모의 FCM·임시 PostgreSQL에서 반복 503, 결과 불명 보존, 나머지 대상만 발송, 실제 워커의 정책 등록·충돌 거절을 검증했다. 재현은 `TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-fcm-exponential-local.sh`. 실제 FCM과 운영 부하·다중 호스트 HA는 미검증이다.

Resend 날짜형 429의 장애 전환 시험: 로컬 PostgreSQL 동기 복제에서 모의 공급자가 `Date`와 날짜형 `Retry-After`로 300초를 안내하고 fallback은 600초로 설정했다. 복제본에 300초 시도와 대기 시각이 복제된 뒤 주 서버를 중지·복제본을 승격하자 실제 새 워커가 후속 작업을 같은 시각까지 연기하고 공급자 연결을 열지 않았다. `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend-cooldown scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh`로 재현한다. 단일 호스트·관리자 DB 계정·모의 공급자 시험이며 비동기 복제, 네트워크 분리, 실제 Resend 및 운영 HA는 검증하지 않았다.

Slack 앱·웹훅 및 Twilio SMS의 429 `Retry-After`도 HTTP 날짜를 지원한다. FCM·Resend와 공통 해석기를 사용하며 서버 `Date` 또는 워커 시계, 24시간 상한 및 오류 처리 조건은 아래 설명과 같다. Slack 공유 대기를 활성화하면 계산한 값이 해당 정책에 반영되고 Twilio에서는 저장된 안내를 선택형 운영자 지연 예약이 사용한다. 기존 설정을 변경하거나 재시도를 자동 활성화하지 않는다. 날짜형 헤더가 이전에는 미지원이었으므로 해당 응답의 대기·예약 동작이 바뀐다. 모의 Slack의 세 발송 경로와 모의 Twilio에서 날짜형 120초가 DB에 기록되는 것을 확인했다. 실제 공급자의 날짜형 응답·운영 HA는 미검증이다. `TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-channel-retry-date-local.sh`로 재현한다.

Resend 429의 `Retry-After`는 초 또는 HTTP 날짜를 지원한다 ([RFC 9110 §10.2.3](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-10.2.3)). 날짜는 유효한 단일 응답 `Date`를 기준으로 계산하며 `Date`가 없으면 워커 시계를 쓴다. 소수 초는 올림하고 과거 날짜는 0초로 취급한다. 중복·잘못된 헤더와 24시간 초과는 안내 없음으로 처리하고 공유 대기 사용 시 설정된 fallback을 적용한다. 응답 `Date`가 실제 시각과 다르거나 중간 프록시가 바꾸면 대기 시간도 달라지며 워커·DB 시계와 프록시를 신뢰해야 한다. 기존 재시도 정책은 변하지 않는다. 모의 Resend와 임시 PostgreSQL에서 날짜형 기록 및 공유 대기를 검증했으며 실제 Resend 날짜형 응답과 운영 HA는 미검증이다.

Resend shared cooldown fallback administration: worker migration `0197` adds a revision to the existing policy and independent environment-scoped DB login grants `novu_rust_resend_cooldown_policy_admins` (`can_read`, `can_write`, `can_audit`). A trusted DBA registers each intended LOGIN with its current role OID and grants only the corresponding EXECUTE privileges on `public.novu_rust_resend_cooldown_policy_read(text)`, `public.novu_rust_resend_cooldown_policy_set(text,uuid,bigint,integer,text)`, `public.novu_rust_resend_cooldown_policy_request(text,uuid)` and `public.novu_rust_resend_cooldown_policy_audit_read(text,bigint)`. The protected security-definer owner needs access to the policy, admin, request and audit tables plus the audit sequence; protect those objects and their owners. Existing workers do not need these admin grants.

Use `delivery-reconcile resend-cooldown-policy-read ENV`, then `resend-cooldown-policy-set ENV REQUEST_ID EXPECTED_REVISION FALLBACK_SECONDS REASON`. Use revision `-1` only to create an absent policy, and a fresh UUID per intended change. A successful change increments revision; updating fallback preserves the existing deadline and affects later 429s without a valid Retry-After. Repeating the identical request with the same database LOGIN, role OID and UUID returns the original result; reuse with different content is rejected. `resend-cooldown-policy-request ENV REQUEST_ID` resolves a request only for that same authorized login/OID, and `resend-cooldown-policy-audit ENV [BEFORE]` reads up to 50 records with an independently granted audit role. Reasons and request/audit records are sensitive; CLI arguments may appear in shell history and process listings. A missing request is not proof that an in-flight request failed. Audit IDs are not commit order, pages are separate snapshots, and direct SQL, failed attempts and preexisting policy changes are not audited. Direct privileged writes can bypass revisions and access checks. Deleting a policy, changing worker registrations or changing the worker enable setting is outside this command; apply the separate coordinated rollout instructions. `scripts/test-resend-shared-cooldown-local.sh` checks independent restricted roles, concurrent revisions, replay, deadline preservation, audit rollback and policy locking on disposable local PostgreSQL. The first run encountered a Rust test compilation error, corrected before re-running. HTTP/UI administration, external audit retention, actual Resend limits and production HA remain unverified or unimplemented.

Resend shared cooldown controlled failover: `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend-cooldown scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` creates and destroys a disposable synchronous PostgreSQL primary/standby pair on one host. A mock Resend 429 commits a rejected attempt and a 300-second deadline on the primary; the test waits until the standby has replayed both, forcibly stops the primary, and promotes the standby. A real Resend worker then reconnects to the promoted DB, defers a newly queued job to the replicated deadline without an attempt or provider connection, and the cooldown health query reports the pending job. The fixture uses a PostgreSQL superuser; separate restricted-role tests cover function access. This controlled case does not prove production HA, async replication durability, split-brain fencing, in-flight request handling, lost responses, real provider behavior or failover under load.

Resend shared cooldown monitoring: apply worker migration `0196`. Independently grant a monitoring DB login environment `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_resend_cooldown_health(text,integer)`; the trusted function owner needs the alert-view check plus SELECT on the cooldown policy and queue. `delivery-reconcile check-resend-cooldown ENV GRACE_SECONDS` accepts 1–86400 seconds and returns `active`, `notBefore`, `fallbackSeconds`, `deferred` and `overdue`; it exits 2 when any active or deferred work exists, 0 when none exists, and 1 on errors. An active cooldown can be an expected protective action, not proof of delivery failure. Only currently pending Resend jobs whose last error equals the guard marker are counted; other workers, altered rows, already claimed work and other environments are not covered. Results are snapshots and a missing policy is an error. The partial index can take locks and space on a populated queue.

For scheduled Slack/email alerting, set `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_COOLDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` to the same valid range and grant the alert login the same health function and independent alert-view privilege in addition to its normal alert-send privileges. With `SWALLOW_ALERT_COOLDOWN_POLICY=new-condition`, enable scoped delivery and `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2=true`; this uses separate v2 bits for active/deferred and failed checks. Upgrade all alert senders for a target consistently: older binaries do not understand these bits, and mixed or direct database changes can weaken cooldown and audit behavior. With destination cooldown the new-condition mode is not required. Alert check errors are themselves reported and cause a nonzero exit; unknown provider outcomes remain subject to manual review. The local script `scripts/test-resend-shared-cooldown-local.sh` covers restricted login, environment and permission isolation, healthy/deferred/overdue cases, blocked alert, v2 masks and mock Slack/Resend message transport. The initial integration fixture used an invalid Slack hostname; after correcting it the test passed. Actual monitoring scheduler, real providers, production load and multi-host HA remain unverified.

Resend shared 429 cooldown (worker migration `0195`): choose `NOVU_RESEND_SHARED_COOLDOWN=true` on every upgraded Resend worker in an environment, or leave it unset/`false` to disable the guard. A 429 with a valid bounded Retry-After extends that environment's PostgreSQL deadline; if the header is absent or invalid, the provisioned fallback applies. While the deadline is active, a cooperating worker returns a newly claimed job to pending at that deadline before creating an attempt. The rejected 429 job is not automatically retried by this guard; existing, independently configured retry rules still apply. A worker already past its dispatch reservation can still send during the cooldown. This is a reactive environment-level guard, not a proactive account-wide rate limiter: credentials shared across environments, multiple accounts in one environment, other senders, old workers, lost responses and external rate limits require separate coordination. A zero-second valid header can result in no delay.

Apply migration 0195 before deploying **any** upgraded Resend worker, even when the guard is disabled: it adds `shared_cooldown` to `novu_rust_resend_idempotency_policy`. Grant the worker the existing policy table SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE rights including the new column, and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_check_resend_shared_cooldown(text,boolean)` in addition to existing grants. New workers atomically register the idempotency and cooldown settings and reject a conflict; the dispatch transaction locks and rechecks the registered setting. A preexisting NULL is bound by the first upgraded worker. To change the setting, stop and quiesce all Resend workers, verify in-flight work, provision or remove the guard policy, then have a trusted administrator reset `shared_cooldown` to NULL before starting all workers consistently. Direct DB writes and older workers bypass registration; mixed versions, async failover, restores and manual edits can weaken the guard. Do not infer cluster-wide enforcement from one worker.

When enabled, a trusted DBA inserts `novu_rust_resend_cooldown_policies(environment_id,fallback_seconds)` with 1–86400 seconds and registers each worker LOGIN and current role OID in `novu_rust_resend_cooldown_workers`; grant that login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_resend_cooldown_check(text)` and `public.novu_rust_resend_cooldown_record(text,integer)`. Their security-definer owner needs access to the two tables and the internal access function. Protect these owners, registrations and policy updates. A missing policy or missing permission causes the enabled worker to reconnect rather than dispatch; loss after claiming a job may leave it leased until recovery. Failed recording rolls back result updates, and a previously sent request can become unknown; do not blindly resend it. `TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-resend-shared-cooldown-local.sh` uses disposable local PostgreSQL and a mock Resend endpoint to check a 429, fallback, another DB connection's deferral, policy locks and authorization, and starts real worker processes to check conflicting settings and existing idempotency behavior. The first regression run used an already fully migrated DB with a legacy test that reapplies only the initial API migrations and failed its migration-history check; the reproducible script runs that test at the appropriate migration stage. The controlled local synchronous failover test is described above. Actual Resend rate limits, multiple hosts, production failover and operating-scale concurrency are unverified.

Operator JWT key reload alerts: `send-database-alert ENV INSTANCE_LABEL COOLDOWN_SECONDS` and `alert-fanout database ...` can instead probe the API's optional `/health/operator-keys` endpoint by setting `SWALLOW_OPERATOR_KEY_PROBE_URL` to its full URL and leaving `SWALLOW_DB_PROBE_URL` unset. Both set is an error. HTTPS is required except for loopback HTTP fixtures; credentials, query, fragment and other paths are rejected. Do not put secrets in the URL or label. The default destination ID is `key-INSTANCE_LABEL`; use a distinct target ID for each monitored API instance and do not share it with database or delivery checks. These checks share an existing cooldown condition bit, so sharing target IDs can suppress alerts or clear each other's observations. Connect directly to each instance when each must be monitored; a load-balanced address tests only whichever instance responds.

The probe accepts only HTTP 200 and JSON `{"status":"healthy"}` within 4096 bytes. Errors, timeouts, malformed/oversized responses, redirects, disabled endpoints and all other statuses are unhealthy. Configure the API's opt-in health route and restrict ingress to the monitoring system; the endpoint is unauthenticated. `SWALLOW_DB_PROBE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` controls the probe timeout. Existing alert-state DB permissions, target binding, cooldown, fallback, Slack and Resend/SendGrid destination settings apply unchanged; schedule regular execution independently. Exit 0 means the probe was healthy and checks completed, 2 means unhealthy (including a blocked/unknown delivery requiring review), and 1 means a configuration or alert-state/dispatch error. Provider acceptance is not proof of receipt. A healthy check does not resolve an unknown delivery; review that attempt using existing alert-resolution procedures. No recovery notification is sent.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/path/to/postgresql/bin scripts/test-operator-key-probe-local.sh` runs the real alert command against a local health fixture and a fully migrated disposable PostgreSQL with a restricted login. It checks healthy, HTTP failure, malformed/oversized and redirect responses, ambiguous Slack delivery, duplicate suppression and recovery observation after fixture-only review. A second test starts the real Rust API against the same local DB, changes its key file from valid to invalid and back, and verifies alert detection, suppression of duplicate attempts and preservation of an unknown outcome. The real `alert-scheduler` and `alert-fanout` also run against that API: the test checks a healthy run, two unhealthy scheduled runs with one unknown delivery attempt, and a subsequent healthy run while the unknown attempt remains under review. Slack delivery in that test is deliberately blocked by an unreachable loopback proxy. Separate in-process mock Slack and Resend tests verify the message and accepted provider responses. The first integration run incorrectly expected an unknown delivery's mask to clear on recovery; the corrected test confirms it remains pending review. The first parallel run hit a PostgreSQL role-catalog conflict; fixture role creation now uses an advisory lock. Actual Slack/email delivery, real filesystem stalls, scheduler operation across multiple hosts and failover are unverified.

## V2 alert state synchronous failover test

Optional FCM cooldown source changes: with `SWALLOW_ALERT_FCM_COOLDOWN_SOURCE_ENABLED=true`, `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2=true`, scoped delivery and `SWALLOW_ALERT_COOLDOWN_POLICY=new-condition`, set `SWALLOW_ALERT_FCM_COOLDOWN_DISTINCT_SOURCE=true` to assign separate V2 condition bits 44, 45 and 46 to active 429, 503 and unknown-source deadlines. The default is false. A successfully observed source change can bypass a previously accepted destination cooldown; repeated observations of the same source remain suppressed. A successful inactive observation clears these conditions; a failed or disabled source check does not clear the last known source. The source records the event that extended the deadline, not every provider response. Unknown, rejected or in-flight destination slots still block or defer sending, and neither successful provider acceptance nor a retry proves receipt. Upgrade **every** V2 sender sharing each target before enabling the option: older senders do not understand these bits and may overwrite their state. The existing 64-bit migrations and detail-function permission are required; this adds no DB migration. A restricted-role PostgreSQL test with mock Slack covers 429 → 503 → 429, repeated-condition suppression and recovery; real CLI configuration and blocked-destination tests verify flag dependencies. Actual Slack/email receipt, concurrent senders, production HA and all outage timing are unverified. Direct Rust callers constructing `Alert` must set `fcm_cooldown_distinct_source`.

FCM cooldown source in delivery alerts: apply worker migration `0194`, set `SWALLOW_ALERT_FCM_COOLDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` as before, and optionally set `SWALLOW_ALERT_FCM_COOLDOWN_SOURCE_ENABLED=true` on `send-delivery-alert` or its fanout/scheduler environment. The flag defaults to false; true without the cooldown check or an invalid flag value fails the invocation. Grant the alert DB login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_fcm_cooldown_health_detail(text,integer)` plus its existing environment `alert_view` and delivery permissions. The trusted detail-function owner needs EXECUTE on the existing cooldown health function and SELECT on the policy table. When the active deadline's recorded source is 429 or 503, Slack and email alert text identifies it; legacy/unknown source is explicitly unknown. This identifies the event that extended the deadline, not every cause. By default the alert's existing condition bit and cooldown are unchanged; the optional distinct-source mode is described above. Without that mode a source change alone does not trigger an immediate new-condition alert, and already accepted, unknown or rejected destination slots follow their usual rules. Missing permission or query errors make this check fail and produce an error alert attempt rather than a healthy result. Source is aggregate data only; restrict access as with the existing alert health check. Direct Rust callers constructing `Alert` must initialize the new `fcm_cooldown_source` field. A restricted PostgreSQL role and real alert CLI test cover independent permission, isolation and blocked destinations; mock Slack and Resend servers verify both outgoing bodies. Actual external receipt, an operational scheduler and multi-host HA are unverified.

FCM cooldown deadline source: apply worker migration `0193` before deploying the updated FCM worker, and grant its DB login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_fcm_cooldown_record_429(text,integer)` and, when shared 503 is enabled, `public.novu_rust_fcm_cooldown_record_503(text,integer)` in addition to its existing check and environment access. Updated workers check function privileges before starting shared-cooldown processing. Trusted owners of the security-definer recording functions need the existing access helper and policy-table rights, including the new columns. After locking the policy row, every extension of `not_before` atomically records the corresponding status and DB timestamp; a shorter or equal proposal leaves the prior source intact. Existing untyped `novu_rust_fcm_cooldown_record` remains usable by older workers but records an unknown source when it extends the deadline, since older versions can use it for either status. Existing deadlines have unknown source after migration. Source only identifies the event that extended the current deadline, not all incidents, the affected project, or proof of delivery; direct SQL, privileged changes, late/in-flight events, and failover with lost data can make it incomplete. Migration changes a table and existing function; plan locks, trusted ownership and rollout carefully. A restricted-role PostgreSQL test covers 429/503 extensions, shorter proposals, waiting for a locked policy row, legacy unknown, access revocation and missing new function privilege on a real worker; mock-FCM queue tests cover dispatch. API migration `0110` and the updated UI expose this information as described in the API README. Actual FCM, production HA and external alerting are unverified.

FCM shared 503 cooldown: apply worker migration `0192` and set both `SWALLOW_FCM_SHARED_COOLDOWN=true` and `SWALLOW_FCM_SHARED_COOLDOWN_503=true` on every updated FCM worker in the environment. The new flag defaults to false; enabling it without the existing shared guard rejects startup. A 503 then updates the same environment-wide cooldown used by 429, using the accepted `Retry-After` or the provisioned fallback and a minimum of 60 seconds; another job's updated worker defers before starting its next send. A 503 target remains unknown and is not automatically retried. The 429 and 503 cooldowns share one timestamp; updated monitoring can show which status extended that deadline as described above. The scope is the entire environment, not the FCM project or device; it can delay healthy integrations and actual in-flight dispatches cannot be stopped. Configure a policy and DB privileges as for the existing guard. New workers register the 503 flag atomically with their other settings. Before changing it, stop all FCM workers, wait for in-flight work, and have a trusted administrator reset only `shared_unavailable` to NULL; then restart all workers with identical settings. Protect and audit that operation. Older binaries ignore the new flag and do not record 503 cooldowns; mixed rollouts, direct DB edits, DB failover with lost state and already-reserved sends can bypass it. A crash or lease loss before the 503 result transaction commits can also prevent recording the cooldown. Migration takes a table lock. A mock FCM and local PostgreSQL test checks enable/disable, valid and missing advice, unknown outcome, another job's deferral and later continuation; two real worker processes check conflicting flags in both directions and reject a flag without the base guard. Existing guard authorization tests use a restricted DB role. Actual FCM, high-load contention, multi-host HA and external alert receipt remain unverified.

FCM multicast policy registration: apply worker migration `0191` before deploying updated FCM workers. Each new worker atomically binds both `SWALLOW_FCM_MULTICAST_429_POLICY` and `SWALLOW_FCM_MULTICAST_503_POLICY`, alongside existing workflow, target, preference and shared-cooldown settings, in the environment's `novu_rust_push_workflow_worker_config` row. Matching workers start; conflicting new workers refuse to process work. NULL means the first updated worker chooses the value; migration does not infer existing process settings. A missing migration or DB permissions causes registration to fail and the worker to reconnect rather than process jobs; grant the worker's existing configuration-table INSERT/UPDATE/SELECT privileges for the new columns. To change either policy, stop every FCM worker for that environment, confirm no sends are in flight, have a trusted administrator reset only the relevant `multicast_429_policy` and/or `multicast_503_policy` to NULL, then restart all with the same policy. Protect and audit that operation. Older workers ignore these columns, already-running processes do not recheck policy during dispatch, and direct DB writes or restored backups bypass registration; this is not automatic fencing. Migration takes a table lock. Disposable PostgreSQL and real worker processes tested matching and mismatched settings in both directions and concurrent conflicting first registrations. Separate local credential and secret-file regression tests passed; the Docker reconnect test was updated for the migration but not run. Real FCM, rolling old-worker deployments, production load and multi-host HA remain unverified.

FCM HTTP 503 guidance: `SWALLOW_FCM_MULTICAST_503_POLICY=continue|delay-known|delay-minimum` controls only the remaining unattempted targets in the same job. Default `continue` retains immediate continuation. `delay-known` waits at least 10 seconds when valid bounded `Retry-After` advice is present; `delay-minimum` waits at least 10 seconds even when the header is missing or malformed. Valid numeric or HTTP-date advice is stored on the original attempt and sets the wait when longer. The 503 attempt remains `unknown` and is not automatically retried; any successful later target does not erase that uncertainty. Existing provider invalid-token classification takes precedence. The shared cooldown applies to 503 and other jobs only when the separate setting above is enabled, and the older policy options do not add exponential backoff, jitter, or provider-wide rate control; the optional exponential policy is described above. New workers register both multicast policies as described above; older workers do not. DB time determines the scheduled time; already in-flight work and unlinked operations are unaffected. `SendError::Unavailable(Option<u32>)` replaces `Provider(503)` for direct Rust callers. The isolated PostgreSQL queue test uses a mock 503 and checks date, missing/invalid headers, policy selection, delay, unknown outcome and no repeated target; a separate worker process rejects an invalid policy. Actual FCM, mixed deployments, production HA and automatic retry remain unverified. [Firebase recommends honoring Retry-After and exponential backoff for 503](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/error-codes).

FCM 429 `Retry-After` accepts HTTP-date as well as numeric seconds. For a date, the worker subtracts a single valid provider `Date` header, or its local clock when `Date` is absent, rounds any fractional second up, and accepts only delays from 0 to 86,400 seconds. Multiple `Retry-After` values, malformed or multiple `Date` values, invalid dates and larger delays yield unknown guidance. Numeric advice retains its previous behavior. Server Date avoids shortening the delay due to worker/provider clock skew; without Date, the worker clock must be synchronized. Time spent in transit is not subtracted, so the wait can be longer than the provider deadline. Database clock skew and invalid or implausible provider timestamps still affect scheduling; a past date gives zero before existing policy minimums. Existing per-job and shared-cooldown policies consume this value; they delay remaining unsent targets but do not automatically retry the rejected target. The behavior changes as workers roll out; coordinate mixed versions. PostgreSQL and mock FCM verify a dated 429 records 120 seconds, delays the remaining target and does not repeat the rejected one. Unit tests cover missing/invalid/duplicate headers, bounds and clock skew. Real FCM HTTP-date responses, provider-wide quotas and production HA remain unverified. The separate 503 policy is described above. See [FCM error guidance](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/error-codes) and [RFC 9110](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc9110#section-10.2.3).

For `alert-fanout`, set `SWALLOW_ALERT_FANOUT_CONFIG_REGULAR_ONLY=true` to reject non-regular target configuration files before reading; default `false` preserves FIFO and other existing input behavior. Invalid values fail the invocation. On Unix the file is opened nonblocking and its type is checked on the opened handle, so a FIFO without a writer fails instead of blocking the scheduler before child timeouts begin. Symlinks to regular files are accepted for mounted or atomically replaced configuration. File contents remain limited to 64 KiB and invalid configurations still prevent every destination in that run; monitor fanout/scheduler failures separately. A regular file on a stalled filesystem can still block, symlinks require trusted directories, and the Unix nonblocking FIFO behavior is not verified on other platforms. A real fanout process test checks FIFO rejection, directory and invalid-policy rejection, regular-file symlinks and legacy FIFO input with a writer. It uses fixture children and sends no external alerts.

Worker migration `0190` adds `shared_cooldown` to the existing environment-level push worker configuration. Current `fcm-worker` processes atomically register `SWALLOW_FCM_SHARED_COOLDOWN` together with the existing workflow, preference and target policies, and refuse startup if any registered value differs. Apply `0190` before deploying these workers even when the shared guard is disabled; their existing configuration-table permissions must cover the new column. A preexisting NULL value is bound by the first upgraded worker, so verify the intended setting before it starts.

To change this setting, stop all FCM workers for the environment, verify they are quiescent and no dispatch remains in flight, provision or remove the shared cooldown policy as appropriate, and have a trusted administrator reset only `shared_cooldown` to NULL in `public.novu_rust_push_workflow_worker_config` before starting consistently configured workers. Protect and audit that administrative operation. New workers reject mixed settings, but old binaries and direct database writes can bypass registration and do not recheck the setting during dispatch. An old process already running, a partial rollout, policy removal, direct edits, async failover or restored backups can still weaken the guard. This is not automatic cluster fencing or proof that every worker runs the guard. Two local worker processes were tested against disposable PostgreSQL for true/false mismatch in both directions; the Docker reconnect test was compiled but not run for this change. Actual FCM, multi-host HA and production load remain unverified.


Worker migration `0189` adds `delivery-reconcile check-fcm-cooldown ENV GRACE_SECONDS` (1–86400 seconds). It returns the environment's active flag, finite `notBefore`, fallback seconds, pending deferred jobs and the subset overdue beyond the grace period; it exits 2 if any are active/deferred/overdue, 0 when none, and 1 for errors. Grant the monitor login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_fcm_cooldown_health(text,integer)` and the environment's `alert_view` permission; its trusted owner needs policy/queue reads and the grant checks. Missing policy is an error. Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_FCM_COOLDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` to include this signal in configured Slack/email alerts. For `new-condition`, v2 conditions and scoped delivery are required; bits 42/43 distinguish this check and its failure. Upgrade all v2 alert senders sharing a target before enabling it, as older senders do not understand the new bits.

The monitor observes queue rows still marked pending with `FCM shared cooldown active`; changed `last_error`, jobs claimed or edited by other workers, old workers, and deleted policies can make the count incomplete. It cannot prove every worker uses the guard, that a worker is alive or that FCM accepted or delivered anything. The migration adds a partial index that can lock a populated table. Local restricted-role PostgreSQL tests and mock Slack/email cover selected authorization, state and payload cases; actual destinations, external scheduling, production load and multi-host HA are not verified.


Worker migration `0188` adds an optional conservative **environment-wide** FCM cooldown. Set `SWALLOW_FCM_SHARED_COOLDOWN=true` on every participating FCM worker after provisioning a row in `public.novu_rust_fcm_cooldown_policies` for the environment with `fallback_seconds` between 60 and 86400, and a `public.novu_rust_fcm_cooldown_workers` row for each DB login with its current `pg_roles.oid`. Grant those logins EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_fcm_cooldown_check(text)` and `public.novu_rust_fcm_cooldown_record(text,integer)`. Protect the security-definer owner, policy, worker grant provisioning and schema; no policy or privileges are granted automatically. The worker checks the policy on connection before claiming jobs. Missing/denied check configuration stops that worker; both EXECUTE privileges are required for a 429 result to commit successfully.

On a lease-fenced HTTP 429, the worker extends the shared `not_before` to the maximum of its existing value and the response's bounded numeric seconds (minimum 60), or the provisioned fallback when the header is absent, malformed, duplicated, date-formatted or beyond 86400. This fallback may be shorter than an unparsed provider instruction. Each participating worker checks and holds a shared policy lock through dispatch reservation; a later job seeing an active cooldown is returned to pending at the recorded time without a provider request. This protects the entire environment even when several FCM projects or individual devices have separate quotas; it can unnecessarily delay unrelated traffic. Already reserved/in-flight requests can still be sent. Different environments and older binaries bypass the guard; current workers with mismatched settings are rejected by the registration described above. Changing a policy or worker access during a dispatch, crash before recording a 429, lease loss, privileged SQL, asynchronous failover or backup restoration can weaken the guarantee. Coordinate upgrades and ensure every participating worker uses the option before relying on it.

This guard does not automatically retry rejected targets, rate-limit at steady state, interpret HTTP-date advice, confirm device receipt, or guarantee a production HA outcome. Optional aggregate monitoring is described above. It adds one policy read/row lock per eligible target when enabled, a policy update per recorded 429, and a queue update when deferred; assess contention, DB roles and production load. Existing per-job `continue|delay-known|delay-default` choices remain separate. Local mock-provider/disposable PostgreSQL tests cover another job's deferral, concurrent monotonic updates, environment/role separation and worker startup denial. Real FCM, multi-host HA and provider-wide quota scope remain unverified.


FCM workers support `SWALLOW_FCM_MULTICAST_429_POLICY=delay-default` in addition to `continue` and `delay-known`. When a 429 has no `Retry-After` header, `delay-default` schedules the remaining unattempted targets of that **same job** 60 seconds later. A valid numeric header uses the greater of its value and 60 seconds, as with `delay-known`. An invalid, duplicate, date-formatted or over-limit header is not treated as missing and does not schedule this fallback. The rejected target itself is not retried and no provider-wide throttling is performed. Configure all relevant workers consistently; old or differently configured workers can schedule immediate continuation. This option follows the 60-second fallback in [Firebase scaling guidance](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/scale-fcm), applied here to remaining targets rather than retries.

The public Rust `SendError` gains `RateLimitedMissing` for 429 without a header; consumers matching the enum must handle this variant. No synthetic provider advice is written to the attempt's `retry_after_seconds` or `retry_not_before` fields for the fallback. Reference invalid-token classification can still take precedence; lease loss or a crash before the result transaction can prevent scheduling. A local mock and disposable PostgreSQL test cover missing and out-of-range headers, worker claim timing and target outcomes; real FCM, date-formatted advice, other jobs, production load and HA are not verified.


`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=fcm-review bash scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` uses a disposable local PostgreSQL 17 primary and synchronous standby. It submits a partial-failure acknowledgement and request record using the restricted API DB login, waits until both are replayed on the standby, stops the primary and promotes the standby. Two restricted sessions replay the same request concurrently; both receive the original result, and only one review and one request record exist. The test also checks the unreviewed count, request lookup, conflicting request contents and revocation after promotion.

This test calls the database functions directly with fixture identity claims. It does not run the HTTP API, validate a real JWT or IdP, test an interruption before commit, asynchronous replication, backup restore, network partition or split brain. It runs on one host with administrative setup and is not proof of production HA. The claims for all other failover scenarios remain independent.


API migration `0106` exposes the durable FCM review ledger to separately authorized operator JWT identities, with idempotent request IDs and lookup; see the Rust API README. The Angular FCM panel supports acknowledgement and request lookup. The CLI and API share the same worker review and revision rules.


Worker migration `0187` adds `delivery-reconcile check-unreviewed-fcm ENV`, which returns `{unreviewedJobs:N}` without a time window and exits 2 for a nonzero count, 0 for zero, and 1 on configuration/DB errors. Grant its DB login `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_fcm_unreviewed_health(text)` and the environment `alert_view` permission; the trusted function owner needs the snapshot and review-table reads and the existing grant checks. To use the same signal for configured Slack/email alerts, set `SWALLOW_ALERT_FCM_UNREVIEWED_ENABLED=true`. The existing rolling-window check remains independently selectable. With `new-condition`, v2 conditions and scoped delivery are required; the unreviewed and check-failure bits are 40 and 41. Upgrade all v2 senders sharing a target before enabling this check, since older v2 binaries do not understand these bits.

The count changes when a reviewed snapshot is acknowledged or its evidence changes; it is not limited to the 100-row CLI list. The check covers completed mixed accepted/rejected jobs only, not every FCM failure or device delivery, and follows the review ledger's deletion, concurrency, revision and HA limitations below. A growing history can make this scan costly even with the new partial job index; index creation may lock a populated table. Test query plans and alert timeout against production-sized data before enabling. If a persistent unhealthy condition remains active, new-condition cooldown does not promise an immediate alert for every additional job. Local PostgreSQL tests cover permission, review/reappearance and alert command handling; local mock Slack/email tests cover payloads, not actual delivery, multi-host HA or production scale.


Worker migration `0186` adds a durable review ledger for completed FCM jobs with both accepted and rejected targets. `delivery-reconcile list-unreviewed-fcm ENV [AFTER_JOB]` returns up to 100 UUID-ordered jobs with counts and an opaque revision; this list has no age window. After independent investigation, `delivery-reconcile ack-fcm-partial ENV JOB REVISION OPERATOR REASON` records the reviewed snapshot and returns `recorded:true`, or `false` for an identical prior acknowledgement. A stale revision is rejected and the changed result remains in the list. The acknowledgement records the operator label, reason, DB session principal and time; it does not retry a rejected target or establish delivery. Restrict and retain this audit data appropriately.

Grant the CLI login `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_fcm_partial_unreviewed(text,uuid)` plus environment `alert_view` to list, and `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_fcm_partial_ack(text,uuid,text,text,text)` plus environment `queue_ack` to acknowledge. Their trusted owner needs the internal snapshot function and queue, attempt, review and grant permissions. No new grants are automatic. Listing and review examine stored attempt metadata; large installations should assess the query plan and transaction cost before enabling. A review locks the job and its attempt rows; normal FCM workers also lock the job before changing attempts. Privileged direct SQL and writers that ignore that order can race or deadlock. Revision derives from job attempt count and each target index, state, HTTP status and `updated_at`; writers that alter evidence without changing these fields can escape revision detection. Deleting a job cascades its review history. Restoration or asynchronous failover can lose or resurrect records.

This review ledger is currently available through the scoped DB/CLI path only: the rolling-window alert and Angular/JWT views do **not** consume these acknowledgements; the optional unreviewed alert above does. The Angular FCM panel supports acknowledgement through API migration `0106`. Old completed partial jobs are included. Test with `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test fcm_partial_review -- --ignored` against a fully migrated disposable DB with role creation; selected authorization, tenant isolation, repeated and stale acknowledgement, lock contention and changed-result cases are covered. Production load, all concurrency paths and real FCM remain unverified; the controlled synchronous promotion test above covers one committed review case.


`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=fcm-delay bash scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` runs a controlled FCM continuation test on a disposable, loopback-only PostgreSQL 17 primary and synchronous physical standby. A queue-function harness receives a mock 429 with numeric `Retry-After`, stores the first target rejection and the delayed job, and verifies both have replayed on the standby. The test stops the primary, promotes the standby, starts two actual FCM worker processes, and verifies neither contacts the mock proxy before the scheduled time. It then administratively advances `available_at` and checks that only the previously unattempted target makes one proxy connection; the proxy closes the connection, so that target is `unknown`. The rejected target is not sent again.

The initial sender is a queue-function harness, the post-promotion connection does not reach FCM, and this test uses an administrative database login on a single host. It does not validate real FCM, full delay elapsed time, a crash during the 429 response or before its result commit, asynchronous replication, network partitions, split-brain fencing, or production HA. Do not use the test as proof of those guarantees.


Worker migration `0185` adds optional monitoring for completed FCM jobs that contain both accepted and rejected targets. `delivery-reconcile check-fcm-partial ENV WINDOW_SECONDS` reports `partialJobs` and exits 2 when nonzero, 0 when zero, or 1 on an error. The window is 1–604800 seconds and uses the rejected attempt's `updated_at`; the same job is counted once. Grant the DB login `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_fcm_partial_health(text,integer)` and the environment-specific `alert_view` permission. Its trusted function owner needs reads of the attempts and queue tables plus privileges required by `novu_rust_assert_alert_view`.

To include this check in Slack/email delivery alerts, set `SWALLOW_ALERT_FCM_PARTIAL_WINDOW_SECONDS` to the chosen window. Existing destination and sender configuration still applies. With `SWALLOW_ALERT_COOLDOWN_POLICY=new-condition`, this check requires `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2=true` and scoped delivery; its v2 condition and check-error bits are 38 and 39. Upgrade **all** v2 senders sharing a destination before enabling the check: older v2 senders do not understand these bits and may weaken condition-based cooldown behavior. The default destination cooldown policy is also available.

This is a rolling-window signal, not an acknowledgement ledger. A rejected attempt ages out even if no operator has reviewed it; updates to attempts can change the counted window, and failed or pending jobs are excluded. It does not prove device delivery, find all failures, resend a target, or change Novu-compatible job/message status. Use FCM result listing and external durable alert handling when review is required. Migration `0185` creates a partial index and can lock/write on a populated table; assess production deployment cost. Local PostgreSQL restricted-role tests and mock Slack/email exercise selected paths; real providers, production load, asynchronous failover and multi-host HA are not validated.


`SWALLOW_FCM_MULTICAST_429_POLICY=continue|delay-known|delay-default` selects how an FCM worker handles the **remaining unattempted targets of the same job** after a 429. The default `continue` preserves existing behavior. `delay-known` schedules remaining targets no sooner than the greater of 60 seconds and a valid bounded numeric `Retry-After`; the already rejected target is not retried. This uses the existing PostgreSQL job `available_at`, so cooperating workers cannot claim that job before the scheduled time. Apply worker migration `0184` first. No new query is added to the hot path; the existing job update receives the delay.

This is not a project-wide or device-wide limiter: other jobs and already started requests continue. For `delay-known`, missing, repeated, malformed, date-formatted or over-86400-second headers do not trigger a delay; `delay-default` also handles a missing header as described above. Reference-mode invalid-token classification or a lost lease/worker crash can prevent either delay. When no usable numeric advice or applicable missing-header fallback is captured, the worker proceeds immediately. All FCM workers that may encounter 429 must use `delay-known` to apply it consistently; old or differently configured workers can schedule immediate continuation. Do not use it as a complete FCM quota policy or automatic retry mechanism; inspect per-target outcomes and monitor rejected messages. A local PostgreSQL test with two clients and a mock FCM response checks persistence and that the rejected target is not resent; real FCM, quota behavior, and process crashes are not verified; the controlled synchronous failover test above covers one committed scheduling case.


FCM 429 guidance: apply worker migration `0184` before deploying this worker. For each target receiving HTTP 429, a single unsigned numeric `Retry-After` of 0–86400 seconds is stored with a DB-clock-based `retry_not_before`. Missing, repeated, malformed and out-of-range headers are not retained. HTTP-date handling is described above. The existing reference invalid-token classification takes precedence. Only valid 429 advice adds one update in the existing result transaction; other sends add no DB operation. Old workers do not record this advice. The public Rust `SendError` reports `RateLimited(Option<u32>)` for present headers or `RateLimitedMissing` when no header is present instead of `Provider(429)`. This is advice only: it does not schedule or enforce retry, provide FCM-wide rate limiting, or prove device delivery. When no usable header exists, no default is recorded. See [Firebase's FCM scaling guidance](https://firebase.google.com/docs/cloud-messaging/scale-fcm) for the 60-second default and backoff guidance; The separate 503 guidance policy is described above. Avoid immediate retry, account for multicast outcomes and possible duplicate delivery, and use the existing reviewed reconciliation path. A local mock and disposable PostgreSQL test exercise a mixed multicast and target-specific 429 advice; real FCM and failover have not been validated.


`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=alert-v2 bash scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` creates a disposable local PostgreSQL 17 primary and synchronous standby, records a V2 accepted condition and target version, verifies replay, stops the primary and promotes the standby. After promotion the same condition remains in cooldown, the scoped legacy reservation is refused, and two concurrent database clients see exactly one reservation and one in-flight result for a new condition. Finishing the synthetic attempt restores cooldown. This test calls reservation functions directly and synthetically marks attempts accepted; it does not run senders or deliver a real alert. It uses one host and an administrative DB login and does not prove actual provider receipt, external scheduling, network partition safety, asynchronous failover or production HA.

## Twilio delayed-retry monitoring

Worker migration 0183 adds `delivery-reconcile check-twilio-delayed ENV GRACE_SECONDS`; the DB login needs environment-scoped `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_twilio_delayed_retry_health(text,integer)`. The trusted function owner needs SELECT on the queue table and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_assert_alert_view(text)` and its dependencies. Grace is 1–86,400 seconds. The JSON has `pendingOverdue` (pending past `available_at` plus grace), `expiredLease` (running past lease expiry plus grace), and `callbackBlocked` (failed after the linked-callback guard). Exit 0 means all zero, 2 means any count is nonzero, and 1 means query, configuration or access error. These are snapshots, not proof of why a worker is late or whether messages were delivered; callback-blocked counts persist until the job state changes. Older or directly edited jobs without the marker are omitted.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_TWILIO_DELAYED_GRACE_SECONDS` to include this check in `send-delivery-alert` over the existing Slack/email destinations; leave unset to disable. A failed check attempts an alert and exits 1. Destination cooldown works as before; `new-condition` requires `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2=true`, where this check uses separate bits 36 and 37. Coordinate all senders on each target to understand those bits; older V2 senders may overwrite their state. See the 64-bit rollout and function-owner grants below. The index added by 0183 can lock the table during migration and adds update overhead; evaluate at production scale. Monitor scheduler, DB and alert delivery separately. A restricted-role disposable PostgreSQL test checks counts, scope, revocation and blocked reservations with default and V2 policies; unit tests use mock Slack and Resend. Actual alert receipt and production HA are unverified.

## Operator-selected Twilio 429 delayed retry

After worker migrations 0178–0179, a maintenance login with the environment's `retry` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_twilio_retry_guidance(text,uuid)` and `public.novu_rust_resolve_twilio_after(text,uuid,uuid,text,text)` can use `delivery-reconcile twilio-retry-guidance ENV JOB` to retrieve the current dispatch ID and bounded guidance, then `delivery-reconcile twilio-retry-after ENV JOB DISPATCH_ID OPERATOR REASON` to schedule it. Function owners need access to the queue, attempt, callback, SMS history and reconciliation audit tables and the operator-grant lock function. Secure and provision these roles. The delayed path requires the exact current failed HTTP 429 dispatch with stored guidance, locks the job and attempt, refuses matching or job-only callback evidence, records the previous dispatch and provider time in the audit, deletes the attempt and queues the job no earlier than that time, atomically. Competing decisions cannot both succeed. It does not automatically retry, nor change the existing unknown-result resolution path.

Worker migrations 0180–0181 mark delayed retries, including existing delayed decisions during upgrade. Updated workers check callback evidence under the job lock before dispatch even without automatic callback configuration. Their DB login must be able to read the reconciliation and Twilio callback tables; without access the operation fails rather than dispatching. The marker remains on the job, so later retry decisions also receive the check. The marker requires an additional queue-row read for each Twilio dispatch. Worker migration 0182 adds a cooperative database trigger that rejects a legacy worker’s dispatching attempt for a marked job unless the transaction-local job/lease marker was set by an updated worker after its evidence check. The trigger owner needs SELECT on the queue table. A legacy worker may repeatedly claim a job, fail before dispatch and leave it for lease recovery; monitor backlog and upgrade all workers. Already reserved or in-flight sends are unaffected. This marker is forgeable by a database role able to execute arbitrary SQL and is not an authorization or protection against privileged writes, disabled triggers, old restored functions or data loss. Callbacks after the check commits or unlinked evidence can still permit duplicates. Apply 0180–0182 before upgrading all Twilio workers; the backfill updates queued/history rows and can take locks and time on large datasets. A disposable PostgreSQL test simulates the legacy insert and checks that the current worker can dispatch. `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... TEST_DISPOSABLE_POSTGRES_LOG=... bash scripts/test-twilio-legacy-fence.sh` additionally builds the old worker from commit `d6a4bef4` in temporary sources, verifies the trigger rejection in the disposable DB log and no outbound connection, then starts the current worker after lease expiry. A local proxy observes and closes its connection, producing `unknown`. This requires local Git history, build tools, a fully migrated disposable PostgreSQL and its readable server log; it does not contact Twilio or establish delivery, and uses an administrative DB login. A lost command response is ambiguous; inspect the queue and reconciliation audit rather than blindly issuing a new decision. The CLI has no request-id replay; API migrations 0100–0101 provide a separately authorized JWT path with request-id replay and lookup. The Angular management view supports this JWT path. Existing privileged direct writes and other retry mechanisms can bypass the schedule. A restricted-role disposable PostgreSQL test covers permissions, stale IDs, missing guidance, callback evidence, audit rollback and concurrent decisions; a queue-function test with mock Twilio confirms the job waits until due and is then submitted after a simulated time advance. Actual Twilio 429, real HTTP callbacks and production HA are unverified.

## Twilio 429 retry guidance

Worker migration 0178 stores a bounded numeric `Retry-After` value (one header, 0–86,400 seconds) and its computed earliest time for HTTP 429 results. Missing, duplicate, date-formatted, malformed or out-of-range values are recorded as unknown guidance. The optional operator-selected delayed path is described above; recording guidance alone does not schedule a retry and other retry paths may not enforce the time. The stored time uses the DB result-transaction clock, not the precise response instant; it cannot prevent retries through old binaries or direct operations. Twilio recommends backoff and respecting the header when supplied ([Twilio retry guidance](https://help.twilio.com/articles/48916449686299)). Apply worker 0178 before deploying updated workers or API migration 0099. Schema changes and per-attempt writes have operational cost. A local mock and disposable PostgreSQL exercise 429 and other responses; actual Twilio 429, automatic retries and production HA are unverified.

## Twilio dispatch account binding

Worker migration 0177 adds `account_sid` to each dispatch identity. Updated workers snapshot the configured Twilio account at reservation and the updated API accepts a signed attempt-specific callback only if its account matches that snapshot. Apply 0177 before deploying either updated API or worker, then roll out both; new API instances query this column even for legacy dispatches. Existing and old-worker rows have NULL account and retain legacy behavior by default; the API can instead require account-bound attempt callbacks as described in the API README, which rejects those legacy callbacks; job-only and unlinked callbacks are also not account-bound to a dispatch. The API's configured Twilio account and signature are still independently checked. This does not verify actual delivery, support multiple webhook accounts per API instance, or stop callbacks arriving after a retry dispatch has already begun. PostgreSQL and mock-Twilio worker tests confirm snapshot creation; synthetic signed API callbacks verify a mismatched dispatch account is refused and a matching account is accepted. Real Twilio and production HA remain unverified. The schema change takes a table lock.

## Optional 64-bit alert conditions

Worker migrations 0175–0176 and the updated `send-delivery-alert` can distinguish failed jobs, Resend automatic retry health and Slack shared cooldown health in `new-condition` mode. Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2=true`, `SWALLOW_ALERT_COOLDOWN_POLICY=new-condition`, and `SWALLOW_ALERT_SCOPED_DELIVERY=true`; leave the legacy `SWALLOW_ALERT_REQUIRE_DISTINCT_SHARED_CHECKS` and `SWALLOW_ALERT_BIND_SHARED_CHECKS` disabled. Without the V2 setting the previous 32-bit behavior remains. The API DB login requires its existing environment `alert_send` and each enabled check's read grants, as well as EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_reserve_alert_v2(text,text,text,integer,bigint)`, `public.novu_rust_observe_alert_conditions_v2(text,text,text,bigint,bigint)`, and the existing `public.novu_rust_finish_alert(text,text,text,uuid,text,integer)`. Failed checks retain the last known problem bit until observed successfully. The existing destination cooldown, blocked/unknown handling, and optional parallel delivery remain in effect.

The old and V2 masks are separate and there is no automatic state migration: choose new target IDs and coordinate **all** senders sharing each target to use the same V2 version and settings. Migration 0176 automatically marks a target when its first V2 scoped reservation succeeds and rejects later scoped legacy reservations and observations on that target; first V2 use rejects an occupied legacy target. Its slot-row lock serializes racing scoped reservations. For custom NOLOGIN function owners, grant SELECT on `public.novu_rust_alert_v2_targets` to the owners of the legacy reserve/observe functions and SELECT, INSERT to the owner of the V2 reserve function. Existing V2 slots with a non-null mask are marked during migration. Direct-table legacy senders, older manually reapplied function definitions, privileged writes and old state restoration bypass the guard; use scoped delivery for every sender. Existing occupied legacy targets require a new target ID; no mask is converted. A table alteration takes a lock; plan upgrades and permissions. This is not proof of provider receipt, and database loss, uncoordinated senders, concurrent checks and missed scheduler runs remain operational concerns. Disposable PostgreSQL tests with a restricted login exercise high bits, recovery, permissions and the real CLI's blocked destination; a local mock Slack verifies a new Slack condition bypasses an existing failed-job cooldown and repeated state remains suppressed. Actual external receipt, real IdP and multi-host HA remain unverified.

## Slack shared cooldown health and alerts

Worker migration 0173 adds `delivery-reconcile check-slack-cooldown ENV GRACE_SECONDS`. A monitoring DB login needs the environment-scoped `alert_view` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_slack_cooldown_health(text,integer)`. The policy must exist. The output contains `active`, `notBefore` (null before the first cooldown), `fallbackSeconds`, `deferred` and `overdue`; the latter counts deferred pending jobs whose `available_at` has passed by at least the selected grace of 1–86,400 seconds. Exit 0 means inactive with no deferred/overdue jobs, 2 means any of those conditions is present, and 1 means configuration, permission, DB or query error. Counts are snapshots, overdue is a subset of deferred, and manually modified jobs or nonparticipating workers are not covered. An active cooldown with no waiting jobs is still considered an alert condition.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_SLACK_COOLDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` to enable this check in `send-delivery-alert`; leave it unset to disable it. Give its DB login the monitoring permissions above and configure existing Slack and/or email delivery permissions and destinations. The alert includes aggregate counts; check failure attempts an alert and results in an error exit. Use an independent scheduler and monitor its execution and destination health: the same Slack workspace may be rate-limited, the DB may be unavailable, and a blocked or unknown alert delivery needs investigation. In legacy 32-bit `new-condition` mode this check shares the failed-job condition and failure bits with Resend automatic retry health, so another active problem in that category can suppress a separate immediate alert. A failed enabled check does not clear that category as healthy. Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_REQUIRE_DISTINCT_SHARED_CHECKS=true` to reject a `new-condition` invocation that enables more than one of these three checks, before database connection or destination binding; unset or `false` preserves the old behavior. The value must be `true` or `false`. This validation alone does not verify other processes or target IDs. Worker migration 0174 adds optional `SWALLOW_ALERT_BIND_SHARED_CHECKS=true`: it also requires `new-condition` and `SWALLOW_ALERT_REQUIRE_DISTINCT_SHARED_CHECKS=true`, and requires the DB login’s environment `alert_send` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_bind_alert_shared_check(text,text,text,text)`. After alert check and threshold configuration is validated, each configured transport and target is bound on first use to its one shared check or `none`; a different check on that target fails closed before alert observation or sending. Concurrent first registrations serialize through the unique key. Use new target IDs when enabling this feature, since existing delivery-slot masks can contain older shared conditions. Provision the function permission, then coordinate every sender using each target to enable both settings; old binaries, disabled settings, direct DB writes and restoration of old database state bypass the protection. The binding remains after a healthy run or later failure; a DBA must manage any replacement explicitly, with the delivery slot and audit implications considered. The selected Slack and email shared-check bindings are committed in one transaction; a binding conflict rolls both back. A lost or timed-out commit response can leave the outcome unknown, so inspect the bindings or retry with the same settings. This transaction does not include the separate credential-based destination binding or alert delivery. This is not independent condition bits and does not guarantee delivery or protect against uncoordinated senders. To isolate these three checks with the existing 32-bit mask, run separate scheduled invocations, enable only one of these three checks in each invocation, and assign a distinct stable target ID for each invocation and transport; all senders sharing a target must use the same check configuration. This can send multiple alerts to the same physical endpoint and has its own destination bindings and delivery slots. Unknown or rejected outcomes still block their individual slots, and delivery is not guaranteed. Coordinated alerts still depend on the existing cooldown state store and must use compatible binaries.

Migration 0173 creates a partial index on deferred jobs; evaluate write amplification, index build locking and query plans on production-sized data. Changes to `last_error`, directly edited rows, old workers, jobs already claimed and policy deletion can make counts incomplete; a missing policy is an error, not a healthy result. The monitor does not prove a worker is running, that a message was delivered, or that all deployments use the guard. Real Slack/email receipt, external scheduler and production HA are unverified. A disposable PostgreSQL 17 test checks restricted login, aggregate counts, environment isolation, blocked alert reservation and revoked permissions. Unit tests send the aggregate to local mock Slack and Resend endpoints. Run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test slack_cooldown_health -- --ignored` on a fully migrated disposable DB with role-creation privileges.

## Optional shared Slack cooldown

Worker migration 0172 adds an optional **environment-wide** cooldown shared by the Slack app, incoming-webhook and workflow-delivery workers. It is disabled unless every participating process explicitly sets `NOVU_SLACK_SHARED_COOLDOWN=true`; omission or `false` retains the previous behavior, and any other value fails startup. Before enabling it, a DB administrator provisions an environment policy and a worker grant for each DB login, and grants both function EXECUTEs. For example, substitute the actual environment and login:

```sql
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_slack_cooldown_policies(environment_id,fallback_seconds)
VALUES ('ENV', 60);
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_slack_cooldown_workers(environment_id,database_principal,principal_oid)
SELECT 'ENV',rolname,oid FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname='worker_role' AND rolcanlogin;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_slack_cooldown_check(text),
 public.novu_rust_slack_cooldown_record(text,integer) TO worker_role;
```

`fallback_seconds` must be 1–86,400 and is used only when a 429 has no supported single numeric Retry-After; a valid 0–86,400 value is used as supplied. Each recorded 429 extends, but never shortens, the shared `not_before`. Before reserving an external dispatch, an opted-in worker checks and locks that row in the same transaction as its dispatch reservation. While active, it returns the job to `pending`, schedules it at the shared time and records `Slack shared cooldown active` without creating a delivery attempt. The failed 429 itself is not automatically retried; use the separate operator decision path. The existing lease recovery and workflow fan-out continue. A policy or grant missing from an opted-in worker makes the DB operation fail; a previously claimed job can remain running until normal lease recovery. Restoring access and monitoring repeated DB failures are operational responsibilities.

This is deliberately conservative: unrelated Slack workspaces and channels within the same environment also wait, while other environments, separate databases, nonparticipating senders and the separate alert sender do not. It does not implement Slack's precise workspace/method/channel rate buckets. Deploy worker 0172 and both EXECUTE grants first, then coordinate all app, webhook and workflow workers for the environment; old binaries, disabled instances, direct library callers and privileged DB edits bypass it. A dispatch whose reservation committed before a 429 was recorded can still start or already be in flight. A 429 whose lease expired or whose result transaction fails does not extend the shared time. The guard cannot stop already-started requests, establish provider delivery or protect against loss of DB state, clock skew, asynchronous failover or split brain. Changing a policy later does not bring already-deferred jobs forward automatically. The feature adds a policy/grant check and row lock per opted-in dispatch, plus an update on 429; evaluate contention and backlog at production scale. The optional health check and aggregate alerts are described above; there is currently no built-in cooldown management UI.

A disposable PostgreSQL 17 test with test-only HTTP Slack adapters exercises app 429, shared deferral of webhook and workflow jobs, missing-header fallback, non-shortening updates, policy lock contention, grant revocation, environment separation and the default unguarded path. `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --features test-mocks --test slack_shared_cooldown -- --ignored` requires a fully migrated disposable DB and role creation privileges. It invokes the queue functions, not actual worker binaries, Slack, a real supervisor, alerting, production load or multi-host HA. During development the first link failed for lack of disk space; after cleaning the regenerable API Cargo target, the test passed.

## Operator-selected Slack 429 delayed retry

Apply worker migrations 0170–0171 before using `delivery-reconcile slack-retry-after ENV JOB ATTEMPT_ID OPERATOR REASON`. Grant the maintenance login the environment's existing `slack_retry` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_resolve_slack_after(text,uuid,uuid,text,text)`. The trusted function owner needs the permissions needed to lock the grant, read and lock the job and attempt, insert the resolution, delete the attempt, and update the job.
