Secure the owner, schema and tables. The current attempt ID and 429 guidance are available from the authorized Slack result API. API 0097 and the Angular Slack decision form support this delayed path using the same `slack_retry` permission. See the API and dashboard READMEs.

The function requires a failed app, incoming-webhook or workflow-delivery job and the exact current rejected HTTP 429 attempt with valid stored guidance. It locks the grant, job and attempt, writes the original provider-advised time and attempt ID in the resolution audit, deletes the attempt and schedules the job no earlier than that time, or immediately if the time has passed. Audit failure rolls back the full change and concurrent decisions on the same attempt cannot both succeed. Existing immediate `slack-resolve` / `slack-resolve-v2` and JWT retry remain available and do not enforce this delay. Permission to execute the new function does not itself grant the legacy path, and direct DB access or older workers may bypass assumptions.

A retry is an operator decision, not proof of non-delivery. Late or unobserved evidence, proxy responses, clock errors, changing endpoints and other senders may cause duplicate messages or further limits. Slack rates can apply across workspace, method and channel; this job-specific schedule is not a shared rate limiter. Cancellation or policy changes after reservation follow the worker's existing checks. Response loss can follow a committed decision; this CLI has no request-ID replay protection, so inspect the audit and current job before retrying.

Disposable PostgreSQL 17 with a restricted login and actual CLI checks missing guidance, permissions and environment, stale attempt ID, audit rollback, two competing decisions and scheduling for three kinds. The actual queue function with a mock Slack app checks that no job is claimed before its scheduled time and that it sends after the test advances the timestamp. It does not send to Slack or test delayed webhook/workflow delivery, original Novu behavior, network loss or production HA. Run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --features test-mocks --test slack_retry_after_schedule -- --ignored` on a fully migrated disposable DB with role creation privileges.

## Slack HTTP 429 Retry-After guidance

Slack's [rate-limit documentation](https://docs.slack.dev/apis/web-api/rate-limits/) describes HTTP 429 and a Retry-After delay for HTTP-based APIs, including incoming webhooks. Worker migration 0170 adds `retry_after_seconds` and `retry_not_before` to Slack attempts. New app, incoming-webhook and workflow-delivery workers record HTTP 429 as `rejected` and accept one nonnegative integer header in the range 0–86,400 seconds; missing, repeated, malformed, HTTP-date or larger values are recorded without guidance. `retry_not_before` is calculated with the DB clock when the result is stored. The raw response header and body are not stored. Slack JSON errors returned with HTTP 200 remain rejected without this HTTP 429 guidance. Existing attempts and old workers have no guidance.

Apply worker 0170 before deploying any new Slack workers and API migration 0096. Restricted worker DB roles need UPDATE on the two new columns. The existing JWT Slack result grant and function EXECUTE expose status and guidance; API 0096 requires worker 0170. The Angular Slack result screen displays it. This is only guidance: existing manual retry paths schedule immediately, do not enforce this timestamp, and deleting an attempt on retry removes its guidance. The optional delayed retry and conservative environment-wide cooldown are described above; no automatic retry policy or precise workspace/channel rate limiter is provided. DB clock skew, modified headers, late evidence, mixed worker versions and independently configured senders remain operational concerns. A 429 from a proxy is not proof about prior requests, and retry can duplicate a message.

Mock HTTP tests cover app and webhook header boundaries and duplicates. A fully migrated disposable PostgreSQL 17 test processes actual app, webhook and workflow queue jobs against a mock 429 and checks the saved delay and failed state; existing queue regression tests also passed. JWT result integration and a local browser with synthetic data checked access and display. Neither real Slack 429, delivery, original Novu behavior for this case, workspace-wide coordination nor production HA was verified. Reproduce the integrated worker check with `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --lib slack_429_guidance_is_persisted_for_each_queue_kind -- --ignored` on a fully migrated disposable DB.

워커 0166은 `delivery-reconcile list-resend-auto ENV GRACE_SECONDS FILTER [AFTER_JOB]`으로 0165의 주의 집계에 해당하는 작업을 UUID 순 20건씩 조회한다. FILTER는 `rate-limited`, `exhausted`, `guard-deferred`, `overdue-auto` 중 하나다. 환경별 `alert_view`와 `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_resend_auto_retry_jobs(text,integer,text,uuid)`가 필요하다. 작업 UUID·상태·대기 시각·현재 시도 상태와 HTTP 상태만 반환하고 payload·수신자·공급자 ID는 반환하지 않는다. UUID와 시각도 운영 정보이므로 접근을 제한한다. 각 페이지와 집계는 별도 스냅샷이라 동시 변경 시 누락·건수 차이가 날 수 있고 UUID는 시간순이 아니다. 정렬 인덱스 생성의 잠금·쓰기 비용과 대규모 조회 부하를 검토한다.

제한 DB 로그인·실제 CLI·임시 PostgreSQL에서 목록을 확인했고, 별도 API JWT 시험에서 네 필터와 21건의 두 페이지, 권한 분리와 payload 비노출을 검증했다. 실제 IdP·외부 발송·운영 HA는 미검증이다.

워커 0165는 환경별 Resend 429·자동 재시도 주의 상태를 집계한다. `delivery-reconcile check-resend-auto ENV GRACE_SECONDS`는 JSON의 `rateLimited`(기준 시간 이상 실패한 429), `exhausted`(그중 활성 정책의 횟수를 소진한 건), `guardDeferred`(보호 설정 불일치로 보류된 pending 건), `overdueAuto`(예약 시각과 기준 시간이 지난 자동 재시도 pending 건)를 반환한다. `exhausted`는 `rateLimited`의 부분집합이며 다른 항목도 겹칠 수 있다. GRACE_SECONDS는 1~86,400초다. 정상은 종료 0, 하나라도 양수면 2, 권한·DB·설정 오류는 1이다. 조회 DB 로그인에는 해당 환경의 `alert_view`와 `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_resend_auto_retry_health(text,integer)`가 필요하다.

선택적으로 `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_AUTO_GRACE_SECONDS=300`을 설정하면 기존 `send-delivery-alert`가 이 집계를 Slack·이메일 대상에 포함한다. 생략하면 비활성화되며 기존 Resend 전달 상태 검사와 독립적으로 켜거나 끌 수 있다. 검사 자체의 실패도 경보를 시도하고 오류로 종료한다. 모니터링 로그인에 위 조회 권한과 기존 발송 경로의 권한을 부여하고, 누락 실행·DB 장애·경보 전송 결과는 별도로 감시한다. `new-condition` 쿨다운에서는 기존 미확인 실패 작업과 같은 조건 범주를 공유하므로 이미 그 범주가 활성 상태인 동안 다른 항목이 추가되어도 별도 즉시 경보가 보장되지 않는다. 한 검사가 실패하면 공유 범주를 정상으로 지우지 않는다.

집계는 조회 시점의 DB 상태이며 발송·수신이나 모든 워커의 정상 동작을 보장하지 않는다. `rateLimited`는 실패 작업의 별도 확인 여부를 반영하지 않아 상태가 바뀌기 전까지 계속 집계된다. `exhausted`는 현재 활성 정책과 cutoff 기준이고 정책 비활성화 시 0이 될 수 있다. `guardDeferred`는 특정 오류 문자열과 pending 상태를 사용하며 직접 DB 수정·구버전 워커·다른 실패 유형은 탐지하지 못한다. 향후 예약 시각이 아직 지나지 않은 작업은 overdue가 아니다. 환경의 pending·failed Resend 작업과 관련 이력 집계가 추가되므로 실제 규모에서 실행 계획·부하를 확인한다. 제한 DB 계정·실제 CLI·임시 PostgreSQL에서 집계·환경 격리·권한 회수 및 차단된 경보 예약을 검증했고, 로컬 모의 Slack·Resend 어댑터에서 경보 내용 전달을 확인했다. 실제 외부 수신·스케줄러·운영 HA는 미검증이다. 재현: `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --test resend_auto_health -- --ignored` (전체 마이그레이션을 적용한 폐기 가능한 DB와 역할 생성 권한 필요).

워커 0164는 Resend 자동 정책에 요청 ID 기반 변경과 결과 조회를 추가한다. 기존 정책 쓰기 권한 `can_write`와 다음 두 함수에 대한 별도 EXECUTE 권한이 필요하다. 요청 ID는 운영자가 전송 전에 생성·보관한다.

```sql
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_resend_auto_policy_set_v2(text,uuid,bigint,boolean,integer,text,text),
 public.novu_rust_resend_auto_policy_request(text,uuid) TO operator_role;
```

`delivery-reconcile resend-auto-policy-set-v2 ENV REQUEST_ID EXPECTED enabled|disabled MAX now|preserve REASON`은 요청·조건부 정책 변경·감사를 함께 기록한다. 같은 환경과 DB 로그인·역할 OID에서 같은 ID와 동일한 내용을 재제출하면 원래 결과와 `replayed:true`를 반환하고 변경과 감사를 반복하지 않는다. 같은 ID에 다른 내용을 사용하면 거절한다. `delivery-reconcile resend-auto-policy-request ENV REQUEST_ID`는 현재 쓰기 권한을 재확인하고 같은 DB 로그인·역할 OID의 요청만 조회한다. 감사 조회에도 요청 ID가 표시되며 기존 기록은 null이다.

응답 유실 시 요청 ID와 내용을 보관한 뒤 조회하거나 정확히 같은 요청만 재제출한다. `found:false`는 이전 요청이 아직 진행 중일 가능성을 배제하지 않는다. 조회 결과는 당시 변경의 결과이며 현재 정책이 이후 변경되지 않았다는 뜻이 아니다. 조회와 재제출은 권한 회수 후 거절된다. DB 직접 변경, 요청 기록 삭제·보존 기간 관리 실패, 오래된 백업 복원·비동기 장애 전환 또는 별도 DB는 중복 방지 보장을 깨뜨릴 수 있다. 사유와 요청 메타데이터는 평문으로 저장되므로 비밀을 포함하지 말고 접근·보존을 관리한다. 이 기능은 동일 DB 로그인에 묶이며 다른 로그인이나 사람의 신원을 보증하지 않는다. 기존 `resend-auto-policy-set` 경로는 요청 ID 없이 계속 사용 가능하다.

임시 PostgreSQL 17, 제한 DB 로그인 및 실제 CLI에서 동일 요청 동시 제출·재제출, 다른 내용의 ID 충돌, 다른 로그인·환경 격리, 이후 정책 변경 후 원래 결과 조회, stale 요청과 감사 오류 롤백, 권한 회수를 확인했다. 클라이언트가 첫 응답을 무시하는 상황을 재제출로 모델링했으며 실제 네트워크 단절·운영 HA는 미검증이다. 재현: `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --test resend_auto_policy_requests -- --ignored` (전체 마이그레이션을 적용한 폐기 가능한 DB와 역할 생성 권한 필요).

워커 0163은 Resend 자동 429 정책을 DB 직접 쓰기 없이 관리하는 선택형 CLI를 제공한다. 최초 권한은 DB 관리자가 환경과 로그인 역할별로 `novu_rust_resend_auto_policy_admins`에 등록하며 `can_read`, `can_write`, `can_audit`을 따로 선택한다. 역할 이름과 로그인 역할 OID를 함께 확인하므로 역할을 삭제·재생성하면 다시 등록해야 한다. 각 경로에는 해당 함수의 EXECUTE 권한도 부여한다. 예를 들어 세 권한을 모두 부여하려면 다음 SQL을 DB 관리자가 환경·역할에 맞게 실행한다.

```sql
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_resend_auto_policy_admins
 (environment_id,database_principal,principal_oid,can_read,can_write,can_audit)
SELECT 'ENV',rolname,oid,true,true,true FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname='operator_role' AND rolcanlogin;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_resend_auto_policy_read(text),
 public.novu_rust_resend_auto_policy_set(text,bigint,boolean,integer,text,text),
 public.novu_rust_resend_auto_policy_audit_read(text,bigint) TO operator_role;
```

`delivery-reconcile resend-auto-policy-read ENV`는 정책 유무와 `revision`을 표시한다. 생성은 `resend-auto-policy-set ENV -1 enabled 3 now REASON`, 변경은 조회한 revision을 사용해 예를 들어 `resend-auto-policy-set ENV 0 disabled 3 preserve REASON`으로 수행한다. 최대 횟수는 1~10이고 `now`는 대상 시작 시각을 현재 DB 시각으로 변경하며 `preserve`는 기존 시각을 유지한다. 신규 정책에는 `now`가 필요하다. 비활성화 후 `preserve`로 다시 켜면 이전 시각 이후의 오래된 실패도 자동 재시도 대상이 될 수 있다. 한 번의 변경마다 revision이 증가하고 사유·이전/변경 내용·DB 로그인은 감사에 원자적으로 기록된다. 이미 예약된 작업은 정책 변경으로 취소되지 않는다. `resend-auto-policy-audit ENV [BEFORE]`는 ID 내림차순 50건과 다음 커서를 반환하며 ID 순서는 커밋 순서나 여러 페이지의 단일 스냅샷을 보장하지 않는다.

동일 revision의 동시 변경은 하나만 성공하고 권한 회수는 진행 중인 변경을 기다린다. 응답 유실은 성공 여부가 불명이며 새 요청으로 자동 재시도하지 않는다. 현재 revision과 감사를 확인해 변경 내용과 사유를 대조한다. 기존 명령에는 요청 ID가 없으며 위 최신 0164 경로는 요청 ID를 지원한다. 감사에는 이 함수 경로만 기록되고 직접 DB 변경은 기록되지 않으며, DB 소유자와 감사 보관·사유의 민감정보를 별도 관리해야 한다. DB 로그인은 사람의 신원을 독립적으로 증명하지 않는다. 기존 직접 SQL 설정도 계속 작동하지만 revision·감사를 우회한다. 제한 DB 역할·실제 CLI·임시 PostgreSQL 17에서 권한 분리, 환경 격리, revision 경합, 감사 실패 롤백, 스케줄러 정책 잠금과 권한 회수 경합을 확인했다. 실제 IdP·운영 HA와 정책 대량 감사 페이지는 미검증이다. 재현: `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --test resend_auto_policy_admin -- --ignored` (전체 마이그레이션을 적용한 폐기 가능한 DB와 역할 생성 권한 필요).

워커 0162는 자동 429 재시도 감사에 별도 표시를 남기고, 새 Resend 워커가 작업을 잠근 상태에서 마지막 재시도 표시를 확인한다. 자동 재시도 작업에 태그·두 증거 가드·retry별 키 중 하나라도 빠졌으면 외부 요청이나 발송 시도를 만들지 않고 5초 뒤 다시 대기열에 넣는다. 정책을 끈 뒤에도 이미 예약한 자동 작업은 보호한다. 대기 중 부적절한 워커가 반복해서 가져가면 시작 지연과 작업 claim 횟수 증가가 발생하며 로그·대기열을 감시하고 워커 설정을 수정해야 한다. 기존 수동 재시도에는 이 추가 조건을 적용하지 않는다.

모든 새 Resend 워커의 DB 계정에 `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_resend_auto_dispatch_allowed(text,uuid,boolean,boolean,boolean,boolean) TO worker_role`이 필요하며, 기능을 끈 경우에도 새 함수 실행 권한이 필요하다. 배포 전 0162를 적용하고 권한을 부여한 뒤 모든 구버전 워커를 교체한다. 0162는 0161에서 기록한 고정 이름·사유의 기존 감사 행을 자동으로 표시한다. 운영자가 동일한 이름·사유를 수동 입력한 과거 행은 보수적으로 자동으로 표시될 수 있다. 감사 테이블 변경·과거 행 갱신의 잠금과 부하를 계획한다. 함수 실행 권한이 있는 DB 계정은 알려진 환경·작업 ID에 대한 자동 여부를 유추할 수 있으므로 워커 계정에만 부여한다. 이전 바이너리, 직접 테이블 변경, 등록 정책을 사용하지 않는 라이브러리 호출과 DB 복원은 이 검사를 우회할 수 있다. DB 작업 잠금 이후 공급자 요청을 보내기 전에도 콜백이 도착할 수 있으며 중복 방지를 보장하지 않는다.

임시 PostgreSQL 17·제한 함수 실행 계정에서 네 설정을 각각 빠뜨린 경우의 차단을 확인했다. 실제 큐 함수·모의 Resend에서 정책 비활성화 후에도 미보호 워커는 외부 요청 없이 작업을 연기하고, 보호 설정을 가진 워커가 발송하는 것을 확인했다. 0161 감사 행을 가진 별도 DB를 0162로 업그레이드하여 기존 자동 판정이 표시되고 차단되는 것도 확인했다. 실제 공급자, 혼합 구버전 운영과 다중 호스트 HA는 미검증이다. 재현은 위 `resend_auto_429` 테스트를 사용한다. 등록 정책을 사용하는 발송 준비마다 DB 함수 호출 1회와 최근 감사 이력 조회가 추가된다.

선택형 Resend 429 자동 재시도는 워커 0161 적용 후 DB 관리자가 환경별 정책을 등록하고, 해당 환경의 모든 Resend 워커를 새 버전과 아래 설정으로 통일해 사용한다. 기본값은 꺼짐이며 기존 수동 지연·즉시 재시도도 계속 선택할 수 있다. 정책의 `not_before` 기본값은 등록 시각이고 그 이전에 시작한 발송 시도는 자동 예약하지 않는다. 비활성 후 다시 활성화할 때는 `not_before=clock_timestamp()`도 함께 지정한다. 운영자가 별도로 과거 시각을 지정하면 오래된 작업도 대상이 될 수 있다. DB 직접 변경과 기존 바이너리는 정책을 우회하므로 활성화 전 모든 이전 워커를 중지·교체한다.

```sql
-- 환경과 최대 횟수는 운영자가 선택한다. 1~10회, 기존 정책은 명시적으로 수정한다.
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_resend_auto_retry_policy(environment_id,enabled,max_retries)
VALUES ('ENV',true,3);
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_resend_auto_retry(text) TO worker_role;
-- worker_role에 환경별 기존 retry 권한도 필요하다.
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_delivery_operator_grants(environment_id,database_principal,decision,principal_oid)
SELECT 'ENV',rolname,'retry',oid FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname='worker_role';
```

모든 해당 환경 워커에 `NOVU_RESEND_AUTO_RETRY_429=true`, `NOVU_RESEND_RETRY_EVIDENCE_GUARD=true`, `NOVU_RESEND_CORRELATION_TAG=true`, `NOVU_RESEND_RETRY_CORRELATION_GUARD=true`, `NOVU_RESEND_IDEMPOTENCY_SCOPE=retry`를 설정한다. 자동 모드에서 필수 설정이 빠진 워커는 시작을 거절한다. 기존 idempotency 정책 및 배포 전환 절차도 지켜야 한다. 자동 검사에는 함수 실행 권한과 환경별 retry 권한이 필요하다. 정책이 없거나 비활성이면 예약하지 않는다. 정책 변경은 검사 트랜잭션과 잠금으로 직렬화되지만 이미 예약된 작업은 취소되지 않는다.

워커는 약 5초마다 해당 환경의 429 실패 건 하나를 검사한다. 지원되는 `Retry-After`가 있고 이전 판정이 모두 429 재시도이며 총 재시도 판정 횟수가 정책 상한보다 작을 때만 기존 지연 판정과 감사 기록을 사용한다. 기존 수동 재시도도 이 상한에 포함한다. 결과 불명, 헤더 누락, 다른 상태·과거 비429 판정과 확인된 콜백은 자동 재시도하지 않는다. 재시도 준비 시 기존의 공급자 ID·상관 태그 증거 검사를 전체 이력에 적용한다. 검사와 발송 준비 후에 도착한 콜백까지 차단하지 못하며, 태그가 없거나 외부 시스템에서 누락된 증거는 식별하지 못한다. 실패한 검사와 DB 연결은 운영자가 감시해야 한다. 이 기능은 환경별 고정 횟수 제한이지 공급자 계정 전체의 처리율 제한이 아니다. DB 시계, 복제 장애 전환과 이전 버전 혼합 운영은 별도 고려가 필요하다. 잘못된 429 판정, 프록시 응답, 지연된 증거 또는 멱등성 기간·요청 내용 변경으로 중복이 발생할 수 있다. 자동 모드는 retry별 멱등성 키를 사용하므로 특히 공급자 접수 여부가 불확실하면 켜지 않는다.

임시 PostgreSQL 17·제한 DB 로그인·모의 Resend와 실제 큐 함수에서 기본 비활성, 권한·환경 격리, 두 동시 예약 중 한 건, 최대 횟수, 오래된 상관 태그의 예약 전·후 콜백 차단을 확인했다. 실제 워커 프로세스에서 모의 429 작업의 미래 예약도 확인했으며 외부 발송은 하지 않았다. 실제 Resend 429와 다중 호스트 HA, 혼합 버전 배포는 미검증이다. 재현: `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --features test-mocks --test resend_auto_429 -- --ignored` (전체 마이그레이션을 적용한 폐기 가능한 DB와 역할 생성 권한 필요). 활성 검사에는 워커마다 약 5초에 DB 호출 1회가 추가되고, 선택된 재시도에는 감사·큐 변경 트랜잭션이 추가된다.

Resend 429의 대기 안내를 적용하려면 워커 0160 적용 후 `delivery-reconcile resend-retry-after ENV JOB OPERATOR REASON`을 선택한다. 환경별 기존 `retry` 권한과 별도의 `GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_resolve_resend_after(text,uuid,text,text) TO 운영자_DB_역할`이 필요하다. 별도 scoped 설정 없이 이 함수 경로를 사용한다. 실패한 Resend 429에 유효한 대기 안내가 있을 때만 기존 증거 검사와 감사·큐 변경을 한 트랜잭션에서 수행하고, 작업을 DB 시계 기준 안내 시각 이후로 예약한다. 안내가 이미 지났으면 즉시 대기열에 넣는다. 기존 `retry ENV JOB - OPERATOR REASON`은 즉시 재시도로 계속 사용할 수 있다.

감사 행의 `resend_retry_not_before`에는 원래 안내 시각이 기록된다. 시도·안내가 없거나 이미 변경된 작업, 해당 공급자 콜백 증거가 있는 경우는 거절한다. 동시 결정은 작업 잠금으로 직렬화하며 감사 기록 오류는 전체 변경을 롤백한다. DB 직접 수정과 기존 즉시 재시도 경로는 이 선택형 대기를 우회할 수 있다. DB 시계 변경, 데이터 손실·비동기 장애 전환, 대기 기간 중 공급자 상태 변경, 다른 작업·계정 전체의 요청률은 보호하지 않는다. 이 명령은 운영자의 명시적 판정이며 별도 자동 정책은 위 최신 항목을 참고한다. 잘못된 판정과 공급자 수신 확인의 한계로 중복 발송이 가능하다.

임시 로컬 PostgreSQL 17, 제한 DB 계정과 실제 CLI에서 권한·환경 격리, 누락된 안내와 콜백 거절, 감사 실패 롤백, 두 동시 결정 중 하나만 성공, 감사 시각 보존을 확인했다. 실제 큐 함수와 모의 Resend에서 기한 전 미처리 및 기한을 테스트에서 앞당긴 뒤 발송을 확인했다. 실제 Resend 429와 다중 호스트 HA는 미검증이다. 재현: `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --features test-mocks --test resend_retry_after_schedule -- --ignored` (전체 서비스 마이그레이션을 적용한 폐기 가능한 DB와 역할 생성 권한 필요).

Resend가 HTTP 429와 단일 `Retry-After` 초 값을 보내면 워커 0159는 0~86,400초 정수만 수집하고 해당 시도의 `retry_after_seconds` 및 DB 시계 기준 `retry_not_before`를 저장한다. 다른 형식·여러 헤더·범위 초과·누락은 값을 저장하지 않는다. 원문 헤더와 응답 본문은 저장하지 않는다. API 0089는 기존 운영자 결과 조회 권한으로 이 안내를 제공한다. 새 워커 적용 전 0159를 배포하고 제한된 워커 DB 계정에 두 새 열의 UPDATE 권한을 부여한다. 이전 워커와 이 변경 전 시도에는 값이 없다. 새 API를 적용하기 전에 워커 0159가 필요하다. [Resend의 속도 제한 안내](https://www.resend.com/changelog/api-rate-limit)는 `Retry-After`를 재요청 전 대기 초로 설명한다.

이 값은 참고 정보이며 재시도를 자동 예약하거나 강제하지 않는다. 수동 재시도는 운영자가 시각과 전송 결과를 독립 확인하고 결정하며 동일 계정의 다른 워커·서비스 요청률도 별도로 관리해야 한다. 저장 시 DB 시계가 정확해야 하고 중간 프록시가 헤더를 바꿀 수 있다. 해당 시도를 재시도하면 기존 시도 행과 안내는 삭제된다. HTTP 날짜 형식은 위 최신 항목을 참고한다. 범위 밖 대기 값은 지원하지 않으며 실제 Resend 429는 미검증이다. 모의 Resend 및 임시 PostgreSQL로 헤더 범위, DB 기록 및 재발송 없음, 제한 JWT API와 실제 브라우저의 표시를 확인했다. 운영 HA는 미검증이다.

FCM HTTP 성공 응답은 스트리밍으로 최대 16 KiB까지 읽는다. `name`이 없거나 비어 있거나 1,024바이트 초과 또는 제어 문자를 포함하면 잘못된 응답으로 처리한다. 본문 초과·잘못된 JSON·읽기 오류도 발송 여부를 확인할 수 없으므로 큐는 해당 시도를 `unknown`으로 기록하고 자동 재전송하지 않는다. 이 제한을 초과하는 공급자의 유효한 성공 응답도 수동 조사가 필요하다. API를 호출하기 전 요청 검증은 바뀌지 않으며 기존 워커를 모두 교체해야 같은 제한이 적용된다. 이 보호는 전송 요청당 메모리를 제한하며 네트워크나 공급자 자체의 과부하를 해결하지 않는다.

로컬 모의 FCM에서 정상·빈 ID·긴 ID·제어 문자·잘못된 JSON·크기 초과 응답을 테스트했다. 임시 PostgreSQL 17과 실제 큐 처리 함수에서는 크기 초과 응답을 받은 작업이 `failed`/`unknown`으로 남고 다음 큐 조회에서 다시 보내지 않는 것을 확인했다. 실제 FCM과 운영 부하·HA는 검증하지 않았다.

## Attempt-fenced Slack resolution

Worker migration `0158` assigns a random UUID to each current Slack delivery attempt, including attempts already present during migration. New app, webhook and workflow-delivery attempts get new IDs automatically. `delivery-reconcile list-slack-v2 ENV [AFTER_JOB]` returns the existing authorized attention list plus `attemptId` (null for entries without an attempt). After independently investigating the current outcome, `delivery-reconcile slack-resolve-v2 ENV JOB ATTEMPT_ID retry|accepted OPERATOR REASON` locks the operator grant, job and attempt, checks the exact ID and eligible state, and atomically records the attempt ID and decision in the audit. An old ID is rejected if the job has since been retried and acquired a new attempt. Two concurrent decisions on one attempt cannot both succeed. `accepted` remains an operator assertion and `retry` can duplicate a message; neither proves delivery.

Use existing environment-specific `slack_view` and `slack_retry`/`slack_accepted` grants. Give the CLI login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_list_slack_attention_v2(text,uuid)` and/or `public.novu_rust_resolve_slack_v2(text,uuid,uuid,text,text,text)`. The trusted list function owner needs EXECUTE on the existing list function and SELECT on attempts; the resolve owner needs EXECUTE on the grant-lock function, SELECT/UPDATE on jobs and attempts, DELETE on attempts and INSERT on resolutions. Grant owners the underlying rights required by their nested functions, and protect owners, schema and tables. Existing audit rows and legacy decisions have null `attempt_id`; UUIDs are local identifiers, not Slack evidence. List pages are snapshots in UUID order, not chronological or exhaustive during concurrent changes.

Deploy the migration and new CLI before using the v2 commands. The legacy `slack-resolve` and `novu_rust_resolve_slack` remain unfenced; to enforce the new path, stop old operators, revoke their legacy function execution and direct table write privileges, and protect privileged owners. Old workers can still create attempts via the column default, but direct writes or changing IDs bypass the guard. The migration adds a volatile default to existing attempts and may rewrite or lock a large table; plan space and maintenance. Lost responses must be reconciled against current state and audit before a new decision. Asynchronous DB loss, split brain and restored snapshots can weaken fencing.

A disposable local PostgreSQL 17 integration test uses a restricted login and actual CLI to verify listing, environment isolation, retry, new attempt ID, stale retry/accept rejection, permission revocation, audit IDs and two simultaneous decisions. It inserts synthetic attempts and does not contact Slack. Actual Slack delivery, every lock interleaving, production migration cost and multi-host HA remain unverified. Run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test slack_attempt_fence -- --ignored` on a fresh disposable database; the test applies migrations and creates a role.

## Manual retry for Resend HTTP 429

A Resend response with HTTP 429 remains a `rejected` attempt and is not automatically retried. With worker migration `0157` and the updated CLI, an authorized operator may use the existing `delivery-reconcile retry ENV JOB - OPERATOR REASON` for that specific status after investigating it. The scoped path requires the existing `retry` grant and `novu_rust_resolve_email_sms` EXECUTE; the direct path uses the existing database privileges. Both preserve the 429 and prior correlation tag in the audit, reject a matching authenticated callback, and atomically reset the job for a later worker. `accepted` is still restricted to unknown attempts, and other rejected HTTP statuses remain ineligible. The existing `list` command lists unknown attempts, not rejected 429s; use failed-job monitoring and separately authorized result/evidence or database inspection to identify the job and status.

[Resend describes HTTP 429 and its `Retry-After` header](https://www.resend.com/changelog/api-rate-limit); Swallow does not persist that header or implement automatic backoff/rate control here. Investigate the provider response and capacity before retrying. A late or missing callback does not establish non-acceptance; a retry can duplicate delivery, particularly with retry-scoped idempotency keys. Existing worker retry guards and idempotency configuration remain independent. Deploy migration and updated CLI together; old direct CLI binaries still reject the 429. Do not reapply older migrations 0110 or 0135 afterward without reapplying 0157. The migration replaces existing functions and needs the same trusted ownership and permissions; it can acquire DDL locks.

A local disposable PostgreSQL 17 integration test uses a restricted operator login and actual CLI to verify 429 retry, denial of manual acceptance and other rejections, tagged callback blocking, audit and both direct/scoped paths. A separate queue-harness test obtains a 429 from a mock provider, applies an authorized retry and records a mocked successful response. No real Resend rate limit, automatic backoff, operational HA or full Novu parity was verified. Run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test scoped_email_sms_resolution -- --ignored` against a fresh disposable DB; the test applies migrations.

## Resend idempotency policy dispatch fence

Worker migration `0156` and updated `resend-worker` check the registered idempotency scope in the transaction that prepares an attempt. Grant the worker login `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_check_resend_idempotency_scope(text,text)` as well as the policy table `SELECT` and `UPDATE` required by the row lock and the registration permissions described below. A missing row, different scope, revoked privilege or database error prevents attempt creation and dispatch. The already-claimed job remains leased until recovery; monitor the worker and queue. The guard locks the policy row through commit, so a concurrent update/delete waits, or a guard arriving after the change rejects the old scope. The worker reconnects after a guard error; a conflicting registration then exits.

The lock ends **before** the external request. A configuration change after commit cannot cancel a request already prepared or in flight, and older workers, direct function callers that omit the guard, DB privilege bypass and inconsistent or lost database state remain outside the fence. Stop all workers and reconcile in-flight work before changing the policy; do not treat the guard as a substitute for coordinated rollout. Each dispatch adds a locked DB lookup and policy changes can wait on active transactions.

A disposable local PostgreSQL 17 test uses actual idle workers and verifies a stale worker does not create an attempt after policy change; the request path uses a closed local proxy and no external provider. It checks a policy update waiting on the guard, stale-scope rejection and restricted SQL function privilege revocation. Actual provider requests, every lock ordering, production load and multi-host HA remain unverified. Run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test resend_idempotency_policy -- --ignored` against a fresh disposable database.

## Resend idempotency scope agreement

Worker migration `0155` adds a per-environment idempotency scope registration. The updated `resend-worker` registers its selected `job` or `retry` scope after connecting and before consuming jobs; a conflicting scope exits with an error. Concurrent first registrations serialize, so one scope wins. Grant the worker DB login `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `public.novu_rust_resend_idempotency_policy` in addition to its existing permissions. Missing migration, registration privileges or DB access prevents processing and causes reconnect attempts; monitor the worker and queue. This registration is required even for the default `job` mode.

To change scope, stop **all** Resend workers for that environment, verify no previous dispatch remains in flight, reconcile unknown and pending jobs, then have a trusted administrator delete the environment's registration using a parameterized `DELETE FROM public.novu_rust_resend_idempotency_policy WHERE environment_id=$1`. Start only updated workers with the same new scope. Protect that table and its owner: registration is checked on connection; the separate dispatch fence described above also checks each new guarded attempt. Older binaries do not register or enforce it. A DB restore, asynchronous failover, split brain, separate databases or direct SQL can lose or bypass the agreement; this does not prevent duplicate email or replace the retry evidence checks.

A fresh local PostgreSQL 17 test runs real idle worker processes and checks identical scopes, conflicting scopes, simultaneous conflicting first registrations and environment isolation. It uses the DB administrator account and sends no email. Limited-role deployment, real provider delivery, rolling upgrades, cross-host failure and production HA remain unverified. Reproduce with `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test resend_idempotency_policy -- --ignored` against a **fresh disposable** database; the test applies migrations and creates environments.

## Optional Resend retry-scoped idempotency key

`NOVU_RESEND_IDEMPOTENCY_SCOPE=retry` selects a key derived from the job UUID and the latest persisted manual retry decision UUID. The first dispatch without a retry decision retains `swallow/<job UUID>`; a retried dispatch uses `swallow/<job UUID>/retry/<decision UUID>`. The default `job` retains the existing job-wide key. Other values reject startup. The retry mode requires worker migration 0056 and SELECT on `novu_rust_delivery_reconciliations` for the worker DB login; a DB error stops dispatch before the attempt transaction commits. Existing webhook evidence guards remain independent and should be configured as appropriate.

This choice permits a new provider request after a manual retry decision, including when a new correlation tag changes the request body. It also removes provider-level deduplication across those decisions: **a mistaken retry can deliver a second email**. An operator must independently establish non-acceptance before deciding to retry; missing or delayed webhooks do not prove it. Resend retains keys for a limited period and can return 409 for a reused key with a changed payload ([Resend documentation](https://resend.com/changelog/idempotency-keys)). No automatic retry is added. Coordinate the same mode across all worker replicas, stop old workers before switching, and review in-flight and pending jobs; older or differently configured workers use different keys, increasing duplicate risk. Reusing a decision ID after DB restore, direct DB changes, concurrent old workers or failover may also change the outcome. Guard against database data loss and unauthorized writes.

The existing disposable local PostgreSQL integration fixture exercises a real manual retry decision and mock provider request, checking the decision-scoped header, acceptance and existing callback guard. It also checks the legacy key. Tests use a DB administrator and a queue harness, not the `resend-worker` process or real Resend. Actual provider delivery, mixed-version rollout, all failure interleavings and production HA remain unverified. Run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --lib resend_queue::tests::workflow_delivery_revalidates_subscriber -- --ignored` against a **fresh disposable** database; the fixture creates schema objects.

`SWALLOW_ALERT_FANOUT_TARGET_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` optionally limits each alert-fanout child to 1–3600 seconds; unset or `0` disables the limit. On timeout the parent attempts to kill and reap that child for up to two seconds, reports `timed out; outcome unknown`, returns failure, and continues the remaining destinations subject to concurrency and shutdown policy. It does not retry the timed-out target. The child may already have sent an alert or committed DB state; inspect its alert slot and provider evidence before taking action. A forced stop can leave a slot in flight until normal recovery, and killing the direct child does not guarantee termination of descendants or a process stuck in the kernel. The timeout starts after the child is spawned and does not cover reading the fanout configuration itself; slow filesystem operations can still block that read; the optional regular-file policy described above rejects FIFOs on Unix. Test fixture child processes verify both alert modes, subsequent destinations, no automatic second attempt, direct-child termination and invalid limits. A separate Unix process test stalls the real delivery alert command on a FIFO and confirms the next target is attempted. These tests do not send an external alert or demonstrate production process supervision, DB recovery or filesystem stalls.

For startup secrets loaded through `novu_rust_contracts::secret::from_env`, `SWALLOW_SECRET_REGULAR_FILES_ONLY=true` opts into reading only regular files, including symlinks to regular files; the default `false` preserves existing behavior. On Unix the opened file is checked after a nonblocking open, so a FIFO with no writer is rejected instead of hanging the process. This applies to the shared `from_env` calls used by delivery and database Slack/email alert commands and other consumers, including alert-fanout child processes that inherit the setting. Invalid policy values and simultaneous inline/file sources are rejected. Existing secret size and content limits still apply. It also covers the FCM worker’s non-reloaded `NOVU_FCM_PRIVATE_KEY_FILE` and `NOVU_FCM_INTEGRATIONS_FILE`, including their multiline size limits. FCM reload modes already require regular files independently of this setting. Other direct file reads and external clients are not covered; changing the variable does not reload a running process. A slow or stalled filesystem may still block regular-file reads; non-Unix FIFO behavior is unverified. Protect secret files and monitor each target: a rejected credential can prevent that target's alert. Unix process tests exercise both actual alert commands with FIFO Slack/Resend credentials, symlinked regular files, conflicting sources and invalid policy. FCM tests reject FIFO private-key and integration files without reload and start the actual worker using symlinks to valid test credentials against local PostgreSQL. No external alert or FCM notification is sent.

# Reloadable credential file types

Reloadable Resend, SendGrid, Twilio and standalone Slack credential files must resolve to a regular file. On Unix the worker opens them with nonblocking mode before checking the opened handle, so a FIFO without a writer is rejected instead of hanging; symlinks to regular files remain supported. This applies at startup and reload only when the reloadable source is selected. Legacy startup-only sources retain their previous behavior. Filesystem operations on a slow or unavailable filesystem may still block; nonblocking mode is not a filesystem timeout. Tests exercise FIFO startup for all four workers, a FIFO during a running Slack worker with Docker PostgreSQL, and symlink-to-regular-file reading. Non-Unix blocking behavior and production secret mounts are not tested.

# Optional completed attachment cleanup

Worker migration `0133` and `attachment-retention` provide an opt-in, environment-scoped cleanup of attachment bytes in completed workflow and workflow-generated Resend/SendGrid/SMTP queue jobs (SMTP requires worker migration 0216). They do not run on a schedule. Pending, running, failed, cancelled and standalone email jobs are excluded. Completion means queue processing completed, not recipient delivery. Parent and child jobs have separate copies and eligibility; a pending child remains untouched. Existing completed jobs start their retention clock when the migration runs. Assess the migration's backfill, index creation and trigger overhead before deployment.

A DBA registers each PostgreSQL LOGIN and its role OID in `novu_rust_attachment_retention_grants` for the intended environment and grants EXECUTE only on the required functions. Example, replacing the environment and login names:

```sql
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_attachment_retention_grants
  (environment_id, database_principal, principal_oid, can_view, can_scrub)
SELECT 'ENV', rolname, oid, true, false FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'retention_view' AND rolcanlogin;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_list_attachment_retention(text,integer,integer),
  public.novu_rust_attachment_retention_audit(text,bigint) TO retention_view;
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_attachment_retention_grants
  (environment_id, database_principal, principal_oid, can_view, can_scrub)
SELECT 'ENV', rolname, oid, false, true FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'retention_scrub' AND rolcanlogin;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_scrub_attachment_retention(text,integer,integer,text) TO retention_scrub;
```

With `DATABASE_URL` configured for the appropriate login and TLS, run `attachment-retention list ENV MIN_AGE_SECONDS LIMIT`, `attachment-retention scrub ENV MIN_AGE_SECONDS LIMIT REASON`, or `attachment-retention audit ENV [AFTER_ID]`. Age is 0–31536000 seconds, limit is 1–100. Listing returns metadata only; scrubbing removes the relevant payload field and records the actor, job and non-secret reason atomically. A scrubbed job cannot normally be requeued or have its payload restored. Choose the age and batch size only after evaluating investigation and replay needs. Empty batches do not prove no eligible rows exist because locked rows are skipped. If a scrub response is lost or times out, reconcile audit and job states before retrying: a new call can scrub a different batch. Grant administration itself is not audited; database owners and privileged direct changes can bypass controls. Shared DB logins do not identify an individual operator.

This does not erase old PostgreSQL row versions, WAL, replicas, backups, logs, provider copies, or copies outside the named fields and job kinds. It does not encrypt attachments or guarantee secure deletion. Protect all such copies and set separate retention and recovery policies. Docker PostgreSQL tests cover role and environment isolation, exclusions, concurrent scrubbing, locked rows, audit rollback, revocation and replay prevention; production load, backup removal and HA failover are untested.

Attachment-job version fence (worker migration `0132`): the current API labels nonempty attachment-bearing workflow jobs `experimental_static_in_app_attachments_v1`; current in-app workers claim that kind and the original kind in one ordered query and recover expired leases for both. The migration's trigger also changes pending legacy-kind jobs containing nonempty or malformed `emailAttachments` into the new kind, including jobs produced by the prior attachment-capable API and expired legacy jobs returned to pending. It migrates existing pending rows, while ordinary legacy jobs remain unchanged. Old workers only claim the legacy kind and therefore cannot claim these fenced pending jobs. Queue health still counts all kinds; configure and monitor it so missing upgraded workers do not leave work waiting silently.

This does not stop an old worker that already claimed a legacy attachment job before the migration, nor an external writer that disables/bypasses the trigger or manually changes job state. Before rollout, stop or drain all old in-app workers and inspect any running legacy attachment jobs and downstream delivery evidence; a running job may already have created or sent a message without attachments. Apply 0132, deploy the updated workers and API, and allow normal lease recovery only after investigating uncertain in-flight work. Do not blindly requeue or replay externally dispatched email. If only old workers remain, fenced jobs remain pending; rollback to them alone does not process attachment jobs. The migration scans/updates pending queue rows and installs a trigger on inserts and selected updates, so assess locking and load before deployment. The generic expired-job recovery is safe here only for the pre-fanout workflow job; channel workers have separate external-delivery recovery rules. Disposable PostgreSQL tests cover upgrade of existing pending jobs, old/new claim isolation, malformed metadata, ordinary and attachment job ordering, legacy and new lease recovery, and an actual new worker process. The API-to-two-mock-provider attachment flow was rerun. Full multi-host rollout, DB failover and production load remain untested.

Experimental workflow email attachments: the trigger API accepts up to 10 attachments and 512 KiB of decoded data in total, using strict standard Base64 strings or JSON-serialized `Buffer` bytes. Each attachment requires `name` and `mime`; optional `cid`, `disposition` (`inline` or `attachment`) and `channels: ["email"]` are supported. Other channel combinations, unknown fields and invalid data are rejected at trigger time. Resend accepts explicit `attachment` without a CID and `inline` with a nonempty CID shorter than 128 characters; it maps the latter to `content_id` and does not send a disposition field. Other combinations fail at dispatch; SendGrid supports these dispositions. The API can therefore accept a request that later fails at the Resend worker. Original Novu accepts other Base64/Buffer inputs and uploads files to external storage; this subset stores decoded bytes in the PostgreSQL workflow queue and duplicates them into per-recipient email jobs. File bytes are removed from the ordinary event payload and notification records, but queue rows, replicas, backups, DB logs, and privileged readers can contain them. No encryption or automatic removal is supplied for these copies; an optional manual cleanup for completed jobs is described below. Protect and manage all copies before use. Large fanouts multiply storage and query traffic, and the API request-body limit also applies. This is not the original storage contract or complete attachment compatibility. Worker migration 0132 and the current API route attachment-bearing jobs to a separate job kind that older in-app workers do not claim; the rollout and in-flight limitations are described below. Docker PostgreSQL tests exercise trigger, fanout and both workers against mock HTTP providers; pinned original provider wire comparisons cover selected attachments, not original-server attachment upload or real delivery. The original Resend SDK serializes its byte content as a Buffer object while this adapter uses Base64; no live attachment comparison was made.

Optional target binding (worker migration `0130`): set a common, randomly generated secret of at least 32 bytes in `SWALLOW_ALERT_BINDING_KEY_FILE` (or `SWALLOW_ALERT_BINDING_KEY`) on every alert sender. Grant its DB LOGIN EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_bind_alert_destination(text,text,text,text)` and the environment's existing `alert_send`; apply this to each alert-state database used. The first successful check binds `(environment, channel, target ID)` to an HMAC-SHA256 fingerprint of the normalized Slack webhook URL, or the email provider and exact recipient address. Later checks with a different destination or key reject that target before condition observation or dispatch while other usable targets remain eligible; the command exits 1. `alert-fanout` inherits this setting for each child. The key and raw destinations are not written to the binding table, but the fingerprint and database parameter logs are sensitive and must be protected.

This is an opt-in first-use consistency check, not validation that a destination is correct or independently authorized. Before enabling it, verify existing target-to-destination mappings: the first binding cannot reconstruct an older slot's destination. Older binaries or senders without the key bypass the check, and privileged DB users can directly modify or pre-bind values; restrict DB permissions and update all cooperating senders. Changing a Slack webhook, email provider/recipient or binding key requires a new non-secret target ID and coordinated deployment; email sender address, provider credentials and SendGrid region are not included. No automatic binding deletion, key rotation or audited management UI is provided. Different alert-state databases have independent bindings and can diverge. When binding is enabled, `send-uncoordinated` fallback cannot validate a destination and fails without sending; choose another fallback or explicitly omit the binding policy after assessing the existing duplicate and audit limitations. This policy adds a database function call per configured target, including healthy checks; database unavailability or timeout fails that target. Docker PostgreSQL tests cover restricted role and environment isolation, concurrent conflicting first bindings, role revocation, actual fanout commands with Slack/email mismatches, and continued dispatch to other targets. Local tests cover uncoordinated fallback rejection. Actual external delivery, production scale and cross-database failover are unverified.

`alert-fanout` runs either existing alert command once per destination, allowing up to 32 Slack webhooks and/or Resend/SendGrid email recipients in one scheduled invocation. Install `alert-fanout`, `send-delivery-alert`, and `send-database-alert` in the same directory. Configure the existing DB, health checks, alert permissions, fallback and timeouts exactly as for the underlying command. Pass a JSON configuration file (maximum 64 KiB) containing absolute paths to secret files, for example:

```json
{"targets":[
  {"channel":"slack","id":"ops-team","webhook_file":"/secure/slack-webhook"},
  {"channel":"email","id":"primary-oncall","provider":"resend","to":"oncall@example.test","from":"alerts@example.test","api_key_file":"/secure/resend-key"},
  {"channel":"email","id":"backup-oncall","provider":"sendgrid","to":"backup@example.test","from":"alerts@example.test","api_key_file":"/secure/sendgrid-key","region":"eu"}
]}
```

Run `alert-fanout delivery ENV GRACE_SECONDS HEARTBEAT_MAX_SECONDS COOLDOWN_SECONDS /secure/targets.json` or `alert-fanout database MONITOR_ENV TARGET_LABEL COOLDOWN_SECONDS /secure/targets.json`. `sender_name` is optional for email and `region` is optional only for SendGrid (`global` or `eu`). IDs use 1–64 ASCII letters, digits, dot, underscore or hyphen and must be unique within each channel. Use stable, non-secret IDs; change the ID when the underlying destination changes and coordinate that change across every sender. Each destination gets the existing independent `(environment, channel, ID)` reservation, cooldown and unknown/rejected review. Do not use the same ID for different destinations or assume different IDs prevent duplicate delivery to the same address. The config includes addresses and paths, so protect it and the referenced secrets; no webhook or API key is embedded in JSON. Destination-specific legacy environment variables are removed from each child and replaced by the selected config. Child output is suppressed; the parent reports only channel, target ID and `completed`, `attention` (child exit 2), or `failed`. Exit 1 means any child failed or an interruption occurred; otherwise 2 means at least one child returned 2, and 0 means all returned 0. These codes do not prove receipt, and for database alerts exit 2 also represents an unhealthy probe.

Destinations execute sequentially by default. Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_FANOUT_CONCURRENCY` to 1–8 to choose bounded parallel child execution; invalid values fail before starting a child. Every child independently repeats health checks, so they observe different points in time; parallel mode increases simultaneous database connections, query load and provider requests, and output order follows completion rather than configuration order. Choose a limit the database and providers can sustain. Total work still grows with target count and runtime can accumulate across batches. Invalid configuration rejects the entire invocation before any child starts; a child configuration or transport failure does not prevent later destinations from being tried. After observing SIGTERM, the runner starts no further targets and waits for active children within the shared grace period; the signal can race with starting a child. A timeout terminates active child processes and may leave their external outcomes ambiguous. `SWALLOW_ALERT_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` bounds the wait and a timeout terminates the child. A request already sent may be ambiguous and must follow the existing lease and operator-review procedure. The database alert's optional uncoordinated fallback still has no durable slots or duplicate protection. Mock child-process tests cover target isolation, continued execution, result aggregation, malformed duplicate targets, the two-child concurrency bound, graceful interruption and timeout cleanup of multiple children. Disposable Docker PostgreSQL tests run both real child commands in sequential and parallel modes with four independent blocked targets and verify a missing credential does not suppress later targets. A separate local proxy closes four real child connections before TLS completes and verifies each slot records an independent unknown result. Run `cargo test --locked --test alert_fanout -- --ignored --test-threads=1` against a fully migrated disposable DB. These tests send no external messages and do not prove provider acceptance or actual receipt. Production load and multi-host orchestration remain unverified.

`send-delivery-alert` and `send-database-alert` handle SIGTERM and Ctrl-C with `SWALLOW_ALERT_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` (default 30; 1–300 seconds). They continue their current scheduled pass, including any remaining checks and configured destinations, for at most the grace period. Existing per-operation deadlines and exit codes still apply; the grace is an additional bound after the signal, not a total execution deadline. Configure the supervisor termination budget longer than the grace period. A timeout exits 1 and may interrupt an external request after acceptance, leaving a durable `dispatching` reservation; normal lease expiry and operator review conservatively convert it to `unknown` and block blind resend. In uncoordinated fallback there is no durable reservation or recovery history. An accepted provider response does not prove human receipt. Docker PostgreSQL and a local black-hole HTTPS proxy verify SIGTERM during each real command's Slack connection, normal unknown recording, a forced grace timeout and subsequent lease recovery; no real Slack or email is contacted. Run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test alert_shutdown -- --ignored` on a disposable fully migrated DB. Actual provider delivery, signal during all other execution phases and production orchestration remain unverified.

`resend-worker`, `sendgrid-worker`, `twilio-worker`, `fcm-worker`, `slack-worker`, `slack-webhook-worker`, `slack-workflow-worker`, and `in-app-worker` handle SIGTERM and Ctrl-C with `SWALLOW_WORKER_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` (default 30; range 1–300). A signal requests a stop, wakes polling/reconnect sleeps, and waits for the currently started operation; after that operation completes the worker stops before starting the next recovery or claim. A signal can race with an operation that has already begun, including claiming a job. If grace expires, the process exits with an error and may interrupt a provider request; use existing lease recovery and operator review for unknown outcomes. A successful process exit does not prove delivery or absence of a preceding database error. Keep the orchestrator's termination budget longer than the grace period and choose grace in relation to the channel's existing DB/whole-operation timeout. SIGKILL or host loss cannot be drained. Other processes do not necessarily use this shutdown mechanism; the three provider projectors are described below. An already started FCM job may continue sending to multiple targets until the operation completes; a Slack workflow stops between its fan-out and delivery operations when possible.

The Resend, SendGrid and Twilio projection workers also use the same shutdown grace. They finish a currently started database operation but stop before fetching another page or projecting another job. An interrupted scan does not advance its completion heartbeat; a completed scan can advance it. The next worker starts a new scan from the beginning. Concurrent projectors still share a heartbeat, so another instance can advance it; it is not proof of a cluster-wide complete scan. A signal can race with a just-started operation. If the grace expires, the process exits with an error and an in-flight transaction or heartbeat may have committed despite a lost response. Existing database transaction semantics and independent monitoring still apply. Docker PostgreSQL tests stop each real projector while its page is locked, verify stale heartbeat and a successful subsequent scan, and test timeout; a Resend projection already in its transaction is also allowed to commit without falsely completing the interrupted scan. Run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test projection_shutdown -- --ignored` against a disposable fully migrated database. Actual production failover, load and all event races remain unverified.

Docker PostgreSQL tests start all eight real binaries, block recovery on a database lock, signal each, release the lock and confirm pending work remains unclaimed; a timed-out Resend worker exits with an error. Unit tests exercise waiting for an in-flight future and timeout. These tests do not send a real external provider request during shutdown, verify deployment orchestration, or prove HA. On a disposable fully migrated database run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test worker_shutdown -- --ignored`; nonignored configuration tests and `cargo test --locked --lib shutdown::tests` cover validation and signal coordination.

For a new shared database, initialize migrations in dependency order: `migrate-worker apply api API_DIR --through 17`, then `migrate-worker apply worker WORKER_DIR`, `migrate-worker apply api API_DIR`, and `migrate-worker apply ws WS_DIR`. API migration `0018` requires the worker FCM integration table. `--through` applies an actual verified prefix, rejects versions older than the recorded history or outside the available files, and does not baseline an existing untracked database. Future inter-service dependencies must also be reviewed.

Worker migration `0096` stores exact-byte SHA-256 recipient hashes by tracked SendGrid dispatch, including `to`, `cc`, and `bcc` order. It snapshots at most 1,000 total addresses; larger sends, tracking-disabled sends and older attempts have no snapshot. Hashes may be guessable and require access controls. API migration `0020` preserves new SendGrid bounce subtypes where provided; historical or missing types remain unknown. Migration `0097` and the optional `sendgrid-projection-worker` use these records to project delivery results as described below. Apply API migration `0020` and worker migrations `0096`–`0097` before starting the projector.

When the optional SendGrid health check fails, `send-delivery-alert` exits 1 even if its destination is cooling down or requires review; other checks and configured destinations are still attempted. Monitor the command's exit status independently. A successful run or provider acceptance does not prove delivery to an operator. Docker PostgreSQL tests cover a missing projection with both a blocked destination and an accepted cooldown; actual Slack/email delivery and scheduler integration remain unverified.

`send-delivery-alert` bounds each database connection, health check, condition observation and destination dispatch with `SWALLOW_ALERT_OPERATION_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 30; 1–300 seconds), and applies the same PostgreSQL statement timeout. A timed-out check is reported as failed while remaining checks and destinations are attempted. Timing out an external send can leave its reservation in flight until the normal unknown-outcome recovery and operator review; it is not evidence that the provider did not accept the alert. Individual limits may accumulate and are not a total runtime deadline. If the state database itself is unavailable, use the independent database alert path or monitor the scheduler failure separately.

## PostgreSQL synchronous failover test

`./scripts/test-postgres-failover.sh` creates a private disposable Docker network, PostgreSQL 17 primary and synchronous physical standby, applies current API and worker migrations, and starts two actual in-app workers. The standby is listed first in a multi-host connection URL with `target_session_attrs=read-write`, so the workers initially skip the read-only standby. The test commits a pending workflow job and an unexpired running lease, verifies both on the replica, forcibly stops the primary, promotes the standby, and makes the pending job runnable and the lease expire. Both workers reconnect to the promoted server; each job completes once, with one notification and one in-app message. The test checks that the committed lease token and pending state survived promotion. It uses test-only trust authentication and loopback ports and removes its own containers, volume and network. It requires Docker, Rust, and the local `postgres:17-alpine` image; `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` can select build output.

Run `SWALLOW_FAILOVER_TEST_SCENARIO=resend ./scripts/test-postgres-failover.sh` for the separate Resend dispatch scenario. The Rust queue function starts a workflow email and sends to a loopback mock, which captures the request and holds its successful response until after the primary is killed and standby promoted. Before the request, the `dispatching` attempt and message state are committed and replayed on the synchronous standby. The original sender cannot store the later success on the dead primary; after the test expires the lease, two actual Resend worker processes reconnect to the promoted node and leave the job failed and both attempt and message delivery state `unknown`. A local proxy records no further worker outbound connections during a six-second observation. No real Resend request is made; the initial sender is the same queue code in a test harness, not a production worker process. Unknown still requires external investigation; it is not evidence of failure or recipient delivery.

Run `SWALLOW_FAILOVER_TEST_SCENARIO=fcm ./scripts/test-postgres-failover.sh` for the FCM scenario. A queue-function test harness sends one prepared push message to a loopback FCM mock and holds its success response while the primary is killed and the synchronous standby promoted. The test checks that the job, dispatching target attempt and push history reached the replica, releases the response, expires the lease, and starts two real FCM worker processes against the promoted node. The job becomes failed, and the attempt and history remain `unknown`; a local proxy records no retry for that target for six seconds. This uses one prepared target, not a full workflow trigger, and does not contact FCM or verify device receipt. With `TEST_FCM_FAILOVER_MULTIPLE=true`, a second prepared target remains after failover. The real workers attempt that target once through a proxy that closes the connection, preserve the first unknown attempt, and leave both histories unknown; the proxy observes exactly one connection during the six-second window. The proxy does not decrypt or inspect the FCM request, and its closure makes the second outcome unknown. Successful second-target delivery, more targets, an actual production worker in flight, network partitions and real multi-host failover are not covered. The preceding database-role fixture previously timed out once waiting for logical/physical quorum. It now emits fresh WAL after creating the logical slot, requests one-second receiver fsync and allows a longer bounded wait with diagnostics. Two subsequent complete FCM scenarios passed; these runs do not prove the fixture is free of timing failures.

Run `SWALLOW_FAILOVER_TEST_SCENARIO=twilio ./scripts/test-postgres-failover.sh` for SMS. The test creates a workflow job and runs the in-app queue function to generate a Twilio SMS job. A queue-function harness sends to a local mock and holds its success response until the synchronous primary is killed and standby promoted. Before promotion it verifies the job, dispatching attempt, SMS history, attempt-specific callback ID and account snapshot are present on the replica. After expiring the lease, two real Twilio worker processes reconnect and mark the job failed and its attempt and history `unknown`; a local proxy records no additional requests during six seconds. After promotion, the real API process enables account-bound callback enforcement and receives a synthetic signed callback tied to the replicated attempt: it rejects an invalid signature, deduplicates repeated events, and the projector leaves the result unknown because provider acceptance and ID were not recorded. No actual Twilio request or provider callback occurs. The initial send uses a queue-function harness rather than a production worker, the workflow is seeded directly rather than triggered via HTTP, and the test DB connection has broad privileges. Callbacks in flight during promotion, production orchestration, actual network partitions and other failover modes are unverified.

The same scenario also enables the outgoing correlation tag, checks its replicated value and recipient fingerprint after promotion, inserts a synthetic delivered event directly into the promoted database and calls the auto-correlation projector from the test harness. The attempt becomes accepted and the job and message become delivered with one auto-correlation audit, without another outbound request. Independently, a synthetic unknown job is automatically correlated before promotion; after its delivered projection and audit are replayed, a same-tag event with a different provider ID on the promoted database invalidates both job and message results to `unknown`. These events bypass the HTTP webhook and signature verification, and the projector invocation is not a production projector process. This does not test a real provider, asynchronous replication, an unfenced primary or all event races.

This verifies one controlled synchronous failover, not an HA system. The test forcibly fences the old primary; `target_session_attrs=read-write` does not elect a leader or prevent split brain, and the application does not supply a failover manager, replication configuration, fencing or automatic standby rejoin. Synchronous replication can block commits when the required standby is unavailable; asynchronous replication or forced promotion before acknowledged WAL is available can lose queue jobs, attempt markers, request deduplication and audit records. Loss of an attempt marker can cause duplicate external sends. Configure durable replication, tested leader fencing, routing, TLS and monitoring for the deployment; choose the connection mode and failover policy explicitly. Real external email/SMS/push requests, network partitions, other unacknowledged transactions, long-running load, and production orchestration are not exercised by these tests. The safety of replaying external requests remains channel-specific.

`SWALLOW_FAILOVER_TEST_SCENARIO=operator-token ./scripts/test-postgres-failover.sh` checks an operator JWT revocation through synchronous promotion. It commits the denylist entry and audit, verifies their replay and the API DB login registration, kills the primary and promotes the standby. A real API process with a restricted DB login reports readiness 503 after primary loss, reconnects to the promoted node without restarting, reports readiness 200, rejects the revoked signed fixture token and accepts another. A separately recreated in-process router verifies the same result. This does not test an in-flight operation/revocation race during failover, an IdP integration, or split-brain or asynchronous replication. The script builds the API test with the API lockfile and its configured `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`.

# Experimental worker operations

FCM Android `notification` supports title/body, icon, sound, color, tag, ticker, boolean `sticky`, click action, channel ID, localization keys and string arguments, image URL, local-only, priority, visibility, proxy, vibration timings, default sound/vibration/light settings and notification count. Selected Firebase Admin field-name, enum and integer-millisecond conversions are applied. Light settings accept #RRGGBB or #RRGGBBAA and integer-millisecond on/off durations, converting them to Firebase RGBA and duration fields. The optional Swallow JSON extension `overrides.fcm.android.notification.eventTime` accepts an RFC 3339 timestamp, converts offsets to UTC and truncates sub-millisecond precision, producing the FCM `event_time` field. Valid UTC range is years 0001–9999; leap seconds and invalid values are rejected. For example: `{"fcm":{"android":{"notification":{"eventTime":"2026-11-14T21:34:56.999987+09:00"}}}}` under the event `overrides` becomes `2026-11-14T12:34:56.999Z`. The pinned Firebase Admin Node SDK requires a JavaScript `Date` for `eventTimestamp` and rejects a JSON string, so `eventTime` is explicitly a Swallow extension rather than the same input contract; a JSON `eventTimestamp` remains rejected. Equivalent SDK `Date` wire conversions were compared for three timestamps. Fractional millisecond durations and unsupported fields are rejected. Image URLs are restricted to HTTP(S). The API accepts platform objects without validating all nested fields; failures can occur later in the worker. SDK comparisons cover `sticky` true and false; Rust rejects non-boolean values while the pinned SDK passes them to the service. Other SDK comparisons and original-versus-Rust API/workflow mock cases, including SDK-normalized LED settings, cover selected cases; actual Android receipt and complete platform behavior remain unverified. A Docker PostgreSQL integration test triggers a Rust push workflow through the HTTP router, runs the workflow worker and FCM queue test harness, and verifies the timestamp in the mocked HTTP request and an accepted attempt. An invalid timestamp fails the push step before any provider request or attempt. Run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... SWALLOW_WORKER_TEST_TARGET_DIR=/absolute/worker/target cargo test --locked --test fcm_event_time_workflow -- --ignored` from the Rust API directory against a fully migrated disposable DB; the nested worker harness uses Cargo offline. No real device or full original-server timestamp workflow was tested.

FCM APNs supports `overrides.fcm.apns` and provider overrides with string `headers`, `payload` (including `aps` and custom keys), and `fcmOptions`. Selected Firebase Admin transformations cover localized alert names, sound/critical/volume, content-available, mutable-content, thread-id, and imageUrl. Conflicting flag aliases and invalid values fail at the worker; image URLs are restricted to HTTP(S). Custom payload fields and unrecognized nested options pass through for provider validation. SDK wire and mock tests and two original-versus-Rust API/workflow cases cover selected examples; actual iOS receipt and the complete APNs contract remain unverified.

FCM supports selected Android (`priority`, integer-millisecond `ttl`, `collapseKey`, `restrictedPackageName`, `directBootOk`, string `data`, and `fcmOptions`), Web Push (`headers`, string `data`, object `notification` and `fcmOptions`) and common `fcmOptions.analyticsLabel` overrides. Set these in `overrides.fcm.android`, `overrides.fcm.webPush` and `overrides.fcm.fcmOptions`, or use the existing provider override path. Android TTL and selected names are converted as in the pinned Firebase Admin SDK; each multicast request receives the same options. Non-integer TTL or values over 2^53-1, unsupported Android notification options (see above) and unknown top-level fields are rejected; see the APNs support above. The API checks only that platform options are objects; invalid or unsupported values may therefore be accepted at trigger time and fail later in the worker. Web notification and `fcmOptions` contents are forwarded without full validation, so the provider may reject them. SDK wire comparisons and original-versus-Rust API/workflow mock delivery comparisons cover selected examples only; live FCM, actual device display, all platform options and load remain unverified.

Android `overrides.fcm.android.fcmOptions` accepts an object and forwards it as the pinned Firebase Admin SDK does. If present, `analyticsLabel` must be a string; other object properties are passed through and may be rejected by FCM. For example, `{"fcm":{"android":{"fcmOptions":{"analyticsLabel":"campaign"}}}}` can be supplied under event `overrides`. Firebase SDK wire comparisons cover a label, an empty object, a forwarded extra property and invalid label/object types. A Docker PostgreSQL test confirms the option survives the Rust trigger, workflow and queue to a mocked FCM request. A pinned original Novu server versus Rust server comparison also checked a selected trigger-to-mocked-provider case and history. These tests do not contact FCM or prove delivery, analytics collection or full option compatibility.

Worker migration `0129` adds an optional revision-fenced failed-job review. Run `delivery-reconcile list-failed-jobs-v2 ENV [AFTER_JOB]`, investigate the returned failure, and use its string `revision` with `ack-failed-job-v2 ENV JOB REVISION OPERATOR REASON`. A changed failure or a job no longer failed is rejected, including when the acknowledgement waited on a row lock; duplicate acknowledgement of the same revision is a no-op. Acknowledgement only records review, not delivery or remediation. Pages contain at most 100 metadata rows and are not a stable snapshot.

Grant the reader environment `alert_view` plus EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_failed_queue_list_v2(text,uuid)`, and the reviewer `queue_ack` plus EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_ack_failed_queue_v2(text,uuid,bigint,text,text)`. Existing functions and privileges remain for compatibility; to enforce revision checks, retire old tools and revoke `public.novu_rust_ack_failed_queue(text,uuid,text,text)` from operational roles. Privileged direct SQL can bypass the policy. The feature is a Swallow extension; Docker PostgreSQL tests cover actual CLI, locking races, recurrence, isolated privileges and pagination, not production workload or deployment.

Worker migration `0128` adds `SWALLOW_PUSH_TARGET_POLICY=reference|registered`. `reference` is the default and uses prepared targets. `registered` checks each token against the current subscriber device list and active integration before reservation. Removed/inactive registrations are recorded as `skipped/unregistered`; targets without verifiable registration (including raw prepared jobs, topics and conditions) are `skipped/unverifiable`. Other targets continue. Missing provider credentials or malformed prepared context may fail earlier. Skips preserve a failed job (`push targets skipped by policy`) and group `error`/`unknown`; no automatic replay or device delivery claim is made. Optional failed-job alerting must be enabled separately.

Apply 0128 and replace all workers: the three push policies must agree per environment; old workers do not enforce target policy. To change it, stop all FCM workers in the environment, have a trusted DBA set `public.novu_rust_push_workflow_worker_config.target_policy` to NULL for that environment, then restart all workers with the same settings. Subscriber API changes serialize on the subscriber row; direct database writes bypassing this convention and concurrent integration activation changes can race. Reserved external requests cannot be cancelled. This extension adds locking and queries; production load and live FCM receipt are untested.

`delivery-reconcile list-skipped-fcm ENV JOB [AFTER_TARGET]` returns at most 100 indexes, reasons and times without tokens; grant the login environment `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_list_skipped_fcm(text,uuid,integer)`. Updated `push-policy-evidence` also returns `skipped` and requires EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_push_policy_evidence_v2(text,uuid,bigint)`. The old function and grants remain, but older tools do not report skipped counts. Operator resolution of another unknown attempt preserves skipped failures. Docker PostgreSQL and mock FCM cover selected paths; selected original-server mock FCM comparisons passed for default-policy routes, multicast, partial failure and restart; these do not establish full compatibility.

Worker migration `0127` adds `SWALLOW_PUSH_PREFERENCE_POLICY=snapshot|recheck`, independent of `SWALLOW_PUSH_WORKFLOW_STEP_POLICY`. Default `snapshot` keeps the preference decision made at preparation. `recheck` evaluates current subscriber global/workflow push preferences before each target reservation, using the existing workflow read-only precedence. It takes the subscriber row lock used by the preference APIs, then queries preferences in a separate statement so an update committed while the worker waited is visible. A disabled preference or missing prepared context cancels remaining targets; subscriber identity protection can instead fail the job. Standalone jobs with no workflow context or prepared messages bypass it.

Apply migration `0127` and deploy matching FCM workers and operator tools. All workers in an environment register both policy values in `public.novu_rust_push_workflow_worker_config`; old workers ignore the new option and must be drained. To change one policy, stop all FCM workers for that environment, have a trusted DBA set only the relevant `step_policy` or `preference_policy` column to NULL, and restart all workers with the same selected pair. The existing worker table SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE grants are required. Existing rows receive `snapshot`. Protect direct access; reapplying older migrations can replace newer functions.

Requests already reserved can still complete. Existing attempts are preserved, partial message groups remain conservatively incomplete, and no automatic replay occurs. The check does not refresh device tokens, rendered output or topic subscription preferences; workflow identity and step validation are controlled separately by `SWALLOW_PUSH_WORKFLOW_STEP_POLICY`. Privileged direct workflow-default updates may bypass the subscriber-lock convention and are not serialized by this check. New cancellation reasons participate in existing push policy alerts, acknowledgement and uncertain-attempt resolution. Docker PostgreSQL tests cover actual subscriber preference API changes, partial dispatch, update lock races, read-only precedence, missing context, mixed-worker configuration and default snapshot behavior. Each guarded target adds a preference read and subscriber lock; production throughput, actual FCM delivery and full Novu equivalence are unverified. Selected default-snapshot scenarios were compared to the pinned original with mock FCM; the optional recheck policy is a Swallow extension.

Worker migration `0126` lets authorized operators resolve an unknown FCM attempt on a policy-cancelled job without resuming the job or dispatching remaining targets. First list unknown attempts with `delivery-reconcile list ENV`; for scoped access set `SWALLOW_MONITOR_SCOPED_ACCESS=true` and grant environment `alert_view` plus EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_list_unknown_attempts(text,uuid,integer)`. Obtain the job's cancellation revision with `list-channel-policy ENV push`. After independent provider investigation, run `resolve-cancelled-push ENV JOB REVISION TARGET accepted|not_accepted OPERATOR REASON`. These are operator assertions, not provider-signed evidence. `accepted` records an assertion of provider acceptance, not device receipt; `not_accepted` asserts non-acceptance and does not retry. A stale cancellation revision, non-unknown attempt or conflicting concurrent resolution is rejected.

Grant the resolution login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_resolve_cancelled_push(text,uuid,bigint,integer,text,text,text)` and environment `accepted` for the accepted decision or `retry` for the not-accepted decision. The latter permission authorizes the assertion only; this command does not schedule a retry. `list-cancelled-push-resolutions ENV JOB REVISION [AFTER_TARGET]` returns up to 100 decisions still applied to current attempts, including their original revision, ordered by target index and without operator name or reason; grant EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_list_cancelled_push_resolutions(text,uuid,bigint,integer)` and environment `alert_view`. Both functions default to no PUBLIC execution. Database session identity, operator-supplied name and reason, previous HTTP status and revision are recorded in a restricted audit table.

The existing attempt state is `accepted` for an operator acceptance and `rejected` for operator non-acceptance, while `operator_resolution` identifies the origin. Thus generic accepted/rejected aggregates combine provider responses and assertions; inspect the resolution list to distinguish them. Existing partial-group message `error`/`unknown` status is not recalculated and may remain after resolution. Guard direct database and audit access, independently verify provider outcomes, and do not treat operator-entered names as authenticated individual identities. The audit row references the job and may block job deletion until the retention policy handles it. Migration adds a column and checked constraint to the attempts table, with locking and validation cost. Docker PostgreSQL tests cover mock FCM unknown → policy cancellation → operator decision, both outcomes, scoped permissions, revision fencing, concurrent decisions, revocation and audit failure rollback. The final prior-revision listing was verified on an isolated local PostgreSQL 17.12 because Docker was unavailable. Actual provider evidence, delivery, operational load and full Novu compatibility are unverified.

Worker migration `0125` extends policy-cancellation review to FCM push. The existing `check-channel-policy` and `list-channel-policy` accept `push`, and `all` now includes push; acknowledgement retains its revision guard. Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_PUSH_POLICY_CANCELLED_ENABLED=true` to include push cancellations in configured Slack/email alerts independently of email and SMS; default is false. Deploy migration and updated binaries together: older alerts do not monitor push. Existing function EXECUTE grants survive the `0125` replacement. Environment `alert_view` and `queue_ack` remain required as documented below.

`delivery-reconcile push-policy-evidence ENV JOB REVISION` reports only aggregate `accepted`, `rejected`, `unknown`, `dispatching` and `unattempted` FCM targets for that observed cancellation; grant `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_push_policy_evidence(text,uuid,bigint)` to the restricted operator. The function rejects stale revisions and does not expose subscriber data, payloads or tokens. `unattempted` is null if no valid prepared target list exists or indices do not match; an FCM accepted attempt does not prove device receipt. Evidence is point-in-time and acknowledgement does not resend. Push cancellations predating `0125` are visible at revision zero but earlier repeated transitions cannot be reconstructed. Protect privileged DB access and function ownership; the partial index adds migration and write cost. Docker PostgreSQL tests cover real mock-FCM partial dispatch, restricted login and environment isolation, push-only blocked alert, acknowledgement, recurring cancellation, stale revisions, and existing function grants on upgrade. Actual external alerts, FCM delivery, load and comparison of this Swallow extension with Novu remain unverified.

`SWALLOW_PUSH_WORKFLOW_STEP_POLICY=snapshot|active|unchanged` selects FCM workflow behavior. The default `snapshot` uses the queued template. `active` checks workflow identity and active workflow/step; `unchanged` additionally compares original title and content. Each target is checked before reservation. Guarded workers cancel old prepared jobs without a workflow snapshot; standalone jobs without a workflow context or prepared messages bypass the guard. New workflow jobs retain the original workflow ID and template in their prepared queue payload, so protect database access and retention.

Previously reserved calls may still complete. Prior attempt records are retained, and a partially dispatched message group is marked `error`/`unknown` when remaining targets are cancelled. That group-level `unknown` is a conservative incomplete-group marker, not proof that each provider response was unknown. A cancelled job can therefore have delivered targets. There is no automatic replay; optional policy-cancellation monitoring is described above. Restored template text and output changed by overrides are not detected. Guarded sends add a row lock and lease checks per target.

Apply worker migration `0124` and grant each FCM worker DB login SELECT, INSERT and UPDATE on `public.novu_rust_push_workflow_worker_config`. New workers in the same environment must register one policy; missing schema or privileges causes reconnecting without processing. Old workers bypass registration and enforcement and must be drained. To change the policy, stop all affected FCM workers, have a trusted administrator set only that environment's `step_policy` to NULL, and restart all with the same policy. Protect the registration table and direct writes. Docker PostgreSQL and mock FCM test real API-queued work, partial dispatch, active-policy continuation, old prepared jobs, concurrent updates, worker policy mismatch and reconnect. Selected requests and histories match the pinned original with a mock FCM endpoint, including one unchanged-policy send. Real FCM delivery, production contention and full Novu compatibility are unverified.

Worker migrations `0122` and `0123` add operator review for queued email and SMS jobs cancelled by the selected workflow-step policy. `delivery-reconcile check-channel-policy ENV email|sms|push|all` outputs a count and exits 0 for none, 2 for unacknowledged cancellations or 1 for an error. `list-channel-policy ENV email|sms|push|all [AFTER_JOB]` lists at most 100 non-payload records ordered by UUID. The list includes `revision` as a decimal string. `ack-channel-policy ENV JOB REVISION OPERATOR REASON` acknowledges only that observed revision without changing or retrying a job; if it has changed, refresh and review the new entry. A subsequent qualifying cancellation is counted again, but an identical no-op UPDATE is not. Existing provider attempts and their outcomes are not changed or evaluated by this acknowledgement.

Grant the environment-specific `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_count_channel_policy_attention(text,text)` and `public.novu_rust_list_channel_policy_attention(text,text,uuid)` for check/list; grant `queue_ack` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_ack_channel_policy(text,uuid,bigint,text,text)` for acknowledgement. Migration `0123` drops the unsafe four-argument acknowledgement function and recreates the list function, removing prior EXECUTE grants on that list. Regrant list and new acknowledgement function privileges and deploy the updated CLI together. Do not manually reapply `0122` after `0123`, because it would recreate the unsafe function.
