This does not cover events that never reach the API, attempts lacking a provider ID at event arrival, direct table edits, or a success response lost after a commit. Docker PostgreSQL and synthetic signed HTTP callbacks test duplicates, environment isolation and a projector-lock race; real Resend callbacks after this upgrade, production workload and all concurrent retry scenarios remain unverified.

Worker migration `0103` adds statement-level triggers for Twilio callback insertion and association changes. A signed event newly inserted or newly linked to the current environment/job/dispatch resets that SMS job and its messages' `twilio_delivery_result` to `unknown` in the callback transaction; a projector later reevaluates it. Repeated unchanged callbacks, unbound events and callbacks for older dispatches do not invalidate the current result. These triggers use the job lock already acquired by current job/dispatch webhook routes, so the API must be upgraded on every instance; old versions, direct SQL, absent callbacks and events without a current dispatch do not receive this concurrency guarantee. Apply API Twilio migrations through `0007` before worker `0103`, and protect the security-definer function owners and schema. Matching callbacks add job and message writes and can wait on locks or fail under contention; configure webhook retries and monitoring. Docker PostgreSQL with signed synthetic HTTP tests new and promoted events, duplicates, old dispatches, tenant separation, and a held job lock. Real Twilio callbacks after this migration, sustained load and all recovery conditions remain unverified.

Worker migration `0104` adds `delivery-reconcile resend-result ENV JOB` and `delivery-reconcile twilio-result ENV JOB`. They return stored job status, aggregate projection, attempt state, whether a provider ID is known, and the environment's last completed projection scan. Missing attempts or projections are reported as null attempt state and `unknown`, not a delivery confirmation. Grant the login both the environment's `alert_view` permission and `EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_channel_result(text,uuid,text)`; direct queue, attempt, event or message table access is unnecessary. Neither the provider ID, address, phone number, payload nor secret is returned. Revoke preexisting direct table privileges separately when appropriate and protect the function owner and schema. Results are asynchronously projected: the heartbeat is not proof that a particular job includes the latest evidence, and no recipient-by-recipient details are provided by these commands. The existing evidence commands remain available for appropriately authorized operators. These read-only commands do not grant retry or acceptance rights and are not Novu APIs. Docker PostgreSQL tests a restricted login, explicit grant removal, wrong provider/environment denial, absent attempt, sensitive value exclusion and both CLIs; real production roles and provider states are unverified.

Worker migration `0105` makes Resend and Twilio health checks and their scoped per-job results account for duplicate nonempty provider IDs in the same environment immediately, without waiting for a projection scan. `providerIdAmbiguous` is true in the scoped result and its effective `result` is `unknown`; old stored projection columns are not rewritten. Overdue non-cancelled jobs with duplicate IDs count as undelivered even if their stored projection says `delivered`. Another attempt's job may itself be cancelled and still causes ambiguity, matching the existing evidence evaluator; IDs in a different environment and missing/empty IDs do not. These checks add indexed attempt lookups and may increase monitoring query cost; performance at production scale is untested. Apply worker migration `0105` and deploy updated monitor/alert binaries together; older binaries using direct table checks will miss this condition. Direct-table monitor roles also need SELECT on the corresponding attempt table. Environment-scoped functions still require only their existing EXECUTE and `alert_view` grants. Docker PostgreSQL checks both channel types, cross-environment isolation, cancelled duplicate jobs, restricted views and consistency with direct health queries. Actual provider ID reuse and concurrent production load remain unverified.

Worker migration `0106` provides optional restricted reconciliation for SendGrid, Resend and Twilio. Set `SWALLOW_RECONCILIATION_SCOPED_ACCESS=true` on `delivery-reconcile retry|accepted ENV JOB - OPERATOR REASON`; grant the login `EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_resolve_email_sms(text,uuid,text,text,text)` and only the environment-specific `retry` and/or `accepted` grants it needs. The function locks the grant and job, checks the unknown attempt and existing SendGrid/Twilio callback guard, then records the decision and updates the job, attempt and history atomically. It records `session_user` in both DB-user audit columns because `current_user` inside a security-definer function is its owner. No direct table writes or reads are required for this command. Revoke old direct table privileges separately and protect the function owner, public schema, audit table and migration history. The supplied operator label and reason are not independently authenticated and must not contain secrets or personal data. Permission revocation waits for an already-running decision, but cannot cancel a committed retry or external dispatch.

Omitting the setting retains the legacy direct-table implementation. Worker migration `0107` additionally supports FCM target decisions as described below. A retry remains an operator assertion that the provider did not accept the request, and an incorrect assertion can cause duplicate delivery. Resend has no matching callback refusal when provider ID is unknown; accepted means provider acceptance, not confirmed recipient delivery. Old binaries can bypass this function and must be retired before relying on its privilege boundary. Both paths were compared for selected SendGrid/Resend/Twilio retry and acceptance outcomes in Docker PostgreSQL. Tests also cover role and environment isolation, callback refusal, concurrent decisions, audit and an actual restricted-login CLI; production identities, all callback races and Novu compatibility remain unverified.

Worker migration `0107` extends `SWALLOW_RECONCILIATION_SCOPED_ACCESS=true` to FCM: use `retry|accepted ENV JOB TARGET_INDEX OPERATOR REASON`, with environment-specific `retry` or `accepted` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_resolve_fcm(text,uuid,integer,text,text,text)`. The restricted function holds the same grant/job/attempt locks, records session identity, and applies the existing target-group history and remaining-target resume rules atomically. It refuses a retry if the job failed for a reason other than an unknown push outcome. Existing direct table permissions are not revoked; retire old tools and revoke unnecessary table access to enforce this boundary. FCM acceptance does not prove a device received or displayed the push. Docker PostgreSQL compares selected scoped and legacy retry/acceptance outcomes, including grouped, incomplete and unrelated-failure cases, malformed metadata, simultaneous decisions, denied permissions and restricted CLI access. Actual FCM behavior, exhaustive malformed payloads, production role provisioning and Novu parity remain unverified.

The scoped reconciliation integration fixture also holds an FCM decision transaction open, observes a concurrent grant revocation waiting on the PostgreSQL lock, and confirms a new decision is denied after revocation commits. This demonstrates that tested ordering; a decision committed before revocation can still proceed to external dispatch.

Worker migration `0108` and the updated reconciler align Resend manual `accepted` decisions across the job, attempt and email history: the attempt becomes accepted, the job becomes completed, and same-environment email history becomes `accepted`/`sent` with old error fields cleared. A known provider ID is copied into the email identifier; if no ID is known, the existing identifier is retained. The separate delivery projection on the job and message is reset to null for later reevaluation, because an operator-confirmed provider acceptance is not recipient delivery. The changes occur with the audit record in one transaction in both scoped and legacy paths. Existing earlier decisions are not backfilled. Apply worker migration `0108` before deploying updated tools, and retire older tools; otherwise the old scoped function or direct implementation preserves the mismatch. Docker PostgreSQL tests both paths with and without a provider ID, old projections, audit and cross-environment isolation. Real provider investigations and full production behavior are unverified.

Worker migration `0109` and the updated reconciler refuse a Resend `retry` in either scoped or legacy mode if the unknown attempt already has a nonempty provider message ID and any authenticated event with that ID is committed in the same environment. The decision, audit and attempt remain unchanged on refusal. A different environment's event does not block the decision. Apply API migration `0010` and worker `0109` before upgrading tools; direct-table operators need SELECT on the Resend event table, and older tools do not enforce this guard. The guard checks evidence already visible to the decision transaction: unknown provider IDs, missing callbacks, callbacks committed later, provider ID collisions and all concurrent webhook/retry schedules remain unresolved. An absent event is not evidence of non-acceptance; an operator must still verify independently before retrying. PostgreSQL tests use synthetic stored events for both access modes and check refusal, audit preservation and environment isolation; real provider retries and race conditions have not been verified.

Worker migration `0110` preserves a known Resend provider message ID in each new retry decision's `retry_provider_message_id` audit field. Old decisions cannot be backfilled and the identifier is operationally sensitive: restrict audit access and retention. Both scoped and legacy reconciliation paths record it. With `NOVU_RESEND_RETRY_EVIDENCE_GUARD=true`, the Resend delivery worker checks the latest retry decision under its job lock before sending. If a matching same-environment authenticated callback was committed after the decision, the worker creates an unknown attempt preserving that ID, marks the job failed for review, and makes no HTTP request. The default `false` retains the prior behavior; users choose the policy. The guard requires API webhook migration `0010`, worker migration `0110`, worker SELECT on the reconciliation and Resend event tables, and message/job/attempt update privileges. Upgrade the API, all reconciliation tools and all Resend workers together before relying on this protection; old tools do not save the ID and old workers do not check it. The guarded path adds an indexed audit/event query per claimed Resend job, with production throughput unmeasured.

This recheck does not make retries exactly-once. It cannot correlate an attempt whose provider ID was unknown, recover IDs from old decisions, or detect an absent event. A callback that arrives after the worker's check, an external request already underway, or a provider-ID collision can still lead to uncertainty or duplicate delivery. The API webhook and worker do not serialize every such race; keep independently verifying non-acceptance before retry and monitor failed/unknown jobs. Docker PostgreSQL and a loopback provider test demonstrate ID preservation in both operator modes, a committed late callback stopping HTTP dispatch, the opt-out behavior and invalid settings. Real Resend callbacks racing a send, prolonged failure, and operational DB grants are unverified.

The loopback test also confirms the opt-out policy and that an unrelated provider ID does not block a guarded send.

Worker migration `0111` adds an optional Resend webhook job lock. Set `NOVU_RESEND_WEBHOOK_LOCK_RETRY_JOBS=true` on every API instance together with `NOVU_RESEND_RETRY_EVIDENCE_GUARD=true` on every corresponding Resend worker. Before inserting a verified callback, the API invokes `public.novu_rust_lock_resend_webhook_jobs(text,text)` in the same SQL statement. It locks matching same-environment Resend jobs in UUID order using current attempt IDs or IDs recorded in prior retry audit rows. Thus an already deleted attempt can still be associated for locking; historical retries may cause conservative extra locks. The default is `false`, preserving the previous webhook path. Invalid values are rejected at API configuration time. A synthetic signed HTTP/PostgreSQL test pauses the callback after it locks the job and verifies that an operator retry waits and then refuses the committed evidence without recording a retry. This exercises that ordering only, not a real Resend callback or every crash boundary.

Use a trusted DB administrator to grant the API login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_lock_resend_webhook_jobs(text,text)` and insert its environment-specific identity into `novu_rust_resend_webhook_lock_grants`, for example `INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_resend_webhook_lock_grants SELECT 'ENV',rolname,oid FROM pg_catalog.pg_roles WHERE rolname='API_LOGIN' AND rolcanlogin;`. Function execution and the matching environment/login/OID grant are both required; the function locks the grant while the request runs. Restrict access to that grant table, function owner, migration history and schema. Role recreation or a restore that changes OIDs requires review and re-granting; existing direct privileges are not revoked. Apply API webhook migrations and worker migrations through `0111` before enabling the setting, and upgrade every API/worker/reconciliation instance. Missing function, privileges or grant makes the callback fail rather than bypass the lock. The API still needs its normal webhook and environment table privileges.

This ordering protects the case where a verified callback acquires the matching job lock before a retry decision or guarded worker check, and makes a callback that is waiting on a prior retry acquire that lock before inserting the event. It does not prevent a worker that already passed its check from starting or completing an external request, correlate a missing provider ID, resolve provider ID collisions, or prove non-acceptance. Deadlocks, lock waits, lost connections and provider retry behavior remain possible; monitor webhook failures and retries. Each enabled callback adds indexed lookups and potentially multiple job locks and an audit-index write on retries; production scale is unmeasured. Docker PostgreSQL tests synthetic signed HTTP callbacks waiting on a retry-only job, configured environment mode, grant revocation and environment isolation; real Resend callback races, all deployment topologies and sustained failover remain unverified.

`NOVU_RESEND_CORRELATION_TAG=true` enables an experimental correlation tag for newly dispatched Resend jobs. Before the HTTP request the worker stores a fresh random UUID on the attempt and sends it as the `swallow_dispatch` Resend tag; default `false` leaves the request unchanged. Apply worker migration `0134` before enabling and deploy updated workers. The API can separately retain the tag from verified callbacks with `NOVU_RESEND_WEBHOOK_CAPTURE_CORRELATION=true` after API migration `0047`; see the API README. Both switches must be enabled to retain both sides. Old attempts, old callbacks, missing/invalid tags and older workers have no correlation. With worker migration `0135`, a retry decision retains the old attempt correlation ID in its audit row before deleting the attempt. Older decisions cannot be backfilled. Keep and protect independent audit evidence as needed.

An authorized operator may inspect matching stored values, for example `SELECT a.job_id,a.state,e.email_id,e.event_type FROM public.novu_rust_resend_attempts a JOIN public.novu_rust_resend_delivery_events e ON e.environment_id=a.environment_id AND e.correlation_id=a.correlation_id WHERE a.environment_id=$1 AND a.job_id=$2;`. Restrict database and output access: IDs and event metadata are sensitive, and the tag is exposed to the Resend account. The match is evidence for investigation, not an automatic acceptance, delivery decision or permission to retry. Worker migration `0135` refuses a retry when a same-environment tagged callback is already committed; the asynchronous projection does not use the tag. A late or missing callback cannot prove non-acceptance. With the default job-scoped key a retry creates a different tag but reuses the job's Resend idempotency key; within Resend's retention window a different body may produce HTTP 409, recorded as unknown. The optional retry-scoped mode and its duplicate risk are described above. Never blindly resend. Resend's idempotency retention is bounded; this feature does not guarantee exactly-once delivery. Docker PostgreSQL, a local mock send and synthetic signed HTTP callbacks validate tag persistence and existing callback locking separately; actual Resend tag delivery, integrated live correlation and automatic reconciliation are unverified.

Worker migration `0135` adds `retry_correlation_id` to reconciliation audit rows and a security-definer insert trigger: for an unknown Resend attempt it copies the existing attempt tag on either direct or scoped operator retry, and refuses the decision if an event with that tag is already stored in the same environment. A refused decision leaves the attempt, job and audit unchanged. Apply API `0047` and worker `0134` before `0135`, enable outbound tagging and API capture before relying on this guard, and deploy current API and workers. If API `0047` is absent when a tagged attempt is retried, the retry fails rather than bypassing the guard. Older untagged attempts, callbacks without captured tags and earlier retry audit rows cannot benefit. The trigger owner must be trusted and its privileges, function, trigger and schema protected; privileged direct writes or disabling the trigger can bypass it. Audit IDs and tags are operationally sensitive. The trigger adds an indexed callback lookup to tagged retries and an audit index write; production lock and load impact is unmeasured.

The 0135 guard alone only checks callbacks visible at the audit insert. Optional tag-aware webhook locking and worker rechecks are described below; none can stop an already started send or establish delivery. Callback delay or loss and retry decisions made before a callback remain unsafe; independently verify non-acceptance and monitor unknown jobs. Docker PostgreSQL tested both real reconciliation paths, a restricted DB login, a committed tagged event with missing provider ID, another environment and unrelated tags, preservation in audit, and migration reapplication. Events were inserted synthetically; a real provider callback race and HA failover were not tested.

Worker migration `0136` and API setting `NOVU_RESEND_WEBHOOK_LOCK_CORRELATION_JOBS=true` extend callback locking to the current attempt tag and preserved retry tags, as well as provider IDs. Both `NOVU_RESEND_WEBHOOK_LOCK_RETRY_JOBS=true` and `NOVU_RESEND_WEBHOOK_CAPTURE_CORRELATION=true` and a valid webhook configuration are required; otherwise API startup fails. Give the API DB login `EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_lock_resend_webhook_jobs_with_correlation(text,text,uuid)` and register its current LOGIN OID in `novu_rust_resend_webhook_lock_grants` for the environment. The new function has no PUBLIC execution privilege and locks all matching jobs in UUID order. Grant/revoke the new function separately from the older two-argument lock. The API requires its normal webhook table privileges too. Missing migration, function or grant makes enabled webhooks fail; monitor HTTP failures and provider retries.

Set `NOVU_RESEND_RETRY_CORRELATION_GUARD=true` on every relevant Resend worker to recheck the most recent retry audit tag against same-environment stored callbacks under the job lock before starting a request. If one exists, the worker restores an unknown attempt with the old tag, marks the job failed for review and sends no HTTP request; it does not infer a provider ID from an event. This switch defaults to `false` and is independent of the older provider-ID retry guard and outbound tagging; configure all relevant workers consistently. It requires API `0047`, worker `0134`–`0136`, worker SELECT on retry audit and event tables and its normal attempt/job/message privileges. Roll out the migrations, permissions, API and all workers together; old binaries can bypass these checks. A missing migration or DB permission causes processing failure, not a safe successful dispatch; monitor queue, expired leases and errors.

If a tagged callback obtains the matching job lock first, the operator retry waits for that callback to commit and the 0135 audit trigger rejects it. If retry commits first and the tagged event commits before the guarded worker check, the worker blocks dispatch. When the worker's check has already passed, a callback arriving later cannot cancel the external request. Missing callbacks, unknown tags, duplicate or misconfigured Resend accounts, unsynchronized deployments, stale DB failover and external requests in flight remain unresolved. These features do not provide exactly-once delivery or automatic confirmation of every unknown outcome. Callback locks and checks add database work; production contention and scale are unmeasured. Docker PostgreSQL and synthetic signed HTTP callbacks verified callback-first retry blocking with no known provider ID, locking a job via a retry-only audit tag, restricted-role/environment checks and startup validation. A separate PostgreSQL/mock-worker test verified a callback committed after a retry blocks the subsequent HTTP send. Actual Resend tagged callbacks, all scheduling races, and HA failover were not tested.

Worker migration `0137` and `NOVU_RESEND_PROJECTION_AUTO_CORRELATE=true` opt the Resend projection worker into automatic recovery of an unknown attempt with no provider ID. The default is `false`. A job must be failed specifically with `Resend outcome unknown`, have a stored random tag and recipient snapshot, and have no retry audit for that job. All captured same-tag events must name exactly one provider ID and match a recorded recipient; at least one must be a recognized acceptance/delivery event. The worker refuses automatic recovery for competing same-ID events without that tag, another current attempt or retry audit using the provider ID, or multiple IDs. Under the job lock it records the tag, provider ID, evidence count and timestamp in `novu_rust_resend_auto_correlations`, changes attempt and message to accepted, completes the dispatch job, and evaluates per-recipient delivery in the same transaction. Acceptance is not delivery: incomplete recipients remain pending. If the audit insert fails, no recovery is committed.

Apply API migrations `0010`, `0012`, `0013`, `0047` and worker migrations through `0137`; enable the outbound tag and signed webhook capture, tag-aware callback locking, both retry guards, and the auto option on **all** relevant instances. Grant the projection DB login its existing projection privileges plus SELECT on the retry audit and events and SELECT/INSERT on `novu_rust_resend_auto_correlations`. Protect that table, its owner, migrations, signed webhook configuration, all DB writers, correlation IDs and the Resend account; a random tag is visible to that account and is not independent proof against someone able to send arbitrary emails there. Existing events and attempts without captured tags cannot be recovered. The additional per-job lookups, writes, indexes and locks have unmeasured production cost; monitor scan errors and stale heartbeats.

When evidence is later missing or conflicting, the auto-aware projector reports delivery `unknown` on a later scan. This does not undo the already recorded accepted/completed dispatch. Worker migration `0140` and the updated CLI automatically include auto-correlation conflicts in `resend-check` and `resend-evidence` when the scoped function is installed; their configuration and required environment-specific permission are described below. Explicit legacy mode and older binaries may still report healthy despite a later tag conflict. Use the auto option consistently on projectors to continue automatic recovery and conflict-aware assessment. Worker migration `0138` additionally guards stored `delivered` projections for auto-correlated jobs even against an opted-out or older projector, as described below. Results are asynchronous snapshots, not a global lock on the provider. Existing job health and alerts depend on those projections and can lag.

Automatic recovery does not resend, reconstruct a missing callback, repair previous retries, distinguish a malicious actor in the same Resend account, prevent an already-started duplicate, or guarantee exactly-once delivery. It does not cover every unknown case. Asynchronous DB failover can lose tags, events or audit and invalidate these assumptions; automated fencing and failover remain external. Docker PostgreSQL tests exercise actual projection-worker execution, a synthetic signed HTTP callback followed by recovery, partial recipients, environment separation, mismatched/missing tags and recipients, multiple IDs, provider ID collision, prior retry, audit rollback, repeated scans and late conflict. No real tagged Resend callback, real provider race, production load or HA failover was tested.

The API integration test `resend_lost_response` now runs the Resend queue dispatch against a loopback provider that receives the actual tagged request and closes without returning a response. It feeds the same captured tag into a synthetic signed webhook through the API router, both before and after the response loss, then checks unknown dispatch recovery and the delivery projection in PostgreSQL. It exercises the queue function and in-process HTTP router, not the external Resend service, a full workflow trigger, a production worker process or a real network failure. Run it through the API PostgreSQL test script or against a fresh disposable DB with `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test resend_lost_response -- --ignored` from `apps/api/rust-api`.

The worker's `test-mocks` Cargo feature exposes a configurable Resend endpoint solely for local tests; the API declares it as a dev dependency. It is disabled by default in production worker builds. An endpoint receives the configured API key, so never enable this feature with production credentials or an untrusted endpoint.

Worker migration `0138` protects stored delivery projections for already auto-correlated jobs. Inserting or deleting a webhook event whose environment and tag or provider ID match an auto-correlation audit locks matching jobs in UUID order and marks the job and matching email messages `unknown` in the same transaction. Before a job or message projection is updated to `delivered`, a database trigger checks the audit against current evidence; if it is missing or conflicting, the trigger substitutes `unknown`, including for an older or opted-out projector. Subsequent eligible scans can restore `delivered` after evidence is consistent. This does not change the dispatch's previously recorded accepted/completed status.

Apply API `0047` before enabling new tagged callbacks and deploy worker migrations `0137` and `0138`; the functions and triggers run as their trusted owner and require that owner to retain the referenced table privileges. Protect the owner, functions, triggers, audit and schema against privileged changes. During a rolling migration before API `0047`, affected webhook inserts, deletions and projection updates may fail rather than bypass checks; monitor failed webhooks and projector health. Each event statement adds audit-index lookups and potentially job/message locks and writes; delivered projection updates add evidence lookups. The two new indexes add migration locking, storage and write cost, and bulk event deletion can lock many jobs. Production load, deadlocks and retention workloads are unmeasured.

This guard is limited to stored projections of jobs with 0137 auto-correlation audits. Worker migration `0139` also invalidates stored projections for matching provider IDs newly written by other attempts or retry audits, subject to the race and deployment limits below; an auto-aware scan remains necessary. With worker migration `0140`, updated direct `resend-check` and `resend-evidence` detect tag conflicts by default when appropriately authorized; explicitly disabled or older commands can report stale or misleading evidence. Event updates, privileged manual writes, disabling triggers, missing callbacks, old audit data, already-started sends, and asynchronous failover are not solved. Docker PostgreSQL tests verify synthetic signed callback insertion, different-ID/same-tag collisions, deletion of evidence, restricted DB logins, a legacy projection attempt, a callback waiting on a job lock, environment separation and reapplication. The synchronous Resend failover scenario above additionally tests replicated auto-correlation followed by a conflicting synthetic event on the promoted node. Real Resend callbacks and operational HA remain unverified.

Worker migration `0139` invalidates the existing auto-correlated job and email projection in the same transaction when another same-environment Resend attempt is inserted or assigned its provider ID, or a retry audit is inserted/updated with that ID. A claim in another environment does not affect it; the original attempt does not collide with itself. Removing a collision does not automatically restore delivery: the auto-aware projector must rescan.

The updated auto projector takes transaction-scoped PostgreSQL advisory locks for the tag and provider ID, in that order, and uses READ COMMITTED to re-read its candidate and conflicts after waiting. The 0139 event insert/delete triggers take corresponding tag and provider locks, and the attempt/retry triggers take the provider lock. Thus these participating transactions serialize even when the auto-correlation audit does not yet exist. Normal non-auto projection keeps repeatable read. Apply API `0047` and worker migrations through `0139` before enabling the feature, deploy the updated projector to every instance, and protect all trigger functions and their trusted owner. Old auto projectors do not take these locks and can still race. Direct writes that disable/bypass triggers, event updates, missing events, independent Resend accounts with ambiguous identity and asynchronous failover remain outside this protection.

These locks and added row triggers can delay webhook ingestion and attempt/audit writes, and cross-job or batch operations can deadlock; PostgreSQL aborts a participant, so configure retry/error monitoring for all affected operations. Advisory hash collisions may serialize unrelated work. Bulk retention, throughput, long-held transactions, all interleavings and actual multi-host HA are unmeasured. Docker PostgreSQL tests cover insert/update ID collisions, retry-audit collision, environment isolation, migration reapplication, both transaction orderings for attempts and tag-conflicting events, plus signed synthetic webhook and lost-response regressions. The synchronous failover scenario above covers post-promotion invalidation, but does not exercise an in-flight correlation lock race during failover. Real Resend and production load remain unverified.

Worker migration `0140` extends the existing scoped `delivery-reconcile resend-result ENV JOB` with `autoCorrelated`, `autoCorrelationConflict` and `autoCorrelatedAt`. It returns `result=unknown` whenever the current auto-correlation evidence conflicts, even if an older stored projection says delivered. The existing environment-specific `alert_view` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_channel_result(text,uuid,text)` remain required. The view returns no provider ID, tag, address, recipient hash, payload or raw webhook; it does expose the auto-recovery time and operational status. Twilio output is unchanged. Existing explicit function grants survive migration; clients that reject new JSON fields must be upgraded. This is a point-in-time assessment, not proof of mailbox delivery.

For direct-table `resend-check` and `resend-evidence`, the updated CLI with `NOVU_RESEND_CHECK_AUTO_CORRELATION` unset detects `0140`'s scoped conflict function and includes `autoCorrelationConflict` in the same repeatable-read transaction. It requires the existing direct table SELECT privileges plus the environment-specific `alert_view` grant. `public.novu_rust_scoped_resend_auto_conflict(text,uuid)` is executable by PUBLIC but independently enforces that grant against the current LOGIN OID and verifies the job's environment; it returns only a boolean. The login does not need direct SELECT on the auto-correlation audit. If EXECUTE or the environment grant is missing, the command exits 1 rather than silently falling back. The function locks the grant row, so this path cannot run in a read-only PostgreSQL transaction/standby. Provision permissions before rolling out updated CLI binaries, monitor command errors and protect function/schema ownership.

Explicit `NOVU_RESEND_CHECK_AUTO_CORRELATION=true` retains the earlier direct-table conflict check and requires its audit/evidence SELECT privileges; explicit `false` retains legacy assessment and may miss conflicts. If `0140` is absent, an unset option falls back to the legacy behavior; upgrade both DB and CLI rather than assuming detection. Older binaries retain their previous semantics. Docker PostgreSQL tests cover actual restricted-login CLI and scoped view, no direct audit access, automatically detected conflict, environment/grant and function-EXECUTE revocation, migration reapplication, legacy opt-out, and a DB without the new function. Actual IdP, external alert systems, production role rollout, load and HA are unverified.

Worker migration `0147` adds an opaque UUID revision to Slack endpoint rows and a trigger that advances it on every UPDATE, including a no-op. When `NOVU_SLACK_SNAPSHOT_ENDPOINTS=true`, newly captured endpoint snapshots contain the revision. A fanout using `NOVU_SLACK_WORKFLOW_ENDPOINT_POLICY=snapshot` compares it alongside the endpoint identity and content, and carries it into the generated delivery job; delivery rechecks it under the endpoint row lock before dispatch reservation. A changed-and-restored endpoint therefore fails review before fanout or before reservation instead of being mistaken for the captured endpoint. The `current` policy remains available and does not carry this revision protection. Legacy snapshots lacking a revision and legacy deliveries retain their previous comparisons and cannot detect an intervening change-and-restore. Existing monitoring and acknowledgement for failed Slack jobs apply; the worker does not automatically replay them. An unchanged endpoint can already have a child while another changed endpoint causes partial fanout.

Apply worker migration `0147` before deploying the new in-app and Slack workflow workers; new fanout and delivery queries require the column even with `current`. Its volatile default may rewrite endpoint rows and hold a table lock; the trigger adds a UUID write on each update. Older consumers reject the new snapshot/delivery fields. For a rolling upgrade, disable snapshot production, upgrade all fanout and delivery consumers first, then upgrade in-app producers and enable snapshot production after reviewing or draining old jobs; snapshot-policy consumers fail closed on jobs produced while snapshot production is disabled, so coordinate the pause or review those jobs; configure snapshot policy consistently for all workers using the existing registration procedure. Old queued jobs without revisions need separate review if this protection is required. Do not rely on the protection with older consumers, privileged trigger bypass or a partial rollout. A restored backup or asynchronous failover can lose the revision, UUID collision is theoretically possible, and changes after dispatch reservation can still race with external send. UUID and endpoint metadata are stored in queue payloads; protect access and retention. PostgreSQL lock contention, table rewrite cost, production load, all interleavings, actual Slack and HA are unverified.

The API integration test `slack_endpoint_revision` triggers an actual HTTP route and uses in-app, fanout and delivery queue functions against disposable PostgreSQL. It changes and restores an endpoint both before fanout and before delivery and confirms no Slack attempt is recorded, then checks that a revision-free legacy snapshot retains old behavior. It uses no external provider and does not execute production worker binaries. The existing Slack workflow management and worker tests also pass with `0147`.

`NOVU_SLACK_WORKFLOW_ENDPOINT_POLICY=snapshot-revision` is an optional stricter choice in addition to `current` and `snapshot`. It requires a revision on every captured endpoint in a fanout and on every delivery job. A legacy payload without the revision is marked failed with `Slack endpoint revision missing`, before dispatch; an empty snapshot still follows the existing no-endpoints cancellation. Normal revision matching and endpoint locks remain in effect. Existing `snapshot` intentionally continues to accept old revision-free snapshots, so it does not provide this stricter guarantee. A strict failure is included in existing pre-dispatch failure monitoring and acknowledgement; it is not automatically retried or sent, and operators must investigate and decide how to handle it.

Apply worker migrations `0147` and `0148` before deploying the matching workers. Upgrade all in-app producers and enable `NOVU_SLACK_SNAPSHOT_ENDPOINTS=true` on each, upgrade all Slack consumers, and drain or review legacy work before selecting strict mode. Stop all Slack workers, have a trusted administrator set `endpoint_policy` to NULL in `novu_rust_slack_workflow_worker_config` for that environment, then start all with `snapshot-revision`. The worker registration rejects different new-worker policies; old binaries and privileged direct changes can bypass this guard. Protect configuration and ensure the producer actually runs with snapshot capture: missing snapshots fail closed and may create operational backlog. Reapplying the older `0115` constraint can fail while strict mode is registered; use ordered migration history. Existing unsent legacy jobs are not upgraded automatically. Snapshot capture still begins when the in-app worker processes a trigger, not at HTTP acceptance; target reassignment outside Swallow, changes after dispatch reservation, backup restoration, asynchronous failover, trigger bypass, and actual delivery remain outside the guarantee. A strict decision can prevent sends, so monitor failures and independently reconcile before taking action.

Docker PostgreSQL tests run the actual Slack worker process against revision-free fanout and delivery jobs and verify both fail without a Slack attempt and appear in pre-dispatch monitoring. Another test verifies strict acceptance of a new revision-bearing job, legacy `snapshot` compatibility, policy registration and rejection of mixed new workers. These tests use no actual Slack provider; external alert receipt, all deployment races, production load and HA are unverified.

Run `SWALLOW_FAILOVER_TEST_SCENARIO=slack ./scripts/test-postgres-failover.sh` to exercise Slack with two disposable synchronously replicated PostgreSQL containers. The test seeds a workflow and uses in-app and fanout queue functions with revision snapshots, then holds a loopback mock Slack success response while it confirms the running job, dispatching attempt and destination revision reached the standby. It kills the primary, promotes the standby, releases the response and expires the lease. Two real Slack workflow worker processes reconnect under `snapshot-revision` and recover the in-flight job as failed/unknown, with one attempt and no repeat external connection observed for six seconds. After promotion, another locally generated delivery has its endpoint changed and restored before the workers start; they fail it before any attempt. The script destroys its disposable containers and volume.

The initial request and fanout are queue-function test harnesses, the workflow is seeded directly, and the second job is created after promotion. The database test login is an administrator. The test uses a local mock and proxy, not Slack or an original Novu server; an unknown outcome still requires independent review. This selected single-host synchronous failover does not establish behavior for network partitions, split brain, asynchronous replication, all commit boundaries, real provider delivery, production orchestration or load.

### SendGrid health consistency

Apply worker migration `0149` and upgrade `delivery-reconcile` together. The scoped function retains its existing EXECUTE grants, but its trusted security-definer owner additionally needs SELECT on `public.novu_rust_sendgrid_attempts` and `public.novu_rust_sendgrid_recipients`. An unrestricted monitor login also needs those reads. For an overdue job to count as delivered, the stored projection must be delivered, its current attempt must be accepted and tracked, and at least one recipient must be recorded with all recorded recipients delivered. Otherwise the job remains overdue. Old unrestricted binaries continue to use the old check; retire them during rollout. This conservative test does not prove the recipient snapshot is complete or that all webhooks have arrived, and the heartbeat does not establish that every job was projected. The additional per-job lookups have not been load-tested.

A temporary local PostgreSQL 17 integration test checks scoped and unrestricted queries and actual `check-sendgrid` exit status for contradictory data, plus accepted, unknown and empty-recipient cases. Docker was unavailable due to read-only storage. Live SendGrid, production load and HA are unverified.

Worker migration `0150` also applies the conservative rule to `delivery-reconcile sendgrid-result` through its existing security-definer function, preserving EXECUTE grants. Non-accepted or untracked attempts and empty recipient snapshots return `unknown`; stored `delivered` or `failed` results are returned as `unknown` if recorded recipients contradict them. Recipient details remain subject to the existing 1000-recipient limit. Matching evidence does not establish a complete snapshot or current provider state. Temporary PostgreSQL 17 tests exercised the restricted CLI and signed synthetic webhook projection with a real worker. Live SendGrid and production HA remain unverified.

### SendGrid IP pool selection

Set `NOVU_SENDGRID_IP_POOL_NAME` on the SendGrid worker for an optional default. A nonempty `ipPoolName` on the trigger email override, SendGrid flat override, SendGrid provider override or matching step/provider override replaces the default; their merge precedence is in that order, with the step last. An empty channel override falls back to the default. An explicitly empty SendGrid provider or step override is sent as an empty `ip_pool_name`, matching the original provider transformation; an unset or empty default omits the field. The setting is per worker process, so configure replicas consistently and restart them to change it. Upgrade all SendGrid workers before enabling overrides: older workers reject jobs containing the new field. Jobs without an override omit the field and retain the existing shape. SendGrid validates the pool and its account permissions; the chosen name is stored in queued workflow payloads. PostgreSQL trigger-to-queue tests and mock HTTP tests cover the supported precedence. The original provider source and pinned SendGrid SDK were compared for six selected default and override cases; actual SendGrid and original-server end-to-end comparison are unverified.

Reproduce the IP pool provider comparison with the dashboard dependencies installed and an isolated npm directory: `npm install --prefix /tmp/swallow-sendgrid-reference --no-audit --no-fund --ignore-scripts @sendgrid/client@8.1.0 @sendgrid/mail@8.1.6 @sendgrid/eventwebhook@8.0.0`, then from this worker directory set `NOVU_REFERENCE_SOURCE` to the repository root and `SENDGRID_REFERENCE_DEPS=/tmp/swallow-sendgrid-reference` and run `cargo test --locked --test reference_sendgrid original_sendgrid_ip_pool_precedence -- --ignored`. The harness bundles the checked-in original provider and base transformer with esbuild and stubs Novu runtime enums only; it intercepts the SDK request without contacting SendGrid. It does not exercise the original API or workflow.

### Projection heartbeat policy agreement

Apply worker migration `0151` before deploying the three projection workers and either grant each projection worker DB login `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on `public.novu_rust_projection_worker_policy`, or choose the restricted function mode described below. The first new worker registers its `REQUIRE_FULL_SCAN` value for its environment and provider; subsequent workers accept only the same value and exit on mismatch. The default remains `false`; setting the existing provider-specific variable to `true` remains a user choice. Environment and provider registrations are independent. Connection failures and missing permissions cause reconnect retries; a mismatched policy terminates the worker. Monitor process exits and connection errors separately. Protect the registry from untrusted writes.

To change policy, stop **all** projection workers for that provider and environment, have a trusted administrator delete only its registration row and its corresponding provider heartbeat row, then start all workers with the desired setting. Clearing the prior heartbeat avoids treating a relaxed scan as evidence of a strict scan. Older workers do not register or check this policy and must be stopped before relying on it. Manual registry edits while workers are running, database restore, asynchronous replication loss and split brain are outside this protection. The agreement concerns full-scan policy only, not scan intervals, provider configuration or a consistent snapshot of all jobs. A new worker registers on each database connection; its ongoing connection does not continuously recheck the row.

Local PostgreSQL 17 tests exercised actual workers for all three providers: a locked job, suppressed strict heartbeat, rejection of a conflicting process, recovery after unlocking, plus interruption and restart. The test DB login was an administrator. Docker storage was read-only; production permissions, sustained load, real provider traffic and multi-host HA remain unverified.

### Restricted projection-policy registration

Apply worker migration `0152` and set `SWALLOW_PROJECTION_SCOPED_POLICY=true` on each updated projection worker to use the restricted registration function. Omit the setting or set `false` for the existing direct-table mode. Invalid values fail startup. A trusted DBA must provision a row for each allowed environment, provider and actual login, and separately grant that login `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_register_projection_policy(text,text,boolean)`. For example, with deployment-specific identifiers substituted by a trusted administrator:

```sql
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_projection_policy_logins(environment_id,provider,database_principal,principal_oid)
SELECT 'ENVIRONMENT', 'sendgrid', rolname, oid FROM pg_catalog.pg_roles
WHERE rolname='WORKER_LOGIN' AND rolcanlogin;
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_register_projection_policy(text,text,boolean) TO WORKER_LOGIN;
REVOKE ALL ON public.novu_rust_projection_worker_policy FROM WORKER_LOGIN;
```

Provision Resend and Twilio independently when needed. The login is checked by `session_user` and role OID, and its grant row is share-locked during registration so a committed revocation wins over a waiting registration. The security-definer owner needs SELECT and row-lock privileges on the login registry and SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE on the policy table. Protect both tables, function ownership, the schema and the trusted DBA role. Revoke any direct policy table privileges or inherited grants when enforcing this boundary. The worker still needs separately provisioned permissions for its projection data and heartbeat; those permissions may expose other environments unless separately constrained.

With migration `0153`, updated scoped workers recheck access while scanning as described below. The check does not immediately interrupt in-flight work; stop or disconnect a worker when immediate termination is required. Shared logins are not individual identities; elevated roles, inherited rights, legacy direct-mode workers and older binaries can bypass this boundary. Local PostgreSQL 17 tests used a restricted login with actual workers for all three providers and checked cross-environment denial, direct-table denial, OID mismatch and revocation racing a waiting registration. Production DB roles, live providers and HA remain unverified.

### Running scoped-worker authorization recheck

Apply worker migration `0153` and grant scoped worker logins `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_check_projection_policy(text,text,boolean)` before deploying updated scoped workers. The security-definer owner needs SELECT and row-lock privileges on both the policy and login registry tables. In scoped mode the worker checks its login and registered policy before each page read, each job projection and heartbeat update. A denied check stops the scan and reconnects; restored access allows it to resume. Failures do not advance the heartbeat after the failed check. Rechecks add database queries and row locks proportional to jobs and pages; production cost has not been measured.

Updated workers hold the authorization locks inside each job projection transaction as described below. Page reads still use a separate check and transaction. With migration `0154`, scoped heartbeat checks and writes are also atomic. Revocation does not cancel a database operation in flight, and direct-mode or older workers do not recheck. Disconnect workers and revoke their other DB privileges when immediate enforcement matters; this function only controls projection-policy authorization. Local PostgreSQL 17 tests ran all three actual scoped workers through revocation, suppressed heartbeat, restoration and resumption; a direct-mode regression also passed. Real providers, production load and HA remain unverified.

### Atomic scoped projection heartbeat

Apply worker migration `0154` and grant scoped worker logins `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_scoped_projection_heartbeat(text,text,boolean)` before upgrading the workers. The trusted security-definer owner needs EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_check_projection_policy(text,text,boolean)` and SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE on all three provider heartbeat tables; the check function owner needs its documented row-lock privileges on policy and login tables. Updated scoped workers do not need direct INSERT or UPDATE on heartbeat tables. Revoke direct and inherited heartbeat writes from those logins when enforcing this boundary. Protect function owners and schema.

The function holds the grant and matching policy row locks until its heartbeat update commits. If revocation commits first, a waiting heartbeat is denied; if the heartbeat locks first, revocation waits for that transaction. Page reads still have a recheck-to-operation race; updated scoped job projections hold the authorization lock through commit as described below. Older binaries, direct mode, elevated DB privileges, direct table writes, restores and split brain bypass this protection. A temporary PostgreSQL 17 test verified both lock orders, denied direct heartbeat writes, actual scoped workers across all three providers and restoration after revocation; a direct-mode regression passed. Production permissions, load and HA remain unverified.

### Transaction-scoped projection authorization

Updated scoped workers invoke `novu_rust_check_projection_policy` inside each SendGrid, Resend or Twilio projection transaction and retain its grant and policy row locks through commit or rollback. If revocation commits first, projection fails; if projection locks first, revocation waits for that transaction. The opt-in Resend automatic correlation uses the same guarded transaction. A blocked or long-running projection can delay revocation, subject to the worker's existing database timeout and cancellation behavior. Each page read is checked separately and can still race revocation. This is not row-level isolation of the projection tables and does not stop privileged or older workers, direct mode, database restores or split brain.

The existing migration `0153` and function EXECUTE grant are required; no additional migration is needed for this change. Local PostgreSQL 17 tests exercised both lock orders and actual workers for all three channels, including Resend auto-correlation being enabled; the lock-contention test used an administrator DB login. A separate restricted-login worker test covers authorization revocation and resumption without queued jobs. Actual providers, all lock combinations, production load and multi-host HA remain unverified.

### SendGrid alert IP pool

For directly invoked delivery or database alert commands with `SWALLOW_ALERT_EMAIL_PROVIDER=sendgrid`, optionally set `NOVU_SENDGRID_IP_POOL_NAME`. An absent or empty value omits the pool; values over 256 bytes or containing CR, LF or NUL fail configuration. SendGrid determines whether the named pool exists and is authorized.

For `alert-fanout`, add an optional `"ip_pool_name":"ops-pool"` to an email target with `"provider":"sendgrid"`. The fanout clears the inherited `NOVU_SENDGRID_IP_POOL_NAME` for every target, then passes only the explicitly configured value to its SendGrid child; Resend targets reject this field. Existing configurations without it omit the pool. Pool choice is not included in the existing alert destination-binding fingerprint, so coordinate changes across senders and review the target configuration. Sending may have occurred when a response is unknown; existing reconciliation and cooldown rules still apply.

Mock HTTP tests verify the request field and outcome classification, process tests cover per-target propagation and inherited-value isolation for both alert commands, and a temporary PostgreSQL test exercises the actual fanout and alert commands with blocked destinations. Actual SendGrid delivery, original Novu end-to-end comparison and operational HA are unverified.

### Optional email alert configuration binding

The default `SWALLOW_ALERT_EMAIL_BINDING_PROFILE=destination` retains the existing HMAC binding to provider and recipient. Set it to `configuration` on all cooperating alert senders to include the recipient, provider, sender address and display name, provider API endpoint, provider API key and (for SendGrid) IP pool in a versioned HMAC. A strong shared `SWALLOW_ALERT_BINDING_KEY` or `_FILE` is mandatory in this mode; a missing key or invalid profile fails configuration. The raw API key and other inputs are not stored in the binding table or printed by this path, but the HMAC key and binding records remain sensitive. The Slack webhook binding is unchanged.

The new fingerprint intentionally differs from existing destination-only bindings. To enable it, stop or coordinate all senders, provision new email target IDs, configure all participating processes with the same profile, binding key and provider settings, then verify their registered destinations before relying on delivery. Changing the provider API key, endpoint/region, sender or pool also requires a new target ID or trusted administrative remediation of the existing binding. Existing direct DB edits have no automatic audit. A sender without binding enabled, an older binary, a different target ID, privileged DB access, restored databases or separate unsynchronized state databases can bypass or lose this association. Binding does not prove that the first configuration is correct or that a provider received an alert.

A temporary PostgreSQL test ran real fanout, delivery-alert and database-alert commands for Resend and SendGrid with blocked fixture destinations, checking mismatched settings, profile conflict, absent key and redacted output. It did not contact real providers or validate multi-host HA.

### Local synchronous failover: alert binding

Run `bash scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` from this directory to create a disposable, loopback-only PostgreSQL 17 primary and synchronous physical standby. The script initializes temporary data directories, applies API and worker migrations, verifies synchronous streaming and starts the Rust integration test. The test stops only its temporary primary with `pg_ctl -m immediate`, promotes the temporary standby and verifies that the configuration-bound SendGrid alert destination and rejected slot survive. After promotion it runs two actual alert commands with the registered pool and one with a conflicting pool concurrently; the matching commands observe the blocked slot, the conflicting command fails binding, and no alert attempt is recorded. The script stops its temporary servers and removes their data on exit. `SWALLOW_TEST_POSTGRES_BIN` selects the PostgreSQL 17 binary directory; the default is `/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin`.

This test uses a superuser, artificial blocked destinations and one host. It does not send email or test a live provider, asynchronous replication, a real network partition, split brain, an external failover controller, load or every crash point. It is evidence for this specific synchronous promotion scenario, not an operational HA guarantee. Docker was unavailable due to read-only storage during this run.

The same local PostgreSQL harness supports `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend bash scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh`. It holds a local mocked Resend success response during primary shutdown and synchronous standby promotion, then starts two real Resend workers against the promoted server. They recover the expired dispatch as unknown without another external connection during a six-second observation. Synthetic database events subsequently exercise correlation recovery and invalidate a separate earlier result after conflicting evidence. The initial request is issued by a queue-function harness, not the production worker process; the callback is inserted directly, not received over HTTP. The test does not prove actual email receipt or cover live Resend, multi-host network partitions, asynchronous failover, split brain or all crash points.

The local synchronous failover harness also supports `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=twilio bash scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh`. A queue-function harness sends to a local mock and holds the response while the temporary primary is stopped and the standby promoted. Two real Twilio workers recover the expired attempt as unknown without another external connection during a six-second observation. The replicated dispatch includes its account snapshot. A real Rust API process with account-bound callbacks required then accepts a synthetic correctly signed HTTP callback, rejects an invalid signature and deduplicates a repeated callback. Because the original provider response and message ID were lost, the projected job and message remain `unknown`. The initial sender is not the production worker; neither the callback nor SMS comes from Twilio. The test uses a superuser and a single host, and does not establish device receipt, split-brain safety, asynchronous failover or production HA.

The local failover harness supports FCM via `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=fcm TEST_FCM_FAILOVER_MULTIPLE=false bash scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh`; set `TEST_FCM_FAILOVER_MULTIPLE=true` for the two-target case. With the initial mock response held during synchronous promotion, two real workers preserve the first target as unknown rather than resending it. In the two-target case they make one connection for the previously unattempted second target; the test proxy closes that connection and both targets remain unknown. The first request uses a queue-function harness, the second is observed only at the proxy, and the test uses an administrator DB login and one host. It does not verify actual FCM or device receipt, split brain, asynchronous failover or production HA.

### Optional alert scheduler

`alert-scheduler` runs the existing `alert-fanout` immediately and then waits a fixed delay **after each run finishes**. It accepts the same fanout arguments after a required 1–86400 second interval, for either delivery or database alerts:

```sh
alert-scheduler 60 delivery ENV 300 120 60 /absolute/path/targets.json
alert-scheduler 60 database ENV DATABASE_LABEL 60 /absolute/path/targets.json
```

Install `alert-scheduler`, `alert-fanout`, `send-delivery-alert` and `send-database-alert` together in the same trusted directory. Configure the existing fanout target file, secrets, database permissions, checks, cooldowns and timeouts as documented above; the scheduler inherits its environment and reloads target configuration on each run. It waits for a run to finish before starting another in the same process. A run's exit 2 is reported as `attention required`; other failures, including a missing executable, are logged as failures. By default it continues after failures; `SWALLOW_ALERT_SCHEDULER_FAILURE_MODE=exit` instead exits nonzero on a failed run, allowing a supervisor to restart or alert. This setting does not turn attention (exit 2) into failure. Monitor logs, process liveness, absent runs and child outcomes independently: a running scheduler does not establish that checks succeeded or alerts reached recipients. An external scheduler remains an alternative.

SIGTERM or Ctrl-C prevents a new run and allows the current fanout to finish for `SWALLOW_ALERT_SCHEDULER_SHUTDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` (default 30; 1–300). If grace expires, the scheduler exits with an error. On Unix it starts each fanout in a separate process group and sends SIGKILL to that group on timeout; elsewhere it kills only the direct child. This cannot stop a descendant that escaped the group, a process stuck in uninterruptible kernel sleep, or survive abrupt scheduler termination such as SIGKILL. A provider may already have accepted a request; never infer non-delivery or immediately retry. Set the grace and supervisor termination limits for the maximum fanout duration, including target timeouts, and separately supervise child processes. The fixed delay is not a clock-aligned schedule or missed-run replay. Multiple scheduler instances do not coordinate scheduling or guarantee exactly-once delivery: existing database reservation and cooldown policies apply per target, while uncoordinated fallback can duplicate alerts. Database outages, shared failure domains, long/hung children and scheduler failure still need independent monitoring.

A process test covers fixed-delay non-overlap, attention, invalid configuration, failure-exit mode and SIGTERM while a run is active. A local PostgreSQL 17 test runs the actual scheduler and fanout twice against a blocked Slack destination; it sends no external alert and uses an administrator DB connection. Production scheduler supervision, real Slack/email receipt, multi-node failure and HA remain unverified. With fully migrated disposable PostgreSQL, run `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test alert_scheduler -- --ignored`.

The disposable PostgreSQL integration suite also starts two actual schedulers against the same Slack target. A local HTTPS proxy holds and drops the first connection; after both schedulers repeatedly observe the target, exactly one connection and one `unknown` attempt remain. This tests one shared-DB reservation and unknown-outcome scenario only: the proxy closes before TLS, so it cannot prove actual Slack acceptance or receipt. Both schedulers and the administrator-connected PostgreSQL run on one host; network partitions, database failover, multi-host timing and uncoordinated fallback are not covered.

On macOS, a process test verifies forced shutdown of both a fixture fanout and its child. A disposable PostgreSQL test holds a real Slack request at a local proxy, terminates the scheduler, expires the recorded lease, and runs the real fanout again: the single existing attempt becomes `unknown` and no second connection is made. The proxy does not establish TLS or actual receipt. This does not test escaped process groups, uninterruptible processes, SIGKILL of the scheduler, real provider outcomes, or multi-host HA.

### Alert scheduler during synchronous PostgreSQL promotion

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=alert-scheduler bash scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` creates a disposable primary and synchronous standby on one host, migrates both through physical replication, and runs the actual `alert-scheduler`, `alert-fanout` and delivery-alert processes. A local proxy holds a Slack connection after the dispatch reservation is committed; the test waits for the standby to replay the `dispatching` slot and attempt, immediately stops the primary and promotes the standby. After closing the held connection it manually expires the 60-second lease to accelerate recovery. The running scheduler reconnects to the promoted read-write server, marks the one attempt `unknown` and performs at least two further checks without opening another provider connection.

The proxy closes before TLS and does not establish whether a real Slack request was accepted. The single-host databases use an administrator account and synchronous physical replication; the test does not verify independent hosts, failover orchestration, split brain, asynchronous replication or actual external delivery. Lease expiry is forced in the test, not waited out in real time. Configure independent monitoring and investigate `unknown` before any retry; provider side effects remain uncertain.


### Discord transport (development only)

`discord::Webhook` is used by an experimental standalone queue worker; an optional workflow integration is described below; the experimental destination binding API and dashboard are described in the API and dashboard READMEs. `request_body` merges trigger content, custom fields, pre-transformed provider fields and passthrough fields in that order. It does not implement Novu's provider casing conversion. `customData` is shallowly spread over content, then provider/passthrough objects merge recursively; arrays concatenate. This changes results for existing jobs whose layers contain the same array. JavaScript prototype-related key handling is not reproduced. The transport preserves endpoint query parameters other than `wait`, replaces `wait` with `true`, and returns the response `id` and `timestamp`. Only official Discord HTTPS webhook host/path shapes are permitted, which is stricter than the original Novu generic safe outbound URL contract. Webhook URLs contain secrets and must not be logged. Redirects are disabled; request and response sizes and timeout are bounded.

A 2xx with missing or malformed result, network failure, redirect, 408/409, or 5xx is an unknown outcome; a caller must not blindly resend it. A 429 returns the parsed Retry-After when available and other 4xx responses are rejected. The transport itself does not persist attempts or provide crash recovery, idempotency, scheduling, or delivery confirmation. The standalone worker behavior and its limits are described below. Tests use loopback mocks and source inspection, not a running Novu server or Discord service. Run `cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --lib discord::tests` from the repository root.

For HTTP 429, the transport accepts a single numeric `Retry-After` header including fractional seconds, the existing HTTP-date form, and Discord's numeric JSON `retry_after`; valid fractional values are rounded up to whole seconds and the longer valid header/body value is recorded. Values outside 0–86400 seconds, duplicate headers, duplicate JSON fields and malformed inputs are ignored independently. The body is limited to 64 KiB and one second of reading; a timeout or invalid body preserves any valid header. This additional read can consume up to one second of the worker's overall DB operation timeout. The selected retry policy is described below; no shared delay for other jobs or global limit enforcement is provided. Never infer a safe retry from the stored delay alone. Discord describes its header and body in the [rate-limit documentation](https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/rate-limits). Loopback tests cover fractional values, invalid responses, conflicting guidance and a stalled body. `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --features test-mocks --test discord_rate_limit -- --ignored` uses a fresh disposable PostgreSQL with no enabled retry policy to check persistence and no automatic retry. Actual Discord and production HA are unverified.

### Discord 429 retry policy

Apply worker migration `0208` before deploying this version of `discord-webhook-worker`; the new worker requires the tables even when retry is disabled. `NOVU_DISCORD_429_RETRY_MODE` is `off` by default, `known` to retry only an HTTP 429 with a valid provider delay, or `fallback` to use `NOVU_DISCORD_429_FALLBACK_SECONDS` when no valid delay is available. `NOVU_DISCORD_429_MAX_RETRIES` defaults to 3 and accepts 0–10; the fallback defaults to 60 and accepts 1–86400 seconds. The delay is at least one second. These are retries after the first attempt, bounded per job across worker restarts. Invalid startup settings fail the worker. `fallback` is an explicit availability tradeoff: missing, malformed, duplicated, timed-out or over-24-hour guidance is indistinguishable from no guidance and can cause a retry earlier than the provider intended. A proxy-generated 429 may not establish what happened at Discord. Choose the mode and fallback with this risk in mind. Responses classified as unknown, including connection loss and 5xx, are never automatically retried by this policy.

The first worker registers the mode, retry limit and fallback for its environment and destination; another worker with different values rejects startup. Before changing an existing policy, stop all workers for that destination, allow in-flight dispatches to finish or recover, update the policy through the audited CLI described below or trusted DBA control, and start only upgraded workers with matching settings. Existing recorded retries still count toward the limit. The database fence rejects new attempt inserts by incompatible workers while `known` or `fallback` is enabled; it cannot cancel an outbound request already reserved before the switch and privileged SQL can bypass it. Direct policy changes while workers run are not audited and take effect for later queue operations; restarting workers with stale settings then fails. An experimental operator JWT HTTP API and Angular policy management screen are described in the API and dashboard READMEs.

Each scheduled 429 is atomically copied to `novu_rust_discord_retry_history`, removed from the current attempt table, and the job is requeued for the calculated delay. Every new workflow attempt rechecks subscriber identity, preferences, workflow and endpoint revision. History remains after success; when the retry limit is exhausted the current 429 remains rejected and visible to existing health checks. Scheduled 429 history is not included in current Discord health or review counts. History retention and monitoring are operator responsibilities. A failure to commit the history or queue update leaves the prior dispatching attempt to expire as unknown; it is not retried automatically. The fence is cooperative and the protected tables and function owner must not be exposed to untrusted writers.

Worker migration `0209` adds `public.novu_rust_discord_retry_health(environment,grace_seconds)`. Grant the monitoring DB login EXECUTE on it and the environment-specific `alert_view` grant; its owner needs access to queue jobs, retry history and the existing grant check. `delivery-reconcile check-discord-retry ENV GRACE_SECONDS` returns aggregate `pending` and `overdue` counts and exits 2 only for overdue jobs, 0 otherwise, or 1 on failure. Grace is 1–86400 seconds after the scheduled `available_at`. Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_DISCORD_RETRY_GRACE_SECONDS` to enable this independent check in `send-delivery-alert`; absence disables it. Pending but not overdue retries are reported when another condition sends an alert, but do not themselves trigger an alert. Slack and/or email destinations use existing configuration and `alert_send` permissions. With `new-condition` cooldown, scoped delivery and `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2=true` are required; v2 bits 56 and 57 represent overdue retries and check failure. A failed check must not clear a previously observed overdue condition.

Before migration 0229, counts include pending Discord jobs with at least one automatic retry-history row; see the manual-retry monitoring section below for the expanded behavior. running jobs are excluded, and a previously retried job returned to pending for another reason is included. This is a point-in-time backlog signal, not proof of a stopped worker or failed delivery. It does not expose destination, recipient, payload, credentials or individual jobs, and it does not count all historical 429s. Long-running queries and table growth require production sizing; the retry policy's shared/global limit limitations remain. `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --test discord_retry_health -- --ignored` uses fresh disposable PostgreSQL and a restricted login to check environment scope, permissions, threshold, CLI and blocked alert selection. `cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --lib discord_retry_overdue_is_optional_and_reaches_both_destinations` checks mock Slack and Resend payloads and condition bits. Actual alert receipt, production load and HA are unverified.

Worker migration `0210` adds `public.novu_rust_discord_retry_list(text,integer,boolean,uuid)` and an index on pending Discord jobs. Grant EXECUTE on the function and the environment-specific `alert_view` grant to the investigating login; its security-definer owner needs queue and retry-history access plus the existing grant check. `delivery-reconcile list-discord-retry ENV GRACE_SECONDS all|overdue [AFTER_JOB]` returns up to 100 entries and a `next` UUID cursor. Each entry contains the job and recorded destination IDs, kind, schedule, claim count, retry count, most recent recorded delay and provider guidance, and overdue status. The destination ID is taken from retry history; no current payload, subscriber, webhook URL or key is returned. IDs and times remain operationally sensitive and access should be restricted. The query is read-only and neither retries nor cancels jobs.

Worker migration `0211` adds revisioned, audited policy administration for an already registered environment and destination. A trusted DBA provisions `novu_rust_discord_retry_policy_admins` for the specific environment, destination and DB login name/OID with independent `can_read`, `can_write` and `can_audit` rights. Grant the login EXECUTE only on the required `novu_rust_discord_retry_policy_read(text,text)`, `novu_rust_discord_retry_policy_set(text,text,uuid,bigint,text,integer,integer,text)`, `novu_rust_discord_retry_policy_request(text,text,uuid)` and `novu_rust_discord_retry_policy_audit_read(text,text,bigint)` functions. The security-definer owner needs table access and audit sequence usage; protect it and direct DB access. No rights are granted by default.

Use `delivery-reconcile discord-retry-policy-read ENV DEST`, then `discord-retry-policy-set ENV DEST REQUEST_ID EXPECTED_REVISION off|known|fallback MAX_RETRIES FALLBACK_SECONDS REASON`; request lookup and audit use `discord-retry-policy-request ENV DEST REQUEST_ID` and `discord-retry-policy-audit ENV DEST [BEFORE]`. The same request and body are replayable; differing bodies or stale revisions fail. A real configuration change increments revision, while an identical update, including a worker's same-settings startup registration, keeps it unchanged. Even an identical CLI set records an audit entry. Audit and policy updates commit together. Direct DB writes increment revision when settings change but do not create audit records. Audit is retained when an environment is deleted; reused environment and destination IDs can expose earlier audit, and request records survive destination deletion until environment deletion. Audit is not tamper-proof; rejected requests are not recorded and pages are not a single snapshot. Avoid secrets in reasons and configure retention separately.

`TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --test discord_retry_policy_admin -- --ignored` uses a fresh disposable PostgreSQL and restricted login roles to verify destination/environment scope, permissions, concurrent changes, replay, no-op registration, audit rollback and revision. The first run exposed an argument-count limit in the CLI, which was corrected; the rerun and a full-migration diagnostics API regression passed using disposable local PostgreSQL 17 because Docker Desktop could not start. Actual Discord, external IdP and production HA are unverified.

The UUID order is not chronological, pages are separate snapshots, and concurrent job changes can cause omissions or repeats. The list includes only pending jobs with retry history, matching the health count's filter; it does not include currently running or completed jobs or establish provider delivery. The index is created by the migration and can lock or add write overhead on large queues; assess rollout and production sizing. The restricted-login Docker test for `discord_retry_health` additionally checks last history, scope, revocation, payload exclusion and 105 entries across pages. The experimental operator JWT API, grant delegation and dashboard are described in the [API README](../../api/rust-api/README.md) and [dashboard README](../../dashboard/swallow/README.md); production load and HA remain unverified.

The worker DB login additionally needs SELECT, INSERT and UPDATE on `novu_rust_discord_retry_policy`, SELECT and INSERT on `novu_rust_discord_retry_history`, USAGE on `novu_rust_discord_retry_history_id_seq`, and DELETE on `novu_rust_discord_attempts` in addition to its previous privileges. The fence owner needs read access to queue jobs and read/lock access to policy rows. Review these broad direct privileges before deployment. The test `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --features test-mocks --test discord_retry_policy -- --ignored` uses a disposable Docker PostgreSQL, mock HTTP and a restricted DB login to check modes, fallback, limits, history rollback, unknown outcome, concurrent claims and a simulated old-worker insert; two actual current workers also check conflicting settings.
