From the repository root, `apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-discord-retry-legacy-worker.sh` builds the actual pre-policy worker at `6821aa6d`, applies current migrations to disposable Docker PostgreSQL, and enables `known` mode. It verifies the old process is rejected before inserting an attempt or connecting to a local proxy, stops it, forces the lease to expire and confirms the current process recovers and connects once to the local proxy. The proxy closes the connection before TLS, so the current attempt becomes unknown; no Discord request is sent. The test uses one host, an administrative DB login and a forced expiry. It does not verify the `fallback` mode with the historical process, real Discord, shared/global throttling, asynchronous replication, network partitions or production HA.

`SWALLOW_FAILOVER_TEST_SCENARIO=discord-retry apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-postgres-failover.sh` uses two disposable Docker PostgreSQL containers with synchronous replication. A queue function and local mock produce a 429 with a 121-second scheduled retry and a separate unknown attempt; both are confirmed on the standby before the primary is killed and the standby promoted. Two actual current workers then leave the scheduled job untouched and do not retry the unknown one. The test moves the schedule forward manually, observes one local proxy connection and verifies that the resulting unknown state is not retried. The proxy closes before TLS and does not reach Discord. This uses one host, an administrative DB login, a forced schedule change and a mock 429 generated through the queue function rather than a real worker-to-Discord response. It does not validate `fallback`, actual Discord, asynchronous replication, split brain or production HA.


### Standalone Discord webhook queue

Apply worker migration `0202` after the earlier API and worker migrations. Start `discord-webhook-worker` with `DATABASE_URL`, `NOVU_DISCORD_ENVIRONMENT_ID`, `NOVU_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_DESTINATION_ID`, and one of `NOVU_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` or `NOVU_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL_FILE`. The destination ID is 1–64 ASCII letters, digits, dot, underscore or hyphen. Each environment/destination is permanently associated with a SHA-256 hash of the exact configured URL; changing any part of the URL requires a new destination ID. The hash and database access are sensitive. Multiple workers for the same destination must use the same URL. `NOVU_DISCORD_WEBHOOK_DB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` is 1–300 (default 30) and covers a whole queue operation, including the outbound request; an overly short value can cancel it and leave the result unknown. PostgreSQL TLS is required by default.

A trusted producer can insert a job with `kind=experimental_discord_webhook`, its environment ID and JSON payload such as `{"destination":"ops","content":"Hello","customData":{},"providerData":{},"passthrough":{}}`. These fields are directly supplied body maps; they are not the complete Novu SDK contract. The experimental binding management API is described in the API README; the optional 429 retry policy is described above and operator reconciliation is described below; Discord health alerts are described below; the optional workflow producer is described below. Queue write access permits sends, so restrict it and avoid putting secrets in payloads. The worker records a dispatching attempt before contacting Discord. Expired leases without an attempt are requeued; those with an attempt are marked unknown and failed, even when the request may not have reached Discord. A success response indicates provider acceptance, not recipient reading. 429 is recorded as rejected with optional Retry-After; the opt-in retry policy is described above. Manually changing jobs or attempt records to retry can duplicate messages. Queue and attempt access must be restricted, and secret URL rotation requires operational planning. Database failover, concurrent hosts, direct SQL misuse, actual Discord delivery and end-to-end comparison with the original Novu server remain unverified.

`TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --features test-mocks --test discord_queue -- --ignored` uses and modifies a fresh disposable database with an administrative login. It checks mocked provider success and a lost response, environment/destination isolation, expired pre-dispatch and dispatching recovery, and actual worker recovery and endpoint conflict. The worker process explicitly disables TLS only for this local test. The mock dispatch uses the queue function, not the actual worker or Discord.

Selected JSON merge cases can be compared against the checked-out original `deepMerge` implementation with `cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --test discord_original_merge -- --ignored`. This requires a Node version with native TypeScript stripping. It compares the merge utility directly, not a running original provider or server.


### Experimental Discord workflow delivery

Apply worker migration `0203` after `0202`. Register a destination by starting its Discord webhook worker as described above. A trusted database administrator can then insert into `novu_rust_discord_workflow_endpoints` using the existing environment, subscriber ID and subscriber internal ID, destination ID, a unique endpoint identifier, and optional context keys. The experimental API and dashboard support authorized, audited binding changes; an independently audited database write function is not provided. Restrict database access and do not expose direct table writes to end users. Deleting a subscriber cascades its endpoint bindings. The URL stays in the Discord worker secret rather than the table. The destination ID list does not indicate whether its worker is running or the endpoint is reachable.

Set `NOVU_DISCORD_WORKFLOW_ENABLED=true` on capable in-app workers and enable the corresponding environment policy as described below; run the updated Discord worker for every mapped destination. The process setting defaults to false. Migration `0206` fences older or disabled workers in an enabled environment, but all workers still need upgrading to avoid backlog. Existing chat steps still enqueue their Slack path independently, so configure that path appropriately. When enabled, the in-app worker snapshots up to 1,000 matching endpoints per subscriber and creates a Discord job per endpoint. Workers select jobs by environment and destination. At dispatch, subscriber identity and chat preferences are rechecked, the workflow must still be active with the same chat template, and the endpoint ID and revision must match. Every endpoint update changes its revision, including a change followed by restoration. Rendering uses the existing limited Rust template engine; the workflow payload is not the complete Novu provider override contract.

The checks hold database locks until the attempt is reserved, not until the external request completes. A deletion or preference change after reservation cannot cancel a request already underway. Direct privileged database writes or disabling triggers can bypass protections. Discord acceptance is not proof that a person read a message. There is no operator recovery UI, automated retry, or guarantee of exactly-once delivery; Discord health alerts are described below. The API trigger to mock-provider integration test `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --manifest-path apps/api/rust-api/Cargo.toml --test discord_workflow -- --ignored` creates its schema in a fresh disposable PostgreSQL database. It covers selected rendering, disabled mode, endpoint revision, preferences, workflow changes and subscriber ID reuse using the API router and worker functions. It does not contact Discord, run the original Novu server, exercise real worker process dispatch, or establish production HA.


### Discord delivery health and alerts

Apply worker migration `0204`. Grant the monitoring DB login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_discord_health(text)` and its environment-specific `alert_view` operator grant; the function owner needs SELECT on the environment, queue and Discord attempt tables and access to the existing operator-grant check. Direct queue and attempt table reads are unnecessary for this aggregate. `delivery-reconcile check-discord ENV` prints `unknownAttempts`, `rejectedAttempts`, `strandedDispatches` and `preDispatchFailures`; it exits 0 when all are zero, 2 when attention is needed and 1 on errors. The count includes both standalone and workflow jobs, is scoped by environment, and contains no message body, URL or subscriber ID.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_DISCORD_ENABLED=true` to include these counts in `send-delivery-alert` and the existing fanout/scheduler. Configure Slack and/or email destinations using the existing alert settings and give the sender its separate `alert_send` grant. A failed health query is itself an error and cannot clear an earlier problem. For `SWALLOW_ALERT_COOLDOWN_POLICY=new-condition`, Discord requires scoped delivery and `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2=true`; the four categories and check failure use separate v2 condition bits. Destination cooldown mode remains available. Multiple replicas, external routing, health-check schedules and alarm reception remain operator responsibilities.

These are cumulative counts of currently recorded failures, not proof of delivery or a worker heartbeat. The revision-bound Discord review CLI is described below. Opt-in 429 retry is described above; ambiguous outcomes are not automatically retried. The check does not detect absent endpoint mappings, pending backlog, a stopped worker before lease expiry, disabled workflow generation or changes after a request has been reserved. Index creation can lock or add write overhead and full historical scans need production sizing; direct privileged writes and database loss can bypass or erase evidence. Real Discord, actual Slack/email receipt, load and multi-host HA are unverified.

`TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --test discord_health -- --ignored` creates the schema and a restricted login in a fresh disposable PostgreSQL with role-creation privileges. It checks scope, counts, CLI, revoked access and real alert command selection with a deliberately blocked delivery slot; no external alert is sent. `cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --lib discord_health_uses_independent_bits_and_both_destinations` uses mock Slack and email servers to check the payload and condition bits.


### Reviewing Discord failures

Apply worker migration `0205` after `0204`. It adds a fresh revision to each Discord attempt and refreshes it on every update. It replaces the health function to exclude reviewed failed attempts and reviewed pre-dispatch failures only while the job failure revision, attempt count, diagnostic and attempt revision/state still match. Existing unreviewed failures remain visible. Stranded dispatching attempts and inconsistent non-failed jobs cannot be acknowledged and remain visible. Review does not change a job, assert provider acceptance, or initiate a retry.

Grant the operator DB login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_discord_attention(text,uuid)` and the environment-specific `alert_view` grant to list; grant EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_ack_discord(text,uuid,uuid,bigint,text,text)` and the existing environment-specific `queue_ack` grant to acknowledge. `queue_ack` also authorizes other queue acknowledgements, so review its broader scope before granting. The function owner needs access to jobs, attempts, reviews, and operator grants. `delivery-reconcile list-discord ENV [AFTER_JOB]` returns up to 100 unreviewed job IDs, status, attempt revision and failure revision without message payload or credentials. Use the final job ID as the next cursor. The listing is not a snapshot across pages and can change between calls.

After independently investigating, run `delivery-reconcile ack-discord ENV JOB ATTEMPT_REVISION|- FAILURE_REVISION OPERATOR REASON`; use `-` only for a failure without an attempt. The CLI returns `recorded: true` for a newly inserted review and `false` for an exact duplicate. A stale revision, changed job, active/stranded attempt or revoked permission is rejected. Each new review records the supplied name and reason, DB session login and state snapshot; names are self-reported, and shared DB logins cannot distinguish people. Reasons must not contain secrets or personal data. The experimental JWT HTTP review path and Angular dashboard are documented in the API and dashboard READMEs; there is no separate audit-view function, retention policy, tamper-proof audit, provider lookup or retry path. DB owners and direct writes can bypass these controls. An update after reservation or an already-running external request cannot be cancelled by acknowledgement. Revision backfill and trigger/index creation may lock and consume space on large tables; deploy the migration before using the new CLI, evaluate production load, and protect database backups.

The `discord_health` disposable PostgreSQL test also covers restricted permissions, current and stale revisions, duplicate and concurrent reviews, audit insertion failure, changed-and-restored attempt state, pre-dispatch review and refusal to review stranded dispatches. Real Discord, original Novu, production load and HA remain unverified.


### Discord worker crash and synchronous database promotion test

`SWALLOW_FAILOVER_TEST_SCENARIO=discord CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/absolute/path/to/worker/target scripts/test-postgres-failover.sh` starts its own Docker PostgreSQL 17 primary and synchronous physical standby, applies all API/worker migrations, and destroys those test containers on exit. It starts a real Discord webhook worker against a test-only local HTTPS proxy, waits until the proxy sees a CONNECT and the dispatch reservation has reached the standby, kills that worker and the primary, and promotes the standby. After the test expires the preserved lease and inserts another job, two real workers on the promoted DB recover the first as unknown and process the second. The proxy closes the second connection, so that outcome is also unknown. The test verifies two attempt rows, exactly two proxy connections and two surviving worker DB connections; it waits another six seconds to detect an immediate repeat.

The proxy never establishes TLS or contacts Discord and cannot prove the provider received either request. This is a single-host, administrative DB login, synchronous-replication test with manually forced lease expiry and promotion. It does not cover a response lost after Discord acceptance, asynchronous replication data loss, split brain, actual provider behavior, workflow endpoint changes or multi-host production HA. Use a disposable environment and do not point this destructive test at production.


### Discord workflow mixed-version fence

Worker migration `0206` creates `novu_rust_discord_workflow_policy`, initially disabled for every existing and newly created environment. With the migration applied, `NOVU_DISCORD_WORKFLOW_ENABLED=true` enables the new worker capability, but each environment still needs its policy explicitly set to true by a trusted DB administrator, for example `UPDATE public.novu_rust_discord_workflow_policy SET enabled=true WHERE environment_id=...`. A capable worker can serve both enabled and disabled environments. The in-app worker DB login needs EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_discord_workflow_guard(text,uuid,uuid)` when the process setting is true. The function and trigger owner must read and lock policy rows and queue jobs; the environment-insert trigger owner must insert policy rows. Do not grant direct policy mutation to application workers. The independently authorized policy CLI is described below; no HTTP policy API or UI is provided yet.

For enabled environments the compatible worker locks the policy and sets a transaction-local marker tied to the job and lease before writing child jobs and completing the parent. A database trigger rejects a legacy or disabled worker that tries to mark a running in-app/attachment job completed or failed without the marker; its transaction rolls back, and lease recovery can make it available to an updated worker. A compatible worker can still mark malformed jobs failed. Policy changes wait on in-flight policy locks. Jobs completed before enablement are not backfilled, and already-running or externally dispatched work is not reversed. Legacy workers may repeatedly claim and fail, causing backlog; monitor queue age and remove them.

To avoid a gap for an existing deployment that used the previous process-only option, stop or drain in-app workers, apply `0206`, explicitly enable each desired environment, deploy compatible workers with the process option and EXECUTE permission, then resume. Migration takes a lock on the environments table, backfills policy rows and adds a trigger; assess locking, privileges, rollout and monitoring before deployment. Disabling policy stops generation only after in-flight guarded transactions finish; changing only the process option while policy remains enabled will block affected jobs. These cooperative checks are not an authorization boundary: a DB role able to run arbitrary SQL and set session variables, a privileged policy editor, disabled triggers or direct data changes can bypass them. Missing or deleted policy rows fail closed. A selected historical worker binary is tested as described below; production load, replica failover and multi-host HA remain unverified.

`TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --test discord_workflow_fence -- --ignored` requires a fresh disposable PostgreSQL with role creation. It checks policy initialization, restricted role, enablement locking, stale leases, transaction-local marker and disabled environments. The API `discord_workflow` integration test exercises a disabled-capability worker failing to commit in an enabled environment, lease recovery, a compatible worker, malformed job handling and mocked delivery. It simulates old behavior with the current queue function; it does not execute an old worker binary.


`apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-discord-legacy-worker.sh` (from the repository root) builds the `75cd2ecc` pre-fence in-app worker from an isolated Git archive and starts its own disposable Docker PostgreSQL. It enables the environment policy, observes the actual old worker rejected in the database log, verifies the parent remains running and its notification and Discord child were rolled back, stops the old process, forces lease expiry, and verifies the current real worker completes the parent and creates one child. No Discord worker or provider is contacted. The test uses an administrative DB login, a manually inserted trigger job, a forced lease expiry, one host and one PostgreSQL instance; it does not establish mixed-version production load, real provider delivery, network partitions or HA. It requires Docker, local Git history containing that commit and Rust build tools, and removes only its own temporary source and container.


### Discord workflow policy administration

Apply worker migration `0207` after `0206`. A trusted DBA provisions a row in `public.novu_rust_discord_policy_admins` for the exact environment and login name/OID with `can_read`, `can_write` and `can_audit` independently selected. Grant the login EXECUTE only on the appropriate `public.novu_rust_discord_policy_read(text)`, `public.novu_rust_discord_policy_set(text,uuid,bigint,boolean,text)`, `public.novu_rust_discord_policy_request(text,uuid)` and `public.novu_rust_discord_policy_audit_read(text,bigint)` functions. The security-definer owner requires access to the admin, policy, request and audit tables and the audit identity sequence. The restricted login needs no direct table access; protect the function owner and direct DB policy writes. No user or DB login receives policy rights by default.

`delivery-reconcile discord-policy-read ENV` returns `enabled` and a decimal-string revision. `discord-policy-set ENV REQUEST_ID EXPECTED_REVISION enabled|disabled REASON` locks the policy, rejects a stale revision and records the change and request atomically. Repeating exactly the same request with the same DB login and role OID returns the stored result; changing its input is rejected. `discord-policy-request ENV REQUEST_ID` looks up that login's request, and `discord-policy-audit ENV [BEFORE]` returns up to 50 reverse-ID entries with a cursor. Every policy UPDATE, including direct updates and same-value writes, increments the revision; direct DB writes do not receive an API audit. A successful same-value CLI write is audited and increments the revision. A failed audit insert rolls back both change and request. Policy updates wait for guarded workers holding the policy lock; enablement alone does not start workers or register destinations.

Audit uses the DB session login and is not tamper-proof; shared logins cannot identify individual operators. Reasons must not include secrets or personal data. Audit is retained when an environment is deleted and a reused environment ID can therefore show older audit entries; requests and administrator grants are deleted with the environment. The audit sequence order is not commit order, and separate pages are not a consistent snapshot. Rejected requests and direct DB changes are not audited. Protect audit data and arrange retention independently. Environment policy settings before migration are not retroactively audited. The experimental JWT policy API, JWT grant delegation and dashboard are described in the [API README](../../api/rust-api/README.md) and [dashboard README](../../dashboard/swallow/README.md); actual production load and HA are unverified.

`TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --test discord_policy_admin -- --ignored` creates a fresh disposable PostgreSQL schema and login roles. It checks independent rights, tenant isolation, replay, stale and concurrent updates, audit rollback, a held policy lock and direct-update revision changes; it does not contact Discord or test multi-host HA.


### Local in-app PostgreSQL failover regression

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=in-app apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` creates a disposable loopback-only PostgreSQL 17 primary and synchronous physical standby, applies all current API and worker migrations, then starts two real in-app workers using a multi-host read-write connection string. It verifies both workers use the primary, forces the primary to stop, promotes the standby, checks the pending and leased job data survived, forces the lease to expire, and verifies the old lease token cannot renew, complete or fail the job. The workers reconnect and complete both jobs with two notifications and two in-app messages. The test cleanup now retains its temporary directory if either test PostgreSQL server cannot be stopped.

The test uses one machine, an administrative DB login, synchronous replication, a deliberately expired lease and a controlled promotion; it does not test partitions, fencing of an isolated former primary, asynchronous data loss, real client reconnect behavior or multi-host production HA. Run only with disposable test instances. The Docker failover script still uses Docker by default.


### Alert scheduler run timeout

`SWALLOW_ALERT_SCHEDULER_RUN_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` optionally bounds an entire fanout run, including its configuration read and all destination children. Set an integer from 1 to 86400; unset or `0` leaves the previous unlimited behavior. An invalid value prevents startup. On timeout the scheduler reports `delivery outcome unknown`, terminates the fanout's private process group on Unix, and follows `SWALLOW_ALERT_SCHEDULER_FAILURE_MODE`: `continue` waits the configured interval before the next run, while `exit` stops with an error for a supervisor. A timeout can happen after the provider has accepted an alert; it is not proof of non-delivery. Existing database reservation, lease recovery and unknown/rejected review remain in force, so investigate the destination's slot rather than assuming a resend is safe. Choose the limit longer than the expected aggregate run duration and configure target timeouts, regular-file checks and independent process monitoring as appropriate. A process stuck in the kernel or a descendant that escapes the private group can outlive termination; non-Unix descendants are not group-terminated. This option does not guarantee alert delivery or replace external monitoring.

`cargo test --locked --test alert_scheduler` exercises timeouts, continuation, failure exit, invalid settings, process-group cleanup and the actual fanout blocked reading a FIFO. With a fully migrated disposable PostgreSQL, `TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test alert_scheduler scheduler_timeout_recovers_uncertain_send_without_resending -- --ignored` runs the actual scheduler and fanout against a held local Slack proxy, times out after reservation, forces lease expiry and checks recovery to `unknown` without a second connection. The PostgreSQL 17 test used an administrative login on one host and a proxy that never completes TLS; real Slack receipt and production HA are unverified.


### Resend signed webhook and scoped projection after failover

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` builds and starts the real HTTP API and Resend projection worker on restricted, separate database logins before stopping a disposable synchronous PostgreSQL primary. The API has its environment-scoped webhook lock grant and webhook table permissions; the projector has scoped policy permissions and direct projection table rights. The test waits for API readiness and projection heartbeat, promotes the standby, then verifies the same API and projector processes reconnect. A synthetically signed HTTP callback with the captured dispatch tag resolves a pre-failover uncertain attempt as accepted and delivered in both job and message, without another outbound connection. Earlier in the same test, a pre-failover auto-correlation and late conflicting evidence are checked separately.

The initial simulated Resend request is made by a queue-function harness to a loopback mock, not an actual outbound worker. The callback is synthetic; neither Resend nor an external mailbox is contacted. The database pair is single-host, uses synchronous replication and controlled promotion, and the initial job lease is forcibly expired. The restricted projector has broad direct table rights, so this does not enforce tenant isolation against arbitrary SQL. Asynchronous replication loss, unfenced former primaries, real provider replay, multi-host orchestration and production HA remain unverified. The prior Docker scenario continues to test direct callback insertion unless these optional process binaries are provided.


### SMTP as an alert email destination

Operators can select SMTP for delivery or database alert email in addition to Resend and SendGrid. For direct `send-delivery-alert` or `send-database-alert`, set `SWALLOW_ALERT_EMAIL_PROVIDER=smtp`, `SWALLOW_ALERT_EMAIL_TO`, `SWALLOW_SMTP_FROM`, and `SWALLOW_SMTP_HOST`. `SWALLOW_SMTP_TLS=starttls` (default) requires STARTTLS and defaults to port 587; `SWALLOW_SMTP_TLS=tls` uses implicit TLS and defaults to port 465; the explicit unencrypted `none` option is described below. `SWALLOW_SMTP_PORT` overrides the port. SMTP AUTH is optional: when used, set both `SWALLOW_SMTP_USERNAME` and exactly one of `SWALLOW_SMTP_PASSWORD` or `SWALLOW_SMTP_PASSWORD_FILE`. Restrict the secret file and apply the existing regular-file secret policy when appropriate. The built-in WebPKI roots are used by default. Optionally set `SWALLOW_SMTP_CA_FILE` to a bounded PEM certificate file to add a private trust root; certificate and hostname validation remain enabled. The file must be a regular file (symlinks to regular files are allowed), is limited to 64 KiB, and is read at process startup. A missing or malformed file fails configuration; it is not automatically reloaded. Protect the file from modification and coordinate trust-root rotation across senders. Plaintext and opportunistic TLS are not offered. Certificate and hostname validation are required; a server that does not offer STARTTLS is rejected before authentication or message transmission. See the [lettre SMTP transport documentation](https://docs.rs/lettre/latest/lettre/transport/smtp/struct.AsyncSmtpTransport.html).

For `alert-fanout` and `alert-scheduler`, select SMTP per target, for example:

```json
{"channel":"email","id":"smtp-oncall","provider":"smtp","to":"ops@example.test","from":"alerts@example.test","smtp_host":"smtp.example.test","smtp_port":587,"smtp_tls":"starttls","smtp_username":"alert-user","smtp_password_file":"/secure/smtp-password"}
```

Omit both username and password file for an unauthenticated relay. SMTP targets reject API-key, region, sender-name and SendGrid IP-pool fields; SMTP-specific fields are rejected for the other email providers. Fanout removes inherited SMTP settings before applying each target. The existing per-target reservation, cooldown, review and optional binding still apply. With destination binding, the provider and recipient are bound but relay configuration is not; the optional configuration-binding profile also fingerprints the host, port, TLS mode, sender and credentials. Coordinate changes across senders and use a new target ID when changing the bound destination or configuration. SMTP has no provider idempotency key: any transport failure, timeout or uncertain response is conservatively recorded as `unknown`, including SMTP rejections, and is not automatically retried. Acceptance by the SMTP relay does not establish inbox delivery. Inspect the relay and alert slot before an operator chooses to retry. Experimental SMTP workflow delivery is described below; the alert provider and its settings remain independently configured.

Unit tests exercised a local SMTP protocol fixture, response loss, STARTTLS downgrade rejection and a successful authenticated STARTTLS exchange with a test-only trusted certificate. Fanout process tests cover target configuration, secret isolation and both alert modes. A fully migrated local PostgreSQL 17 test ran the actual fanout and delivery alert command against a mock server without STARTTLS and checked `unknown` plus no automatic second connection; its first run failed due to a nonblocking test socket and passed after correction. Actual SMTP relay delivery, customer CA deployments, multi-host HA and original Novu behavior are unverified.


For fanout, add `"smtp_ca_file":"/secure/smtp-ca.pem"` to an SMTP target. The path must be absolute; other email providers reject this field, and fanout clears inherited SMTP CA settings between targets. The configuration binding hashes the exact PEM bytes, so even formatting or certificate rotation changes the fingerprint when that profile is enabled; plan target IDs and audit/slot state before rollout. Supplying a CA extends rather than replaces the built-in roots. Restart direct alert processes after a CA file change; fanout children read it each invocation. A local test using the actual fanout, alert command, PostgreSQL and an authenticated STARTTLS server trusted via the configured PEM confirms acceptance and cooldown. Wrong-host and malformed-CA tests reject connections/configuration. The first DB test expected the wrong success exit code and passed after correction. No real external relay or production HA was tested.


### Experimental SMTP workflow delivery

Worker migrations `0212` and `0213` add an SMTP attempt table and permit the experimental `email_provider=smtp` workflow setting. The in-app workflow generator creates `experimental_smtp_email` jobs for that setting. Start `smtp-worker` with `DATABASE_URL`, `NOVU_SMTP_ENVIRONMENT_ID`, and the SMTP settings described above (`SWALLOW_SMTP_HOST`, `SWALLOW_SMTP_FROM`, optional TLS/port, optional username/password and optional CA file). The worker registers the existing `SWALLOW_EMAIL_WORKFLOW_STEP_POLICY=snapshot|active|unchanged` against the environment's shared email worker policy; mismatched current workers fail startup. `NOVU_SMTP_DB_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` is 1–300, default 30, and covers each operation including SMTP I/O. SMTP credentials and CA settings are read at startup by default; optional password and CA reload are described below.

The worker validates the supported message and writes one `dispatching` attempt before connecting to SMTP. An accepted SMTP response records `accepted` and completes the dispatch job; that is relay acceptance, not mailbox delivery. An error after reservation or a lease expiration after reservation becomes `unknown` and is never automatically resent. An expired lease with no recorded attempt is requeued. The workflow subscriber's internal identity and preferences are checked under lock before reservation, and `active`/`unchanged` step policies use the existing workflow checks. Changes after reservation cannot cancel an external request. Only one attempt per job is permitted. A currently unsupported message fails before reservation; do not reinterpret that as a provider rejection. The supported subset includes HTML, optional plain text, from/name, reply-to and to/cc/bcc; Bcc addresses are not included in MIME headers. Ordinary and inline attachments are supported as described below. Selected custom headers are supported as described below. Custom provider data and IP pools are rejected; additional MIME alternatives are supported as described below. Other Novu email features, multi-step ordering and full input compatibility are not implemented.

The management API and dashboard can select SMTP after API migration 0127; a trusted DBA must provision SMTP worker table permissions. Selecting SMTP does not configure or start a worker. The worker needs appropriate queue, workflow, subscriber, preference, message and outcome privileges, plus SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE on `novu_rust_smtp_attempts`; direct DB access is sensitive. SMTP unknown-attempt resolution is available via the separately provisioned CLI described below. The optional aggregate SMTP health check and alerts are described below; monitor worker liveness independently. Migration 0213 rejects new SendGrid jobs generated for an SMTP workflow by older generators, but cannot stop previously queued or in-flight work; drain old workers and old email jobs before switching a workflow, upgrade all generators and workers, and protect the trigger owner and schema. Privileged direct SQL and disabled triggers bypass the fence. Switching SMTP off does not cancel already generated SMTP jobs.

`TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test smtp_queue -- --ignored` uses a fully migrated disposable PostgreSQL, administrative DB account, actual SMTP worker, locally trusted STARTTLS fixture and a manually queued in-app workflow trigger. It verifies generated SMTP routing, relay acceptance, unsupported fields, subscriber ID reuse, an expired attempt left unknown and a simulated old generator rejected by the fence. The first workflow fixture lacked a required trigger field and passed after correction. SMTP server receipt was simulated; real provider delivery, Novu comparison, rejection/unknown operator recovery, production credentials, load and multi-host HA remain unverified. The separate API integration test covers provider management and API-triggered job generation.

### Experimental SMTP workflow health and alerts

Worker migration 0214 adds `public.novu_rust_smtp_health(environment)`. Grant the monitoring DB login `EXECUTE` on this function and its environment-specific `alert_view` grant; the function owner needs access to the SMTP attempts, queue and existing grant check. `delivery-reconcile check-smtp ENV` returns counts for unknown attempts, stranded dispatches (dispatching without a live running lease), and failed jobs without an attempt. It exits 0 when all counts are zero, 2 when any is nonzero, and 1 on an error. No message contents or recipient addresses are returned.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_SMTP_ENABLED=true` to include these counts in `send-delivery-alert` and its existing fanout/scheduler; it is disabled by default. Configure Slack and/or email destinations and a separate environment `alert_send` grant as for other alerts. With `SWALLOW_ALERT_COOLDOWN_POLICY=new-condition`, scoped delivery and `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2=true` are required. V2 condition bits 58–61 represent unknown attempts, stranded dispatches, pre-dispatch failures and check failures. A failed check does not clear the last observed condition. These alerts detect possible problems, not confirmed delivery; an SMTP acceptance does not prove mailbox delivery. Unreviewed failures continue to count; the SMTP review commands below can acknowledge an exact failure snapshot. There is no safe operator retry command. Polling, worker liveness and real alert reception need separate operational monitoring.

The `smtp_health` integration test creates a fresh disposable PostgreSQL schema and a restricted DB login, and checks environment isolation, permission revocation, CLI status and alert selection. The alert unit test checks both mock Slack and mock Resend payloads and v2 masks. These do not establish external SMTP or alert delivery, multi-host HA or Novu parity.

### SMTP workflow failure review

Worker migration 0215 adds `novu_rust_smtp_attention` and `novu_rust_ack_smtp`. Grant the operator database login EXECUTE on the functions and the existing environment-specific `alert_view` and `queue_ack` grants respectively. `delivery-reconcile list-smtp ENV [AFTER_JOB]` returns at most 100 aggregate-safe failure entries with a `next` cursor. For a failed job, use `delivery-reconcile ack-smtp ENV JOB ATTEMPT_REVISION|- FAILURE_REVISION OPERATOR REASON`, taking both revisions from the listing (`-` indicates no attempt). An active or stranded dispatch cannot be acknowledged. The snapshot is recorded with the database login and operator-supplied reason; duplicate acknowledgments return `recorded: false`. An acknowledgment suppresses that exact snapshot in SMTP health and alerts, but does not confirm delivery, change a job or message, or resend anything. A changed job or attempt becomes visible again; inspect external evidence before acknowledging. Direct privileged database access can bypass these controls.

`TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test smtp_health -- --ignored` on a fresh disposable PostgreSQL tests separate login permissions, environment isolation, concurrent duplicate review, stale revisions, audit-insert failure rollback, dispatch rejection, revocation and renewed attention, including CLI list and acknowledgment. Initial compilation required extending the lifetime of concurrent query arguments. The separate SMTP JWT API and browser fixtures exercise the dashboard and HTTP operator API. A live SMTP provider and multi-host HA remain unverified.

### SMTP workflow attachments

The experimental SMTP worker accepts up to 100 attachments with at most 20 MiB total raw bytes per message. It emits ordinary attachments in `multipart/mixed` and inline CID attachments within `multipart/related`, with plain text and HTML in `multipart/alternative` when text is provided. A CID defaults to inline; an explicit `inline` disposition requires a nonempty CID. Ordinary attachments must have no CID and may use `attachment` or an omitted disposition. Invalid MIME types, empty or unsafe filenames/CIDs, duplicate CIDs, unsupported disposition combinations and limits are rejected before a send attempt. Provider-specific disposition behavior is not supported; additional MIME alternatives are described below. Size limits are not a guarantee that the relay accepts the encoded message; account for MIME overhead and relay limits.

The SMTP worker integration fixture sends a manually queued workflow trigger through the in-app generator and actual SMTP worker to an authenticated STARTTLS mock. Python's MIME parser verifies exact ordinary and inline attachment bytes and CID. Unit tests cover limits and invalid metadata. Initial test code needed compilation fixes and a corrected assumption about transfer encoding. The separate HTTP fixture below covers API-to-mock-relay execution. External SMTP relay delivery, a full Novu comparison, and multi-host HA remain unverified.

### SMTP attachment retention

Worker migration 0216 includes completed workflow-generated SMTP jobs in the existing opt-in attachment retention commands and replay fence. Previously completed eligible SMTP jobs start their retention clock at the migration; the migration updates matching rows and can acquire locks, so assess the impact and deploy it before relying on cleanup. Parent and SMTP child copies have independent clocks. Failed, pending, running, cancelled, and standalone SMTP jobs remain excluded. The existing restricted retention grants, audit and limitations above apply. The `smtp_attachment_retention` integration test covers upgrading old completed rows, new jobs, environment and permission separation, audit failure rollback, revocation, exclusions and replay prevention on disposable PostgreSQL. This is not secure deletion of WAL, backups, replicas, or external copies, and production load and multi-host HA remain unverified.

`tests/test-smtp-attachment-http.sh` starts the real HTTP API, in-app worker and SMTP worker against a disposable PostgreSQL and authenticated STARTTLS fixture, posts Base64 and Buffer attachments, parses the MIME bytes, and verifies parent and child retention timestamps. It defaults to Docker PostgreSQL; `TEST_POSTGRES_MODE=local` uses a temporary local PostgreSQL and was used for this run. The API and in-app worker use a PostgreSQL administrator; the SMTP worker uses a separate limited table-privilege login. Optional scoped SMTP DB isolation is described below; external relay delivery, Novu behavior, and multi-host HA remain unverified.

### SMTP worker interruption after relay DATA

`TEST_DATABASE_URL=... cargo test --locked --test smtp_crash_recovery -- --ignored` exercises two actual SMTP worker processes, disposable PostgreSQL and a local STARTTLS fixture. The fixture receives the first message's DATA and withholds its final response; the test kills the worker, forces the lease to expire, and starts another worker. It verifies that the first attempt becomes `unknown` without another connection for that job, while a different pending job is accepted. This simulates an ambiguous handoff; the mock's receipt is not proof of real relay acceptance or final mailbox delivery. It uses a DB administrator, forced expiry and one local PostgreSQL instance; no database failover, asynchronous replication, split-brain fencing, restricted worker role, production load or multi-host HA is proven.

### SMTP synchronous database promotion fixture

Run `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=smtp scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` with local PostgreSQL 17 binaries. The disposable primary and standby use synchronous replication. The test runs two real SMTP workers and a STARTTLS fixture: after the first message DATA is received and its final reply withheld, it verifies the dispatch reservation on the standby, kills that worker, stops the primary, and promotes the standby. A worker already connected before the outage reconnects through a multi-host URL; after forced lease expiry it preserves the first attempt as unknown and sends a different job. The fixture checks for an additional connection over a short window. The run uses one host, administrator DB credentials, a mock relay and forced lease expiry. It does not establish that a real relay accepted or delivered the first message, nor validate asynchronous replication, split-brain fencing, production credentials, long outages, real multi-host networking or overall HA. Docker was unavailable for this run.

### SMTP workflow custom headers

The SMTP worker accepts up to 100 custom string headers from workflow email overrides, with names up to 76 ASCII bytes and values up to 4096 UTF-8 bytes; values are encoded by the MIME library. Control characters, invalid names, non-string values and case-insensitive duplicate names are rejected before dispatch. Addressing and message-structure fields remain controlled by the message builder: From, Sender, Reply-To, To, Cc, Bcc, Subject, Date, Message-ID, MIME-Version, Received, Return-Path, Delivered-To, Authentication-Results, DKIM-Signature, all `Content-*` and all `Resent-*` are rejected. This is a bounded subset, not full provider compatibility. Custom headers can be visible to recipients and downstream relays; do not place secrets in them.

The SMTP unit tests check duplicate and injection rejection. `TEST_POSTGRES_MODE=local tests/test-smtp-attachment-http.sh` exercised `X-Swallow-Test` and `List-Unsubscribe` through a real HTTP API, actual workers and mock authenticated STARTTLS server; the SMTP worker used a separate limited login while the API and in-app worker used an administrator. Real relay behavior, the original Novu server and production HA remain unverified.

### SMTP worker database privileges and isolation limits

The HTTP fixture provisions `smtp_fixture_worker` without superuser privileges and grants only the tables used by its tested workflow path. It verifies the worker selects only its configured environment in normal execution. Before dispatch, the test revokes INSERT on `novu_rust_smtp_attempts`; the worker fails before any SMTP connection. After restoring the permission and forcing lease expiry, the worker recovers and sends. It also proves that the same role can directly read another environment's queue row: these table privileges are **not row-level tenant isolation** and include sensitive data and mutation privileges across environments. Do not treat the fixture grants as a hardened production role or use an untrusted worker login in a shared multi-tenant database. Use independent database/network isolation or assess the optional scoped SMTP RLS mode described below before relying on this boundary. Policy branches, administrative role setup, production load, malicious SQL and HA remain unverified by this fixture.

### Optional SMTP worker environment isolation

Worker migration 0217 adds an **opt-in** PostgreSQL RLS mechanism for the SMTP worker's 11 shared tables. Applying the migration alone does not enable RLS. In a reviewed deployment window, a trusted table owner/DBA can call `SELECT public.novu_rust_install_smtp_scoped_rls();` once. The installer refuses any covered table that already has RLS or a policy and installs all policies transactionally; it can take table locks and add query overhead. Existing unregistered logins retain their prior row access. Do not use this installer over a separate RLS implementation.

For a dedicated non-owner LOGIN, bootstrap the login's scoped registration and needed table privileges before starting it. Migration 0220 also provides the delegated CLI path below. For example, substituting values as SQL parameters or safely quoted identifiers:

```sql
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_smtp_scoped_logins(principal_oid,database_principal,environment_id)
SELECT oid,rolname,'ENVIRONMENT_ID' FROM pg_catalog.pg_roles WHERE rolname='SMTP_WORKER_LOGIN';
```

Set `SWALLOW_SMTP_SCOPED_RLS=true` on that worker. With worker migration 0218, on every DB connection the worker verifies the registration, configured environment, absence of superuser/BYPASSRLS privileges or membership in such roles, non-ownership and presence of the policies before processing. The RLS condition is based on `session_user`, including when a worker changes current role. The login must not own the tables or inherit ownership, hold superuser/BYPASSRLS, or receive extra privileged SECURITY DEFINER functions or roles that bypass the policies. Use a dedicated login per environment and protect the registration table, policies, function owners and role administration. The feature does not grant table privileges or configure DB TLS; the fixture shows one set of grants for its tested snapshot-policy path, not a complete production privilege prescription.

To revoke, set `enabled=false` on the registered row; the RLS condition then denies that login all covered rows and the startup guard fails. Normal deletion or identity changes are blocked by a trigger to avoid accidentally restoring the unregistered, unrestricted path. A DBA who bypasses triggers, deletes the registration, changes policies, grants privileged roles, or alters objects can bypass these controls. Coordinate revocation and in-flight transactions; row policies cannot recall an SMTP request already sent. Unregistered logins remain unrestricted by this feature, and other services and tables are not isolated by it. The worker can still read and modify authorized rows within its own environment, so grant only trusted workers the required rights. This does not establish whole-platform tenant isolation.

`TEST_SMTP_RLS=true TEST_POSTGRES_MODE=local tests/test-smtp-attachment-http.sh` exercises an actual SMTP worker, limited login, HTTP API and local PostgreSQL 17: correct-scope delivery; wrong-scope startup rejection; cross-environment SELECT, UPDATE, INSERT and row movement; `SET ROLE`; disabled registration; protected registration identity; existing-policy install rejection; and a separate unregistered login retaining existing behavior. The script also verifies revoked attempt permission stops relay connection and recovers after restoration. The API and in-app worker are administrators in this fixture. The same fixture also passed with `TEST_EMAIL_POLICY=active`, exercising the active-workflow happy path and its workflow row lock; active-policy cancellations and unchanged mode were not covered in that scoped fixture. Existing production policies, malicious privileged functions, concurrency during rollout, external SMTP and multi-host HA remain unverified. Without `TEST_SMTP_RLS=true` the fixture confirms the earlier unrestricted behavior.

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=smtp-rls scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` repeats the synchronous promotion test with a non-superuser SMTP worker, scoped policies and a second environment. It verifies the worker cannot read the other environment before or after promotion, its scoped guard succeeds after promotion, the old uncertain attempt stays unknown and the pre-existing worker reconnects to process a different job. Schema setup and assertions use an administrator; SMTP jobs in this scenario are standalone, and the local mock relay, forced lease expiry, one host and short observation window do not prove real delivery, asynchronous replication, split-brain fencing or production HA.

Worker migration 0218 strengthens the scoped startup guard to reject membership in a superuser or BYPASSRLS role, including indirect membership. Without 0218, a login that is itself non-superuser/non-BYPASSRLS may still be able to `SET ROLE` to a bypass role. Upgrade the migration before relying on this check. Role memberships are checked at connection time, not continuously: do not grant privileged roles to a running scoped login; stop and drain affected workers and review sessions before changing membership. The local HTTP fixture tests a real worker being refused before connecting to the mock SMTP relay with a BYPASSRLS membership, then successfully starting after revocation. The scoped synchronous promotion regression also passed; external relay and production HA remain unverified.

### SMTP scope registration audit

Worker migration 0219 adds an audit record in the same transaction for each new scoped-login registration or actual change of its environment or enabled state. If the audit insert fails, the registration change rolls back. An unchanged update does not create a record. Existing registrations from before this migration are not backfilled; their historical changes cannot be reconstructed. Audit records contain the database session login and role OIDs, not an individual identity behind a shared login, and do not capture a change reason. The audit table and its owner must be protected; DB owners, superusers, trigger disabling and privileged direct SQL can bypass or alter records. No tamper-evidence or external export is provided.

A DBA can separately authorize a LOGIN to read each environment's audit records:

```sql
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_smtp_scope_audit_viewers(environment_id,database_principal,principal_oid)
SELECT 'ENVIRONMENT_ID',rolname,oid FROM pg_catalog.pg_roles WHERE rolname='AUDIT_VIEWER_LOGIN';
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_smtp_scope_audit_read(text,bigint) TO AUDIT_VIEWER_LOGIN;
```

With that login, run `delivery-reconcile smtp-scope-audit ENV [AFTER_ID]`; the cursor is a nonnegative decimal audit ID and the query returns up to 100 records in ID order. A change between environments is visible to authorized viewers of either side, but the other environment identifier is redacted. ID order is not a guaranteed commit order; concurrent transactions can commit out of order, so poll with overlap or use an independent audit export when complete collection matters. Registration audit does not cover role grants, RLS installation, policy changes or attempts by unauthorized users. Viewer grants themselves require trusted DBA administration.

The local SMTP HTTP fixture verifies registration and disable audit, no-op updates, audit-write rollback, environment redaction, viewer separation and revocation, plus the CLI cursor. The synchronous promotion regression also passed with a scoped worker. Actual external auditing, role-administration audit, HTTP/UI access to these direct-DB audit records, production concurrency and multi-host HA are not implemented or verified. The separate JWT-path audit is available through API migration 0130.

### SMTP scope administration

Worker migration 0220 provides optional environment-scoped database-login administration of SMTP scope registrations. A trusted DBA installs the RLS policies first, provisions the worker's database privileges separately, and grants a dedicated administrator read and/or write access for the environment. Grant only the functions that login needs. For example:

```sql
INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_smtp_scope_admins(environment_id,database_principal,principal_oid,can_read,can_write)
SELECT 'ENVIRONMENT_ID',rolname,oid,true,true FROM pg_catalog.pg_roles WHERE rolname='SCOPE_ADMIN_LOGIN';
GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.novu_rust_smtp_scope_admin_read(text,text),
 public.novu_rust_smtp_scope_admin_set(text,uuid,text,text,boolean,text),
 public.novu_rust_smtp_scope_admin_request(text,uuid) TO SCOPE_ADMIN_LOGIN;
```

With that login, `delivery-reconcile smtp-scope-read ENV LOGIN` reports `absent`, `enabled` or `disabled` for an available target; `smtp-scope-set ENV REQUEST_ID LOGIN absent|enabled|disabled enabled|disabled REASON` creates or changes the registration conditionally; `smtp-scope-request ENV REQUEST_ID` recovers the caller's recorded request and reason. Replaying the same request ID and body returns its recorded result, while a different body or stale state fails. The function refuses self-change and an already registered login in another environment. Both enable and disable require the installed policies; enabling additionally rejects a target with privileged-role membership or ownership of a covered table. It does not grant or revoke the target's SQL privileges, verify every callable SECURITY DEFINER function, or install RLS. Scope changes still trigger the separate registration audit described above; request records retain the reason but are not linked to that audit by a shared ID. DBAs can bypass this path and administrator grants are themselves DBA-managed.

Stop and drain workers before changing their privileges, role memberships or scope. An already reserved external request cannot be recalled; authorization and role checks occur at operation/connection time and cannot prevent a trusted DBA changing them later. Existing registrations are not assigned historical request records. Request lookup requires the same DB LOGIN and current write grant; lost responses should be reconciled before issuing a different request. API migration 0130 and the Settings screen provide a JWT-authorized HTTP management path; see the API README. JWT grants require separate DBA provisioning. External IdP, production load and multi-host HA are unverified.

The local SMTP HTTP fixture tests restricted admin permissions, install prerequisites, self/cross-environment denial, privileged targets, concurrent identical request replay, conflicting bodies, stale state, audit-failure rollback, request lookup, revocation and real SMTP workflow recovery. This uses one local PostgreSQL instance and a mock relay. The separate scoped worker promotion regression ran with migration 0220 applied; it does not exercise the admin operation across failover.

### SMTP unknown outcome resolution (migration 0221)

A trusted DBA provisions an environment-specific row in `novu_rust_smtp_resolvers` using the operator login name and its current `pg_roles.oid`, setting `can_accept` and `can_retry` independently, and grants that login `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_resolve_smtp(text,uuid,uuid,uuid,bigint,text,text)`. No operator is granted access by default. The function locks the permission row until the transaction finishes; revocation prevents subsequent calls. The DBA retains control of grants. The protected `novu_rust_smtp_resolutions` audit table is accessible to provisioned viewers through the CLI described below; the API and dashboard can also display DB-level decision history with separately provisioned permissions; see the API README.

Use `delivery-reconcile list-smtp ENV` to obtain the job ID, attempt revision and failure revision, then `delivery-reconcile resolve-smtp ENV REQUEST_ID JOB ATTEMPT_REVISION FAILURE_REVISION accepted|retry REASON` with a fresh UUID request ID. `accepted` records the operator's evidence that the relay accepted the message, updates the attempt, message and job, and does **not** assert final mailbox delivery. `retry` records the operator's evidence that the relay did not accept it, removes the old attempt and requeues the job for a worker; it can cause a duplicate if that evidence is wrong. A repeated identical request by the same authorized login returns `recorded: false`, while changed request contents or a stale job/attempt are rejected. The audit insert and state changes commit together. A previously acknowledged failure is hidden from `list-smtp`; use the job-specific `get-smtp` lookup described below to recover its current revisions. Direct privileged SQL can bypass these safeguards.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-resolution-local.sh` uses a fresh local PostgreSQL and a restricted login to verify permission separation, environment isolation, stale revisions, audit failure rollback, concurrent identical calls from two connections, CLI retry, request conflicts and revocation. The same script also runs two real SMTP worker processes against a local STARTTLS fixture: it kills the first after DATA, checks that no automatic resend occurs, selects retry using a restricted operator login and verifies the next worker sends again, creates a new attempt and updates the message, job and audit. The fixture deliberately receives the first DATA, so this demonstrates the duplicate risk when retry is chosen without reliable external evidence. It uses forced lease expiry and a local database; it does not test a real relay, final mailbox delivery, an HTTP API, dashboard, external identity provider or multi-host HA.

### SMTP resolution history (migration 0222)

A trusted DBA independently provisions an environment-specific `novu_rust_smtp_resolution_audit_viewers` row with the viewer login and its current role OID and grants `EXECUTE` on `public.novu_rust_smtp_resolution_history(text,uuid,oid,uuid)`. A resolver is not automatically a viewer. `delivery-reconcile smtp-resolution-history ENV JOB` returns up to 100 records in descending time and identity order; use the returned `next.actorOid` and `next.requestId` as the two optional cursor arguments for the next page. Results include the decision, reason, actor and original failure revisions, but no message payload or recipient. The cursor is bound to the same environment and job; revoking viewer access blocks subsequent reads. Records committed concurrently may appear ahead of an already-read cursor, so refresh from the first page or use an independent audit export for complete ongoing collection. Database owners can alter or delete audit records; this is not a tamper-proof external log.

The local test script checks denied direct table access, independent viewer authorization, environment isolation, 103 records across two pages with tied timestamps, CLI cursor and permission revocation. It also reruns the SMTP resolution and real-worker mock-relay scenarios. HTTP/UI, an external identity provider and production HA remain unverified.

### Lookup of acknowledged SMTP failures (migration 0223)

Grant the operator DB login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_failure_lookup(text,uuid)` and the existing environment-specific `alert_view` permission. `delivery-reconcile get-smtp ENV JOB` returns the current failed SMTP snapshot, including whether that exact snapshot was acknowledged, even if it is hidden from `list-smtp`. It returns `found: false` for a missing, completed, running or otherwise ineligible job. No recipient or payload is returned; a subsequent decision still requires independent permission and the displayed revisions may become stale. This lookup does not alter the job, review or alert state.

The local SMTP resolution test script also runs the restricted-login SMTP health and CLI test against disposable PostgreSQL, including a previously acknowledged snapshot and cross-environment denial. Real relay delivery and production HA are not tested.

### SMTP operator retry across synchronous promotion

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=smtp-resolution scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` creates a disposable local PostgreSQL primary and synchronous standby. An actual SMTP worker sends DATA to a local STARTTLS fixture and is killed before receiving the final reply; the test forces lease expiry and recovers the unknown attempt. A restricted operator DB login chooses retry through the CLI. After the retry audit and pending job are visible on the standby, the test stops the primary, promotes the standby and starts an SMTP worker through the multi-host connection string. It verifies a new accepted attempt, job and message state, retained retry audit and harmless replay of the same request after promotion. The fixture observes both DATA transfers, demonstrating that retry after an ambiguous first handoff may duplicate delivery.

This is a single-host, synchronous replication test with forced expiry and a mock relay. Worker database access uses an administrator, the operator uses a restricted login, and no running worker reconnects across the outage in this particular scenario. It does not prove actual relay acceptance or mailbox delivery, asynchronous replication, split-brain fencing, external IdP, production credentials, multi-host networking or full production HA.

### Retrying pre-dispatch SMTP failures (worker 0224, API 0138)

The existing SMTP `retry` decision can also requeue a failed job with no current SMTP attempt. Use `delivery-reconcile get-smtp ENV JOB` and, if `attemptRevision` is null, `delivery-reconcile resolve-smtp ENV REQUEST JOB - FAILURE_REVISION retry REASON`. The function locks the failed job, verifies its failure revision and that no attempt exists, then records the decision and requeues atomically. `accepted` still requires an unknown attempt. A concurrent or newly created attempt, changed failure revision or conflicting request is rejected; an identical request is replay-safe. The same API and dashboard path accepts a null attempt revision for retry, records the JWT actor, and displays it in decision history.

**This extends the authority of existing SMTP retry grants to pre-dispatch failures.** Review existing JWT and DB `can_retry` grants before applying worker migration 0224 and API migration 0138. Apply the worker migration before the API migration, then update API and UI clients together; older clients may not understand null attempt revisions in history. A job without a current attempt is not proof that no earlier attempt or external delivery ever occurred: a prior operator retry removes that attempt, and other services may have sent independently. Investigate the failure, correct its cause and consider prior delivery evidence before requeueing. The retry remains an operator choice; the system does not automatically repair or resend the job.

The local resolution script tests restricted CLI operation and real SMTP worker delivery to a mock STARTTLS relay after a pre-dispatch failure. API tests cover stale revisions, rejecting a null revision when an attempt exists, JWT attribution, request replay and history. The dashboard browser test verifies selection and request lookup against the real API. Actual external relay delivery, original Novu behavior and production HA remain unverified.

### Discord operator resolution (migration 0225)

A trusted DBA provisions an environment-specific row in `novu_rust_discord_resolvers` with the operator DB login and its current role OID, setting `can_accept` and `can_retry` independently, and grants that login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_resolve_discord(text,uuid,uuid,uuid,bigint,text,text)`. No grant is created by default. `delivery-reconcile list-discord ENV` provides failure and attempt revisions for unacknowledged failures. Use `delivery-reconcile resolve-discord ENV REQUEST_ID JOB ATTEMPT_REVISION|- FAILURE_REVISION accepted|retry REASON`. `accepted` is allowed only for an unknown attempt and records an operator-determined acceptance without inventing a Discord message ID. `retry` accepts an unknown or rejected attempt or a failed job without a current attempt, records the previous state, HTTP status and retry-after guidance, deletes the current attempt if present and immediately requeues the job. The `retry` choice does not wait for 429 guidance; the `retry_after` alternative is described below. The operator must account for rate limits and other senders. It can duplicate a message if independent evidence is wrong. No current attempt does not prove no earlier delivery, since past retries can remove attempts.

A request is bound to the actor, environment, observed revisions, decision and reason. Exact replays return `recorded: false`; stale state or changed request contents fail. An active attempt cannot be resolved. Audit insertion and state updates commit together; grant revocation blocks later calls. Direct privileged SQL can bypass controls. Acknowledged failures are not returned by the attention list; `get-discord` can retrieve a current failed snapshot with the existing alert-view permission after worker migration 0228. The decision audit table has a separately authorized job-specific viewer after worker migration 0227; The JWT API and dashboard support decisions after API migration 0139 and grant delegation after 0141; a separately authorized decision history viewer is available after worker 0227 and API 0142.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-discord-resolution-local.sh` uses a restricted DB login and disposable local PostgreSQL, exercising permission and environment isolation, stale revision, concurrent replay, audit failure rollback, rejection of an accepted decision for a rejected attempt, and retry of rejected or pre-dispatch failures. It runs the actual Discord queue processor with a local mock HTTP endpoint and checks the 429 evidence retained in audit. No real Discord, full workflow delivery, real IdP, Novu comparison or production HA is verified.

### Discord operator retry after 429 guidance (worker 0226, API 0140)

For a failed rejected 429 attempt with a valid recorded retry-after value, an authorized operator can choose `retry_after` in `resolve-discord`, the operator API or the dashboard. The locked attempt revision must match; the recorded value must be between 0 and 86400 seconds. The queue becomes available no earlier than the recorded attempt update time plus that duration, or immediately if that time has already elapsed. The audit retains the original seconds and calculated `retry_not_before`; a repeated identical request is replay-safe. Missing guidance, non-429 or changed attempts are rejected. The existing `retry` choice remains immediate.

Existing `can_retry` grants now authorize both choices; review those grants before applying worker migration 0226 and API migration 0140. Apply worker 0226 before API 0140. This is not a global Discord rate limiter: time calculations use the PostgreSQL clock, and other destinations, environments, processes and earlier requests can affect the same provider limit. Queue delay is not a guarantee of provider acceptance. The test uses a restricted DB role, local PostgreSQL and mock endpoint to verify the locked snapshot, missing guidance rejection, audit and inability to claim before the scheduled time. API and browser tests verify selection, JWT authorization and request recovery. Actual Discord 429, production load and multi-host HA are not verified.

### Discord decision history (worker 0227, API 0142)

A trusted DBA provisions the environment and database login in `novu_rust_discord_resolution_audit_viewers` with its current role OID and grants EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_discord_resolution_history(text,uuid,oid,uuid)`. `delivery-reconcile discord-resolution-history ENV JOB [BEFORE_ACTOR_OID BEFORE_REQUEST_ID]` returns up to 100 records, with a `next` cursor for older records. The records include previous attempt state, HTTP status, stored retry guidance, scheduled time, reason and DB actor. Neither the payload nor webhook credential is returned. Authorization is independent of decision permissions. The API adds independently granted JWT history access and new-decision attribution as described in the API README.

The local test script checks restricted login, cross-environment access denial, 103 entries with tied timestamps, CLI pagination and revocation. UUID/time ordering is not commit ordering: concurrent commits may appear ahead of an already-read cursor, so refresh or use a separate audit export. Direct privileged changes can bypass or alter these records; they are not a tamper-proof log. Real Discord and production HA remain unverified.

### Lookup of acknowledged Discord failures (worker 0228, API 0143)

Grant the operator DB login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_discord_failure_lookup(text,uuid)` and the existing environment-specific `alert_view` permission. `delivery-reconcile get-discord ENV JOB` returns an eligible current failed snapshot, including whether that exact state was acknowledged and any recorded 429 guidance. Missing, running, completed and ineligible jobs return `found: false`. No payload, subscriber or webhook secret is returned. The query does not change job, review or alert state. Revisions can become stale and decision permissions remain separate.

The local worker script verifies the CLI after acknowledgment and cross-environment denial using a restricted login and disposable PostgreSQL. The API and browser tests cover lookup, permission revocation and a guided retry. Actual Discord, external IdP and production HA remain unverified.

### Monitoring operator-scheduled Discord 429 retries (worker 0229)

The existing Discord retry health check, optional alert and investigation list also include pending jobs with an operator retry or guided retry from a rejected 429 attempt. Automatic and operator 429 retry records are combined for the displayed retry count and latest delay; this does **not** add rows to the automatic retry history or consume the automatic policy's retry budget. Non-429 manual retries are outside this particular diagnostic. Existing alert thresholds can therefore observe additional pending or overdue jobs after migration. A previously retried job later returned to pending for another reason can still be counted, as with the existing automatic-history diagnostic; this is a backlog indicator, not proof of a stopped worker or delivery failure.

The most recent operator schedule stores a validated destination ID and actual chosen queue delay. Older decisions without a recorded destination are displayed as `unknown`; existing decision audit data remains intact. Immediate operator retries can produce `lastDelaySeconds: 0`, so update consumers that assume a positive value; the dashboard accepts zero. Event time order is not guaranteed commit order. Counts and list pages are separate snapshots, direct privileged writes can bypass evidence, and history indexing adds database write/storage and query cost. Apply migration 0229 before relying on this expanded diagnostic, and review existing alert thresholds and function-owner permissions for the resolution table.

The restricted-login local test creates an operator-guided 429 retry, verifies it appears in the count and list, confirms no automatic retry-history row was consumed, and simulates an overdue job to exercise the existing alert path. The browser fixture verifies a real API/dashboard sees an operator retry with fixture JWT and disposable PostgreSQL. No actual Discord or alert receipt, large-scale query load or production HA is proven.

### Discord operator-scheduled 429 retry across synchronous promotion

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=discord-manual-retry scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` uses a disposable local PostgreSQL primary and synchronous standby. A mock Discord 429 is processed with automatic retry disabled; a restricted operator login schedules `retry_after`, and the test verifies the pending job and decision audit on the standby before stopping the primary and promoting the standby. After promotion, the restricted login sees the pending retry in health, two actual Discord workers do not connect to the local proxy before the scheduled time, and the exact operator request remains replay-safe. The test then forces the job due; the proxy drops the connection, so one worker records an unknown outcome and neither worker automatically repeats it during the short observation window. An independent unknown job remains unresolved.

The scenario uses one host, synchronous replication, a mock 429, a deliberately failing proxy, forced schedule advancement and administrator worker DB credentials. Workers are started after promotion in this test. It does not prove real Discord delivery, network partitions, split-brain fencing, asynchronous replication, external IdP or production multi-host HA. Docker was not used.

The local `discord_resolution` test also verifies the guided retry is not claimable before its scheduled time. It then forces the fixture job due, races two queue consumers on separate database connections against a local mock Discord endpoint, and checks one request, a new accepted attempt and the retained operator decision. This uses the queue processing function, an administrator for the queue consumers, a forced time change and a 500 ms duplicate-observation window; it does not prove real Discord delivery, production process behavior or multi-host HA.

### Optional shared Discord destination cooldown (worker 0230)

A trusted DBA may enable a PostgreSQL-backed cooldown for an individual `(environment_id, destination_id)` by inserting a row into `public.novu_rust_discord_shared_cooldown`; no row means the existing behavior. After a current worker records an explicit Discord HTTP 429 with valid guidance, it extends the row's `not_before` using the maximum of the existing deadline and the guidance (rounded up to whole seconds and at least one second). This applies even when the rejected job's own automatic retry policy is off or exhausted. Current workers lock the row before reserving another dispatch and defer a queued job until that deadline without recording a send attempt or consuming a queue attempt. The job's own retry policy and manual decisions still determine whether that particular rejected job is retried.

Apply migration 0230 before deploying this worker, including when cooldown is disabled. The worker database role needs `SELECT, UPDATE` on the new table as well as its existing privileges; the trigger's security-definer owner needs access to lock the cooldown row and read the queue. Stop and drain old workers and in-flight requests before enabling a row, then deploy compatible workers and have a trusted DBA insert, for example, `INSERT INTO public.novu_rust_discord_shared_cooldown(environment_id,destination_id) VALUES ('ENVIRONMENT','DESTINATION');`. The trigger refuses old workers' new dispatch reservations while a row exists, even after the deadline. Before removing a row, stop and drain workers again. Removing a row does not advance jobs already deferred to a later `available_at`; the DBA must make an explicit operational decision if those jobs should become eligible sooner. Protect direct table privileges: the fence is not protection against a privileged or deliberately marker-spoofing client. Worker migration 0231 adds a scoped, audited CLI alternative described below; API migration 0145 adds a JWT management API and the dashboard can use it.

This is not a global Discord limiter. Already reserved or in-flight requests can still reach the provider after a 429; a 429 is only shared after its result transaction commits. It only coordinates workers using the same database row, not other environments, destination IDs, installations, external senders, Discord global limits, or different endpoints that share a provider bucket. Missing or invalid guidance does not set a cooldown; a proxy 429 is not proof of Discord state. The database clock determines the deadline. A loss of the result transaction or an expired lease can leave an unknown outcome without a shared cooldown. HA depends on the deployment's replication and fencing guarantees.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-discord-cooldown-local.sh` uses disposable local PostgreSQL and mock HTTP, exercising opt-in, explicit 429 guidance, concurrent consumers, legacy reservation rejection, unrelated destinations, expiry, existing retry policy and a restricted queue role. The tests use a single host and do not verify real Discord, shared buckets, real network partitions or production HA; Docker is not used.

### Discord cooldown administration (worker 0231)

Apply worker migration 0231 after 0230. A trusted DBA assigns each login and its current `pg_roles.oid` a row in `novu_rust_discord_cooldown_admins` for one registered environment and destination. `can_read`, `can_write`, and `can_audit` are independent and default to false; existing Discord retry-policy grants do not grant these permissions. Grant EXECUTE only on the desired `novu_rust_discord_cooldown_read(text,text)`, `novu_rust_discord_cooldown_set(text,text,uuid,bigint,boolean,text)`, `novu_rust_discord_cooldown_request(text,text,uuid)`, and `novu_rust_discord_cooldown_audit_read(text,text,bigint)` functions.
