No access is granted by default. The security-definer owner needs access to the relevant tables and the audit sequence; protect that owner and direct table access.

Use `delivery-reconcile discord-cooldown-read ENV DEST` to obtain `enabled`, the revision and the current `notBefore` if known. Use `discord-cooldown-set ENV DEST REQUEST_ID EXPECTED enabled|disabled REASON` to change it. The request UUID is scoped to the database principal and destination: retrying identical arguments returns the original result, while reusing it with different arguments fails. `discord-cooldown-request ENV DEST REQUEST_ID` retrieves a committed request after a lost response; `discord-cooldown-audit ENV DEST [BEFORE]` pages through changes. A stale revision is rejected. Changes, request results and audit rows commit together. An unchanged setting is audited but does not increase the revision. Existing rows installed before 0231 are readable as enabled at revision 0.

These commands do not drain workers or enforce quiescence: operators must stop and drain all affected workers and in-flight requests before enabling or disabling the option, as described above. Disabling does not undo queue delays. Direct privileged table changes bypass revisions and audit; audit is not tamper-proof, and reused environment/destination IDs may expose retained historical entries. The DB login is recorded, not an individual dashboard user. API migration 0145 adds HTTP and individual JWT attribution; multi-host rollout coordination is not yet implemented.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-discord-cooldown-local.sh` additionally exercises the CLI with a restricted local DB login: environment and destination scope, direct-table denial, request replay/conflict, concurrent stale revisions, permission revocation, and rollback when audit insertion fails. The full-migration Discord resolution/health regression uses `scripts/test-discord-resolution-local.sh`. These are disposable single-host PostgreSQL tests, not an external identity-provider, real Discord, or production HA test. Docker is not used.

### Discord shared cooldown diagnostics (worker 0232)

Apply migration 0232, then deploy the worker version that marks jobs it actually defers with `last_error='Discord shared cooldown active'`. An authorized monitoring login with the environment-specific `alert_view` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_discord_cooldown_health(text,integer)` can run `delivery-reconcile check-discord-cooldown ENV GRACE_SECONDS` (1–86400). It prints aggregate `activeDestinations`, `deferred` and `overdue` counts and exits 2 when any count is nonzero, 0 when all are zero. Query or authorization failures produce a nonzero error rather than a healthy result. No destination, subscriber or message is returned. Grant function EXECUTE explicitly; no access is granted by default, and protect the security-definer owner.

`activeDestinations` counts enabled destination rows whose deadline is in the future, including those without jobs. `deferred` counts currently pending Discord jobs marked when a compatible worker moved their eligibility to a cooldown deadline; `overdue` is the subset still pending beyond that eligibility plus the chosen grace period. The marker replaces the previous `last_error`; separate automatic retry and operator-decision histories remain. Jobs not yet claimed by a worker, jobs deferred by older worker versions, completed jobs and jobs subsequently changed by other operations are not reliably represented. A marker can remain after cooldown is disabled, until the job is processed or its state changes. A job can also appear in existing retry diagnostics; do not add the two totals as distinct job counts. Counts are a point-in-time backlog signal, not proof of delivery failure or a stopped worker. Query cost and index maintenance should be sized for production. API migration 0146 and the dashboard can display this diagnostic with separately provisioned permissions. An external scheduler may use the command; the optional built-in Slack/email alert integration is described below.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-discord-cooldown-local.sh` verifies the deferral marker with mock Discord responses. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-discord-resolution-local.sh` applies all worker migrations and checks the diagnostic with a restricted DB role, environment isolation, overdue threshold, CLI exit status and revocation. These run against disposable local PostgreSQL on one host, without Docker, real Discord, real alert receipt or production HA.

### Optional Discord shared cooldown alerts

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_DISCORD_COOLDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` to 1–86400 on `send-delivery-alert` to include the 0232 environment-scoped diagnostic; leave it unset to disable this check. The alert sender DB login needs the environment `alert_view` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_discord_cooldown_health(text,integer)`, along with the configured destination's existing alert-send permissions. `SWALLOW_ALERT_DISCORD_COOLDOWN_MODE=all` (default) treats any active destination, deferred job or overdue job as an alert condition; the grace period only changes the overdue count. Set the mode to `overdue` to alert only when a deferred job has passed its eligibility time plus grace. Invalid modes fail startup. A check failure is reported in either mode. The payload includes the selected mode and aggregate counts, so evaluate expected noise before enabling `all`. Use the same mode across senders sharing a target, and coordinate mode changes because v2 condition history uses one shared bit for either mode. An unavailable/unauthorized check is reported as a check failure, not healthy. Only aggregate counts enter Slack and email payloads.

When using `new-condition` cooldown policy, enable scoped delivery and v2 conditions as with the other extended checks. V2 uses bit 62 for active/deferred/overdue and bit 63 for a check failure. The latter is the sign bit of the PostgreSQL `bigint`, so stored masks can be negative; integrations inspecting masks must use signed 64-bit bit operations, not JavaScript number precision or positivity as a health test. These consume the last two v2 bits; worker migration 0234 adds the v3 extension described below. A failed check does not mark the active condition as observed healthy. Roll out compatible senders consistently for each target; older senders do not evaluate this condition.

`cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --lib discord_cooldown_alert_uses_distinct_bits_and_reaches_both_destinations` verifies condition masks and mock Slack/Resend payloads. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-discord-resolution-local.sh` verifies the actual alert executable's health selection against a restricted DB login and blocked destination, plus PostgreSQL signed-bit reservation and recovery. These tests use mock HTTP or deliberately blocked sends; actual Slack/email receipt, a real IdP and production HA remain unverified.

The `discord_cooldown_health` local integration test also runs the real alert executable with `overdue` mode: an active cooldown with only future jobs is healthy, while a job past the grace period triggers alert handling. Payload delivery tests use local mock endpoints.

### Discord shared cooldown across synchronous promotion

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=discord-cooldown scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` creates a disposable PostgreSQL primary and synchronous standby on one host. The queue processor receives a mock 429 and commits its rejection and shared deadline; the test confirms that deadline, rejection and a second pending job on the standby before stopping the primary and promoting the standby. Two actual Discord workers start after promotion and connect to the promoted DB. They defer the second job without incrementing its attempt count or connecting to the proxy, and a restricted monitoring login reads the active/deferred counts. After the test forcibly expires the deadline and makes the job due, the proxy drops the connection; the workers record one unknown outcome and do not repeat the send during the two-second observation window.

This is a controlled local synchronous-replication test with workers started after promotion, administrator DB credentials for workers, a mock 429, a deliberately failing proxy and artificial time advancement. It does not demonstrate real Discord delivery, asynchronous replication durability, active requests during failover, network partitions, split-brain fencing, real monitoring or multi-host production HA. Docker was not used.

### Discord in-flight dispatch during synchronous promotion

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=discord-inflight scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` runs a queue processor against a local mock Discord endpoint that accepts a request and holds its HTTP 200 response. Once the standby shows the `dispatching` attempt and running job, the test immediately stops the primary and promotes the synchronous standby, then releases the mock response. The original processor cannot commit to the stopped DB. After forcing the lease to expire, two actual Discord workers start and connect to the promoted DB; they recover the first job as unknown without connecting to the proxy. A second job connects once to a deliberately failing proxy and becomes unknown; neither job is automatically repeated during the two-second observation window.

The first dispatch uses the queue-processing function in the test process, not a separately supervised worker. Workers start only after promotion and use administrator DB credentials; the lease is forcibly expired and the endpoint and proxy are local mocks. Synchronous replication and one host are used. This does not establish whether Discord actually accepted or delivered the first message, nor prove behavior under asynchronous replication, a surviving old primary, split brain, real network partitions, full workflows, or production multi-host HA. No Docker was used.

### Optional SMTP worker password rotation

The SMTP delivery worker can re-read a mounted password before claiming each new job: set `NOVU_SMTP_RELOAD_PASSWORD=true`, `SWALLOW_SMTP_USERNAME` and `SWALLOW_SMTP_PASSWORD_FILE`. Inline `SWALLOW_SMTP_PASSWORD` conflicts with this option and startup rejects missing or invalid credentials. The default is disabled, preserving startup-only loading. The reload file must resolve to a regular file (symlinks are allowed), be bounded UTF-8 and contain one nonempty line; the SMTP password itself is limited to 4096 bytes. Atomic replacement is recommended. The worker retains the original host, port, username, sender, TLS mode and startup CA; CA certificates can be independently reloaded as described below; these options do not change direct alert/fanout SMTP processes. Protect the file and its parent directory.

After startup, an unreadable or invalid file prevents new job claims. `NOVU_SMTP_RELOAD_FAILURE_POLICY=pause` (default) keeps the worker alive, continues expired-job recovery, and retries the file; `exit` closes the DB driver and exits nonzero for an external supervisor. `exit` requires reload to be enabled. In-flight messages continue with their already constructed transport, and successful file validation does not prove that the relay accepts the new credential. An SMTP response or transport failure after reservation retains the existing outcome semantics and is not automatically retried just because credentials rotate. Each worker reads independently, so coordinate file rollout across workers and monitor failures; worker migration 0233 adds the optional database and CLI health check described below; API migration 0148 and the dashboard can display it.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` runs a real SMTP worker against a local authenticated STARTTLS fixture and disposable PostgreSQL, verifying the old and atomically replaced password on the wire, invalid-file pause without a claimed attempt, resumption, conflicting sources, `exit` behavior, and the existing SMTP queue regression. This uses an administrator DB login and test certificates on one host; it does not verify a real relay, production secret manager or multi-host HA. Docker was not used.

### SMTP password reload health (worker 0233)

Apply worker migration 0233. To observe password reload state, enable both `NOVU_SMTP_RELOAD_PASSWORD=true` and `NOVU_SMTP_CREDENTIAL_HEALTH_ENABLED=true` on each participating worker. A trusted DBA registers the worker's actual PostgreSQL LOGIN and current role OID in `novu_rust_smtp_credential_workers` for that environment, and grants the login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_credential_heartbeat(text,uuid,text)`. No grants or registrations are created automatically. The security-definer owner must have `pg_read_all_stats` membership; protect it and restrict table access. If the optional observation fails, the worker stops claiming and reconnects. This is independent of the optional SMTP RLS registration.

`delivery-reconcile check-smtp-credentials ENV MAX_AGE_SECONDS MIN_HEALTHY` uses `public.novu_rust_smtp_credential_health_check(text,integer)` and requires EXECUTE plus the environment's `alert_view` grant. Age is 1–86400 seconds and minimum healthy is 1–1000. The command prints healthy, unhealthy, stale and minimum counts; it exits 2 when healthy is below the chosen minimum or a recent failure exists, 0 otherwise, and nonzero on errors. A healthy observation counts only while the same PostgreSQL backend PID, start time and login are connected; recent unhealthy observations count even after disconnect. Stale records are reported but do not themselves cause exit 2 if the minimum is met.

The observation records no password or path. It reflects successful local file reading and validation, **not** SMTP authentication or recipient delivery. Updates occur on state changes and about every 10 seconds while the worker is polling; long-running sends, database timeouts or clock skew can make an active worker appear stale. Pick thresholds accordingly and monitor DB availability and scheduler execution independently. Rows persist across reconnects and can accumulate; plan retention and access controls. A registered worker login is trusted and can deliberately submit misleading observations; privileged direct database changes can also bypass this check. A live database session does not prove that the process can reach the relay. API migration 0148 and the dashboard can display the check with separate permissions. The optional built-in Slack/email alert integration is described below.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` exercises the actual worker's healthy → unhealthy → healthy transitions and the restricted CLI check, including environment isolation, failed or stale observations, disconnected sessions, backend identity fencing and revocation. It uses local disposable PostgreSQL, a mock STARTTLS server and administrator worker credentials; actual relay authentication, secret-manager failures, production load and multi-host HA are not verified. Docker was not used.

### SMTP credential reload alerts

Set both `SWALLOW_ALERT_SMTP_CREDENTIAL_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` (1–86400) and `SWALLOW_ALERT_SMTP_CREDENTIAL_MIN_HEALTHY` (1–1000) on `send-delivery-alert`; omit both to disable the check. The alert DB login requires the environment `alert_view` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_credential_health_check(text,integer)`, in addition to existing alert-send permissions. A healthy count below the selected minimum or any recent unhealthy observation triggers configured Slack/email alerts. A failed check is reported and the process exits nonzero. Only counts and thresholds are included in the message. Stale records do not independently trigger an alert if the minimum is satisfied. These are local file observations, not proof of relay authentication or delivery.

This check supports the existing `destination` cooldown policy and, after migration 0234, `new-condition` with v3 conditions as described below. It rejects `new-condition` with legacy or v2 conditions rather than silently sharing another check’s bits. Actual alert delivery and the scheduler must be monitored independently.

`cargo test --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --lib` checks payloads with local mock Slack and Resend endpoints; `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` exercises the actual alert executable with local PostgreSQL, restricted role, a blocked destination and healthy, unhealthy and unsupported-policy cases. Actual Slack/email receipt, a real SMTP relay and production HA remain unverified.

### Extended alert conditions v3 (worker 0234)

Apply worker migrations 0234 and 0235, then use `SWALLOW_ALERT_COOLDOWN_POLICY=new-condition`, `SWALLOW_ALERT_SCOPED_DELIVERY=true` and `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V3=true`. Do not also enable `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V2`; destination cooldown remains available without either condition version. The v3 mask retains the v2 low 64-bit assignments and adds a second signed 64-bit word. SMTP credential unhealthy and check-failure conditions occupy high-word bits 0 and 1, respectively. Both observation and reservation retain prior condition bits whose checks are unavailable. A successful observation of recovery clears them, allowing a subsequent recurrence to alert. Checks that are disabled are also unobserved, so their previous condition history remains until the check is enabled and successfully observes recovery. The alert login needs environment `alert_send`, EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_reserve_alert_v3(text,text,text,integer,bigint,bigint,bigint,bigint)`, `public.novu_rust_observe_alert_conditions_v3(text,text,text,bigint,bigint,bigint,bigint)` and the existing `public.novu_rust_finish_alert(text,text,text,uuid,text,integer)`, plus each enabled health check's permissions. No grants are created by default. Protect the security-definer owners and table access.

Stop and drain all v3 senders before applying 0235; it rejects the earlier six-argument reservation signature, so the new sender and its new EXECUTE grant are required before sending resumes. It cannot reconstruct history already erased by an older reservation. Stop and drain all senders for a target before switching it to v3. On the first v3 observation of a quiescent accepted v2 target, or first v3 reservation, the DB serializes on its slot, preserves its v2 low-word history and marks the target as v3. A new or unused target is also supported. A legacy-only used target cannot be adopted: choose a new target ID and review the consequences for alert cooldown and history. In-flight, rejected and unknown slots retain their existing review behavior. Once marked v3, scoped v1/v2 reservation and v2 observation functions reject that target; switching back requires a separately designed, reviewed migration and is not provided. Existing unscoped/direct-table senders and privileged writes bypass these fences. Do not mix them with v3. Targets, destination binding, stale replicas and direct SQL still require coordinated rollout.

Both words are signed PostgreSQL `bigint`; do not use sign or JavaScript floating-point numbers to infer health. The additional column and target table have database lock/storage costs. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` checks scoped role access, v2 history adoption including healthy observation, signed low bits, old-version rejection, concurrent reservations, expired attempt recovery, preservation of unobserved history across another reservation and recovery/recurrence, SMTP alert binary selection and local mock Slack/Resend delivery. `scripts/test-discord-resolution-local.sh` checks existing v2 diagnostics. Tests use disposable local PostgreSQL on one host; real Slack/email receipt, all mixed-version deployment scenarios, network partitions and multi-host HA are not verified. Docker was not used.

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=alert-v3 scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` starts two disposable loopback PostgreSQL 17 instances with synchronous replication. A restricted sender records accepted and in-flight v3 reservations with unobserved history, waits for replay, stops the primary and promotes the standby. After promotion it checks the in-flight fence, forcibly expires the lease and races two restricted senders; both remain blocked, the attempt becomes unknown and both mask words survive. The test calls DB functions directly, uses an administrator only for setup and forced expiry, and sends no external request. It does not test automatic expiry timing, actual Slack/email delivery, asynchronous replication, split brain, independent hosts or production HA. Docker is not used.

### SMTP credential observation retention (worker 0236)

Credential observations from disconnected sessions accumulate. After migration 0236, a DBA can register a dedicated retention login and its current role OID in `public.novu_rust_smtp_health_retention_operators` for each environment. Grant that login schema usage and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_prune_smtp_credential_health(text,integer,integer)`; the security-definer owner needs access to the operator and health tables, INSERT and sequence USAGE for `public.novu_rust_smtp_health_retention_audit`, and membership in `pg_read_all_stats`. No logins or grants are created automatically. The independent audit reader function `public.novu_rust_smtp_health_retention_audit_page(text,bigint,integer)` also requires registration and its own EXECUTE grant; its owner needs access to the operator and audit tables. Protect the function owners, role membership and direct table access.

Run `delivery-reconcile prune-smtp-credentials ENV OLDER_SECONDS LIMIT REASON` under the dedicated login. Age must be 86401–31536000 seconds and limit 1–1000. The function deletes at most the limit of oldest rows for the environment only when older than the selected threshold and the recorded PostgreSQL backend PID, start time and login no longer match a live session. Active sessions and observations eligible for any supported health threshold are retained. Locked rows are skipped, so repeat batches as needed. `stale` counts can decrease; historical observations are permanently deleted. The optional reason is trimmed, bounded to 500 bytes and must not contain control characters or secrets. The CLI prints the committed deleted count; a lost response requires checking the audit before another invocation. Each successful invocation, including zero deletions, inserts an audit record transactionally; an audit insertion failure rolls back deletion. Read pages using `SELECT * FROM public.novu_rust_smtp_health_retention_audit_page('ENV',NULL,50)` and pass the last ID for the next page. IDs are not commit-ordered and pages are not a single snapshot. The DBA must separately manage retention of the audit itself. Direct privileged SQL can bypass the controls and audit is not externally tamper-proof.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` uses disposable local PostgreSQL and a restricted login to test environment isolation, age limits, live and recent observation preservation, bounded deletion, audit, failure rollback, CLI and revoked access. It does not test production load, external audit, process scheduling, multiple hosts or failover. Docker is not used.

### Optional SMTP worker CA rotation

Set `NOVU_SMTP_RELOAD_CA=true` and `SWALLOW_SMTP_CA_FILE` to reload the bounded regular PEM file before each new SMTP job. The default is false. A missing file, malformed PEM or invalid TLS configuration pauses new claims and retries, or exits if `NOVU_SMTP_RELOAD_FAILURE_POLICY=exit`; the same policy applies to password and CA reload failures. Startup requires a valid CA file. Replace the file atomically and protect its directory. Each polling iteration reads up to 64 KiB; changed content also rebuilds the TLS transport, so size the filesystem and rotation cadence accordingly. The new transport retains the current password and other settings; if password and CA rotation are both enabled, both must validate before the next claim. An already claimed/in-flight request is not changed or cancelled. Built-in roots remain enabled. Alert/fanout processes are separate and do not use this option. The existing SMTP **credential** health records only password-file status; the separately enabled CA observation is described below. Monitor worker logs, process status and queue delay separately. This does not prove the relay presents a certificate trusted by the updated file.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` uses an actual SMTP worker, local PostgreSQL and a mock STARTTLS relay to check invalid CA pauses before claim, password health remains independent, atomic CA replacement resumes delivery, missing configuration fails startup and the `exit` policy stops a CA-only worker. The fixture tests replacement with an equivalent test certificate, not a relay certificate change, real CA infrastructure, an actual provider or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

### SMTP CA reload health (worker 0237)

Enable `NOVU_SMTP_CA_HEALTH_ENABLED=true` together with `NOVU_SMTP_RELOAD_CA=true` to report CA file validation state independently of password health. A trusted DBA registers the worker's PostgreSQL LOGIN and current role OID in `public.novu_rust_smtp_ca_workers` for the environment and grants that login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_ca_heartbeat(text,uuid,text)`. The security-definer owner needs `pg_read_all_stats` membership and access to the registration and observation tables. No registrations or privileges are created automatically. A failed observation stops new claims and triggers reconnect; an initial invalid CA prevents startup before an observation.

A monitoring DB login needs the environment `alert_view` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_ca_health_check(text,integer)`. Run `delivery-reconcile check-smtp-ca ENV MAX_AGE_SECONDS MIN_HEALTHY`; accepted ages are 1–86400 seconds and minimum healthy 1–1000. It returns aggregate healthy, unhealthy, stale and minimum counts and exits 2 for too few healthy workers or a recent failure. A healthy observation counts only while its exact DB session is connected; recent unhealthy records continue counting until they age out. It records no CA bytes or paths. This is local file validation, not proof that the relay's certificate is trusted, that authentication works, or that email was delivered. Long operations or DB disconnections can make observations stale; monitor the process, database and queue separately. Registered worker logins and privileged DB users are trusted and can falsify observations. Records accumulate; optional CA observation retention is described below. API migration 0150 and the dashboard provide separately authorized CA health access; see the API README. Optional Slack/email alerting is described below.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` verifies real worker invalid/repaired CA observations and a restricted login's registration, spoof prevention, environment isolation, CLI and revoked access on disposable local PostgreSQL. It does not test a real CA infrastructure, external alerts or production HA. Docker is not used.

### SMTP CA reload alerts

Set both `SWALLOW_ALERT_SMTP_CA_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` (1–86400) and `SWALLOW_ALERT_SMTP_CA_MIN_HEALTHY` (1–1000) on `send-delivery-alert`, or omit both to disable this check. The alert login needs the environment `alert_view` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_ca_health_check(text,integer)` in addition to existing alert-send permissions. Too few healthy workers, any recent unhealthy observation or a failed check triggers the configured Slack and/or email alert. Only aggregate counts and thresholds enter the message. Stale observations do not independently alert if the healthy minimum is met; observation records reflect file validation, not trust of a particular relay certificate or delivery.

Select destination cooldown or `new-condition`; the latter requires scoped delivery, migration 0235 and `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V3=true`. V3 high-word bits 2 and 3 independently represent CA unhealthy and check failure. Existing v3 senders that do not include this check do not report CA failures; coordinate rollout of all senders sharing a target. File changes between checks and unscheduled alert processes can delay detection. Monitor the scheduler, database, worker and actual alert delivery independently.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` tests the actual alert executable with a restricted DB login and blocked Slack target, rejects unsupported condition policies, and checks aggregate messages with mock Slack and Resend endpoints. Real Slack/email receipt, operational CA trust and multi-host HA are not verified. Docker is not used.

### SMTP CA observation retention (worker 0238)

Apply worker migration 0238 to use an independently registered retention login for CA observations. A trusted DBA registers its current LOGIN and OID per environment in `public.novu_rust_smtp_ca_health_retention_operators` and grants schema usage and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_prune_smtp_ca_health(text,integer,integer)`. Its security-definer owner needs access to the registration and CA health tables, INSERT and sequence USAGE for `public.novu_rust_smtp_ca_health_retention_audit`, and membership in `pg_read_all_stats`. No role or grant is created automatically. Audit access requires separate EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_ca_health_retention_audit_page(text,bigint,integer)` and registration for that environment; protect function owners and direct table access.

Run `delivery-reconcile prune-smtp-ca ENV OLDER_SECONDS LIMIT REASON`. Age must be 86401–31536000 seconds and limit 1–1000. Only older observations of disconnected sessions are removed; current sessions and observations eligible for any supported health threshold remain. Rows locked by another transaction are skipped, so repeat bounded batches if needed. Deletion and its audit record (including zero-deletion calls) commit together; audit failure rolls back deletion. A reason is trimmed, limited to 500 bytes and excludes control characters; it must not contain secrets. A lost CLI response requires checking the audit before repeating. For audit pages call `public.novu_rust_smtp_ca_health_retention_audit_page('ENV',NULL,50)` and pass the last ID on the next call. IDs are not commit order and pages are separate snapshots. Removed observations cannot be restored; stale counts decrease. Direct privileged writes bypass controls, audit is not externally tamper-proof, and audit retention and scheduling remain the operator's responsibility.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` checks a restricted role, environment isolation, live/recent record preservation, bounded deletion, audit failure rollback, the CLI and revocation on disposable local PostgreSQL. Actual load, external audit and multi-host HA remain unverified. Docker is not used.

### SMTP additional MIME alternatives

SMTP queue messages can include `alternatives`, an array of up to 10 objects with `content_type` and UTF-8 string `content`; total content is limited to 1 MiB. The type must be a valid MIME content type, at most 255 bytes, without control characters, and cannot be a multipart or message container. The worker validates the parts before reserving an external dispatch. It emits the optional plain-text and HTML bodies followed by alternatives inside `multipart/alternative`; inline CID images remain associated with HTML and ordinary attachments remain in the outer `multipart/mixed`. For example: `"alternatives":[{"content_type":"text/calendar; method=REQUEST; charset=utf-8","content":"BEGIN:VCALENDAR\r\nEND:VCALENDAR\r\n"}]`. Recipients' mail clients decide which representation to display; this does not guarantee that a calendar invitation is accepted.

The trigger API accepts `overrides.email.alternatives` entries as `{ "contentType": "text/calendar; method=REQUEST; charset=utf-8", "content": "..." }` and maps them into the queued SMTP job. At most 10 entries and 1 MiB total UTF-8 content are accepted; unknown fields or invalid shapes return 501. MIME type syntax and SMTP-specific restrictions are checked by the worker, so a trigger can be accepted and subsequently fail before dispatch. Content is forwarded literally without template rendering. These values persist in workflow and email job payloads; ordinary attachment retention does not erase them. Separate opt-in alternative retention is described below. Resend currently rejects nonempty alternatives at delivery; an optional trigger-time reject policy is documented in the API README. SendGrid uses its own provider handling. The channel, supported provider and matching workflow-step provider layers accept alternatives; the most specific present array replaces the preceding array. SMTP provider layers support only the `alternatives` field and are a Swallow extension. Management UI support and full Novu compatibility are not implemented. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` tests type/size validation and runs the real SMTP worker with a generated workflow job whose queue payload is augmented with a calendar part, inline image and attachment; a local STARTTLS fixture parses the received MIME. The SMTP server is a mock and the test does not establish real client rendering, original Novu equivalence or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

`PATH="/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin:$PATH" TEST_POSTGRES_MODE=local tests/test-smtp-attachment-http.sh` additionally triggers the real local API, in-app worker and restricted SMTP worker using an email alternative and attachments, checks malformed alternative shape rejection, and parses the mock relay MIME. It uses administrator access for API/in-app setup and a local fixture, not a live provider or original Novu comparison.

### Email alternative retention (worker 0239)

MIME alternative content can occur in both the parent workflow queue payload and a workflow-generated SMTP, SendGrid or Resend email job. Migration 0239 adds separate completion and scrub timestamps, a replay fence, an audit table and opt-in removal functions. Existing eligible completed rows start their retention clock at migration, not their original completion. The migration backfills queue rows and creates an index, so plan its locks and storage impact. Pending, running, failed, cancelled, standalone email and other environments are excluded. Parent and child have independent clocks; deleting the completed parent does not remove an active child’s content. Previously attachment-scrubbed jobs are eligible, and alternative-first or attachment-first removal is supported. Existing attachment-only commands do not remove alternatives.

The DBA must register a LOGIN/OID in `public.novu_rust_attachment_retention_grants` with `can_view` and/or `can_scrub`, and separately grant EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_list_alternative_retention(text,integer,integer)`, `public.novu_rust_scrub_alternative_retention(text,integer,integer,text)` and/or `public.novu_rust_alternative_retention_audit(text,bigint)` as appropriate. Trusted security-definer owners need the existing retention authorization function, queue and audit access, and audit sequence usage. No privileges are granted by default. Use `attachment-retention list-alternatives ENV AGE LIMIT`, `scrub-alternatives ENV AGE LIMIT REASON`, or `audit-alternatives ENV [AFTER_ID]`. Age is 0–31536000 seconds and limit 1–100. Scrubbing removes only the known alternative fields, writes a per-job audit atomically and prevents replay of scrubbed jobs. Locked jobs are skipped; a lost response requires reconciling audit and job state before retry. Reasons can appear in process listings and audit records; do not include secrets.

This is not secure erasure: PostgreSQL WAL, replicas, backups, prior row versions, other application copies and provider copies remain. Direct privileged DB changes can bypass controls, and auditing is not externally tamper-proof. `scripts/test-smtp-rotation-local.sh` checks scoped permission, audit rollback, both cleanup orders and replay denial. `scripts/test-alternative-retention-upgrade-local.sh` checks backfill of preexisting completed jobs, including an attachment-scrubbed job. `TEST_POSTGRES_MODE=local tests/test-smtp-attachment-http.sh` checks the actual HTTP workflow through SMTP followed by alternative cleanup of parent and child, with attachments preserved. These tests use disposable local PostgreSQL and a mock relay; production load, replicas, backups and multi-host HA are unverified. Docker is not used in these runs.

### Provider-layer alternative retention (worker 0240)

Worker migration 0240 extends the existing opt-in `list-alternatives` and `scrub-alternatives` operations to include alternatives from flat SendGrid, provider and step-provider overrides stored in the parent workflow job. All known layer copies are removed, including a provider that was not selected for dispatch. Completed parent jobs already marked alternative-scrubbed are eligible again if one of those copies remains, and the cleanup is audited again while retaining the original scrub timestamp. Other override fields remain. Completion times for previously ineligible completed jobs start at migration. The migration updates queue rows, replaces retention functions and broadens the scope of existing scheduled scrub operations; review schedules, grant holders, backfill locks and storage before rollout. Previously granted function EXECUTE and the existing environment retention grant continue to govern the action. Parent and child eligibility remains separate, and pending/failed/standalone jobs are not scrubbed. As before, the operation does not erase WAL, replicas, backups, logs or provider copies.

`TEST_POSTGRES_MODE=local tests/test-smtp-attachment-http.sh` exercises channel, SMTP provider and matching step-provider alternatives through real local API and workers, verifies step precedence in a mock relay, then verifies removal of selected and unselected provider copies. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-alternative-provider-upgrade-local.sh` checks a previously attachment- and alternative-scrubbed parent is selected again, remaining layer copies are removed, other data is retained and replay stays blocked. The local test suite also covers scoped grants and both retention orders. Actual Novu comparison, live SMTP clients, production load and multi-host HA are unverified. Docker is not used.

SendGrid alternative-content comparison: `reference_sendgrid.cjs` can bundle the checked-out Novu provider source when `SENDGRID_REFERENCE_DEPS` is set, without requiring a prebuilt provider distribution. With the repository snapshot at `3b245867` for the provider file, this compares nine complete request bodies, including a calendar alternative with text, multiple alternatives without text, and an empty text/alternative case. The bundled provider uses isolated `@sendgrid/client@8.1.0`, `@sendgrid/mail@8.1.6` and `@sendgrid/eventwebhook@8.0.0`; Novu enums are stubbed and the SDK request is intercepted. Reproduce from the repository root:

```sh
npm install --prefix /private/tmp/swallow-sendgrid-reference --no-audit --no-fund --ignore-scripts @sendgrid/client@8.1.0 @sendgrid/mail@8.1.6 @sendgrid/eventwebhook@8.0.0
NOVU_REFERENCE_SOURCE="$PWD" SENDGRID_REFERENCE_DEPS=/private/tmp/swallow-sendgrid-reference CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0 cargo test --locked --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --test reference_sendgrid original_sendgrid_wire_body -- --ignored
```

This is a provider-level mock comparison, not an original Novu server or trigger-to-worker comparison. It does not verify live SendGrid delivery, all override combinations, provider versions or complete Novu compatibility.

### Resend validation before dispatch reservation

The Resend worker now builds and validates the provider request after workflow/subscriber preparation but before committing its dispatch marker. An invalid message, including unsupported MIME alternatives, records a `rejected` attempt, failed job and (when present) error message atomically without making a provider request. Its `invalid Resend request` diagnostic means local validation failed; it is not an HTTP rejection from Resend. If this transaction fails, the attempt and message changes roll back; the already claimed job remains leased and normal pre-attempt lease recovery can requeue it. The worker also rechecks the lease after preparation and again before committing a valid dispatch marker. An expired lease rolls back rather than starting the request. A crash after a valid marker commits but before or during the HTTP request is still ambiguous and remains `unknown` on recovery; do not assume this eliminates that gap. Existing older workers do not have this behavior. Request preparation now holds the transaction and subscriber/job locks while building the provider body, including Base64 attachment encoding; assess large-attachment throughput and database timeout settings.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-resend-preparation-local.sh` exercises a workflow-produced invalid alternative using a local PostgreSQL and loopback endpoint, forces an error during the failure transaction to verify rollback and lease recovery, checks the rejected attempt/job/message with no network request, then sends a valid job to a mock endpoint. It also runs the 429 queue and adapter regression tests. This uses the queue function and mock HTTP rather than a separate production worker process or real Resend; actual provider outcomes, original Novu parity, lock contention and production HA remain unverified. Docker is not used.

A controlled synchronous promotion test covers the invalid-request boundary: `SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend-invalid scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` starts a local PostgreSQL 17 primary and synchronous standby, creates a workflow-generated Resend job with an unsupported MIME alternative, and runs an actual Resend worker. A test-only DB trigger blocks its uncommitted rejection update; the fixture confirms the claimed job but no attempt is visible on the standby, stops the primary immediately, and promotes the standby. After an administrator forcibly expires the lease, a second actual worker recovers the job and commits one rejected attempt and error message. A loopback proxy listener receives no connection. This validates a specific pre-dispatch failure boundary, not the gap after a valid dispatch marker; that remains ambiguous. The test uses administrator database privileges, a forced lease expiry, a synthetic trigger and two PostgreSQL processes on one host. Actual Resend, real network partitions, asynchronous replication, split brain and production multi-host HA remain unverified. Docker is not used.

### SendGrid validation before dispatch reservation

The SendGrid worker constructs and validates the provider request inside the job transaction before committing a dispatch marker. Invalid requests are recorded atomically as a `rejected` attempt, failed job and, when present, error message without a provider request. `invalid email request` means local validation failed, not that SendGrid returned an HTTP rejection. When dispatch tracking is enabled, a rejected local-validation attempt can still have a dispatch ID and recipient hashes; those are bookkeeping, not proof of a send. If recording fails, the transaction rolls back; the earlier claim remains leased and can be recovered as a pre-attempt job after expiration. The worker rechecks the lease after request preparation and before committing a valid marker for every step policy. A valid marker committed before process failure remains ambiguous and is not automatically retried. Older worker binaries do not provide this ordering. Building the request, including Base64 attachments, now holds the transaction and subscriber/job locks; assess timeouts and production throughput.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-sendgrid-preparation-local.sh` exercises a workflow-produced invalid dynamic-template value with tracking enabled, forces message-update failure and checks rollback, then recovers the lease and checks the rejected attempt and message with no network connection. A valid job reaches a loopback mock, and SendGrid adapter regression tests run. The source-provider comparison can be run using the earlier documented command. These tests use queue functions and mock HTTP, not a separate production worker process, real SendGrid delivery, production contention, original workflow parity or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

### Twilio validation before dispatch reservation

The Twilio worker now builds and validates the form body, including the generated per-attempt status callback, before committing a dispatch marker. Invalid queue data is atomically recorded as a `rejected` attempt, failed job and, when present, error message without contacting Twilio. `invalid sms request` is a local validation diagnostic, not a Twilio HTTP response. A dispatch ID and account snapshot may still exist for such an attempt; they do not prove that a request was sent. A failure while recording this state rolls the transaction back; the earlier claim can be recovered after its lease expires. The worker checks the lease before the attempt and after request preparation and message updates, for every SMS step policy. A valid marker committed before a crash or lost response is still ambiguous and requires the existing review process. Old worker binaries retain the earlier behavior. Form construction occurs while the job transaction and subscriber lock are held; evaluate production contention and timeouts.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-twilio-preparation-local.sh` exercises a workflow-produced SMS job whose queued provider field is deliberately malformed (this shape is rejected by the current trigger API). On local PostgreSQL it forces a failure during rejection recording, checks attempt and dispatch-ID rollback, recovers the expired claim, verifies no loopback request and the failed job/message, then sends a valid job to a mock Twilio endpoint. Adapter and callback construction tests also pass. `scripts/test-channel-retry-date-local.sh` separately passes the Twilio queue recovery, 429, competing consumers and callback-evidence regression. This invokes queue functions, not a separate production worker process; actual Twilio delivery, full original Novu compatibility, production lock contention and multi-host HA remain unverified. Docker is not used.

### FCM lease checks before dispatch

The FCM queue rechecks the job lease for every step policy after preparing the target and again after writing the dispatch attempt and history, immediately before committing them. If the lease has expired at either check, the transaction rolls back and no FCM request is started; the expired claim remains for the existing recovery process. This also applies to the default snapshot policy. Old workers retain the earlier behavior. A check cannot prevent the lease from expiring during the following commit or the provider request; a committed dispatch marker followed by a crash or uncertain response still requires review. These checks hold existing transaction locks and add database queries.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-fcm-lease-local.sh` uses disposable local PostgreSQL and a loopback listener. Test-only triggers simulate expiration during history insertion and during attempt insertion; it checks rollback, no connection, and recovery after the first case. This invokes the queue function, not a separate worker process, and does not test real FCM, commit stalls, all concurrency cases, production load or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

### Slack and Discord lease checks before dispatch

Slack app, incoming webhook and workflow delivery, as well as Discord webhook delivery, recheck the lease after inserting the dispatch attempt and before committing it. Slack workflow delivery also rechecks after preparation under the default snapshot policy. If a lease is expired at these checks, the transaction rolls back and no provider request starts; normal expiry recovery can requeue the job without an attempt. Older worker binaries retain the previous behavior. The checks add a database query to each dispatch and hold existing locks until commit. Lease expiry during the commit or after the check remains possible, and a crash after a committed dispatch marker still requires review as an unknown outcome.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-chat-lease-local.sh` uses disposable local PostgreSQL and a loopback listener. Test-only database triggers simulate expiration while inserting attempts for the four queue paths; the test checks rollback and no connection, forcibly expires the claims and verifies their recovery to pending. A separate regression checks Slack 429 persistence with mock HTTP. The tests call queue functions, not separate production workers, and do not establish actual Slack or Discord delivery, commit-stall behavior, production contention or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-discord-cooldown-local.sh` also passed the existing scoped queue, retry, rate-limit and shared-cooldown regression tests against local PostgreSQL and mock endpoints.

### Per-target delivery alert checks

Each `alert-fanout` target may optionally specify `delivery_checks` as an object of boolean overrides. For example:

```json
{"targets":[
  {"channel":"slack","id":"delivery-ops","webhook_file":"/run/secrets/slack-ops","delivery_checks":{"resend":false,"failed_jobs":true}},
  {"channel":"email","id":"email-ops","provider":"resend","to":"ops@example.test","from":"alerts@example.test","api_key_file":"/run/secrets/resend","delivery_checks":{"resend":true,"failed_jobs":false}}
]}
```

Available fields are `resend`, `attempts`, `slack_jobs`, `smtp`, `discord`, `failed_jobs`, `slack_missing_endpoints`, `email_policy_cancelled`, `sms_policy_cancelled`, `push_policy_cancelled` and `slack_policy_cancelled`. Each overrides the corresponding `SWALLOW_ALERT_*_ENABLED` setting only for that target's child process; omitted or null fields inherit the parent setting and existing defaults. Unknown fields and non-booleans reject the entire configuration before starting any destination. `slack_missing_endpoints` only takes effect when `slack_jobs` is enabled. Additional checks and thresholds are described below. Credentials and condition-version policies remain inherited and independently configured; per-target positional timing is described below. The management UI does not configure these delivery checks. Database alert children ignore these delivery check settings. Turning off all effective checks causes the delivery child to fail rather than send; monitor fanout failures. A disabled check can hide an incident, so choose coverage deliberately. Distinct checks on senders sharing a destination ID can affect shared condition history and suppression; coordinate every sender and use stable separate target IDs as appropriate, especially when using `new-condition`. Existing destination binding protects destination identity, not the complete check selection. Older fanout binaries reject the new field and must be upgraded before using it. NUL in either configured email address is now rejected before any child starts.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-alert-target-checks-local.sh` tests per-target overrides, inheritance and invalid-configuration atomicity using fixture child processes. It also runs the actual delivery alert children against disposable local PostgreSQL with one failed job and blocked Slack slots: the target monitoring failed jobs reports attention while the target monitoring only attempts completes normally. The first database test lacked the separate `alert_view` grant and passed after the fixture was corrected. Tests do not send external alerts, exercise a real IdP or prove production HA. Docker is not used.

`delivery_checks` also accepts `sendgrid_webhook_keys`, `fcm_cooldown_source`, `fcm_cooldown_distinct_source` and `fcm_unreviewed`. Their existing policy dependencies remain in force. Optional `delivery_thresholds` accepts the following fields, each corresponding to the `SWALLOW_ALERT_` environment variable with the same uppercase name:

- `discord_cooldown_grace_seconds`, `discord_retry_grace_seconds`, `fcm_cooldown_grace_seconds`, `twilio_delayed_grace_seconds`, `resend_cooldown_grace_seconds`, `slack_cooldown_grace_seconds`, `resend_auto_grace_seconds`, `queue_max_wait_seconds`.
- `smtp_credential_max_age_seconds`, `smtp_credential_min_healthy`, `smtp_ca_max_age_seconds`, `smtp_ca_min_healthy`, `fcm_credential_max_age_seconds`, `fcm_credential_min_healthy`.
- `fcm_unrecorded_claim_min_gap`, `fcm_unrecorded_claim_grace_seconds`, `fcm_partial_window_seconds`, `twilio_grace_seconds`, `twilio_heartbeat_max_seconds`, `sendgrid_grace_seconds`, `sendgrid_heartbeat_max_seconds`.

Use a positive integer to set a threshold, `"disabled"` to remove that environment variable for that child, and omit the field (or use null) to inherit it. The fanout validates each value's supported range and rejects unknown fields before starting any child. The child still checks combinations and inherited configuration: for example, both Twilio thresholds and both values of each credential check must be configured or disabled together, and FCM cooldown source monitoring requires its cooldown threshold. Optional preflight for the six paired thresholds is described below; other invalid combinations can fail an individual child after other targets start, so monitor the fanout exit status. Configured numeric thresholds enable their associated checks even if other boolean checks are off. Keep cooperating senders' settings consistent for shared target IDs; bindings do not enforce these selections. The command-line Resend grace, heartbeat and cooldown can be overridden as described below. Database alert children ignore these settings. Old fanout binaries reject the new fields.

The local target-check script covers every threshold's environment-variable mapping and `disabled`, plus inherited values and rejection of malformed input. It also uses actual alert children and a pending PostgreSQL job to verify that 60- and 600-second per-target queue thresholds produce different decisions. This is not external alert receipt or production HA validation.

### Per-target alert timing

An optional `alert_timing` object on a Slack or email target can override the fanout command's positional timing, for example `"alert_timing":{"resend_grace_seconds":60,"resend_heartbeat_max_seconds":120,"cooldown_seconds":300}`. The first two values apply only to delivery alerts and each must be 1–86400 seconds. `cooldown_seconds` applies to both delivery and database alert children and must be 60–86400 seconds. Omitted or null values inherit the command arguments; they cannot disable the associated checks. Unknown keys, non-integers and out-of-range values reject the complete configuration before any child starts. Database mode ignores the two Resend fields. These options affect only the target's invocation: all senders sharing the same channel and target ID should use consistent cooldown and condition policies or they can suppress each other unexpectedly. Destination binding does not enforce timing settings. Older fanout binaries reject `alert_timing`; update them before changing the configuration. The UI does not edit this file.

The local target-check script runs actual delivery alert children against disposable local PostgreSQL with blocked Slack slots, an overdue Resend job and a synthetic projector heartbeat: different per-target grace values, then different heartbeat ages, each lead to different alert decisions. Fixture child tests separately verify all timing arguments and inheritance in both delivery and database modes and reject invalid configurations before launching a child. They do not demonstrate actual external alerts, different cooldowns over repeated live sends, production contention or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

### Optional paired-threshold preflight

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_FANOUT_PREFLIGHT_PAIRS=true` to check the effective per-target configuration for the SMTP auto worker, SMTP credential, SMTP CA, FCM credential, FCM unrecorded claim, Twilio delivery and SendGrid delivery threshold pairs before starting any delivery-alert child. A target override value counts as present, `"disabled"` as absent, and omitted or null as the inherited environment variable's presence. If either half of any pair is present without the other on any target, the entire run fails without launching a child. Invalid policy values also fail; unset or `false` retains previous behavior. Database-alert mode does not evaluate delivery pairs. This change can reject inherited incomplete pairs that previous versions allowed to reach a child. Apply it consistently to each fanout/scheduler process and monitor failures. It only checks presence of these seven pairs: it does not validate inherited values, DB permissions, other policy combinations such as FCM cooldown source, whether any check is enabled, or provider credentials. Existing child validation remains necessary and different child failures can still occur after others run. Fanout configuration and parent environment can change between separate scheduled runs; protect and coordinate both. This is not a UI or a live configuration API.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-alert-target-checks-local.sh` checks all six pairs, inheritance, explicit disable, the disabled policy, and database mode with fixture children. A real fanout invocation against disposable PostgreSQL checks that an invalid second target stops before any child reports. The fixture does not use production scheduler instances or external delivery; production HA remains unverified.

### PostgreSQL-backed alert timing

Worker migration `0241` adds an optional environment-scoped store for per-target `alert_timing`. Destination configuration, email addresses, webhook URLs and secret paths remain in the fanout file. Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_TIMING_SOURCE=postgres` on the fanout or scheduler to read settings from `DATABASE_URL` once per fanout run before any child starts; the default `file` preserves the existing behavior. PostgreSQL connection and query each have a 10-second timeout. A connection, permission, migration or read failure fails the entire run without launching children: monitor the scheduler independently. The existing PostgreSQL TLS settings apply.

A trusted DBA registers separate LOGIN/OID entries in `novu_rust_alert_timing_grants` with `can_read` for each fanout login and `can_write` for each configuration writer, and grants EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_alert_timing_list(text)` or `public.novu_rust_alert_timing_set(text,text,text,bigint,integer,integer,integer,uuid,text)` respectively. There are no default grants. The security-definer owner and schema must be protected. `set` takes environment, channel (`slack` or `email`), stable target ID, expected revision (0 for creation), Resend grace, Resend heartbeat, cooldown, request UUID and reason. The three timings may be NULL. The first write sets revision 1; later writes require the current revision. An identical request UUID and payload from the same LOGIN/OID is replayed without another audit entry; a changed payload or stale revision is rejected. Settings, request record and audit insert commit atomically. Reasons and request records are retained, so do not put secrets in them. The JWT management API described in the API README has separate request and JWT-attributed audit lookup; there is no restricted worker request/audit lookup or delete operation; the dashboard provides timing management through the JWT API. A trusted administrator must manage retention/cleanup. Protect direct access and backups.

A stored row replaces the matching file target's entire `alert_timing` object for both alert modes. Its NULL values inherit the fanout command arguments, not the file target's timings. A missing DB row preserves the file settings; rows for targets not present in the file are ignored. The query reads at most 1025 rows and the fanout fails if more than 1024 exist for an environment. The list and each child run are separate snapshots; a change or grant revocation after the read cannot cancel already started or about-to-start children. Existing file-based or old senders bypass the DB settings. Coordinate all senders sharing a target, explicitly provision DB access, and account for database restore/failover and stale workers. DB settings do not bind or change destinations and do not prove delivery.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-alert-target-checks-local.sh` uses disposable local PostgreSQL, separate restricted read/write LOGINs, actual fanout and delivery children, blocked mock Slack targets and a synthetic overdue Resend job. It checks scoped grants and revocation, concurrent identical request replay, stale/conflicting requests, audit failure rollback, DB-source versus file-source decisions, and fail-closed behavior on denied access or unavailable DB. The first timing migration run was included in this script. No actual external alert, API/UI, full concurrency audit, production scheduler or multi-host HA was tested. Docker is not used.

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=alert-timing scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` exercises the timing setting and JWT delegation across a controlled synchronous PostgreSQL 17 promotion on one host. It starts an actual Rust API with a restricted DB login and signed fixture JWTs, writes a timing setting and delegates read access, waits until both are visible on the standby, immediately stops the primary, promotes the standby and restarts the API using a multi-host write-target URL. After promotion the target JWT reads the saved setting, identical timing and grant requests replay without duplicate audits, and an actual fanout/delivery child with blocked Slack and a synthetic overdue Resend job uses the stored threshold. The administrator then revokes the target's read grant and the target is denied; the audit reflects both grant changes. The fanout uses a PostgreSQL administrator account and no external alert is delivered. The API is explicitly restarted; this test does not establish uninterrupted connections, automatic failover orchestration, asynchronous replication safety, fencing/split-brain protection, production scale or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

### Database-alert timing fallback

When `SWALLOW_ALERT_TIMING_SOURCE=postgres` is used, an unavailable timing database would otherwise prevent `alert-fanout database` from starting its children, including the existing database-alert secondary-state or uncoordinated fallback. An operator can explicitly set `SWALLOW_ALERT_TIMING_CONNECTION_FAILURE=file` to use each target's file timing (or the command-line default) **only for database-alert mode and only when the initial timing DB connection fails or times out**. The default `fail` starts no children. Invalid DB configuration, successful connection followed by query/permission failure, and delivery-alert mode still fail closed. A connection error can include authentication or TLS failure; this option does not distinguish a database outage from those cases. The fanout writes a non-secret warning and returns exit code 1 even if children complete; supervise and alert on that degraded state.

This setting merely allows the database-alert child to start. Configure `SWALLOW_DB_ALERT_STATE_FAILURE_MODE=secondary-store` or `send-uncoordinated` separately for the child's existing failure behavior; `report-only` will still send nothing when the state DB is down. File timing can differ from the last DB setting and change cooldown or suppression. Independent state stores and uncoordinated delivery have the duplicate, ambiguity, security and monitoring limitations described above. A timing DB connection that succeeds but later fails or times out on its query does not use the fallback. Older binaries do not support this option; coordinate all instances and protect the file and environment. An existing file target's credentials remain required.

The local target-check script tests the real fanout and database-alert child with a deliberately unreachable primary, local secondary PostgreSQL and a loopback HTTPS proxy that drops the connection. With `file` selected it observes one attempted connection and an `unknown` secondary slot; default fail, delivery mode, and a connected but unauthorized timing query do not start children. This is a synthetic network failure, not actual external receipt, TLS authentication failure, production scheduling, independent hosts or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

### SendGrid tracking overrides

The trigger API accepts a bounded `trackingSettings` object under `overrides.providers.sendgrid` and under `overrides.steps[STEP_ID].providers.sendgrid`. The same supported object is also accepted inside each provider layer's `_passthrough.body.trackingSettings`. Supported SDK-style camelCase fields are `clickTracking` (`enable`, `enableText`), `openTracking` (`enable`, `substitutionTag`), `subscriptionTracking` (`enable`, `text`, `html`, `substitutionTag`) and `ganalytics` (`enable`, `utmSource`, `utmMedium`, `utmTerm`, `utmContent`, `utmCampaign`). Booleans and strings are validated, strings are limited to 4096 UTF-8 bytes, and unknown fields or other passthrough keys are rejected. Global and matching-step known settings merge recursively; global and matching-step passthrough settings then merge recursively on top, with the later step taking precedence within each group. The worker converts supported names to SendGrid wire format and validates persisted settings again before committing a dispatch reservation. The default body is unchanged when no tracking fields are supplied. Unknown direct queued settings produce a local invalid-message failure rather than an HTTP request.

For example, `{"providers":{"sendgrid":{"trackingSettings":{"clickTracking":{"enable":true,"enableText":false}}}}}` under event `overrides` enables click tracking. Tracking and subscription settings affect recipient privacy and message links; choose them deliberately. Values, including subscription text or HTML, remain in parent and email-job payloads and are not removed by attachment/alternative retention. Separate opt-in tracking retention is described below. Protect and manage those records. Upgrade all SendGrid workers before accepting these settings: older worker binaries reject queued jobs containing the new field. This is a bounded subset, not arbitrary SendGrid passthrough, casing normalization, integration-level settings, or full Novu compatibility. Resend and SMTP do not accept these settings.

The original provider/SDK comparison in `tests/reference_sendgrid.rs` now covers 12 request bodies including click/open tracking, subscription/analytics and a known-versus-passthrough merge; the last three use the original checked-out provider bundled with isolated SendGrid SDK dependencies and intercept the SDK HTTP request. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-sendgrid-tracking-local.sh` exercises the Rust in-process trigger router, PostgreSQL workflow processing and SendGrid queue function against a loopback mock, including global/step/passthrough precedence and invalid input. It does not run the original server, a separate SendGrid worker, actual SendGrid, recipient tracking, complete SDK field semantics or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

### SendGrid tracking retention

Worker migration `0242` adds a separate, opt-in cleanup of completed workflow jobs. `attachment-retention list-tracking ENV AGE_SECONDS LIMIT`, `scrub-tracking ENV AGE_SECONDS LIMIT REASON` and `audit-tracking ENV [AFTER_ID]` use the existing environment-scoped attachment retention LOGIN/OID `can_read` or `can_write` grants, plus explicit EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_list_tracking_retention(text,integer,integer)`, `public.novu_rust_scrub_tracking_retention(text,integer,integer,text)` and `public.novu_rust_tracking_retention_audit(text,bigint)` respectively. No new grants or schedule are installed automatically. Existing retention operators receiving these function grants gain the ability to erase this additional data; review their authority. Protect function owners, schema, audit records and privileged DB access. Reasons must not contain secrets. A timed-out scrub may have committed; inspect audit and current job state before another request.

Cleanup removes the **entire** supported SendGrid tracking-settings object, not just its text, from provider and step override copies in completed parent workflow jobs and from the top-level and workflow override copies in completed SendGrid email jobs. Other user payload, message history, failed/pending jobs and standalone email jobs remain untouched. It does not remove other copies, database WAL, backups or information already sent to providers. The separate attachment and alternative cleanup commands do not remove tracking settings, and the three operations can run in any order. A scrubbed job is fenced from replay or arbitrary payload edits; do not bypass the triggers. New completion timestamps for already completed eligible jobs begin at migration time, not their original completion time. The migration scans existing jobs, creates an index and adds trigger work on queue updates; assess locks and load before rollout. Direct privileged changes, disabled triggers, older restored database functions or purging audit may bypass these protections. API/UI, automatic scheduling and external immutable audit are not provided.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-tracking-retention-local.sh` tests disposable local PostgreSQL and a restricted LOGIN, the actual CLI scrub/audit, six cleanup orders for parent and child jobs, concurrent cleanup, audit failure rollback, revocation, pending/standalone exclusion and replay fences. A separate fresh-database upgrade test backfills a completed job already scrubbed of attachments and alternatives. The first concurrent test had a Rust temporary-borrow compilation error, corrected before the passing run. Production data volume, external backups, scheduling and multi-host HA remain unverified. Docker is not used.

### SendGrid in-flight failover regression

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=sendgrid-inflight scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` tests a tracked SendGrid request during a controlled synchronous PostgreSQL 17 promotion on one host. An in-process queue handler sends a request with click/subscription settings to a loopback mock, which holds its 202 response. After the `dispatching` attempt and dispatch ID are visible on the standby, the test immediately stops the primary, promotes the standby and releases the mock response; the old handler cannot persist the result. An administrator forcibly expires the lease. Two actual SendGrid worker processes recover it as `unknown`/failed and make no further proxy connection during the observation period. A separate newly queued job reaches the proxy exactly once and becomes unknown when the proxy closes the connection, confirming that the promoted workers are processing. Dispatch tracking is enabled. The first send uses a queue function and mock HTTP rather than the actual worker executable; both recovery workers use an administrative DB login. No actual SendGrid service, delivery callback, genuine lease wait, asynchronous replication, network partition, automatic promotion/fencing or multi-host operational HA is tested. Docker is not used.

### SendGrid sandbox validation

Operators can explicitly validate one adapter request without creating a queue job or claiming delivery. `sendgrid-validate` reads a single `Email` JSON object from stdin (maximum 1 MiB) and uses the same `NOVU_SENDGRID_API_KEY` or `NOVU_SENDGRID_API_KEY_FILE`, `NOVU_SENDGRID_FROM`, `NOVU_SENDGRID_SENDER_NAME`, optional `NOVU_SENDGRID_REGION`, and optional `NOVU_SENDGRID_IP_POOL_NAME` as the worker. Never put a key in the JSON or command line. For example, with those environment variables configured:

```sh
printf '%s\n' '{"to":["test@example.com"],"subject":"Test","html":"<p>Test</p>"}' | cargo run --locked --manifest-path apps/worker/rust-queue/Cargo.toml --bin sendgrid-validate
```

The adapter unconditionally sets `mail_settings.sandbox_mode.enable` to `true`; only HTTP 200 returns `{"result":"validated","delivered":false}`. Other statuses and network errors fail without printing the response body, message, or credentials. According to [SendGrid's sandbox documentation](https://www.twilio.com/docs/sendgrid/for-developers/sending-email/sandbox-mode), this validates the request shape but does not deliver an email or generate webhook events; it does not validate dynamic template contents. The command contacts SendGrid and sends the supplied message data to it. The standalone command is not a delivery check, does not produce Novu history or a durable audit record, and is not itself connected to the management API or UI. Loopback tests cover the request flag and 200, 202 and 400 responses; a real SendGrid account and Novu end-to-end parity have not been tested for this command.

The standalone validator rejects `workflow_context`: subscriber preferences, identities, templates and recipient addresses are resolved inside the worker transaction and cannot be faithfully reconstructed by this command. Supply an already resolved standalone email. This rejection happens locally before any network request.

Workflow triggers can opt in with `overrides.providers.sendgrid.mailSettings.sandboxMode.enable` or `overrides.steps[STEP_ID].providers.sendgrid.mailSettings.sandboxMode.enable`; the same setting is supported in each layer's `_passthrough.body.mailSettings`. Only the exact object `{"sandboxMode":{"enable":true}}` or the boolean `false` form is accepted. Global known settings merge first, then step known settings, then global and step passthrough; later values take precedence. This is a bounded subset of SendGrid mail settings, not arbitrary passthrough. `false` explicitly requests normal SendGrid delivery. An omitted setting keeps existing behavior.

For `true`, the workflow worker resolves subscriber identity, preferences and templates normally, records a dispatching attempt, and sends a sandbox request with no dispatch-tracking ID. Only HTTP 200 completes the job with attempt and message `delivery_state=validated`; message status is `warning`, and the operator result UI says that no email was delivered. No delivery result is asserted. A database guard prevents changing an attempted job's sandbox mode or marking a sandbox attempt as accepted. Only confirmed validations are excluded from overdue delivery counts; rejected or unknown sandbox attempts remain visible to alerting and require investigation. A crash after the dispatch marker remains unknown and is not automatically retried. The existing operator manual-accept operation cannot turn an unknown sandbox attempt into a real accepted delivery. This guard depends on enabled database triggers and trusted owners; administrators can bypass it.

Apply worker migration `0243`, upgrade all workers, projectors, monitors, API instances and dashboard before using this option; older workers reject the new queue field, older monitors can alert on validated jobs, and older UI versions cannot display the new state. Review migration locks and privileges before production rollout. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-sendgrid-tracking-local.sh` covers API validation, precedence, the workflow queue, local mock HTTP 200, 202, 400 and connection close, message/job status, protection against accepted-state and mode changes, projection and both health query paths using an administrator session with an alert-view grant; dashboard unit tests cover the displayed state. Provider-body comparisons test true and false against the pinned original provider SDK. Real SendGrid sandbox, actual network timeout through the queue, least-privilege health roles, forged events, subscriber preference changes, mixed-version deployments, browser-to-provider end-to-end behavior and original Novu end-to-end behavior remain unverified. The dashboard can select the global SendGrid setting for a trigger using an environment API key; it does not support step-specific selection. The API also supports separately provisioned operator JWT execution.

### SendGrid sandbox in-flight failover

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=sendgrid-sandbox-inflight scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` uses a disposable synchronous PostgreSQL primary and standby on one host. An in-process queue handler sends a sandbox request to a loopback server which delays HTTP 200; the test stops the primary after the attempt is replicated and promotes the standby before releasing the response. It forcibly expires the lease and starts two actual workers: the attempt becomes `unknown`, cannot be manually marked accepted or changed to a real-send payload, and makes no proxy connection during the observation period. A newly queued normal send makes one proxy connection, confirming the workers continue processing. This is not a real SendGrid request, real lease wait, network timeout, asynchronous replication, split-brain/fencing, multi-host deployment or automated production failover test; test workers use an administrative DB login. Docker is not used.

### Workflow generation guard

Generation-bound operator JWT triggers require worker migration `0245`, function EXECUTE privileges for each in-app worker DB login, and all older in-app workers to be drained and upgraded before the feature is enabled. Older workers ignore the generation field; there is no automatic mixed-version fence. See the API README's optional workflow generation binding section for deployment order, tests and limitations.

### Per-target Discord cooldown alert mode

In `alert-fanout` configuration, each Slack or email target may set `"delivery_checks":{"discord_cooldown_mode":"all"}` or `"overdue"`. `all` alerts for active cooldowns, deferred jobs or overdue jobs; `overdue` alerts only when a deferred job exceeds its grace period. A health query failure alerts in both modes. Omission or null inherits `SWALLOW_ALERT_DISCORD_COOLDOWN_MODE` and its existing default (`all`). Set the target's `delivery_thresholds.discord_cooldown_grace_seconds` or the inherited `SWALLOW_ALERT_DISCORD_COOLDOWN_GRACE_SECONDS` to enable this check; selecting a mode alone does not enable it. Existing scoped `alert_view` and `alert_send` permissions and condition-policy requirements still apply. Database alert children do not use this delivery check.
