Invalid modes reject the entire fanout configuration before any destination is started. Older fanout binaries reject this new field: upgrade all cooperating fanout and scheduler processes before changing the shared file. Destination binding does not include this mode, so coordinate configurations for processes sharing a target ID; different settings can influence suppression and recovery history. The dashboard does not manage this field.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-alert-target-checks-local.sh` checks per-target values, inheritance and invalid-mode rejection with fixture children. It also runs actual fanout and delivery commands against disposable local PostgreSQL with blocked Slack destinations: an active cooldown with no overdue job selects only the `all` destination, and an overdue deferred job selects both. No external alert is delivered; real Slack/email receipt, mixed-version rollout and multi-host HA remain unverified. Docker is not used.

### Optional Discord cooldown alert binding

Worker migration `0246` and `SWALLOW_ALERT_BIND_DISCORD_COOLDOWN=true` allow an operator to bind each delivery-alert destination ID to its effective Discord cooldown check: `none` if disabled, or `all`/`overdue` together with its grace seconds. The default is `false` and preserves existing behavior. Apply the migration, give every participating sender's DB LOGIN its environment-specific `alert_send` grant and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_bind_discord_alert_mode(text,text,text,text,integer)`, and protect the security-definer owner and schema. The function owner needs access to the environment, operator-grant and binding tables. Use new target IDs when starting this protection if old condition history may already reflect different settings. Upgrade and configure **all** senders sharing each target before relying on it: older binaries, senders with the option disabled, privileged SQL and restoring older DB data can bypass it. This mechanism does not fence other check settings or secure the destination identity.

On each invocation the sender validates checks, then atomically binds all configured Slack/email destinations in one short transaction. First use creates the binding; matching later uses succeed. A different mode, grace value or disabled/enabled state fails that sender before health observation and delivery. A conflict on one destination rolls back binding changes for the others in that invocation, but separate fanout children are independent and may still run. The separate destination-credential binding may already have been written. A timeout or lost commit response leaves the binding outcome uncertain; inspect it using a trusted DBA or retry identical settings. There is no management UI, automatic version fence, binding audit or delete operation. For a deliberate change, coordinate and drain all senders and use a new target ID; changing existing bindings directly needs review of existing suppression and delivery-slot state. Each enabled invocation adds a DB transaction and an insert-or-conflict plus read per target.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-alert-target-checks-local.sh` verifies the actual fanout/delivery process rejects an inconsistent target and uses restricted DB logins to exercise permission and environment isolation, concurrent first use, identical reuse, disabled state, changed grace and revocation. The fixture sends no external alerts and the actual alert process uses an administrative DB login. Real providers, production role provisioning, binding maintenance, overlapping senders with older binaries and multi-host HA are unverified. Docker is not used.

### Optional immediate Resend same-request replay

Worker migration `0247` and `NOVU_RESEND_REPLAY_UNCERTAIN_ONCE=true` opt a Resend worker into one immediate replay of an uncertain **first dispatch** while that worker is still processing the same job. The default is `false`. A transport error, malformed success response, HTTP 408 or 5xx triggers a one-second wait, then a fresh subscriber/preference and selected workflow-step-policy check. The worker re-renders from the original job, and continues only if the prepared request is identical, no prior retry decision exists, the original dispatch is still active and the queue lease has more than 20 seconds remaining. It reserves the replay in the attempt row, then repeats the lease, subscriber, workflow policy and identical-body checks immediately before sending the same body, correlation tag, provider configuration and idempotency key. If the second check fails, the original outcome remains unknown. A 409, 429 or other rejected first response is not replayed. If the replay is rejected or uncertain, the original uncertain result remains `unknown`; only a valid success response records provider acceptance. The `same_request_replays` value records a reserved replay, not proof that a second request reached Resend.

The worker DB login needs SELECT and UPDATE on `novu_rust_resend_attempts.same_request_replays` and SELECT on `novu_rust_delivery_reconciliations`, in addition to its normal queue, subscriber, workflow and attempt rights. When enabled, startup checks the new column and its privileges and reconciliation-table SELECT access before claiming jobs; missing migration or access causes reconnect attempts. With this option enabled, keeping the prepared body for possible replay also increases peak memory usage, including for large attachments. Both replay rechecks add DB reads and template work for each qualifying uncertain dispatch. Migration `0247` takes a table lock and validates its new constraint; assess table size and deployment impact. Deploy compatible migrations before enabling the option. No management UI controls this worker option. The option is per worker; mixed settings do not provide consistent replay behavior across jobs. Existing workflow compatibility settings, including subscriber-ID reuse behavior, continue to apply.

This feature does **not** automatically replay after a process crash, lease recovery, earlier operator retry or a failed recheck. It does not freeze data after the recheck transaction, cancel a request already sent, prove mailbox delivery, or guarantee exactly-once delivery. A process pause between its lease check and the network request, database restore, direct DB changes, old binaries or reused idempotency keys can invalidate assumptions. The outer worker DB timeout also bounds the whole job; if it interrupts either request the result can remain unknown. Resend documents a 24-hour idempotency window for identical requests ([Resend documentation](https://resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/idempotency-keys)); provider behavior, account scope, expiration boundaries and outages remain external dependencies. Use reconciliation and alerts for all remaining unknown outcomes. The option is not a Novu compatibility claim.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-resend-immediate-replay-local.sh` runs a disposable PostgreSQL 17 and mock HTTP provider. It checks identical headers, correlation-tagged body and key after a 5xx, 408, lost response and malformed success; valid second acceptance; preservation of unknown after second rejection; default-off, first-response 409 or 429, changed subscriber address or preference, short lease and prior retry decision. A real worker process also verifies missing replay-column privileges prevent startup of job processing and invalid configuration is rejected. The queue-handler test uses an administrative DB login. The same fixture also runs a real in-app worker and two competing Resend worker processes against a loopback mock that drops the first response; it checks one job, one replay, identical request bytes and idempotency key, and stored acceptance. The worker processes use an administrative DB login. The fixture builds with the test-only `test-mocks` feature to permit a strictly loopback HTTP endpoint and rebuilds the normal binary before running the mock; never deploy a test-feature build or use real provider credentials with it. It does not run a real Resend service, a process pause after the final recheck, all preference races, mixed versions or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

The local process fixture also uses a `test-mocks`-only barrier after replay reservation. It pauses the owning Resend worker, forcibly expires the lease so a second actual worker recovers the job as unknown, and verifies the resumed worker makes no second request. A separate case changes the subscriber email while the worker is paused and verifies the same outcome. This tests that specific window with mock HTTP, an administrator DB login and accelerated lease expiry. There is still a gap between the final database check and the external request; suspension or changes in that gap are not fenced by PostgreSQL and exactly-once delivery is not guaranteed.

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend-replay-inflight scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` checks the immediate replay reservation across a controlled synchronous PostgreSQL 17 promotion on one host. Two real test-feature Resend workers use a loopback provider that drops the first response. A test-only barrier pauses the owning worker after its replay reservation commits; the fixture confirms that reservation on the standby, stops the primary and manually promotes the standby. It forcibly expires the lease, observes the second worker mark the original attempt unknown, stops that worker, and resumes the paused worker. The resumed worker processes a new job without making another request for the original. This test uses an administrative DB login, synthetic messages and a mock provider. It does not verify real Resend idempotency, automatic promotion, actual lease-duration waiting, asynchronous replication, a pause after the final database recheck, network partitions, fencing or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

Worker migration `0248` snapshots the current Resend attempt ID and same-request replay reservation into each normal retry or acceptance reconciliation record before the attempt can be removed. The snapshot commits or rolls back with the decision. It records reservation, not proof of HTTP transmission or delivery. Existing records are null and cannot be reconstructed; direct attempt deletion without a reconciliation, privileged modification, database restore and asynchronous failover can lose evidence. API migration 0164 exposes only the latest decision under the existing scoped result grant; consult protected reconciliation records for earlier decisions. Deploy worker migration before API 0164. The local PostgreSQL API test covers a real retry and replacement attempt, not an actual Resend request or production HA.

The Resend adapter accepts an explicit `attachment` disposition when no CID is present and an explicit `inline` disposition when its CID is nonempty and shorter than 128 characters. It serializes inline CID as `content_id`; it does not transmit a disposition property. An attachment with a CID, inline without a usable CID, or unknown disposition fails locally before dispatch. A CID must be nonempty and shorter than 128 characters even when disposition is omitted; the existing 255-byte input limit also applies. Previously queued messages with a longer CID now fail locally before dispatch after workers are upgraded. Resend documents CID-based inline attachments at [Embed Images using CID](https://resend.com/changelog/embed-images-using-cid); rendering depends on the receiving email client. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-email-attachments-local.sh` covers trigger API, workflow fanout, mock Resend and SendGrid request bodies and invalid combinations with disposable PostgreSQL. The original provider option mapping is also compared for these two dispositions using a mocked SDK and normalized wire values. The mock does not establish actual Resend acceptance or email-client rendering; original Novu end-to-end attachment parity is unverified. Queue and backup retention limitations above continue to apply.

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend-reconciliation scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` verifies a committed Resend retry decision and its replay-reservation snapshot on a local synchronous PostgreSQL 17 standby. A real API process with a restricted DB login and a signed fixture JWT reads the result before the primary is stopped and after manual standby promotion and reconnection. The test checks that the pending job, snapshot and environment authorization persist and that missing or cross-environment credentials are denied. A second reconciliation pauses inside its transaction after inserting the audit and deleting the attempt, before updating the job; an immediate primary stop and promotion leaves the old failed job and unknown attempt intact with no partial audit. After removing the test-only pause, a new decision succeeds on the promoted server and the API reads its snapshot. It does not dispatch mail or test every in-flight timing, asynchronous replication, automatic promotion, split-brain fencing, multi-host networks or production HA. Docker is not used.

SMTP workflows and SMTP email alerts share `SWALLOW_SMTP_TLS`. In addition to the default `starttls` and implicit `tls`, `SWALLOW_SMTP_TLS=none` explicitly selects unencrypted SMTP, defaulting to port 25; `SWALLOW_SMTP_PORT` can override the port. Fanout email targets select the same behavior with `"smtp_tls":"none"`. **The connection, message content and any SMTP AUTH credentials are transmitted without TLS in this mode.** Use it only when this is an intentional property of the deployment; TLS modes remain the default. A CA file with `none` is rejected rather than silently ignored. Switching transport mode changes the configuration fingerprint used by optional destination binding, so follow the existing binding-change procedure. Upgrade all workers and alert children before using this setting: older versions reject it. Local relay tests cover plaintext with and without SMTP AUTH, SMTP acceptance and a lost reply; a disposable PostgreSQL test runs the actual fanout and alert child and a separate actual SMTP workflow worker against local unauthenticated relays. The first worker fixture failed because the accepted test socket inherited nonblocking mode; it passed after the fixture was corrected. Relay acceptance is not proof of final delivery, and external relays and production HA have not been verified for this mode. Run `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-plain-smtp-alert-local.sh`.

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=smtp-plain-rls scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` tests two actual RLS-scoped SMTP workers and a loopback plaintext relay with a synchronous PostgreSQL 17 primary/standby on one host. The relay reads the first standalone email's DATA and withholds its response; the fixture kills the sending worker, stops the primary and manually promotes the standby. It forcibly expires the lease and observes the surviving worker mark that attempt unknown without sending it again, then confirms a new job is accepted. The restricted worker cannot read another environment's queued job. The existing `smtp-rls` STARTTLS scenario was rerun after extending the fixture. These tests use synthetic messages and forced lease expiration; they do not verify actual lease-duration waiting, authentication on the plaintext relay, a real SMTP provider, workflow-generated jobs in this failover case, automatic promotion, fencing, asynchronous replication, network partitions or multi-host production HA. Plaintext transport still exposes message content and any SMTP credentials.

Worker migration `0249` adds an optional environment-level SMTP transport rollout fence. The default `allowed_mode=NULL` retains the previous behavior. To require one mode (`starttls`, `tls` or `none`), first upgrade and drain all SMTP workers, then have a trusted DBA set `public.novu_rust_smtp_transport_policy.allowed_mode` for that environment and configure every replacement worker with `SWALLOW_SMTP_TRANSPORT_GUARD=true`. Grant its DB login EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_transport_check(text,text)` and `public.novu_rust_smtp_transport_guard(text,uuid,uuid,text)`; the policy table remains private. A guarded worker refuses a missing or different mode at startup and rechecks it under a row lock while reserving each attempt. When a policy is enabled, the attempt-insert trigger rejects legacy or unguarded workers that lack the transaction marker. A policy update waits for a reservation transaction, but a previously committed SMTP request cannot be cancelled. Stop and drain old workers before changing policy: otherwise they can repeatedly claim jobs and leave them for lease recovery. To disable the fence, drain workers and set the mode back to NULL; guarded workers must then be restarted with the guard disabled. Without worker migration 0250, policy changes require trusted DBA access and have no dedicated change audit. Migration 0250 adds the scoped CLI described below; API migration 0165 adds a separately authorized operator JWT API and the dashboard provides a matching panel.

This is a cooperative mixed-version rollout check, not a security boundary proving network encryption: a privileged or malicious database client can forge the transaction setting, disable triggers or alter policy, and the DB cannot verify what bytes a process sends. Restrict DB roles and filesystem/environment configuration. Switching to `none` exposes message content and any SMTP credentials; this setting does not affect SMTP alert processes. Replication loss, database restore, in-flight processes and multi-host fencing remain operational concerns. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-plain-smtp-alert-local.sh` tests a real guarded worker with a mock relay, startup rejection of a different mode, restricted DB login access, legacy insert rejection, policy-change locking and the default policy. It does not test a real SMTP provider or production HA.

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=smtp-plain-fenced scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` additionally runs the transport fence with two RLS-scoped workers across a controlled synchronous single-host PostgreSQL promotion. It verifies that the survivor continues with the policy in place, the interrupted attempt becomes unknown without another request, and a later job is accepted by a loopback relay. This does not establish automatic failover, split-brain protection, asynchronous replication safety or a real relay's behavior.

Worker migration `0250` adds a revisioned and audited SMTP transport policy CLI. A trusted DBA separately provisions each LOGIN/OID and independent `can_read`, `can_write` and `can_audit` flags in `public.novu_rust_smtp_transport_admins`, then grants only the required EXECUTE rights on `novu_rust_smtp_transport_policy_read(text)`, `novu_rust_smtp_transport_policy_set(text,uuid,bigint,text,text)`, `novu_rust_smtp_transport_policy_request(text,uuid)` and `novu_rust_smtp_transport_policy_audit(text,bigint)`. For example, a provisioned login runs `delivery-reconcile smtp-transport-read ENV`, then `smtp-transport-set ENV REQUEST_UUID REVISION disabled|none|starttls|tls REASON`; use `smtp-transport-request ENV REQUEST_UUID` after an uncertain response and `smtp-transport-audit ENV [BEFORE]` for pages of up to 50 records. The same request ID and identical content return the original result; reusing it for different content fails. A different request with a stale revision fails. An absent request lookup is not proof that a concurrent transaction cannot still commit. The result is a snapshot, not a guarantee the policy remains current; read again before acting. Each successful change increments the revision, including setting the same mode. Revocation is checked under a lock, and policy update, request and audit commit or roll back together.

The audit attributes the database LOGIN/OID, not a person using a shared login. Bootstrap grants and direct DBA table edits are outside this audit and can bypass revisions; protect trusted owners and retain the audit independently. The command does not drain workers or prove transport security: follow the rollout and plaintext warnings above. API migration 0165 adds separately authorized HTTP/JWT management and a dashboard panel. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-plain-smtp-alert-local.sh` uses a disposable local PostgreSQL and restricted logins to exercise the CLI, independent read/write/audit grants, environment isolation, concurrent identical requests, conflict, stale revision, revocation and audit-failure rollback. Real identity providers, concurrent production load, external auditing and production HA are unverified.


`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=smtp-policy scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` exercises a real API process with a restricted DB LOGIN and fixture JWTs against local synchronous PostgreSQL 17 primary/standby. It changes the SMTP transport policy, delegates read permission to another identity, confirms both JWT audit records on the standby, stops the primary and manually promotes the standby. After API reconnection it checks the saved policy, identical-request replay, environment isolation, grant revocation and target denial. No SMTP worker or relay is involved. This does not test in-flight policy changes, asynchronous replication, automatic promotion, fencing, external IdP or multi-host production HA.

### Optional SMTP retry of unknown outcomes

Worker migration `0251` adds an **opt-in at-least-once** recovery policy and `smtp-auto-retry-worker`. SMTP does not supply a Swallow idempotency key: a relay can accept an email and lose its response, so automatically retrying an `unknown` attempt can deliver the same email more than once. The default policy is disabled. Enabling it is an explicit operational choice for the whole environment, including standalone and workflow-generated SMTP jobs; the worker's existing subscriber and workflow checks still apply when a queued workflow job is dispatched again.

A trusted DBA provisions the dedicated recovery LOGIN/OID in `public.novu_rust_smtp_auto_retry_workers`, grants USAGE on the schema and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_auto_retry_batch(text,integer)`, and configures `public.novu_rust_smtp_auto_retry_policy` for the environment. `enabled=true` requires an explicit `eligible_since` timestamp. `max_retries` is 1–10 and `delay_seconds` is 10–86400. For example, setting `eligible_since=clock_timestamp()` opts in only failures whose attempt `updated_at` is at or after activation; choosing an earlier cutoff includes older unknown failures. Updates to an attempt can change that timestamp and revision. Set the cutoff deliberately. Run `smtp-auto-retry-worker` under a supervisor with `DATABASE_URL`, `NOVU_SMTP_ENVIRONMENT_ID`, and optionally `SWALLOW_SMTP_AUTO_RETRY_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (1–3600; default 15) and `SWALLOW_SMTP_AUTO_RETRY_BATCH_SIZE` (1–25; default 10). The recovery worker needs no SMTP credentials or direct queue-table access. Migration 0252 adds the separately authorized CLI described below; API migration 0167 adds a separately authorized operator JWT policy and history API; the dashboard supplies policy and history access; history retention remains unavailable; optional automatic-retry alerting is described below.

The recovery function locks the policy and eligible failed jobs, skips locked jobs, and atomically records the prior attempt revision and failure revision, removes the attempt and requeues the job. It skips an exact failure already acknowledged in the SMTP review ledger, checking again after acquiring the job lock. A concurrent operator decision and automatic retry race for the job lock; only the winner can resolve that snapshot. The retry count is per job across policy changes and applies only to automatic retries, not manually authorized retries. Jobs exceeding the limit remain unknown for operator investigation. Failed/pre-dispatch jobs without an unknown attempt, historical attempts before the cutoff, and scrubbed jobs are not retried. Audit rows are in `novu_rust_smtp_auto_retries` and contain database principal, policy bounds and prior revisions, not message content; their foreign key can prevent job deletion until retention is handled. Audit insertion failure rolls back the batch. Protect table ownership and privileges; direct privileged changes can bypass these controls.

Disable or change the policy under a trusted DBA when needed. Policy changes wait for an in-progress reservation transaction, but already requeued or in-flight sends are not cancelled; direct DB edits, stale replicas, restore, network partitions, clock changes, disabled triggers and old binaries remain outside this coordination. SMTP acceptance is not mailbox delivery, and exactly-once delivery is not claimed. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-auto-retry-local.sh` runs two restricted recovery workers and a real SMTP worker against a disposable local PostgreSQL and mock relay. It verifies the disabled default, cutoff, reviewed failure exclusion, retry cap, environment authorization and audit failure rollback; the fixture advances attempt timestamps instead of waiting for the configured delay. The mock intentionally accepts DATA then loses a reply, demonstrating that two deliveries can occur. Actual SMTP provider behavior, real-time delay, sustained load, mixed-version rollout and production HA remain unverified.


Worker migration `0252` adds scoped, revisioned administration and redacted recovery history for the optional SMTP unknown-outcome retry. A trusted DBA provisions `public.novu_rust_smtp_auto_admins` with independent `can_read`, `can_write`, and `can_audit` rights for the administrator LOGIN/OID, and grants EXECUTE on `novu_rust_smtp_auto_policy_read(text)`, `novu_rust_smtp_auto_policy_set(text,uuid,bigint,boolean,integer,integer,text,text)`, `novu_rust_smtp_auto_policy_request(text,uuid)`, `novu_rust_smtp_auto_policy_audit(text,bigint)` and/or `novu_rust_smtp_auto_history(text,bigint)`. For example, `delivery-reconcile smtp-auto-read ENV` returns the current revision and cutoff; `smtp-auto-set ENV REQUEST_UUID REVISION enabled|disabled MAX DELAY now|preserve|all REASON` changes it. `now` starts the cutoff at the DB time of the change, `preserve` retains it, and `all` includes all historical unknown attempts. Enabling with `preserve` is rejected if no cutoff exists. **Selecting `all` can immediately make old unknown messages eligible for duplicate delivery** after the delay; `now` and `preserve` also do not eliminate duplicate risk. The upper retry count is 10 and delay is 10–86400 seconds. The policy and permission changes do not cancel already queued or in-flight sends.

`smtp-auto-request ENV REQUEST_UUID` retrieves that DB login's original request; `smtp-auto-audit ENV [BEFORE]` and `smtp-auto-history ENV [BEFORE]` page up to 50 descending records. History includes job and attempt identifiers, prior failure revision, DB principal and applied limits, but no message body or recipient. Audit and history permissions are independent of read and write. Identical concurrent requests by the same login return the same recorded result; a reused ID with different values or a stale revision fails. A lookup returning no record does not establish that an in-flight request cannot commit. Every successful change increments revision, including a no-op; a replay returns a historical snapshot, not necessarily current policy. Policy update, request and audit commit together, and direct DBA edits bypass revisions and audit. DB-login audit attribution is not individual identity when logins are shared; bootstrap, external audit and deletion/retention remain operational responsibilities. API migrations 0167–0168 and the dashboard supply separately authorized HTTP/JWT policy, history and delegated grant management. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-auto-retry-local.sh` covers restricted role separation, policy choices, same-request concurrency, cutoff, stale/conflicting requests, audit failure rollback, real worker retry and redacted history on disposable PostgreSQL. Production load and multi-host HA remain unverified.

Worker migration `0253` adds aggregate monitoring for optional SMTP automatic retry. Grant a monitoring LOGIN the environment-specific `alert_view` permission and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_smtp_auto_retry_health(text,integer)`, then run `delivery-reconcile check-smtp-auto ENV GRACE_SECONDS` (1–86400). It reports `enabled`, `eligible`, `overdue` and `exhausted`, exits 2 for overdue or exhausted, 0 otherwise, and nonzero on errors. `eligible` counts unreviewed, unsanitized unknown failures within the active cutoff whose delay has elapsed and retry cap remains; `overdue` is the subset past the delay plus grace; `exhausted` counts matching unknown failures at the cap. These are point-in-time counts, not delivery confirmation or worker liveness. Disabled policy returns zero counts even with unresolved failures; SMTP health and process liveness require separate monitoring. Review acknowledgments, retention scrubbing and cutoff exclude jobs; pending retries and dispatches are not included. Timestamp and history changes can alter counts; use a reliable DB clock and size the query against production data. No recipient or payload is returned. API migration 0169 and the dashboard expose this aggregate under a separate JWT health grant. This command can also be connected to an external monitor; optional built-in alert integration is described below.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-auto-retry-local.sh` exercises restricted access, disabled policy, overdue and exhausted outcomes with two real recovery workers, a real SMTP worker and a local mock relay. It does not test an external alert service, real SMTP provider, or multi-host production HA. Docker is not used.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_SMTP_AUTO_GRACE_SECONDS` to 1–86400 to enable SMTP automatic-retry backlog checks in `send-delivery-alert`; omit it to disable. The alert LOGIN needs the environment `alert_view` grant and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_smtp_auto_retry_health(text,integer)`, as well as existing destination `alert_send` rights. Overdue or exhausted counts trigger Slack and/or email; an unavailable or unauthorized check is an error and triggers a check-failure alert. Counts, not recipients or payloads, enter messages. The optional `new-condition` cooldown requires scoped delivery and V3 conditions; V3 high-word bits 16, 32 and 64 separately represent overdue, exhausted and check failure. A failed check does not clear observed backlog conditions. Coordinate upgrades and check configuration among senders sharing a target ID. Alert fanout targets can set `delivery_thresholds.smtp_auto_grace_seconds` to a number, `disabled`, or omit it to inherit the parent setting. Disabled policy reports zero even if SMTP failures remain: monitor SMTP health and process liveness separately. The dashboard does not configure this threshold.

The local tests use real SMTP and recovery worker processes, disposable PostgreSQL and a blocked Slack slot to verify selection, plus mock Slack and Resend endpoints to verify alert contents and V3 masks; fanout threshold forwarding is tested with a fixture child. They do not establish actual external alert receipt or production HA.

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=smtp-auto-policy scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` runs a real API and recovery worker with restricted DB logins against a disposable, synchronous PostgreSQL 17 primary/standby on one host. It sets the auto-retry policy and delegates read permission through fixture JWTs, verifies both audit records reached the standby, starts the recovery worker, immediately stops the primary and manually promotes the standby. After promotion it verifies API and pre-existing observed worker reconnection, policy and grant persistence, identical-request replay, environment isolation, revocation, stale prior-session versus healthy new-session counts through the API, and requeue of a synthetic unknown attempt. The requeue is explicitly enabled with `all` and can duplicate externally accepted mail. No SMTP worker or relay is involved. This test does not establish in-flight retry atomicity during promotion, automatic promotion, fencing/split-brain protection, asynchronous replication safety, external IdP or production multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=smtp-auto-inflight scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` separately starts the real retry-batch database function under a restricted worker LOGIN inside an uncommitted transaction, waits until that function has selected and changed one synthetic job and the transaction is sleeping, then immediately stops the synchronous primary and manually promotes the standby. The promoted database retains the failed job and unknown attempt and has no retry-history row; a subsequently started recovery worker requeues it once and records one history row. The fixture also checks policy/grant persistence and idempotent replay. It does not exercise the actual worker process during that particular interrupted transaction, SMTP delivery, asynchronous replication, automatic failover, split-brain fencing or multiple hosts. Test DBs are disposable and Docker is not used.

Worker migration `0254` optionally records SMTP automatic-retry worker activity. Grant the registered recovery LOGIN EXECUTE on `novu_rust_smtp_auto_retry_batch_observed(text,integer)` and set `SWALLOW_SMTP_AUTO_WORKER_HEALTH=true`; default `false` retains the previous batch and permissions. The observed wrapper checks the same worker authorization and commits a heartbeat in the same transaction as each successful batch, including a disabled-policy batch. Its trusted SECURITY DEFINER owner needs `pg_read_all_stats`; protect that owner and grant only required functions. Failure to record rolls back that batch; missing EXECUTE or statistics rights prevents the opted-in worker from operating. Monitor worker logs independently. Deploy migration and grants before enabling this option.

A monitoring LOGIN with environment `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_smtp_auto_worker_health_check(text,integer)` can run `delivery-reconcile check-smtp-auto-workers ENV MAX_AGE_SECONDS MIN_HEALTHY` (age 1–86400 seconds, minimum 1–1000). Exit 0 means the observed live session count meets the minimum, 2 means it does not, and other failures are errors. A session counts only when its observation is recent, its exact PostgreSQL PID/start/login/OID remains connected, and its worker registration remains present. `stale` includes expired, disconnected or unregistered observations; it does not itself trigger exit 2. Set the age above the configured polling interval and expected batch/DB latency. This is a successful DB batch observation, not evidence of actual SMTP acceptance or mailbox delivery. It cannot identify multiple workers multiplexed through one DB session or distinguish an idle proxy-held connection from a live worker; session pooling, privileges, clocks, failover and scaling can distort counts. Old rows accumulate; optional audited cleanup is described below. API migration 0170 and the dashboard expose this worker count with independent JWT authorization; optional Slack/email alerting is described below.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-smtp-auto-retry-local.sh` tests two observed real worker processes, detects one stopped worker, checks registration revocation and environment isolation, and exercises the existing retry/alert flow against disposable PostgreSQL and a local mock SMTP relay. It does not test a connection pooler, external monitor or production multi-host HA.

Set both `SWALLOW_ALERT_SMTP_AUTO_WORKER_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` (1–86400) and `SWALLOW_ALERT_SMTP_AUTO_WORKER_MIN_HEALTHY` (1–1000) on `send-delivery-alert` to alert on too few observed recovery-worker sessions; omit both to disable. The alert LOGIN requires `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_smtp_auto_worker_health_check(text,integer)`, plus existing alert-send rights. An unavailable check alerts and fails the process. Stale records are displayed but do not alert independently when the minimum is met. `new-condition` requires scoped delivery and V3; high-word bits 128 and 256 separately represent insufficient workers and check failure. Failed observations preserve prior worker-health condition state. Choose an age that accommodates polling and DB latency. Each fanout target can override `delivery_thresholds.smtp_auto_worker_max_age_seconds` and `smtp_auto_worker_min_healthy`, select `disabled` for both, or inherit. Optional `SWALLOW_ALERT_FANOUT_PREFLIGHT_PAIRS=true` now checks this pair too; enabling preflight can reject previously accepted incomplete inherited settings before children start. Coordinate upgrades, per-target settings and V3 use among all senders sharing a destination. Earlier fanout binaries reject the new fields.

Local tests exercise real workers and a blocked alert target, fixture-child threshold forwarding and pair preflight, plus mock Slack/Resend payloads and V3 condition bits. Actual alert reception, session poolers and production multi-host HA are unverified.

Worker migration `0255` adds optional, separately authorized cleanup of old SMTP auto-worker observations. A trusted DBA registers the operator LOGIN/OID in `novu_rust_smtp_auto_worker_retention_operators` and grants EXECUTE on `novu_rust_prune_smtp_auto_worker_health(text,integer,integer)` and, if needed, `novu_rust_smtp_auto_worker_retention_audit_page(text,bigint,integer)`; their SECURITY DEFINER owner needs `pg_read_all_stats`. Run `delivery-reconcile prune-smtp-auto-workers ENV OLDER_SECONDS LIMIT REASON` with age 86401–31536000 seconds and limit 1–1000. The reason is mandatory, up to 500 bytes and contains no control characters. Only observations older than the cutoff whose exact DB session is no longer connected are deleted, up to the batch limit; active sessions are preserved even if old or no longer authorized. The deletion and its audit entry commit together. `smtp-auto-worker-retention-audit ENV [BEFORE]` reads up to 50 descending audit entries. Each call, including a zero-deletion call, is audited; IDs are not commit order and pages are not a single snapshot. Direct DBA changes bypass auditing, audit rows themselves are not pruned, and command-line reasons can appear in process listings and shell history. A failed or lost response is ambiguous; this operation has no request ID and is not automatically retried. Cleanup can change displayed stale counts, but not active observations or retry history. Protect DB ownership, audit records and monitoring.

The local auto-retry test exercises a restricted role, bounds, environment isolation, revocation, audit-failure rollback, and retains an artificially old but connected worker while deleting old disconnected observations. It does not prove external audit, production scale or multi-host HA.

Resend workflow overrides now accept `_passthrough.body` containing string `subject`, `html`, `text`, `from`, `replyTo`, string arrays `to`, `cc`, `bcc`, a `headers` object, a `tags` array and/or `scheduledAt` under `overrides.providers.resend` and the matching `overrides.steps[STEP_ID].providers.resend`. Known provider fields are applied first; global and matching-step passthrough fields are then applied in that order, so passthrough wins over known values and step passthrough wins over global passthrough. Empty content and reply-to strings are preserved; `from` must be nonempty. From and reply-to reject CR/LF. These fields are literal provider data, not rendered templates. A passthrough `from` replaces the complete formatted sender, including its name; `replyTo` supports a string, not an array. Other passthrough body fields and top-level passthrough HTTP headers or query objects remain unsupported and are rejected at the trigger. Body `headers` is a map of valid HTTP header names to strings of at most 4096 bytes, with at most 100 fields per layer and in the final merged message. Global and step passthrough header objects merge by exact key; the step wins on an exact collision. Known provider header keys also undergo the original provider casing transform, so differently cased keys may coexist. The provider may interpret header names case-insensitively; avoid ambiguous duplicates. A final merged header count over 100 fails before dispatch even if the individual trigger layers passed validation. Existing content overrides and other providers are unchanged. This is a bounded subset of the original provider's passthrough contract; attachment and arbitrary provider options are not implemented; scheduled timestamps are described below. Each passthrough recipient array may contain at most 50 nonempty strings without CR/LF, the final Resend `to` list is limited to 50, and the combined `to`/`cc`/`bcc` list is limited to 1000. Requests above that combined limit are rejected locally so they cannot be sent without recipient snapshots; older workers did not enforce that limit. Arrays follow the original provider merge: the previously constructed mail-data recipients, known provider recipients, and passthrough recipients are concatenated, so the same address can appear more than once. Global and step passthrough arrays are merged with the step value replacing the global value before that concatenation. The final array is also used for per-recipient delivery evidence; it may differ from the subscriber email shown in message history. Review unintended duplicate and additional recipients before use. These strings persist in workflow and email queue payloads and are not erased by the existing attachment/alternative retention commands; protect and retain them appropriately. Upgrade all workflow and Resend dispatch workers before accepting these requests: older workers do not apply these fields and can send different content.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-resend-passthrough-local.sh` tests the trigger router, local PostgreSQL, workflow and Resend queue processing against a loopback mock, including invalid field rejection, global-versus-step precedence, empty HTML, literal text, sender/reply-to overrides, header merging and recipient snapshots. Signed synthetic recipient webhooks pass through the Rust API router and check the aggregate pending-to-delivered transition. A separate comparison bundles the checked-out original Resend provider and its actual BaseProvider transform with a mocked SDK and checks the same selected body fields. The original full Novu server, real Resend delivery, arbitrary passthrough and production HA are not validated by this test. Docker is not used.

Resend `_passthrough.body.tags` accepts up to 31 `{name,value}` string pairs; names and values are nonempty ASCII letters, digits, `_` or `-`, up to 256 characters. Names are unique case-insensitively and `swallow_dispatch` is reserved case-insensitively even when automatic correlation is disabled. These are Swallow restrictions; the [Resend send API documentation](https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/emails/send-email) describes the provider tag format. The matching-step array replaces the global passthrough array; an explicit empty array is preserved. When correlation is enabled, Swallow appends its own `swallow_dispatch` tag while retaining user tags. Never include secrets or personal data in tags: they persist in workflow and email queues and may be sent to the provider and appear in webhooks. Existing retention commands do not remove these tags. Upgrade all workflow and Resend workers before using this option; older dispatchers can omit user tags or replace them with the internal tag. Older accepted queued jobs with reserved or malformed tags fail locally rather than dispatch.

The local passthrough test exercises original provider transform versus a mocked SDK and the Rust trigger→workflow→mock Resend path with step precedence and both user and internal correlation tags; it checks reserved-name and invalid-tag rejection. Signed synthetic webhooks carrying both user and internal tags are accepted by the Rust API router, forged signatures are rejected, and the captured correlation is checked against the dispatch attempt. Actual external delivery and provider-originated webhooks with user tags are unverified.

Worker migration `0256` supports Resend `_passthrough.body.scheduledAt` as an RFC 3339 timestamp with an explicit offset, under global and matching-step Resend provider overrides. Step passthrough wins. It is sent as `scheduled_at` to Resend; natural-language schedules are not yet supported. Swallow checks syntax but leaves future-window and provider-specific validation to Resend. Resend's [scheduling announcement](https://resend.com/changelog/extended-email-scheduling) describes its supported scheduling window. Upgrade API, workflow and Resend workers together after migration; older workers can ignore or reject the field and older health checks can report scheduled mail overdue prematurely.

The dispatch transaction records the requested schedule on the queue job before the external request. This is the requested timestamp, not proof that Resend accepted or retained it. A provider-accepted attempt whose projected result is pending or not yet projected is excluded from Resend overdue health until the later of its queue availability and requested schedule plus the configured grace; failed or unknown attempts and non-pending projected outcomes continue using queue availability so they are not hidden. Both direct and scoped health paths use this rule. Worker migration `0257` also makes a duplicate provider message ID immediately eligible for investigation even when the projected result is null or pending; upgrade direct health-check binaries as well as applying the migration before relying on this behavior. The collision check only detects IDs visible in the same environment and database snapshot; missing IDs, disconnected databases and other forms of ambiguity still require independent monitoring. `email.scheduled` is evidence of provider acceptance, not final delivery; with explicitly enabled automatic correlation, a uniquely matched signed scheduled event with matching recipient evidence can resolve an unknown attempt to accepted while the delivery result remains pending. All existing ambiguity and conflict guards still apply. Swallow does not cancel or reschedule a mail already accepted by Resend, including after subscriber deletion or preference changes. Provider-side schedule edits and cancellations are not synchronized into this requested timestamp; operators must monitor those separately. Alerting can still be affected by clock skew, old binaries, unavailable webhooks and external provider behavior. The timestamp and other overrides remain in queue records; cleanup and backup considerations apply.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-resend-passthrough-local.sh` tests the Rust trigger, workflow and mock provider, scoped and direct overdue checks, a signed synthetic scheduled webhook, recipient projection and original provider option mapping. The test advances stored times instead of waiting and uses local fixtures. It does not prove actual scheduled delivery, external webhook behavior, provider-side cancellation or operational HA.

The local passthrough integration test also verifies both health paths against a deliberately duplicated provider ID, an unknown attempt, a failed projection, a pending projection and an overdue schedule. It uses a disposable PostgreSQL database and a mock provider; external provider behavior and concurrent production load are not validated.

### Resend scheduled cancellation adapter (not wired to operations)

`Resend::cancel_scheduled` implements a single `POST /emails/{id}/cancel` request per the [Resend API](https://resend.com/docs/api-reference/emails/cancel-email). It accepts only 1–1024 ASCII letters, digits, hyphens or underscores for the ID, uses the configured bearer key without redirects, and bounds the response body to 16 KiB. A successful response must contain `object=email` and the same ID to return `Confirmed`. A 4xx yields the request-level `Rejected(status)`; it does not prove the email was never cancelled by an earlier request. Network failures, redirects, 5xx, malformed, oversized or mismatched success bodies yield `Unknown`. No automatic retry occurs. A confirmed response is the provider response, not independent proof of mailbox delivery or non-delivery.

The adapter can also be used by the experimental CLI described below. There is no HTTP API, UI, webhook reconciliation, or queue/message/health update for cancellation. It also cannot cancel an unknown provider ID or a message whose ID falls outside the local character restriction. The mock HTTP unit test covers request path and authorization, response matching, 400/429/500/302, invalid ID and a failed connection. Actual provider cancellation, crashes after a request, concurrent delivery, rate limits, mixed versions and HA are unverified.

### Experimental Resend cancellation CLI

Worker migration `0258` and `resend-cancel` provide a restricted DB-login command for an accepted, future-scheduled Resend job. A trusted DBA registers the LOGIN and current OID in `public.novu_rust_resend_cancel_operators` with independent `can_cancel` and `can_read` flags, grants schema USAGE and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_reserve(text,uuid,uuid,text)`, `novu_rust_resend_cancel_finish(text,uuid,text,integer)` and/or `novu_rust_resend_cancel_get(text,uuid)` as needed. Protect the SECURITY DEFINER owner and all direct table access; registration, privileges and production deployment are not automatic. `resend-cancel cancel ENV REQUEST_UUID JOB_UUID REASON` uses `DATABASE_URL`, `NOVU_POSTGRES_ALLOW_INSECURE` only for an intentionally unencrypted test DB, and `NOVU_RESEND_API_KEY` or `NOVU_RESEND_API_KEY_FILE`. `resend-cancel get ENV REQUEST_UUID` requires separate read permission and no provider key. Reasons and IDs can appear in process lists and shell history: do not include secrets.

Before network I/O, reservation locks the job and stores a durable `unknown` request with actor LOGIN/OID, job, provider ID and reason. It requires a completed accepted attempt, a future requested schedule, a null or pending projected result and no visible same-environment provider-ID collision. A partial unique index permits at most one unknown or confirmed cancellation per job. Repeating the same request and parameters returns its existing state without another request; conflicting input is rejected. A newly rejected request permits a deliberate new request ID, but rejection does not establish prior provider state. Unknown and confirmed requests block new reservations for that job; the independently authorized manual resolution below can release an unknown request only after investigation. A validated successful HTTP response is recorded as `confirmed`; most 4xx as `rejected`; 408, 409, transport, 5xx and invalid responses as `unknown` (worker 0270 and updated worker required). Non-confirmed cancel invocations exit 2. A DB failure after the external call can leave unknown even if Resend canceled it. Use provider records and independent operational review; do not bypass this record or repeat calls blindly.

This is **not a complete cancellation workflow**. No API or dashboard integration, human identity binding, separate tamper-resistant audit, provider-state synchronization, queue/message/health updates or automatic cancellation on subscriber deletion exists. Thus a confirmed cancellation can still appear pending or overdue. Once the reservation commits, a provider-ID collision, webhook, retry, schedule change or delivery may race the external request; there is no distributed lock with Resend, and a DB restore or lost asynchronous replication can erase the marker and permit duplicate calls. Operators with cancel function access can invoke `finish` directly, so this is not proof that a provider response occurred. Old binaries and direct privileged SQL can bypass coordination. Resend credentials must belong to the correct account and are not bound to the job; no cross-account verification is performed. Provider rejection or a confirmed response is not proof of mailbox delivery or non-delivery.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-resend-cancel-local.sh` tests a restricted login and real CLI with disposable PostgreSQL and a loopback mock: confirmed response, dropped reply, same-ID replay, conflicting request, environment and login denial, concurrent reservation and a crash-like reservation with no external call. It uses a fixture-only binary and restores the normal build. No actual Resend, real concurrent delivery or cancellation, authorization revocation during the network request, production HA or multi-host failover was tested; Docker is not used.

### Manual resolution of unknown Resend cancellations

Worker migration `0259` adds a separate `can_resolve` permission and `novu_rust_resend_cancel_resolve(text,uuid,uuid,text,text)` EXECUTE grant. A trusted DBA explicitly grants both to the environment-specific resolver LOGIN/OID; the default is false. Existing cancellation and read rights do not grant resolution. `resend-cancel resolve ENV RESOLUTION_UUID REQUEST_UUID confirmed|not_cancelled REASON` records the operator assessment and reason atomically. `resend-cancel resolution-get ENV RESOLUTION_UUID` needs `can_read` and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_resolution_get(text,uuid)`; it returns the recorded decision, DB principal and reason. A replay of the same resolution ID, parameters and DB identity returns the same decision; conflicts are rejected. Failed audit insertion rolls back the state change. The audit is an ordinary DB table, not tamper-proof, and identifies a DB login rather than a person using a shared login. Reason and UUID arguments may be visible in shell history and process lists.

Only resolve an unknown request after **stopping and draining the originating cancellation process**, checking independently with Resend and documenting the evidence. The DB cannot establish that a request is no longer in flight or prove the operator's assessment. `confirmed` records a human assessment, not a newly verified provider response; it blocks future cancellation reservations. `not_cancelled` releases the job for a new, explicit cancellation request with a new request ID, subject to the existing eligibility checks; it does not automatically call Resend. A late `finish` after either manual decision is rejected rather than overwriting it, but it cannot undo a network operation already in progress. Duplicate or misleading cancellations remain possible after an incorrect decision, DB restore, asynchronous failover, direct privileged SQL or an older process. Existing queued/message/health state is unchanged, so confirmed cancellations can still be reported overdue. No UI, JWT identity, external audit or provider-side synchronization is included.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-resend-cancel-local.sh` uses restricted cancellation and separate resolver logins, disposable PostgreSQL and a mock provider. It checks denial without resolve rights, both decisions, same-ID replay and conflicting replay, audit-insert rollback, late response fencing, and explicit subsequent retry. It does not test real Resend evidence, real process draining, production roles, asynchronous failover or multi-host HA.

### Monitoring unknown Resend cancellation requests

Worker migration `0260` adds `novu_rust_resend_cancel_health(text,integer)`. A monitoring LOGIN needs the environment-specific `alert_view` grant and EXECUTE on this function. `delivery-reconcile check-resend-cancel ENV GRACE_SECONDS` reports `unknown` and its `overdue` subset; grace is 1–86400 seconds from reservation creation. Exit 2 indicates an overdue unknown, 0 means none, and other errors fail the command. Unknown includes calls in progress, crashed before sending and ambiguous provider responses; it does not determine whether an email was cancelled or delivered. Confirmed, rejected and manually resolved records are excluded. Values are point-in-time, rely on the DB clock and may change concurrently; records and reasons are not returned. This aggregate has no listing UI; the separately authorized CLI listing is described below. Protect the function owner and database privileges and evaluate index creation and query costs on existing data.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_CANCEL_GRACE_SECONDS` to 1–86400 on `send-delivery-alert` to enable an optional Slack and/or email alert for overdue unknown cancellations; omit it to disable. The alert LOGIN also needs existing destination `alert_send` rights. An unavailable or unauthorized check is a check-failure alert and process error; counts do not include recipient or message data. For `new-condition`, scoped delivery and V3 are required: V3 high-word bit 512 represents overdue unknown requests and bit 1024 represents check failure. A failed observation preserves prior overdue-condition history. Fanout targets can override `delivery_thresholds.resend_cancel_grace_seconds` with a number, `disabled`, or omit it to inherit. Old fanout binaries reject this new target field; upgrade all participating senders before configuring it. Coordinate per-target thresholds for processes sharing a target ID because existing destination binding does not bind this check. This alert does not prove provider or mailbox outcome and does not replace separate delivery or process monitoring.

The local cancellation script exercises the real restricted CLI, an artificially aged unknown request and real alert command using an already-blocked Slack slot, plus environment denial. Mock Slack and Resend alert tests check message contents, V3 condition bits and failure-observation masks; the fanout process test verifies threshold forwarding. It does not send a real external alert or validate actual provider cancellation, production load, mixed versions or multi-host HA. Docker is not used.

### Listing unknown Resend cancellations

Worker migration `0261` and the updated `resend-cancel` provide `resend-cancel list ENV GRACE_SECONDS [AFTER_REQUEST_UUID]`. The LOGIN requires the existing environment-specific `can_read` registration and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_list(text,integer,uuid)`; `alert_view` alone is not enough. Each page returns up to 50 unknown requests with request UUID, job UUID, reservation time and whether the supplied 1–86400 second grace has elapsed. It omits provider ID, cancellation reason and recipients. A non-null `next` can be supplied as the next cursor. Use the existing `get` command for authorized detail and `resolve` for a separate, independently authorized decision.

UUID order is not chronological and pages are separate snapshots: new, resolved or changed requests can be missed during a scan. Repeat scans or use an independently monitored durable feed if necessary. The grace is a display threshold, not a lock, deadline or evidence of cancellation; in-flight calls can appear unknown. Request and job UUIDs and timestamps can still be sensitive. Review migration index creation and query load. API/JWT access, UI, continuous discovery, provider reconciliation and production HA are not implemented or verified by this CLI.

The local cancellation script exercises the real CLI against restricted read and unauthorized accounts and a disposable DB with 56 unknown requests. It checks 50/6 pagination, UUID ordering, environment isolation, invalid grace and omission of provider IDs and reasons. The fixture inserts synthetic records and does not test a changing dataset, actual Resend or multi-host HA.

### Experimental queued Resend cancellations

Worker migration `0262` adds a command queue and `resend-cancel-worker`; API 0175 and the dashboard can submit commands as described in the API README. A trusted DBA registers an environment-specific submitter LOGIN/OID in `novu_rust_resend_cancel_submitters` and grants EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_enqueue(text,uuid,uuid,text)` and optionally `novu_rust_resend_cancel_command_get(text,uuid)`. The latter only exposes the caller LOGIN's own commands. A separate worker LOGIN/OID is registered in `novu_rust_resend_cancel_command_workers` and in `novu_rust_resend_cancel_operators` with `can_cancel=true`; grant EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_claim(text)` and `novu_rust_resend_cancel_finish(text,uuid,text,integer)`. Protect both LOGINs, SECURITY DEFINER owners and direct table access. The service must run with `DATABASE_URL`, `NOVU_RESEND_ENVIRONMENT_ID` and either `NOVU_RESEND_API_KEY` or `NOVU_RESEND_API_KEY_FILE`. By default the worker reads the key at startup; file rotation requires a restart unless the optional reload mode below is enabled. Credentials do not enter the queue. Choose TLS and role provisioning deliberately; migrations alone do not configure them.

Submission records a pending command and serializes active commands per environment/job. Repeating the same request ID, job, reason and submitter returns the existing command without adding another. The worker claims one command with `SKIP LOCKED`, creates the existing durable unknown cancellation marker, and transitions the command to dispatching in the **same DB transaction** before making one provider call. Ineligible jobs or competing cancellation reservations become failed commands without a provider call. A confirmed/rejected provider result or later manual resolution closes the command atomically; unknown stays dispatching and blocks a new command until reviewed. A crash before claim leaves the pending command for another worker. A crash or lost DB response after claim leaves unknown, and the worker does not reissue the call. An unknown does not tell whether a provider call was made; stop and drain all possible originating workers and check provider evidence before manually resolving. Commands are not themselves authorization to bypass the worker's eligibility checks.

The command getter only reports state and attempt state; it has no retention or failure diagnosis. Optional pending/failed monitoring is described below; monitor worker liveness separately. The unknown-cancellation alert detects dispatching unknown attempts after its grace. Both worker and command statuses can persist indefinitely and job foreign keys can block deletion. Accepted cancellation is not delivery proof, and cancellation still does not synchronize the message/job result or automatically handle subscriber deletion. Credential/account mismatch, in-flight calls, incorrect operator resolutions, privileged SQL, restored DB state, asynchronous failover, split brain and multi-host fencing are not solved by this queue. Use consistent configuration and reliable DB clocks; shutdown signals do not retract a call already started.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin scripts/test-resend-cancel-local.sh` uses separate restricted submitter, worker and resolver logins with two actual worker processes and a loopback mock. It checks replay, active-job conflict, authorization, single provider call under worker competition, worker termination while the mock holds a request, no automatic re-call on restart and manual resolution reopening the job. It artificially kills a process and does not test real Resend, network partitions, PostgreSQL failover, user JWT/UI submission, sustained load or multi-host HA. Test-only worker endpoint builds are copied to a temporary directory and normal binaries rebuilt; Docker is not used.

### Resend cancellation command backlog and failure review

Worker migration `0263` provides separately authorized monitoring for queued cancellation commands. A monitoring LOGIN with environment `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_command_health(text,integer)` can run `delivery-reconcile check-resend-cancel-commands ENV GRACE_SECONDS` (1–86400). It returns pending count, pending past the grace, and failed commands whose current failure has not been reviewed. Exit 2 indicates overdue pending or unreviewed failures; query errors are nonzero. This is independent of the existing unknown provider-call monitor. A pending command may be waiting for a worker or DB recovery; a failed command means eligibility/reservation failed before an external call, but the system intentionally does not retain detailed error text. Counts are snapshots based on DB clock, not provider status or worker liveness.

With `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_command_failed_list(text,uuid)`, `delivery-reconcile list-resend-cancel-failed ENV [AFTER]` lists up to 50 unreviewed request/job IDs and failure timestamps with a UUID cursor. Pages are separate snapshots, UUID order is not time order, and IDs can be sensitive. Review is separately authorized: a DBA registers the reviewer LOGIN/OID in `novu_rust_resend_cancel_command_reviewers` and grants EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_command_ack(text,uuid,timestamptz,text)`. `delivery-reconcile ack-resend-cancel-failed ENV REQUEST 'FAILED_AT' REASON` records the reviewer and reason against the exact failure timestamp under a row lock; stale timestamps fail and repeated acknowledgement returns `recorded=false`. Review clears that failure from this alert; it does not cancel an email, retry, delete a command or prove delivery. A failure with a different update timestamp is unreviewed again. Reasons may be visible in shell history and process listings. Review rows are ordinary DB records and direct privileged changes can bypass the audit. API/JWT and dashboard listing/review are not implemented.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_CANCEL_COMMAND_GRACE_SECONDS` to 1–86400 on `send-delivery-alert` to opt in to Slack/email alerts for overdue pending or unreviewed failures, plus query failure; omit it to disable. The alert LOGIN needs `alert_view`, function EXECUTE and existing destination `alert_send` rights. `new-condition` requires scoped delivery and V3; high-word bits 2048, 4096 and 8192 represent overdue pending, unreviewed failure and check failure, respectively. A failed check preserves prior condition history. Each fanout target may set `delivery_thresholds.resend_cancel_command_grace_seconds` to a number, `disabled` or omit it to inherit. Upgrade all senders before using the field: older fanout binaries reject it. Coordinate thresholds for shared destination IDs; the existing destination binding does not bind this check. The dashboard does not configure the threshold.

The local cancellation script creates an ineligible command using a real worker, ages a pending command artificially and checks restricted health/list/review access, a stale timestamp, audit-insert failure rollback, repeated review and a blocked Slack alert destination. Mock Slack/Resend unit tests cover message contents and V3 condition masks; a separate fanout test checks threshold forwarding. Actual alert receipt, user JWT/UI review, sustained load, external Resend and multi-host HA are not verified. Records and audit rows have no retention mechanism, and they may prevent job deletion. Docker is not used.

### Optional Resend account-label check for cancellation

Worker migration `0264` adds an **operator-provided label**, not an authenticated Resend account identifier. Configure `SWALLOW_RESEND_ACCOUNT_TAG` (1–128 ASCII letters, digits, `.`, `_` or `-`) on each Resend dispatch worker; newly dispatched attempts store the label before the external request. The dispatch DB LOGIN needs UPDATE on the new `account_tag` column. Choose a stable, non-secret label for an account, and use the same label when rotating keys that belong to that account. Existing attempts without a label are not automatically backfilled; do not infer an account from an unknown historic attempt. Applying the migration before starting labeled workers is required. Incorrect or unavailable tags fail startup or dispatch; monitor the process and queue.

To make the cancellation worker require a matching stored label, set `SWALLOW_RESEND_CANCEL_REQUIRE_ACCOUNT_TAG=true` and the same `SWALLOW_RESEND_ACCOUNT_TAG`; the default is false for existing deployments. Grant the worker LOGIN EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_claim_tagged(text,text)` and `novu_rust_resend_cancel_finish(text,uuid,text,integer)`. An absent or mismatched attempt label causes the command to fail before a provider call and appears in the command failure monitor; investigate rather than assuming the email was cancelled. For `resend-cancel cancel`, the same two settings select `novu_rust_resend_cancel_reserve_tagged(text,uuid,uuid,text,text)`, whose EXECUTE must be granted separately. For lookup commands the guard does not apply. To make this a cooperative deployment gate, drain and upgrade all relevant workers and CLI processes, restrict their DB roles, and revoke their EXECUTE on the unguarded `novu_rust_resend_cancel_claim(text)` or `novu_rust_resend_cancel_reserve(text,uuid,uuid,text)` where applicable. Grant only the checked functions they need. Older processes, disabled guards, privileged SQL, restoring old DB state and direct table writes can bypass the check. An already started request cannot be recalled.

Matching labels **do not prove the keys belong to the same Resend account**. Operators must independently provision and verify the mapping between labels, keys and accounts; different accounts can be mislabeled, and API key rotation or provider account changes are not validated. Protect credentials, DB ownership and configuration. Wrong-account prevention is only as reliable as this configuration. The label persists in PostgreSQL and backups; it is not a secret and is not currently shown in the UI. Commands failing this check use the existing generic failed-command review path. Existing accepted scheduled emails and old binaries need a deliberate migration plan; no automatic tag backfill or management UI is provided. Actual Resend account identity and production HA have not been verified.

The local scheduling test runs the real dispatch worker against a loopback mock and verifies a label was stored on the accepted attempt. The local cancellation test runs tagged real workers with separate restricted DB roles and mock provider: matching labels allow one call, mismatched and missing labels fail before calling, and the CLI mismatch is rejected. The provider and labels are fixtures; tests do not establish actual account identity, concurrent tag edits, key rotation, mixed-version fencing or multi-host HA.

### Cancellation worker during synchronous PostgreSQL promotion

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend-cancel-inflight scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` tests a real tagged cancellation worker with restricted DB logins and a loopback provider on a disposable, synchronous PostgreSQL 17 primary/standby on one host. The mock receives the first cancellation and holds its response. After the `dispatching` command and `unknown` request are visible on the standby, the test immediately stops the primary, manually promotes the standby and kills the in-flight worker. A fresh worker connects through a multi-host libpq URL, does not automatically repeat the original cancellation, and successfully completes a second command. The original remains dispatching/unknown for operator investigation. The test uses test-only endpoint binaries and restores the normal worker build.

This is a controlled test of one synchronous replica and a forced worker kill, not proof of automatic failover or exactly-once provider effects. It does not exercise a provider reply arriving during DB failure, an API submission transaction in flight, actual Resend, asynchronous replication or lost acknowledged transactions, fencing/split brain, separate hosts, real account identity, production traffic or manual unknown resolution. Worker reconnection and monitoring remain necessary. Docker is not used.

### Cancellation evidence in Resend results

Worker migration `0265` extends the existing scoped `delivery-reconcile resend-result ENV JOB` output. The existing environment `alert_view` permission and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_channel_result(text,uuid,text)` are required; the new fields do not expose provider IDs, recipient addresses or investigation reasons. `cancellationState` reports a live unknown or confirmed cancellation, or null when neither exists. `cancellationConfirmationSource` distinguishes `provider_response` from an `operator` assessment. For confirmed cancellations, `cancellationEvidence` is `conflicting` when a send or delivery outcome webhook is present, `ambiguous` for a provider-ID collision, mismatch or auto-correlation conflict, and otherwise `no_conflict_observed`. The last value is only an absence of currently recorded conflicting evidence, not proof that no email was sent; missing or delayed webhooks remain possible. Event order is not inferred. Results are snapshots and can change after later events or collisions.

The delivery projection, message/job status and overdue alerts are unchanged: a confirmed cancellation is not counted as delivered and is not excluded from overdue alerts by default; migration `0268` adds the optional policy below. Migration `0266` adds the optional evidence alert described below. Dashboard presentation is available with API migration `0178`; live Resend cancellation verification remains unverified. The disposable local PostgreSQL test exercises both confirmation sources, a conflicting event, collision, unchanged delivery result and unauthorized/cross-environment access with a mock cancellation endpoint; it does not establish production HA or actual provider behavior. Upgrade the migration before relying on the fields.

### Optional cancellation evidence alerts

Worker migration `0266` adds `delivery-reconcile check-resend-cancel-evidence ENV` (JSON counts and exit 2 on conflict or ambiguity) and `list-resend-cancel-evidence ENV [AFTER]` (50 request/job IDs and evidence class, UUID cursor). The LOGIN needs environment `alert_view` plus EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_evidence_health(text)` and, for the list, `novu_rust_resend_cancel_evidence_list(text,uuid)`. Results are snapshots; new or changed evidence between pages may move or be missed. The check scans confirmed cancellations and evaluates the current scoped result, so benchmark and set a suitable operation timeout for large histories. IDs may be sensitive.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_CANCEL_EVIDENCE_ENABLED=true` to include conflict, ambiguity and check failures in the existing Slack/email alert sender. Default is false. With fanout, each target can override the inherited setting through `delivery_checks.resend_cancel_evidence` (true or false). `new-condition` requires scoped delivery and V3; high-word bits 16384 and 32768 signal evidence attention and check failure. A failed check preserves the previous evidence condition. Use a consistent check configuration for senders sharing a target ID. Older fanout binaries reject the new target field. These alerts remain active while evidence remains conflicting or ambiguous; there is no acknowledgement or retention mechanism, and alerts do not change delivery or cancellation state. False negatives are possible with missing or delayed events; ambiguous identity is not proof of a cancellation failure. Dashboard alert configuration and live provider or production HA validation remain outstanding.

The disposable local PostgreSQL cancellation test covers a webhook event-table fixture, a provider-ID collision, scoped access, cursor and blocked Slack alert; the alert unit test uses mock Slack and email endpoints. The fanout process test verifies per-target override and inheritance. No real alert was sent.

### Additional Resend cancellation conflict events

Worker migration `0267` includes `email.delivery_delayed`, `email.opened` and `email.clicked` in cancellation evidence conflicts, in addition to the existing sent and delivery outcome events. A lone `email.scheduled` event is not a conflict. The monitor uses stored event type and provider ID; it does not determine order, prove which person opened a message or prove successful delivery. Opens and clicks may result from automated scanning or proxies. Ambiguous identity takes precedence over a conflict. Apply the migration to existing databases; the health check and dashboard use the same updated classification without a new flag. Existing historical webhook events are evaluated at query time. See [Resend's webhook event documentation](https://resend.com/changelog/webhook-event-visibility).

The local PostgreSQL test inserts each event separately and checks the result and health alert, verifies that scheduled alone is not a conflict and retains identity-collision precedence. The API/dashboard integration also posts a synthetically signed opened event through a real local webhook API, and checks the restricted alert runner against a blocked test destination. This does not verify real cancellation or real tracking events.

### Optional exclusion of provider-confirmed cancellations from overdue alerts

Worker migration `0268` adds `novu_rust_resend_health_exclude_confirmed_cancel(text,integer,integer)`, a separately granted, environment-scoped health view. In `send-delivery-alert`, set `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_EXCLUDE_CONFIRMED_CANCEL=true` to opt in; default false preserves the previous counts. This requires Resend checking enabled, `SWALLOW_ALERT_CHECK_SCOPED_ACCESS=true`, `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_CANCEL_EVIDENCE_ENABLED=true`, the environment `alert_view` grant, and EXECUTE on the new function plus `novu_rust_resend_cancel_evidence_health(text)`. Otherwise startup fails or the check reports an error. Fanout targets can override the inherited setting using boolean `delivery_checks.resend_exclude_confirmed_cancel`; older fanout binaries reject this field. Configure both required checks for each target and use consistent configurations for senders sharing a destination ID. Alert text reports when this policy is active.

Only scheduled jobs whose delivery projection is null/pending and whose cancellation was confirmed by a provider response, with no recorded conflicting or ambiguous evidence, are excluded. Manual operator confirmation, unknown/rejected cancellations, unknown or terminal delivery projections, ID collisions and evidence conflicts remain in the normal health calculation. When a webhook invalidates a projection, the resulting unknown status is counted until projection is recomputed; a scheduled-only event is not itself a cancellation conflict. Counts and evidence checks are separate snapshots. Late or absent webhooks and provider/account identity errors can still hide a real delivery; a 2xx cancellation response is not independent proof of non-delivery. Keep webhook and projector health monitored. The policy does not alter job/message status, and other CLI/API health queries retain their previous behavior. At scale, benchmark the extra evidence lookup and configure suitable DB timeouts.

The disposable local PostgreSQL test checks default versus opt-in counts, operator-confirmed and provider-confirmed cases, event invalidation, conflict/ID ambiguity, restricted permissions and actual alert-runner selection with a blocked test target.
