The fanout process test checks per-target override/inheritance. The test uses mock provider responses and synthetic database events, not live Resend cancellation, external alerts or production HA.

### Cancellation evidence after synchronous PostgreSQL promotion

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend-cancel-evidence scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` creates a disposable, synchronous primary and standby on one host. It inserts a synthetic provider-confirmed scheduled cancellation, checks the optional overdue exclusion with a restricted monitoring login, waits for replication, stops the primary and manually promotes the standby. The same API process uses a multi-host read-write connection string. It becomes unready and returns 503 for a synthetically signed `email.delivery_delayed` callback while the primary is down, then reconnects to the promoted standby and becomes ready without a restart. The test resubmits that callback twice; the promoted database reports one evidence conflict, the overdue count rises, and the real alert runner reaches a deliberately blocked Slack test destination. No actual Resend or Slack call occurs. The cancellation confirmation is a database fixture, not an actual cancellation response. This test does not cover asynchronous replication, automatic failover, fencing, split brain, a load balancer, multiple hosts or production HA.

### Cancellation evidence fence

Worker migration `0269` makes a fresh cancellation reservation fail before the provider call when the current provider ID is present in a recorded email retry decision, or when a stored signed webhook already indicates a send, delivery attempt, delivery outcome or engagement. A scheduled-only event does not block reservation. It uses the existing environment/provider advisory lock and rechecks the current attempt and committed evidence after acquiring the lock; reservation now requires `READ COMMITTED` isolation and rejects other isolation levels. A replay of an existing reservation remains non-dispatching. An ineligible queued command becomes a failed command and follows the existing review process; the direct CLI fails without calling the provider. Existing confirmed cancellations sharing a provider ID with a retry decision are reported as `ambiguous`, so the evidence alert activates and the optional overdue exclusion no longer applies. Matching includes retry decisions for the same or another job in the environment; pre-migration or missing retry IDs cannot be reconstructed.

These checks cover evidence committed before reservation. The DB lock is released before the external HTTP call; a later webhook, retry decision or external sender can still race with that call. A provider ID or account label is not proof of account identity, and older binaries or privileged direct writes can bypass the fence. Concurrent job/provider locks may be aborted by PostgreSQL deadlock detection; failed or unknown commands require investigation, not automatic repetition. Apply the migration before upgraded workers and monitor command failures and evidence ambiguity. This changes eligibility for existing callers; review rejected commands and existing deployment isolation defaults. The additional lookup uses the existing partial retry-provider index; test cost and lock contention under production load.

The local PostgreSQL test checks a committed retry ID and delivery event block the provider call, a retry transaction holding the provider lock is re-read after commit, repeatable-read reservation is rejected, and later retry evidence reactivates health. API/dashboard cancellation regression tests use a local API and synthetic webhooks. Actual Resend cancellation, late external evidence, multi-host HA and production load remain unverified.

### Ambiguous Resend cancellation HTTP responses

Worker migration `0270` and updated cancellation CLI/worker treat HTTP 408 and 409 as `unknown`, not `rejected`, because either response alone does not establish whether a cancellation happened. Unknown requests retain the existing per-job fence and require the independently authorized manual resolution; the queued command remains dispatching until resolved. HTTP status is not retained for these new unknown responses. Other 4xx remain rejected as responses to the request, not proof of the email's provider state. The DB finish function rejects attempts to write new 408/409 rejected results, including from an older worker; an old worker encountering this leaves the request unknown and reconnects. Apply the migration before updating all workers and CLI instances.

Historical 408/409 rows already stored as `rejected` are **not automatically changed**: there may be later requests or provider activity. The existing unknown-request alert does not count these rows. Migration `0271` fences new requests until they are assessed, as described below. Before further cancellation attempts, a separately authorized reader can run `resend-cancel list-legacy-ambiguous ENV [AFTER_REQUEST_UUID]` after granting EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_legacy_ambiguous(text,uuid)` and the existing environment `can_read` permission. It returns at most 50 request/job IDs, HTTP statuses and timestamps, with a UUID cursor and no provider ID or reason. Investigate each using provider records and existing detailed audit; do not infer delivery or blindly retry. There is no automatic historical correction. Migration `0272` adds an optional dedicated alert, and API `0179` adds JWT-protected listing and dashboard access. Migration `0271` adds explicit operator assessment, including through the existing detail API and screen; investigation and handling later requests remain operator responsibilities. Pages are separate snapshots and direct DB changes or old binaries can bypass this protection.

Unit and local PostgreSQL tests exercise mock 408/409 responses through a real cancellation worker, ensure the job fence prevents new requests, test DB rejection of incorrect classifications and list historical rows with restricted access. Actual Resend 408/409, network intermediaries and production HA remain unverified.

### Review and fence historical ambiguous cancellation responses

Worker migration `0271` blocks fresh cancellation reservations for any job with an unreviewed legacy `rejected` 408/409 request; queued commands fail eligibility before provider I/O, and already submitted commands must be investigated and resubmitted explicitly after review. Same-request replays remain non-dispatching. The existing separately authorized `resend-cancel resolve ENV RESOLUTION_ID REQUEST_ID confirmed|not_cancelled REASON` and existing JWT resolution API can now assess those legacy records. Existing `can_resolve` and JWT `confirmed`/`not_cancelled` grants therefore gain this ability; review these grants before rollout. The decision and reason are audited atomically, and the request retains its original HTTP status for investigation. The existing dashboard can load a known request ID and prepare a decision; API `0179` and the dashboard also provide historical listing. `not_cancelled` removes that record's fence but does not itself retry; every ambiguous historical record for the job must be assessed. `confirmed` blocks fresh requests as before. If a later unknown/confirmed request already exists, marking the older record confirmed conflicts and rolls back; investigate all related attempts rather than assuming a delivery or cancellation outcome.

Review and reservation take the same job lock, so a fresh reservation observes committed reviews; this does not stop external provider calls already in progress or privileged direct DB changes. Stop or coordinate old workers, inspect provider evidence and retain audit records. Existing records are not automatically rewritten, and unknown-cancellation alerts do not count unreviewed legacy rows; use the separate 0272 alert and run the legacy listing until all have been investigated. The isolated PostgreSQL test covers authorization, audit failure rollback, multiple historical rows, same-request replay, racing review/reservation and conflict with a later unknown request. Restricted JWT API and Angular component integration tests cover a known legacy request. Actual Resend outcomes, live alerts, full historical discovery UI and production HA remain unverified.

### Optional alerts for historical ambiguous cancellations

Worker migration `0272` adds `delivery-reconcile check-resend-cancel-legacy ENV`, returning the environment's count of unresolved historical `rejected` 408/409 records and exit 2 when nonzero. Grant the monitoring LOGIN `alert_view` for the environment and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_resend_cancel_legacy_health(text)`. The partial index and count should be evaluated against production table size and migration lock windows. The count is a DB snapshot; a manual resolution moves an entry out of the count without proving what the provider actually did. Use the separately authorized `resend-cancel list-legacy-ambiguous` to investigate individual requests.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_CANCEL_LEGACY_ENABLED=true` to opt in to the existing Slack/email alert sender; the default is false. With fanout, `delivery_checks.resend_cancel_legacy` overrides the inherited choice per target. New-condition cooldown requires scoped delivery and V3: high-word bits 65536 and 131072 represent legacy records and check failure. Failed checks preserve previously observed record conditions. Deploy the migration and upgraded sender/fanout to all participating instances before enabling the option; old fanout rejects the new field. Coordinate checks among senders sharing a target ID. There is no grace period, dashboard alert configuration or live provider validation; API `0179` provides JWT-protected historical listing. The alert stops counting a reviewed record, not necessarily a delivered or cancelled message.

The isolated local PostgreSQL test checks scoped counts before and after review, permission denial, a blocked alert target and invalid policy. Unit tests use mock Slack and email, and the fanout process test checks override/inheritance; no real external alert or production HA is verified.


### Resend cancellation worker key rotation

Set `NOVU_RESEND_RELOAD_API_KEY=true` and provide only `NOVU_RESEND_API_KEY_FILE` to reload a bounded regular file before each cancellation command claim. The default is `false` and preserves startup-only loading. Reload mode rejects an inline key or a missing file setting at startup, and a malformed or unavailable file pauses new claims rather than using the last successful key. Startup also fails if the initial key is invalid. `SWALLOW_RESEND_CANCEL_RELOAD_FAILURE_POLICY=pause|exit` selects retrying the file every two seconds (the default) or exiting with an error for a process supervisor; `exit` requires reload mode. Invalid values reject startup. Error messages omit file paths and credentials.

Use an atomic file or secret-mount replacement and protect the file and its parent directories. A key change after a claim or request starts does not change or cancel the in-flight provider call; an ambiguous response still requires operator investigation, and the worker never automatically repeats that cancellation. Each instance reads its own file, so coordinate rotation, permissions, account ownership and deployment across all workers; the account tag is an operator-supplied label, not proof that the rotated key belongs to that account. Reload cannot detect a valid-looking but revoked or wrong-account key before the provider responds. In `pause` mode monitor the existing pending-command overdue alert and process logs; an optional reload-health check is described below.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-resend-cancel-local.sh` exercises two actual workers/limited DB roles and a mock provider on disposable local PostgreSQL. It checks key changes in outbound authorization, no claim while a file is empty, atomic replacement recovery, exit policy and invalid configuration; existing tests cover uncertain cancellation and duplicate-call fencing. Docker, live Resend key rotation, multiple hosts and production failover were not verified.


### Resend cancellation credential health (worker 0273)

Apply worker migration 0273. For each participating cancellation worker, set `NOVU_RESEND_RELOAD_API_KEY=true` and `SWALLOW_RESEND_CANCEL_CREDENTIAL_HEALTH_ENABLED=true`. A trusted DBA must register the worker's actual PostgreSQL LOGIN and current role OID in `public.novu_rust_resend_cancel_credential_workers`, grant it EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_resend_cancel_credential_heartbeat(text,uuid,text)`, and give the protected security-definer function owner the table access and `pg_read_all_stats` membership needed for `pg_stat_activity`. No roles or grants are created automatically. The worker records local file status on change and at least every ten seconds between calls. If the observation fails, it stops claiming and reconnects; `exit` mode attempts to record an unhealthy observation before exiting.

Register the monitoring login's environment `alert_view` grant and grant EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_resend_cancel_credential_health_check(text,integer)`. `delivery-reconcile check-resend-cancel-credentials ENV MAX_AGE_SECONDS MIN_HEALTHY` outputs healthy, unhealthy, stale and minimum counts and exits 2 for a recent unhealthy observation or too few healthy instances; invalid input and permission errors exit nonzero. Age must be 1–86400 and minimum 1–1000. Healthy counts require a matching live PostgreSQL session (PID, backend start and login); recent unhealthy observations count even after disconnect. New DB connections have new instance IDs. Choose an age above provider-call and DB delays plus the ten-second interval. A worker without this option, or one that fails before its first observation, is visible only indirectly through the minimum healthy count. Stale rows accumulate; no automatic retention is provided. Session pooling, connection failure detection, clock changes, privileged writes and DB outages can affect results. A successful file read does not verify provider authorization or cancellation. The CLI can be independently scheduled and monitored. Optional Slack/email alert integration is described below; API 0180 and the dashboard provide independently authorized read-only counts.

The local cancellation integration test uses actual workers, restricted PostgreSQL logins and a mock provider to check healthy/unhealthy recovery, command pause, worker termination, environment isolation, permission denial and invalid configuration. Actual Resend, a scheduler, external alert receipt, production load and multi-host failover remain unverified.


### Resend cancellation credential alerts

To enable the check for `send-delivery-alert`, set both `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_CANCEL_CREDENTIAL_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` (1–86400) and `SWALLOW_ALERT_RESEND_CANCEL_CREDENTIAL_MIN_HEALTHY` (1–1000); omit both to disable it. The alert DB login needs environment `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_resend_cancel_credential_health_check(text,integer)`, as well as the existing destination permissions. `alert-fanout` inherits these settings and supports per-target `delivery_thresholds.resend_cancel_credential_max_age_seconds` and `resend_cancel_credential_min_healthy`, including `"disabled"` for each. If enabling `SWALLOW_ALERT_FANOUT_PREFLIGHT_PAIRS=true`, effective values must be supplied or disabled as a pair for every target.

Recent unhealthy observations or too few healthy workers trigger the existing Slack/email delivery path; query failures also alert and cause a nonzero exit. Only aggregate counts and thresholds enter the alert. `new-condition` cooldown requires `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V3=true`: the new check uses separate high-word condition bits for an unhealthy observation and check failure. Upgrade all senders sharing the destination and use worker migration 0273 before enabling it. Existing cooldown, target binding, ambiguous send and recovery behavior still applies; these observations only check local key-file reads. Set a threshold long enough for in-flight calls and monitoring intervals, and independently monitor the scheduler and database.

The local PostgreSQL cancellation test exercises healthy, unhealthy and recovered actual worker observations through the actual alert binary with a blocked Slack fixture, as well as invalid thresholds. Unit tests send payloads to mock Slack and Resend and check the independent condition bits; the fanout process tests cover threshold forwarding and preflight validation. No real Slack/email alert, Resend credential check, browser, multi-host or production failover was tested.


### Cancellation credential health during local PostgreSQL promotion

`SWALLOW_LOCAL_FAILOVER_SCENARIO=resend-cancel-credential-inflight apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-postgres-failover-local.sh` runs an actual cancellation worker with credential reload and observations, restricted worker/monitor DB logins, and a loopback Resend fixture against a disposable PostgreSQL 17 synchronous primary/standby pair on one host. It holds an in-flight provider response until the unknown marker and healthy observation replicate, corrupts the key file, stops the primary and manually promotes the standby. The promoted DB does not count the old disconnected session as healthy. The **same worker**, without restart, reconnects through a multi-host read-write URL, records unhealthy, pauses a new queued cancellation, and resumes after an atomic valid-key replacement. The existing alert binary reports the unhealthy state through an intentionally blocked Slack fixture and reports healthy after recovery. The first cancellation stays unknown and is not sent again; a later separate cancellation completes once.

This uses a mock provider and a blocked alert destination; no real external request is made. It does not test live Resend/Slack, asynchronous replication, automatic promotion, fencing, split brain, load balancers, connection poolers, multiple hosts or production HA. Recent disconnected unhealthy observations can still keep the health check unhealthy until they age out; choose thresholds with this behavior in mind. The older `resend-cancel-inflight` scenario still separately exercises a killed worker and replacement process.


### Ambiguous Slack alert HTTP outcomes (worker 0274)

Slack alert HTTP 408 and 409 are treated as `unknown`, not definite rejection. The worker also defensively maps `AlertError::Rejected(408|409)` to `unknown` for both direct and scoped writes. Unknown alert slots require independent operator investigation and block another automatic send; neither a received HTTP status nor an operator assertion proves Slack did or did not deliver the original alert. The stored `unknown` attempt does not retain the HTTP status. Other 4xx responses, including 429, retain the existing rejected-and-review behavior.

Migration 0274 additionally refuses a scoped `novu_rust_finish_alert` call attempting to store rejected 408/409, including calls from older binaries. Such a failed finish leaves the in-flight lease for existing recovery to mark unknown; monitor errors and replace all alert senders. Direct-write mode and privileged SQL bypass this database guard. Existing historical `rejected` 408/409 records are not rewritten or automatically resolved; they remain blocked and must be investigated in provider records before any operator decision. An operator should not infer non-delivery solely from their old state. Reapplying an older migration that replaces the finish function can remove this guard.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-alert-ambiguous-local.sh` uses disposable PostgreSQL and a restricted sender to verify a mock Slack 409 becomes unknown, repeat dispatch is blocked, and the scoped function rejects legacy 408/409 writes. The transport unit test checks both statuses. Actual Slack behavior, old historical record remediation and production HA remain unverified.


### Find currently blocked historical 408/409 alert attempts (worker 0275)

After migration 0275, grant a monitor LOGIN the environment's `alert_view` and EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_alert_legacy_ambiguous(text,text,text)`. The protected security-definer owner needs the slot/attempt table and grant-helper privileges. `delivery-reconcile list-alert-legacy-ambiguous ENV` returns up to 50 currently blocked `rejected` slots whose current attempt was recorded with HTTP 408 or 409. Each row contains channel, non-secret target ID, attempt UUID, HTTP status and start time. If `hasMore` is true, continue with `delivery-reconcile list-alert-legacy-ambiguous ENV NEXT_CHANNEL NEXT_TARGET` from the returned `next` object. Ordering uses C collation; pages are separate snapshots, so repeat scans when concurrent changes matter. The function does not return payloads, webhook URLs or credentials, but target IDs and timing can still be sensitive.

This finds only the current blocked attempt: resolved or replaced history, direct privileged changes and attempts with no recorded status are excluded. A matching status does not prove whether an alert was delivered. Independently investigate the provider and use the existing attempt-bound `alert-resolve-v2` with separately authorized `alert_retry` or `alert_accepted` only after investigation; a mistaken retry can duplicate an alert. This listing does not change state, prevent a separately authorized operator decision, send alerts, or itself provide API/dashboard access. API 0182 separately marks matching current attempts in the existing alert list. Existing monitoring of blocked slots remains separate.

The local `test-alert-ambiguous-local.sh` test checks 408/409 selection, 400 and resolved exclusions, two-page traversal, CLI output, invalid cursors, environment isolation and revoked `alert_view` using disposable PostgreSQL. Actual provider history, large-table performance and production HA remain unverified.


### Acceptance assertion for a historical ambiguous alert (worker 0276)

Migration 0276 extends only `novu_rust_resolve_alert_v2` and callers already using that attempt-fenced function. A separately authorized `alert_accepted` operator may assert acceptance for a current `rejected` attempt whose stored HTTP status is 408 or 409, after independently investigating the provider. The function locks the slot, checks exact attempt ID and matching attempt state and status, records the existing resolution audit, and changes the slot to accepted with a fresh cooldown timestamp in the same transaction. The original attempt and HTTP status remain unchanged. It does not send, check the provider, or prove recipient delivery. A mistaken assertion can suppress a needed alert; a mistaken retry can duplicate one.

Existing `alert_accepted` permissions gain this ability: review direct DB and API/JWT/console grants before applying. The six-argument unfenced `novu_rust_resolve_alert` remains unchanged and does not permit this case. Direct privileged DB edits can bypass checks. API 0182 shows the current status and the updated dashboard supports the decision. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-alert-ambiguous-local.sh` tests scoped role, both codes, ordinary 400, stale ID and audit rollback; the API local test checks console/JWT and UI with synthetic identities. Real provider evidence, operational load and multi-host HA are unverified.


### FCM topic normalization

For a single FCM topic route, the worker removes one leading `/topics/` and rejects an empty topic or characters outside ASCII letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `.`, `~` and `%` before making an HTTP request. This follows the pinned Firebase Admin Node 13.3.0 `validateMessage` topic conversion; for example `/topics/orders` is sent as `orders`. It affects previously queued topic messages: malformed topics now fail before dispatch and may need correction and a new trigger. Token, multicast and condition routing are unchanged. The trigger API checks the provider value's type but does not validate the full topic syntax, so an invalid topic may be accepted by the API and later fail before any provider attempt.

The `reference_fcm_wire` comparison uses the actual pinned Firebase Admin SDK, including prefixed and unprefixed topics and invalid cases. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-fcm-topic-local.sh` exercises the Rust trigger router, workflow queue and worker with disposable PostgreSQL and mocked FCM, verifying the normalized HTTP body and invalid-topic failure before dispatch. The fixture uses the shared-provider test harness, not a real FCM device, original Novu server or production HA. The validation source is [Firebase Admin Node 13.3.0](https://github.com/firebase/firebase-admin-node/blob/v13.3.0/src/messaging/messaging-internal.ts).


### Experimental standalone Telegram delivery (worker 0277)

Migration 0277 and `telegram-worker` add a standalone PostgreSQL queue consumer for Telegram Bot API `sendMessage`. Start the worker with `DATABASE_URL`, `NOVU_TELEGRAM_ENVIRONMENT_ID`, `NOVU_TELEGRAM_DESTINATION_ID`, and exactly one of `NOVU_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` or `NOVU_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN_FILE`. Destination IDs are 1–64 safe ASCII characters and are bound to a SHA-256 fingerprint of the configured bot token in the DB; different tokens under the same environment/destination ID fail startup. The fingerprint is sensitive account metadata and must be protected. The worker reads the token only at startup and sends only to `https://api.telegram.org`; restart for rotation. Use a new destination ID or stop and drain all affected workers and coordinate a trusted DB binding update before switching bots. A binding is not rechecked before each claim, and privileged DB changes or old processes can bypass coordination.

`telegram-enqueue ENV < message.json` submits a standalone job, prints a job UUID, and reads a bounded JSON object from stdin, for example `{"destination":"ops","chatId":"123456","text":"Hello","passthrough":{"parse_mode":"HTML"}}`. The `passthrough` object is already Telegram snake_case and overrides base `chat_id` and `text`; it can change the actual recipient. Validate it before use. The chat ID, message and passthrough persist in queue payloads and backups; they are not covered by existing attachment retention. A bot token is not intentionally placed in the queue. Worker 0280 offers separately configured scoped enqueue below, while worker 0279 offers scoped worker functions; there is no tenant RLS, integration-management UI or endpoint provisioning. Restrict DB access and treat this as experimental, not a production-ready tenant interface.

The worker records one dispatch attempt before HTTP. A valid successful Telegram response with message ID and timestamp is recorded as provider acceptance, not user receipt. HTTP 4xx other than 408/409, including 429, becomes rejected; 408/409, transport errors, 5xx, redirects and malformed success become unknown. Expired in-flight attempts become unknown and are not automatically resent. A lease expired before an attempt is requeued. Worker 0278 adds a separately authorized CLI operator decision, described below. Webhook reconciliation, UI, and automatic Retry-After policy remain unimplemented; aggregate alerting is described below. Investigate provider state before manually creating any replacement job; it may duplicate a message. This standalone job type does not use workflow fanout or subscriber identity/preferences; the separate workflow path is described below. Novu Telegram integration management, full provider casing/merge, rich cards and exact error parity are not implemented.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-telegram-local.sh` uses disposable local PostgreSQL, the real enqueue and two actual workers, and a mock HTTP server to test accepted, 409 unknown, 429 rejected, worker termination after the mock provider receives a request, expired-attempt recovery, environment/destination isolation and different-bot binding rejection. Unit tests cover token validation and bounded responses. The payload shape corresponds to selected checked-in Novu Telegram provider examples, but original provider code was not run in this test; actual Telegram delivery, crash during a live request, restricted DB roles and production HA are unverified. Docker is not used.


### Telegram operator decisions (worker 0278)

Apply worker 0278 before using these commands. A trusted DBA inserts each operator's environment, PostgreSQL LOGIN and current role OID into `public.novu_rust_telegram_operator_grants` with independently selected `can_view`, `can_retry`, and/or `can_accept`, and grants EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_telegram_attention(text,uuid)` and/or `public.novu_rust_telegram_resolve(text,uuid,uuid,uuid,text,text)`. No role, grant or registration is automatic. Secure the function owner and tables; the worker still uses direct queue access. The functions lock the grant and validate the LOGIN's current role OID.

`delivery-reconcile list-telegram ENV [AFTER_JOB_UUID]` shows up to 50 failed jobs with a current rejected or unknown attempt; use `next` when `hasMore` is true. It returns job and attempt IDs, state, HTTP status, counts and time, not chat IDs, messages or tokens. Pages are separate snapshots ordered by UUID. Failed jobs that never created an attempt are excluded.

After independently investigating the provider, `delivery-reconcile resolve-telegram ENV REQUEST_UUID JOB_UUID ATTEMPT_UUID retry|accepted REASON` records a decision under the exact current attempt and operator permission. `accepted` is allowed only for unknown, is an operator assertion and does not prove receipt. `retry` permits both rejected and unknown and can duplicate a message. A retry archives previous state, HTTP status, observation time, actor, reason and attempt ID in `novu_rust_telegram_resolutions`, removes the current attempt, and requeues the job in the same transaction; the new worker attempt gets a new UUID. Audit insertion failure rolls back the decision. Reusing the same request ID with the same actor and body replays the recorded result; altered content or actor conflicts, and a stale attempt is rejected. Keep request ID and reason in a protected record; command arguments may appear in shell history and process lists. No request-lookup API, automatic retry, review UI, resolution audit reader, retention scheduler or Telegram callback is provided yet. Privileged DB writes, old binaries, lost asynchronous replication and independent databases can bypass these guarantees.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/worker/rust-queue/scripts/test-telegram-local.sh` checks scoped viewer/decision roles, environment isolation, pagination, concurrent same-ID replay, conflict and stale attempt, audit failure rollback, accepted assertions and explicit retry through the real worker and mock provider. Actual Telegram, an external IdP, live crash recovery after a retry and production HA are unverified.


### Scoped Telegram workers (worker 0279)

Set `NOVU_TELEGRAM_SCOPED_DB=true` to use restricted database functions for destination registration, claim, preparation, finish and expired-lease recovery. The default `false` retains direct-table behavior; invalid values fail startup. A trusted DBA registers each actual PostgreSQL LOGIN and current role OID per environment and destination in `public.novu_rust_telegram_worker_grants`, then grants schema USAGE and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_telegram_worker_register(text,text,text)`, `novu_rust_telegram_worker_claim(text,text,uuid)`, `novu_rust_telegram_worker_prepare(text,text,uuid,uuid,boolean)`, `novu_rust_telegram_worker_finish(text,text,uuid,uuid,text,integer,text,text)` and `novu_rust_telegram_worker_recover(text,text)`. No table privileges or registrations are created automatically. Protect each security-definer owner and grant it only the required access to the grant, destination, queue and attempt tables and helper functions. Revoke direct table privileges from the worker login and verify inherited/PUBLIC privileges separately. A granted worker can read job payloads in its authorized destination.

Each function checks and locks the current LOGIN/OID registration. A committed revocation blocks later calls; registration failure makes the worker reconnect and retry, so monitor process logs and backlog rather than relying on process exit. Revocation after preparation cannot cancel an external request already started; failure to record its result leaves the attempt for authorized recovery as unknown. The lease and destination checks fence stale or wrong-scope mutations. Drain older/direct workers and revoke their table privileges before relying on scoped enforcement; enabling this option does not itself enable the separate scoped enqueue mode, configure RLS or protect against privileged SQL. Provider sends and DB failover are still not exactly once.

The local Telegram test runs two actual scoped workers using a login denied direct queue/attempt table access, checks environment and destination isolation, different-token binding, 409/429 outcomes, invalid job and stale lease, committed grant revocation and recovery, and kills a worker after the mock provider receives its request. Direct mode and operator tests also run. It does not exercise real Telegram, external secret mounts, production privilege deployment or multi-host HA.


### Scoped Telegram enqueue (worker 0280)

Set `NOVU_TELEGRAM_ENQUEUE_SCOPED=true` and use `telegram-enqueue ENV REQUEST_UUID < message.json`. The UUID is mandatory in scoped mode and must be stored before submitting. The same environment, request UUID, LOGIN/OID, destination and canonical payload replay the original job ID instead of enqueuing twice; changed content, destination or caller conflicts. Validation checks the bounded input and supported structure; passthrough can still override the recipient, so authorize submitters accordingly. With scoped mode disabled (the default), the earlier one-argument CLI uses direct table INSERT and creates a new job each time. Invalid mode settings or request UUIDs fail before enqueue.

A trusted DBA inserts each publisher LOGIN and current role OID per environment and destination into `public.novu_rust_telegram_enqueue_grants`, and grants schema USAGE plus EXECUTE on `public.novu_rust_telegram_enqueue(text,uuid,jsonb)`. Protect the security-definer owner and grant it only the required grant, request and queue table privileges. Revoke direct queue and request-table privileges from the publisher and verify inherited/PUBLIC privileges. Authorization locks the grant; committed revocation blocks subsequent calls, including replays. The request record and job insertion commit atomically. It stores a SHA-256 hash of canonical JSON, actor and IDs, not a second copy of message content; hashes can still leak information about guessable inputs. Queue payloads and backups still contain the message and recipient.

A replay proves only that the request was previously recorded in the current DB state; it does not prove provider delivery or recreate a job deleted later. No read-only request lookup, request retention policy, management API/UI, RLS, or asynchronous-replication/failover dedup guarantee is provided. The grant allows the selected bot destination but does not authorize individual chat IDs. Old/direct workers and privileged DB writes bypass these boundaries; an already submitted job survives grant revocation. `test-telegram-local.sh` checks isolated DB publisher and worker roles, forbidden direct reads and cross-scope requests, direct-function malformed input, concurrent identical replays, changed content/caller conflict, audit rollback, revocation and actual scoped worker delivery through a mock server. Actual Telegram, original Novu parity and production multi-host HA remain unverified.

### Experimental Telegram subscriber endpoint management (worker 0281, API 0183)

The experimental API exposes `GET /v1/experimental/telegram-destinations`, `GET|POST /v1/experimental/telegram-endpoints`, `GET|PATCH|DELETE /v1/experimental/telegram-endpoints/{identifier}`, and `GET /v1/experimental/telegram-endpoint-audit`. Create accepts `identifier`, `subscriberId`, `destinationId`, `chatId` and optional `context`; update requires `expectedRevision`, `destinationId`, `chatId` and optional `context`. Delete requires the `revision` query parameter. A successful update changes the revision; stale writes fail. The binding references the subscriber's immutable internal identity and is deleted with that subscriber. It references an already registered bot destination; the API never returns its token fingerprint. Chat IDs are sensitive recipient data and are returned to authorized endpoint readers.

A trusted DBA must apply the worker migration before the API migration, explicitly insert API-key hashes and `read`, `write`, and/or `audit` grants into `novu_rust_telegram_endpoint_permissions`, and grant the API database role the required table and function privileges. The API role needs SELECT on users, API keys and Telegram destinations, SELECT/UPDATE on subscribers for row locking, SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on endpoint bindings, INSERT on endpoint audit, and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_lock_telegram_endpoint_grant(text,text,text)` and `novu_rust_telegram_endpoint_audit_list(text,text,uuid)`. The audit table and permission table should not be readable by the API role directly. These table grants are broad; the API role is trusted, not a database tenant-isolation boundary. Restrict access to the API database credential, protect the security-definer owner and check inherited/PUBLIC privileges. Audit records capture explicit API changes, not cascaded deletion. Back up and retain endpoint data according to the operator's policy.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-telegram-endpoints-local.sh` tests API key and environment isolation, revision races, grant revocation, audit rollback and access, recipient validation, and subscriber deletion/recreation on a disposable local PostgreSQL. This is a Swallow-specific experimental API, not the original Novu endpoint contract. Workflow delivery is a separate opt-in described below; creating an endpoint alone does not enable delivery. The Angular console provides endpoint management as documented in `apps/dashboard/swallow/README.md`; real Telegram and production HA remain unverified.

### Experimental Telegram workflow delivery (worker 0282)

To opt in, migrate all workers first, start every in-app worker with `NOVU_TELEGRAM_WORKFLOW_ENABLED=true`, start a Telegram worker for each environment/destination with the same setting (direct or scoped, as described below), then have a trusted administrator set `novu_rust_telegram_workflow_policy.enabled=true` for that environment. Both the worker flag and environment policy are required to create workflow jobs. The environment policy locks against in-flight parent completions and fences workers that do not participate, preventing silent completion without Telegram fanout. Disable the policy before rolling back compatible workers; examine in-flight and queued jobs separately. Slack chat fanout remains independent.

Each chat step snapshots matching subscriber endpoint identities and revisions. Before reserving a Telegram attempt the worker locks and rechecks the subscriber's immutable identity, chat preferences, workflow generation and content, endpoint revision and context, then renders the content with payload, subscriber, actor and context data. Endpoint changes or subscriber recreation cannot redirect a queued job. Reservations commit before the HTTP request; a later configuration change cannot retract a request already reserved or sent. No provider passthrough is applied to these workflow jobs. Queue payloads contain workflow content and subscriber information; endpoint records contain chat IDs. Protect and retain these as sensitive data.

**Experimental limitations:** Worker 0283 extends the existing operator review commands to workflow jobs, as described below. Workflow status integration, real Telegram verification, and full Novu parity are not implemented; aggregate alerting is described below. A local workflow crash test is described below. Test in isolation before any operational use. `TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-telegram-workflow-local.sh` exercises API trigger through PostgreSQL workers and a mock Telegram server, rendering and chat ID, policy fencing, stale endpoint revision, disabled chat preference, changed workflow and subscriber ID reuse.


### Telegram workflow operator decisions (worker 0283)

`delivery-reconcile list-telegram ENV [AFTER_JOB]` now includes both standalone and workflow attempts requiring attention and adds `jobKind` to each result. The same existing environment-scoped `view`, `retry`, and `accepted` database-login grants apply to both kinds; no new role or privilege is granted by migration. `resolve-telegram ENV REQUEST JOB ATTEMPT retry|accepted REASON` uses the existing attempt identity, idempotency key and atomic decision audit. Retry returns the original job to pending: a workflow-capable worker rechecks subscriber identity, endpoint revision, chat preference and workflow before any new attempt. Acceptance is an operator assertion based on independent evidence, not proof of user receipt. Retrying an unknown outcome can duplicate a message. Disable or revoke permissions using the existing grant process.

The workflow integration test uses a mock HTTP 409 response, a separately authenticated database login, environment and permission rejection, concurrent identical decisions, conflict detection, audit failure rollback, and a changed endpoint before retry. The existing Telegram regression script tests both standalone operator decisions and restricted workers. Automatic result reconciliation, actual Telegram and production multi-host HA remain unverified or unimplemented; aggregate alerting and the local interruption test are described below.


### Scoped Telegram workflow workers (worker 0284)

`NOVU_TELEGRAM_SCOPED_DB=true` and `NOVU_TELEGRAM_WORKFLOW_ENABLED=true` may be used together. In addition to the worker 0279 registration, standalone preparation and finish permissions, a trusted DBA grants schema USAGE and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_telegram_workflow_claim(text,text,uuid)`, `novu_rust_telegram_workflow_snapshot(text,text,uuid,uuid,boolean)`, `novu_rust_telegram_workflow_prepare(text,text,uuid,uuid,text,boolean)` and `novu_rust_telegram_workflow_recover(text,text)` to the scoped worker login. Its existing environment/destination/login/OID registration remains mandatory. These functions must be owned by a trusted non-login owner with only the necessary access to worker grants, queue, attempts, subscriber profiles/preferences, workflows, endpoints and contexts, and any functions they call. Revoke direct table access and verify inherited/PUBLIC privileges separately. Claim and snapshot return sensitive job and subscriber data within the granted destination. The role may request actor recreation compatibility where configured; the security-definer owner and worker process are trusted.

The snapshot derives its inputs from the live leased job, rechecks authorization, and locks the subscriber, workflow and endpoint through the transaction. Preparation repeats the database checks before recording an attempt. Rendering and attempt reservation commit before HTTP; after commit, revocation or a configuration change cannot cancel an already reserved send. A denied or lost lease before reservation does not start a send. If a worker dies after reservation, recovery records an unknown outcome without automatic resend. Direct-table and pre-existing standalone scoped modes remain available.

`test-telegram-scoped-workflow-local.sh` tests the API-triggered workflow with a login denied direct queue and subscriber reads, a mock provider, permission/environment isolation, endpoint revision, preferences and operator resolution. `test-telegram-local.sh` also starts two actual scoped worker processes and kills one during an in-flight workflow request, verifying unknown recovery and no automatic repeat, as well as grant revocation. Tests use a local PostgreSQL and mock HTTP; actual Telegram, multi-host failover, RLS, automatic outcome reconciliation and original Novu parity remain unverified or unimplemented.


### Telegram delivery health and alerts (worker 0285)

`delivery-reconcile check-telegram ENV` returns only counts for unknown and rejected attempts, stranded dispatches and failed jobs without an attempt, across standalone and workflow jobs. It exits 0 if healthy, 2 if any count is nonzero and 1 on errors. A trusted DBA grants the caller EXECUTE on `novu_rust_telegram_health(text)` and the existing environment-scoped `alert_view` permission, using the established login/OID grant process. The function does not return job payloads, subscriber identities, chat IDs or bot tokens. Revoked or wrong-environment access fails. Like the existing channel checks, unresolved historical failures remain counted until addressed; cancelled preference decisions are not counted.

Set `SWALLOW_ALERT_TELEGRAM_ENABLED=true` for `send-delivery-alert`, or configure `"telegram": true` under a fanout target's `delivery_checks`; each destination can choose independently. Existing Slack, Resend/SendGrid or SMTP email delivery, scoped dispatch, destination cooldown and recovery policies apply. With `SWALLOW_ALERT_COOLDOWN_POLICY=new-condition`, use `SWALLOW_ALERT_CONDITIONS_V3=true` and the existing scoped-delivery v3 grants; this check has distinct v3 high-word bits for its four conditions and check failure. New-condition targets must follow the existing v3 migration/target adoption rules. A failed health query raises a check-failure condition rather than falsely reporting recovery; alert delivery can itself fail or require operator review, so monitor its status separately.

`TEST_PG_BIN=/opt/homebrew/opt/postgresql@17/bin apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-telegram-health-local.sh` exercises the health function and CLI with a restricted login, environment isolation, grant revocation, all four counts and an alert destination requiring review, using local PostgreSQL. The alert unit test sends aggregate-only payloads to mock Slack and email endpoints and checks v3 masks; the fanout test verifies per-target choice. Live alert destinations, actual Telegram, production HA and Novu compatibility are not established by these tests.

### Telegram workflow policy administration (worker 0286, API 0184)

The environment policy now has a monotonic revision. A trusted DBA grants a PostgreSQL LOGIN/OID environment-scoped read/write/audit authority in `novu_rust_telegram_policy_admins` and only the necessary EXECUTE privileges on `novu_rust_telegram_policy_read(text)`, `novu_rust_telegram_policy_set(text,uuid,bigint,boolean,text)`, `novu_rust_telegram_policy_request(text,uuid)` and `novu_rust_telegram_policy_audit_read(text,bigint)`. `delivery-reconcile telegram-policy-read|set|request|audit` exposes these operations. An expected revision prevents stale writes; a request UUID supports safe replay of the same decision; both the decision and audit commit atomically. Enablement continues to acquire the policy row lock and preserves the existing legacy-worker fence. It does not cancel already queued or reserved sends.

For the experimental JWT operator API, migrate worker 0286 before API 0184, register the API DB login in the existing JWT API login table, and explicitly grant that login the corresponding DB policy permissions and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_policy_read(text,text,text,text,bigint)`, `..._change(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid,bigint,boolean,text)`, `..._request(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid)` and `..._audit_read(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid)`. A trusted DBA must also register the operator identity and either grant its independent read/write/audit flags or configure a grant administrator as described below. Protect the security-definer owners and verify PUBLIC and inherited privileges. JWT operations are recorded separately from direct DB/CLI operations; UUID audit order is not chronological. The console's existing workflow policy card offers Telegram after these permissions are configured.

`test-telegram-policy-local.sh` in the worker and API scripts covers scoped DB/JWT permissions, idempotency, conflicting requests, revision races, audit rollback and policy locking. `apps/dashboard/swallow/scripts/test-telegram-policy-browser.sh` covers the real browser, Rust API and isolated local PostgreSQL, including unauthorized operator and other-environment denial. These tests do not establish external IdP behavior, production deployment/HA or Novu parity. The policy controls fanout only; operators must deploy compatible in-app and Telegram workers and configure their destination and credentials before enabling it.

### Telegram policy permission delegation (API 0185)

The experimental `/v1/experimental/operator/telegram-policy-grants` API and existing console permission card support read/write/audit policy grants, grant request lookup and a separate grant audit. A trusted DBA must first register each grant administrator identity in `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_policy_grant_admins`, each grant audit viewer in `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_policy_grant_audit_viewers`, and the API database LOGIN in the existing JWT API login table; these roles are independent of Telegram policy permissions. The API login needs EXECUTE on `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_policy_grant_read(text,text,text,text,bigint,text,text,text,text,text)`, `..._change(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid,text,text,text,text,text,boolean,boolean,text)`, `..._request(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid)` and `..._audit_read(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid)`. Protect the definer owner and verify inherited/PUBLIC privileges. A privileged administrator still configures the API DB login's separate policy permissions and the first grant administrators; delegation does not replace this bootstrap.

Changes check the expected prior value, prohibit self-grant changes for the same issuer/subject, use request UUIDs for replay, and commit grants, request records and audit atomically. Grant administration and audit visibility are independently authorized; audit pagination is UUID-ordered, not chronological. Revocation affects subsequent calls, not already committed operations or requests sent to providers. The local API test exercises restricted logins, JWT isolation, conflicts, replay, self-change rejection and rollback. The browser test uses a restricted API role, local PostgreSQL and synthetic JWTs to grant and revoke permissions and verify policy access and audit. External IdP, production HA and original Novu parity are not established by these tests.

### Telegram health in the operator console (API 0186)

`GET /v1/experimental/operator/telegram-health?environmentId=...` exposes the four aggregate counts through the operator JWT API. A trusted DBA or authorized grant administrator registers each permitted JWT identity in `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_health_grants`, grants the API database LOGIN the existing environment-specific `alert_view` permission, and grants EXECUTE on `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_health(text,text,text,text,bigint)`. The API login must also be registered for operator JWT access. The JWT permission is independent of Telegram policy, endpoint, and CLI operator permissions. No permission is granted automatically; delegation is described below. Lock down the function owner and direct table access. The response contains aggregate counts only, not individual jobs, chat IDs or message content.

The console's alert page displays these counts with the operator JWT. `apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-telegram-health-jwt-local.sh` tests JWT and database-grant requirements, wrong user/environment, revocation and data minimization against disposable PostgreSQL. `apps/dashboard/swallow/scripts/test-telegram-health-browser.sh` exercises the console against the real Rust API with synthetic JWTs and a restricted API login. The console also provides a separately authorized Telegram attempt list and resolution, described below. External IdP, real Telegram and production HA remain unverified.


### Telegram health permission delegation (API 0187)

The existing Telegram policy grant administration API and console now accept `telegram-policy:health`, independently stored from policy read/write/audit. Existing Telegram policy grant administrators automatically gain authority to delegate and revoke this new permission when migration 0187 is applied; review their membership before upgrading. The separate grant-audit viewer permission applies to its audit records. Request IDs, expected prior value, self-change prohibition, authorization checks, rollback and audit use the same rules as the existing policy grants. Granting JWT health access does not grant the API database LOGIN `alert_view` or EXECUTE; a DBA must configure those separately. No operator permission is automatically assigned by the migration.

The API integration test adds and removes health access, checks independent policy permissions, simultaneous identical requests and audit-failure rollback. The console grant test exercises all four permissions and the browser script checks health access and revocation against a restricted API login and isolated local PostgreSQL. Real identity providers and multi-host HA remain unverified.


### Telegram attempt review and decisions (API 0188)

The console and experimental JWT API provide `GET /v1/experimental/operator/telegram-attention`, `POST /v1/experimental/operator/telegram-resolution`, and `GET /v1/experimental/operator/telegram-resolution-request`. Each uses `environmentId`; list accepts optional `after` UUID, and request lookup requires `requestId` and `decision`. The list contains failed unknown/rejected attempts only and omits message contents and recipients. An operator can request retry or assert acceptance for an unknown attempt; rejection cannot be marked accepted. Retry can duplicate a message if the previous outcome is unknown. Decisions are tied to an attempt ID and require a reason; the console prepares a request, requires explicit confirmation and does not automatically retry an uncertain response. Request lookup is scoped to the original JWT identity and decision.

A trusted DBA registers operator `can_list`, `can_retry` and `can_accept` flags in `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_resolution_grants`, explicitly registers the API DB login in `novu_rust_telegram_operator_grants` with corresponding `can_view`, `can_retry`, `can_accept` privileges, registers that login for operator JWT access, and grants EXECUTE on `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_attention(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid)`, `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_resolve(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid,uuid,uuid,text,text)` and `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_resolution_request(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid,text)`. Both layers are required. JWT permission delegation and cross-operator decision history are described below. Protect the security-definer owner and revoke direct table access. JWT request records include the identity and an internal worker request ID, which correlates the JWT decision with the existing database resolution audit; neither record independently proves receipt. Retention or environment deletion can remove the JWT request record.

`apps/api/rust-api/scripts/test-telegram-resolution-local.sh` tests both authorization layers, environment isolation, concurrent request replay, conflicting payload, audit rollback, successful acceptance, rejected-attempt refusal and grant revocation. `apps/dashboard/swallow/scripts/test-telegram-resolution-browser.sh` tests the real API and UI against local PostgreSQL with a restricted API login and synthetic JWTs. The UI uses in-memory request state, so record request IDs before leaving or refreshing. Actual Telegram, external IdP, production HA and complete Novu parity remain unverified.


### Telegram review and decision permission delegation (API 0189)

The experimental `telegram-resolution-grants` API and console permission card independently delegate `list`, `retry`, and `accepted` JWT permissions. A DBA must bootstrap independent administrators in `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_resolution_grant_admins` and independently provision audit viewers in `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_resolution_grant_audit_viewers`; existing policy grant administrators do not gain these rights. Grant the registered API DB login EXECUTE on the `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_resolution_grant_read`, `..._change`, `..._request`, and `..._audit_read` functions, and protect their security-definer owners and underlying tables. The API DB login's Telegram `can_view`, `can_retry`, `can_accept` privileges still require separate DBA setup; JWT delegation does not grant database access.

Changes are environment-scoped, conditional on the expected prior permission, reject self-changes by issuer/subject, use request UUID replay, and commit the grant, request and audit atomically. Audit access is separate and UUID ordered. Revocation blocks subsequent requests but cannot undo already committed decisions or an external send. The local API test checks two isolated authorization layers, stale changes, concurrent replays, self-change rejection, audit failure rollback, and independently grants each permission. The browser test exercises delegation, listing and revocation against the real Rust API and a restricted PostgreSQL login. External IdP and production HA remain unverified.


### Telegram decision history (worker 0287, API 0190–0191)

`delivery-reconcile telegram-resolution-history ENV JOB [AFTER_REQUEST]` returns up to 50 decisions for a job in worker request UUID order, not chronological or a snapshot across pages. A DBA grants the DB login `novu_rust_telegram_history_viewers` for the environment and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_telegram_resolution_history(text,uuid,uuid)`. This is independent of list/retry/accept permissions and holds the viewer grant lock during the query.

The JWT endpoint `GET /v1/experimental/operator/telegram-resolution-history?environmentId=...&jobId=...&after=...` additionally requires a JWT identity in `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_resolution_history_viewers` and EXECUTE on `novu_rust_operator_jwt_telegram_resolution_history(text,text,text,text,bigint,uuid,uuid)` for the API login. The console can delegate the independent `telegram-resolution:history` JWT permission using existing Telegram resolution grant administrators. **Migration 0191 extends those existing administrators' authority to grant and revoke history access**; review their membership before upgrading. The DB login viewer remains separately provisioned. A unique index added by 0190 rejects duplicate worker request correlations rather than returning ambiguous attribution. Protect definer owners and check inherited/PUBLIC privileges.

History contains the decision, reason, previous attempt state, DB principal/OID and recorded time. Where a JWT request record still exists, it also includes issuer, subject, client and original request ID; CLI decisions or removed attribution have no JWT identity. A missing JWT identity does not prove that a CLI made the decision. Reasons may contain sensitive operator-entered information, so restrict access and establish retention. JWT request records cascade on environment deletion; the worker resolution foreign key restricts deletion while its records exist. Privileged changes, backups and replicas are outside this audit's guarantees. The screen and CLI do not prove provider delivery.

The local API test covers JWT and DB viewer isolation, CLI/JWT attribution, revocation and a 53-record page boundary. The console tests validate history response and stale-read cancellation; the browser fixture checks a JWT decision and its history against the real Rust API and isolated local PostgreSQL. External IdP, real Telegram and multi-host HA remain unverified.
